Śrī-gīta-govinda-vyākhyāna of Prabhodānanda Sarasvatī. Bhanu Swami’s translation.

# Gīta-govinda (Prabhodānanda's ṭīkā)

## Part 1: Joyful Dāmodara

### Text 1

*sāmoda-dāmodaraḥ*  
*meghair meduram ambaraṁ vana-bhuvaḥ śyāmās tamāla-drumair*  
*naktaṁ bhīrur ayaṁ tvam eva tad imaṁ rādhe gṛhaṁ prāpaya \|*  
*itthaṁ nanda-nideśataś calitayoḥ praty-adhva-kuñja-drumaṁ*  
*rādhā-mādhavayor jayanti yamunā-kūle rahaḥ-kelayaḥ \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

meghaih - “With rain clouds; meduram - dense; ambaram - the sky \[is\], vana-bhuvah - The forest ground; syamah - \[is\] dark; tamala-drumaih - with tamala trees, naktam - \[It is\] night, bhiruh ayam - This person \[is\] trembling, tvam - You; eva - alone; tat - for this reason; imam - this boy; radhe - О Radha; grham - to home; prapaya - should take.” ittham - Thus; nanda-nidesatah - because of Nanda’s order; calitayoh - \[they are\] leaving; praty-adhva-kunja-drumam -endowed with paths, groves, and trees; radha-madhavayoh - of Radha and Madhava; jayanti - exist eternally (or exist preeminently); yamuna-kule - on the banks of Yamuna \[River\]; rahah-kelayah - the private pastimes (or amorous play).

**Перевод:**

**(Nanda ordered)“O Rādha! Take this boy home. He is trembling. It is night. The sky is dense with rain clouds and the forest ground is dark because of the tamāla trees.” Rādha and Madhava are leaving (to the bank of Yamuna) because of the order of Nanda and their private pastimes  exist eternally on the bank of the Yamunā with its paths, groves and trees.**

**Комментарий:**

The author Jayadeva offers a prayer for auspiciousness by remembering his protecting deity, in order that he may describe the sweetness of pastimes of Rādhā and Mohana (Kṛṣṇa) which increases the bliss within the lotus hearts of all his followers. Those two are the crest jewels among boys and girls of kaiśora age in Vṛndāvana and submerge the heart in an ocean of rasa which arises from their pastimes containing millions of varieties of love. Those pastimes take shelter of the deep forests endowed with charming trees and paths and a variety of groves, filled with the sweet, soft songs of swarms of virtuous bees which are greedy for the fragrance of blossoming *kadamba* flowers and *mādhavī* creepers on the bank of the Yamunā River. The couple’s lotus hands begin to quiver by the blissful order of Cupid as his lotus hand creates patterns on the budlike breasts of the queen of Vṛndāvana, the sweetest form of moon.

The commentator, though unqualified, explains this work, following after the author’s sentiments. The word *jayante* means “the pastimes exist with all excellence.” This is because of the extreme excellence of Kṛṣṇa’s *prakaṭa* and *aprakaṭa* pastimes, for it is said *krṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*: Kṛṣṇa is the full manifestation of all qualities. ((SB 1.3.28) *Rahaḥ kelayaḥ* refers to amorous play. *Raho guye rate: rahah* means secret and amorous union. Or *rahaḥ kelayaḥ* refers to the most intimate pastimes. The excellence of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes with Rādhā, above all others of similar or different type, is indicated by these words. *Yan no vihāya govindaḥ prīto yām anayad rahaḥ:* Govinda abandoned the rest of us and brought her to a secluded place. (SB 10.30.28) Or the word *kelayaḥ* by itself can indicate amorous play according to the context. This amorous play is enacted privately (*rahaḥ*) since the sakhīs are situated at some distance at this time. It will be said *yate gehād bahir avahitālī-parijane:* her attentive followers went outside the bower. (GG 11.33)

The word *jayanti* also indicates an offering of respect to indicate the beginning of a poetic work. The present tense (*jayanti*) can also be used to indicate the imperative (may the pastimes be victorious) in order to give blessings. According to *āśiṣi liṅ-loṭau* (Pāṇini 3.3.173) the imperative and potential forms indicate blessings, and even the present tense can indicate the potential form. It is suitable for the devotee at the beginning to give auspicious blessings to the Lord with great devotion since the Lord possesses the most auspicious form. The word *kelayaḥ* defines the substantial nature of the topic. Thus the great poet has indicated the beginning of the work in three ways (respect, blessings and defining the topic)

By using the present tense, the eternal nature of the pastimes is also indicated. The plural in *kelayaḥ* (pastimes) indicates that the pastimes are infinite. The great devotion of the author is evident by his great respect for the pastimes.

The place of the pastimes is indicated. They take place on the bank of the Yamunā, endowed with the paths, groves and trees (*prati adhva-kuñja-drumam*). This description indicates Kṛṣṇa’s great ability to charm. *Cāpalya-sīmaḥ:* he is the height of dexterity (*Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmrta* 74). The pastimes take place on the paths.

*kasyāḥ padāni caitāni yātāyā nanda-sūnunā*  
*aṁsa-nyasta-prakoṣṭhāyāḥ kareṇoḥ kariṇā yathā*

**Here we see the footprints of some** ***gopī*** **who must have been walking along with the son of Nanda Mahārāja. Her arm is on his shoulder, just as an elephant is accompanied by a she-elephant. SB 10.30.27**

*na lakṣyante padāny atra tasyā nūnaṁ tṛṇāṅkuraiḥ \|*  
*khidyat-sujātāṅghri-talām unninye preyasīṁ priyaḥ \|\|*

*imāny adhika-magnāni padāni vahato vadhūm \|*  
*gopyaḥ paśyata kṛṣṇasya bhārākrāntasya kāminaḥ \|\|*

*atrāvaropitā kāntā puṣpa-hetor mahātmanā \|*  
*atra prasūnāvacayaḥ priyārthe preyasā kṛtaḥ \|*  
*prapadākramaṇe ete paśyatāsakale pade \|\|*

*keśa-prasādhanaṁ tv atra kāminyāḥ kāminā kṛtam \|*  
*tāni cūḍatayā kāntām upaviṣṭam iha dhruvam \|\|*

**We can’t see her footprints over here! The lover lifted up his beloved whose tender feet were pained by the spouts. Please observe, my dear** ***gopīs*****, how in this place lusty Kṛṣṇa’s footprints are pressed more deeply into the ground because of carrying the weight of his beloved. Over here that intelligent boy must have put her down to gather some flowers. Just see how in this place dear Kṛṣṇa collected flowers for his beloved. Here he has left the impression of only the front part of his feet because he was standing on his toes to reach the flowers. Certainly this lusty person arranged the hair of the lusty woman here. He must have sat here making a crown for his beloved. SB 10.30.30-34**

*priya-sakhi parirambhān ābhimukhyānubandhād*  
*asadṛśa-viniveśān narma-laulyorjitāni \|*  
*iyam aviṣama-manda-nyāsato jalpa-goṣṭhīṁ*  
*pada-tatir iha rādhā-kṛṣṇayor ātanoti \|\|*

**Here Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa's footprints face each other, showing that here they must have embraced. Here the footprints are uneven, showing that they must have enjoyed playful pastimes. Here the footprints are soft and regular showing that they must have had a long talk. Vidagdha-mādhava** ***6.32***

*manohārī hāra-skhalita-maṇibhis tāra-taralaiḥ*  
*parimlāyan-mālyo milita-puraṭālaṅkṛti-kaṇaḥ \|*  
*ayaṁ kuñjas talpīkṛta-kusuma-puñja-praṇayavān*  
*samantād uttuṅgaṁ piśunayati raṅgaṁ murabhidaḥ \|\|*

**This charming grove, where there are scattered jewels and pearls from broken necklaces, wilted garments, fragments of golden ornaments, and a bed fashioned from many flowers, clearly shows that Kṛṣṇa enjoyed very blissful pastimes here. Vidagdha-mādhava 7.43**

To get relief from the fatigue of amorous play the words “on the bank of the Yamunā” indicate as well a wealth of water sports, gathering lotuses and the cool wind.

Whose amorous play is this? It is the play of Rādhā and Mādhava. The word Rādhā means “she who has attained (*rādhyate*) perfection in all qualities such as beauty, desired by the greatest hero among men.” Or Rādhā can means “she who brings under control the crest jewel among all heroes by the excellence of the sweetness of her extraordinary love.” *Anayārādhito nūnaṁ bhagavān harir īśvaraḥ:* Certainly this particular *gopī* has perfectly brought under control the all-powerful Lord who takes away distress. (SB 10.30.28) This name indicates her unlimited qualities.

The word Mādhava means “he who makes (*dhunoti*) beauty or the queen of Vaikuṇṭha (*mā*) tremble because of his beauty or makes her attracted to him because of his beauty.”

*yat-pāda-kalpa-taru-pallava-śekhareṣu*  
*līlā-svayaṁvara-rasaṁ labhate jaya-śrīḥ \|\|*

**Just by the beauty of the toes of his two feet, which are buds of a desire tree, the highest beauty or Lakṣmī attains joy. Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta 1**

In the above verse *jaya-śrīḥ* means “the highest beauty arising from the introduction of conjugal *rasa*.” Or it can mean Viṣṇu’s famous consort. Or it can mean “she who has the good fortune to remain eternally on the chest of her husband (*jaya)*.”

*śrīr yat padāmbuja-rajaś cakame tulasyā*  
*labdhvāpi vakṣasi padaṁ kila bhṛtya-juṣṭam*

**Goddess Lakṣmī, whose glance is sought by the devatās with great endeavor, has achieved the unique position of always remaining on the chest of her Lord, Nārāyaṇa. SB 10.29.37**

*yad-vāñchayā śrīr lalanācarat tapo*  
*vihāya kāmān su-ciraṁ dhṛta-vratā*

**For this end, the goddess of fortune performed austerities for centuries, giving up all other desires and taking austere vows. SB 10.16.36**

Mādhava can also mean “the master *(dhava*) of a wealth of qualities such as beauty (*mā* for *māyā*)” since *dhava* means master, dear one, and maintainer. Or Mādhava can mean “he who was born in the Madhu dynasty.” Or Mādhava can mean “he whose nature is *mādhyura-rasa (madhu).* It will be said *śṛṅgāraḥ sakhi mūrtimān iva*: O friend, it is as if Kṛṣṇa is the personification of conjugal rasa. (GG 1.47) Or Mādhava can mean “the person who exists in spring (the season called *madhu)*” or Cupid, according to the rules *tatra bhavaḥ* (Pāṇini 4.3.53) and *or guṇaḥ* (Pāṇini 6.4.146) It is also said *sākṣān mamatha-manmathaḥ:* he directly appeared as one who can bewilder the mind of Cupid, though Cupid himself bewilders the minds of ordinary people. (SB 10.32.3) *Strīṇāṁ smaro mūrtimān*: he was Cupid incarnate for the women of Mathurā. (SB 10.43.17) By these explanations of the meaning of Mādhava, his nature is revealed.

Specifically the pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are mentioned in the introductory verse. Though he was desired by Lakṣmī, he was attached to Rādhā. Thus Rādhā’s supreme excellence is indicated. She is mentioned first since she is predominant in amorous play because of his great reverence for her host of most exalted qualities. *Alpāc taram*: in a *dvandva* compound, the word with fewer vowels should be placed first. (Pāṇini 2.2.34) In this verse Kṛṣṇa is a hero of the type *āstika-kāmuka* (devoted to her).

*śyāmāṁ pari striyaṁ prāpya cumbanāliṅganādibhiḥ \|*  
*navāṁś ca janayed bhāvān sa syād āstika-kāmukaḥ \|\|*

**The devoted lover (*****āstika-kāmuka*****), on attaining a beautiful woman, will generate new emotions by kissing and embracing etc.**

Some explain that the gopīs are actually Lakṣmī since the Lord should enact his pastimes without material women. Thus it is said

*rāghavatve bhavet sītā rukmiṇī kṛṣṇa-janmani \|*  
*anyeṣu cāvatāreṣu viṣṇor eṣānapāyinī \|\|*

**Lakṣmī became Sītā when Rāma appeared and became Rukmiṇī when Kṛṣṇa appeared. The wife of Viṣṇu appears with other** ***avatāras*** **also. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9.144**

That Rādhā is actually Rukmiṇī is refuted by statements like *nāyaṁ śriyo’ṅga u nitānta-rateḥ prasādaḥ:* when Kṛṣṇa was dancing with the *gopīs* in the *rāsa-līlā*, the *gopīs* were embraced by the arms of the Lord, but this favor was never bestowed upon the goddess of fortune, though she was intensely attracted to him, or to other consorts in the spiritual world whose bodily luster and aroma resemble the lotus flower. (SB 10.47.60) Rukmiṇī should be understood to be perfect since it is said *mūla-prakṛti rukmiṇī:* Rukmiṇī is the primary energy. (*Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad*) However it is also said that the *gopīs* have superior *bhāvas*. *Tad bhāvam āpur api nitya-yujāṁ durāpaṁ:* the *gopīs* became totally absorbed in ecstatic meditation on him, although those who are the queens find such absorption difficult to achieve. (SB 10.82.39) *Nitya-yujām* means the queens who were eternally related to Kṛṣṇa as his wives.

Though Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are eternally the highest couple, it is hinted that the climax of *madhurya-rasa* is the appearance of *parakīya-bhāva.* It is said:

*bahu vāryate khalu yatra pracchanna-kāmukatvaṁ ca \|*  
*yā ca mitho durlabhatā sā manmathasya paramā ratiḥ \|\|*

**That love which causes great opposition because of being against normal** ***dharma*****, which necessitates the love being kept secret, and which makes it hard for the lovers to meet is the highest type of love. Bharata Muni (quoted in Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 1.20)**

*vāmatā durlabhatvaṁ ca strīṇāṁ yā ca nivāraṇā \|*  
*tad eva pañca-bāṇasya manye paramam āyudham \|\|*

**Coyness, unavailability, and rebuffing in a woman’s behavior are considered to be the ultimate weapon of Cupid. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka (quoted in Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 3.20)**

*ananya-śaraṇā svīyā dhanāhāryā parāṅganā \|*  
*asyās tu kevalaṁ prema tenaiṣā rāgiṇī matā \|\|*

**A woman completely dedicated, a woman married to oneself, a woman worth a fortune, is the best woman. She has pure love. By that love, she is respected among women lovers*****.*** **Śṛṅgāra-tilaka**

How are Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa described? They were sent off by the order of Nanda. The conventional meaning according to local tradition is that Rādhā brought Kṛṣṇa to milk the cows at her place, actually out of longing for meeting him, using bold behavior. At that time, Nanda came to her place and seeing the rain clouds and his son, dearer than millions of his lives, he spoke with loving anger. “Dear Rādhā! Since you brought him here, you should take him home.” With such words he gave the order. With that order, he would be able to sometimes dismiss certain people who were obstacles to their pastimes in order to attain his desired goal without delay.

The pastimes stimulated by a wealth of objects related to time and place always remain resplendent on the bank of the Yamunā with trees, paths and groves. Nanda regarded the clouds with fear but the couple, the crest jewels of tasting *rasa*, took the clouds as a cause of conjugal *rasa*. It is well known that Kṛṣṇa milks the cows of Vṛṣabhānu (Rādhā’s father), since he has affectionate for them. Śrīdāmā and other boys milk Nanda’s cows, dear to Kṛṣṇa.

Some say that there is a *sakhī* called Nandanī. She gave the order to the couple. However, this is not accepted since her name is not found in the scriptures. According to the scriptures, another meaning is as follows. Nanda means a person who gives joy according to the rule *nandi grahi pacādhibhyo lyuṇinnacaḥ* (Pāṇini 3.1.134) Thus the meaning is that they took the order which caused bliss (*nanda-nideśataḥ*) since it gave an opportunity for their pastimes and since was uttered by a *sakhī* of Rādhā as a joke. The *sakhīs* nourish their *rasa*.

*svātmano’py adhikaṁ prema kurvāṇānyonyam acchalam \|*  
*viśrambhiṇī vayo-veṣādibhis tulyā sakhī matā \|\|*

**The woman who gives up pretense and show great affection for the heroine, who shows great trust, and who has a similar age and dress as the heroine is known as a friend (*****sakhī*****). Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 7.70**

*mithaḥ prema-gunotkīrtis tayor āsakti-kāritā \|*  
*abhisāro dvayor eva sakhyāḥ kṛṣṇe samarpaṇam \|\|97\|\|*

*narmāśvāsana-nepathyaṁ hṛdayodghāṭa-pāṭavam \|*  
*chidra-saṁvṛtir etasyāḥ paty-ādeḥ parivañcanā \|\|98\|\|*

*śikṣā saṅgamanaṁ kāle sevanaṁ vyajanādibhiḥ \|*  
*tayor dvayor upālambhaḥ sandeśa-preṣaṇaṁ tathā \|*  
*nāyikā-prāṇa-saṁrakṣā prayatnādyāḥ sakhī-kriyāḥ \|\|*

**The activities of the** ***sakhīs*** **consist of excellently glorifying the love and qualities of Kṛṣṇa to the** ***nāyikā*** **and of the** ***nāyikā*** **to Kṛṣṇa; creating attachment between the couple; arranging meetings of the couple, offering the** ***sakhī*** **to Kṛṣṇa; joking, consoling, dressing the couple, expertly revealing the feelings of one lover to the other; hiding faults of the woman; cheating the husbands and others; advising; associating with the couple after they meet; serving the couple by fanning etc.; scolding the couple; delivering messages; and protecting the life of the** ***nāyikā*****. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 8.97-99**

The *sakhī* spoke as follows. “O my friend Rādhā! For this reason, you alone should take (*prāpaya)* to his home (*gṛham*) the *cakora* which drinks the moon of your face.” This means that she should take him to the bower. *Gṛham* means a place for experiencing all enjoyments. The deep meaning is “Give him happiness with profuse enjoyment.” You alone should do this. This indicates that all others are unqualified. Or the instruction can mean “manifest all actions of enjoyment by becoming the supreme mistress of enjoyment.” The word *gṛha* can be taken secondarily as “mistress of enjoyment (*gṛhiṇī).*” By using the word *gṛham,* the *sakhī* gives honor to Rādhā alone, since she indicates Rādhā’s pastimes in the forest. The word *prāpaya* can mean “manifest” according to Cūḍamaṇi. The word *eva* excludes others. He can be made to enjoy nicely only by you. In this way the *sakhī* expresses a secret joke. This is according to the commentary of Kumbhakarṇa, king of Mewar.

Why should they go? This person, dependent on you, is trembling out of passion (*bhīruḥ*). He is unable to tolerate the piercing of the arrows of Cupid because of his emotions. It is night. He is trembling. Take this trembling man home. There is no need to use the rule *dvitīyā ṭossvena* (Pāṇini 2.4.34) since there is only one verb.

The condition is described. The sky is dense with clouds (*meghair meduram*) and as well the forest is dark with *tamāla* trees. It is night. Thus the emotional states of place, time and objects are described. The mention of *śyāma* hints at conjugal *rasa* since the color of *mādhurya-rasa* is *śyāma. Śyāmo bhavati śṛṅgāraḥ:* conjugal *rasa* is *śyāma* in color. (Nāṭya-śāstra) The dark forests are also assistants of Rādhā and the *tamāla* trees and clouds are assistants of Kṛṣṇa.

*prema-pravṛddha uditaḥ kusumāvalībhiḥ*  
*sakhyur vyadhāt sva-vapuṣāmbuda ātapatram*

**The cloud in the sky has expanded himself out of love, rises high and constructs out of his own body, with its multitude of flower-like droplets of water, an umbrella for the sake of his friend. SB 10.21.16**

Because of the predominance of *śṛṅgāra-rasa* in this work, the clouds, forest and tamāla trees act as *uddīpanas* within *vibhāva*. Rādhā and other *sakhīs* are the *ālambana* in *vibhāva.* Trembling is the *anubhāva*. The *vyabhicāri-bhāvas* of joy, agitation, worry, eagerness, shame and fickleness will be described. This are causes and effects which assist the *sthāyi-bhāva*. The *sthāyi-bhāva* is *śṛṅgāra*, the king of all *rasas*, in the form of *vipralambha* (separation), characterized by desire, hatred and jealousy. This will be displayed along with its parts and subparts.

*kāraṇāny atha kāryāṇi sahakārīṇi yāni ca \|*  
*ratyādeḥ sthāyino loke tāni cen nāṭya-kāvyayoḥ \|\|*  
*vibhāvānubhāvās tāvat kathyante vyabhicāriṇaḥ \|*  
*vyaktaḥ sa tair vibhāvādyaiḥ sthāyi bhāvo rasaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\|*

**In drama and poetry the causes, effects and assistants of rati and other sthāyi-bhāvas are known as vibhāvas (causes), anubhāvas and vyabhicāri-bhāvas. The sthāyi-bhāva manifests along with these and is known as rasa. Kāvya-prakāśa**

These are described by Bharata Muni. *Vibhāvānubhāva-vyabhicāri-saṁyogād rasa-niṣpattiḥ:* rasa arises from the combination of *vibhava*, *anubhāva* and *vyabhicāri-bhāvas*. (Nātya-śāstra)

In this way, the author establishes the main rasa. Some who do not understand his intention may feel that the father’s order will cause fear in the production of *śṛṅgāra-rasa*, and that Kṛṣṇa is an infant and Rādhā is proud. But these misconceptions should be rejected. The *rasa* established by the author is something else. The previous explanations are correct.

*Nanda-nideśataḥ* can also mean simply “the couple went from Nanda” or “the couple went from Vraja.” Or they went from the special place within Vṛndāvana which gave bliss (*nanda)* where they had met.” In these three cases, they leave on Kṛṣṇa’s instruction. He called out to her with pain while sighing. “O Rādhā! This person is trembling since I cannot give you up.” In this case the hero is obedient (*anukūla)* and the heroine is independent (*svādhīna-bhartṛkā).* Their characteristics will be described later. The first half of the verse describes desire in separation and the second half describes union in conjugal *rasa*.

### Text 2

*vāg-devatā-carita-citrita-citta-sadmā*  
*padmāvatī-caraṇa-cāraṇa-cakravartī \|*  
*śrī-vāsudeva-rati-keli-kathā-sametaṁ*  
*etaṁ karoti jayadeva-kaviḥ prabandham \|\|2\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vag-devata - By the goddess of speech (spiritual Sarasvati); carita - extraordinary topics (or deeds or pastimes); citrita - was created (or decorated); citta-sadma - the abode of whose heart; padmavati-carana - at the feet of Padmavati \[Radha\]; carana-cakravarti - the best dancer; sri-vasudeva-of in \[Radha\] and Vasudeva \[Krsna\]; rati-keli katha - the topics of amorous pastimes; sametam - containing; etam - this; karoti - writes (or makes); jayadeva-kavih - the poet Jayadeva; prabandham - work.

**Перевод:**

**The poet Jayadeva, whose heart is the abode of extraordinary topics created by spiritual Sarasvatī, and who is the best dancer at the feet of Rādhā, writes this work containing the amorous pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

The author, whose heart is absorbed in the proper object of worship, promises to make a work filled with the highest *śṛṅgāra-rasa* by describing the pastimes of the Lord who is attractive to all people having knowledge of *rasa*.

The poet Jayadeva writes this work. The word Jayadeva means “he who reveals (*deva*) Kṛṣṇa (*jaya*), by causing most fortunate persons to engage in worship of the Lord intimately.” Or it means “the person who is enlightened by Kṛṣṇa” and is thus wise (*kaviḥ*). Or Jayadeva means “the Lord who is victory (*jaya*) and sport (*deva*)—Bhagavān Svayam.” *Jayadeva-kaviḥ* then means “the poet who is named after the Lord known as Jayadeva.” Or Jayadeva can mean “he who rejoices (*deva*) by the victory of Rādhikā (*jaya*).” *Jayadeva-kaviḥ* also means “Kṛṣṇa and the poet.” The poet and Kṛṣṇa have written this work which decisively binds up (*prabandham*) the hearts of the listeners.

The cause of the work is explained. Śrī refers to Rādhā or “one who worships (from the root *śri)* Kṛṣṇa in order to obtain rasa*.”* Or it means “she who is worshipped *(śriyate*) by Kṛṣṇa.” In any case *śrī* refers to Rādhā. Vasudeva means Nanda, since Vasudeva literally means “he who shines *(deva)* among the Vasus (*vasu*).” Explaining the previous life of Nanda, it is said *droṇo vasūnām pravaḥ*: Droṇa, the best of the Vasus, along with his wife Dhārā, spoke to Brahmā. (SB 10.8.48) Or Vasudeva means “he who shines with all wealth.” The son of Vasudeva (Nanda) is Vāsudeva, Kṛṣṇa. This name suggests that Kṛṣṇa is endowed with qualities such as complete wealth. Or *vasu* can also indicate “residence” and thus *vāsu* means “those who live in Vṛndāvana, the gopīs.” Or *vāsu* can mean “that which makes everything fragrant by the perfume of his pastimes.” These suffice *u* is added to make a noun from a verb.

*Deva* means “one who shines with all qualities like beauty” or “one who illuminates everything with his beauty.” Or it can mean “he who conquers all by his skill.” Or it can mean “he who, enjoying *śṛṅgāra-rasa*, causes taste in his devotees.” Or it can mean “he who gives joy to his devotees by his pastimes.” Or it can mean “he who is delighted by his devotees.” The root *div* means to play, to try to conquer, to shine, to praise, to enjoy, to become intoxicated, to dream, to move and to be beautiful. Thus *deva* means the person who possesses the highest pastimes, qualities, conjugal *rasa*, and enjoyment. He who is both *vāsu* and *deva* is called Vāsudeva.

Jayadeva has written this work, which endowed with topics of amorous pastimes (*rati-keli-kathā*) of Rādhā (*śrī*) and Kṛṣṇa (*vāsudeva*). He has written this work endowed with descriptions of the couple performing pastimes because of great anurāga (*rati).*

“How does one have qualification for the knowledge and conversations most inconceivable in beauty and other qualities, of the couple possessing astonishing attraction?” The poet’s heart is the dwelling place made colorful with extraordinary poetry endowed with qualities like sweetness and purity, produced by the spiritual Sarasvatī. His heart is like a house since it is the abode of the greatest wealth of various poetic forms describing the Lord’s various qualities and gives comforting shelter to all the devotees who need to rest.

Or it is said *yato vāco nivartante*: words cannot describe the Lord. (*Taittirīya Upaniṣad*) The poet’s heart is a house made astonishing because it takes on a form of eternal bliss because of the appearance of the descriptions of the Lord’s form and qualities, the highest human goal (*devatā)*, which normally cannot be described. Or the poet’s heart is a residence which has become capable of expressing in words *(citrita)* the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa, the most worthy of worship, dear to Rādhā (*vāk-devatā*). It is implied that other residences are decorated with various other types of writing whereas this poet’s residence, supremely private, is decorated with the direct appearance of the Lord and with the Lord’s personal writing of the verses. This story is well known among the devotees.

“How did the poet become such a vessel of the Lord’s mercy?” *Padmāvatī* means “she who has a lotus in her hand.” *Padma* becomes *padmā* according to the rule *śārāḍinam ca.* (Pāṇini 6.3.120) This word refers to Rādhā endowed with all auspicious qualities. *Padmā* also means “having beauty which controls the Lord.” *Cakravartī* means “the best,” having received her mercy. The poet is the best dancer (*cārana-cakravartī)* at Rādhā’s feet. He is skilful at worshipping Rādhā at all times by dancing etc. Or the poet is like a wheel by dancing (*cāraṇa)* at the feet of Rādhā. This indicates the great bhakti of the poet. *Tad-yoga-yuktā hi gaṇayanti na laukikam*: those deeply absorbed in meditation do not speak in common language.

The meaning according to common language is as follows. He is the chief of those who serve the feet of Rādhā. This indicates the poet’s absorption in *śṛṅgāra-rasa* and his qualification for writing a work on *rasa*. It is said:

*śṛṅgārī cet kaviḥ kāvye jātaṁ rasa-mayaṁ jagat \|*  
*sa eva ced a śṛṅgārī sarvaṁ virasatāṁ vrajet \|\|*

**If poet appreciates** ***śṛṅgāra-rasa*****, his work makes the whole world full of** ***rasa*****. If the poet does not appreciate** ***śṛṅgāra-rasa*****, he makes the world devoid of** ***rasa*****. Dhvanyāloka-vrṭti**

*Citra* means a picture or composition according to *Amara-koṣa. Cāraṇa* means an actor. *Kavi* means a poet or sage.

### Text 3

*yadi hari-smaraṇe sarasaṁ mano*  
*yadi vilāsa-kalāsu kutūhalam \|*  
*madhura-komala-kānta-padāvalīṁ*  
*śṛṇu tadā jayadeva-sarasvatīm \|\|3\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

yadi - If; hari-smarane - remembers Krsna; sarasam - in full of love (in madhurya-rasa)', manah -\[your\] mind; yadi - \[and\] if; vilasa - for playful (or coquettish gestures); kalasu - pastimes (or sixty-four types of arts); kutuhalam - \[you have\] eagerness; madhura -sweet; komala - easy to understand (or delicate); kanta - pleasing; padavalim - a series of verses; srnu - hear; tada - then; jayadeva-sarasvatim -Jayadeva’s eloquence.

**Перевод:**

**If you have devotion to Kṛṣṇa in madhurya-rasa, if you have eagerness for the playful pastimes, then please hear this collection of verses composed by Jayadeva, which are sweet with rasa, easy to understand and pleasing.**

**Комментарий:**

O devotees! “If the mind remembers Kṛṣṇa (*yadi hari-smaraṇe*)” means “If you have bhakti for the youthful son of Nanda who steals the hearts of all the girls of Vraja, and for no one else.” It is said:

*etāḥ paraṁ tanu-bhṛto bhuvi gopa-vadhvo*  
*govinda eva nikhilātmani rūḍha-bhāvāḥ*

**Among all persons on earth, these cowherd women alone have attained prefect bodies, for they have achieved** ***mahābhava*** **for Govinda, the source of all forms of the Lord. SB 10 47.58**

*bhaktir asya bhajanaṁ tad ihāmutropādhi-*  
*nairāsyenāmuṣmin manaḥ-kalpanam etad eva naiṣkarmyam*

**Bhakti is worship of the Lord, concentrating the mind on him, renouncing all material desires for enjoyment (*****upādhi*****) in this world and the next. It destroys all karmas. Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad, 1.15**

The word *sarasam* indicates śṛṅgāra-rasa*.* The second lines means “If you have eagerness (*kutūhalam*) for the pastimes *(kalāsu*) containing coquettish gestures *(vilāsa*) of Rādhā and other gopīs.”

*tāt-kāliko viśeṣas tu vilāso’ṅga-kriyādiṣu:*

**Vilāsa refers to actions particular to the time involving movement of the limbs. Dāśa-rūpaka**

It is said:

*skhalati bahulaṁ pāda-nyāse vilokinī… nyataḥ*  
*sthiram api tadāmbhojaṁ prayāti vidhūnanam \|*  
*smitam api mudhā vyarthaḥ krodho vṛthaiva camatkṛtir*  
*dayita-savidhaṁ yāntyās tanvyā vilāsa-raso’py ayam \|\|*

**Glancing, she stumbles excessively while walking. She begins to shake a stationary lotus. Smiling is useless. Anger is useless. Astonishment is useless. This is the sweetness of** ***vilāsa*** **of the slender woman going towards her lover.**

The first two lines describe the devotion of the devotees of the Lord. *Kalā* means a phase of the moon and pastimes according to *Viśva.*

If you have devotion for the Lord and have eagerness for the pastimes involving the *gopīs*, then *(tadā)* please hear *(śṛnu*) the poem written by Jayadeva. Or please understand with reverence (*śṛṇuta*), O people with great piety *(dā*)! *Kaḷāsu* can also refer to the sixty-four types of action in *rati.*

*hasitaṁ gītaṁ paṭhitaṁ nṛtyaṁ tathā vīṇā veṇu-vādyaṁ*  
*tālaḥ pustaka-jñānam āyuṣo vedaḥ \|*

**Joking, singing, reciting, dancing, playing the vīṇā and flute, playing precussion instruments, studying books, ayurveda.**

*dhanur-vedaś ca śikṣā ca rāja-śikṣā ca ratnayoḥ śikṣā \|*  
*kalpo vyākaraṇaṁ chando jyotir niruktikaḥ \|\|*

**Military arts, teaching, learning to govern, gemology, rules for sacrifice, grammar, meter, astrology, etymology.**

*para-bhāṣā purāṇaṁ ca smṛtī rāmāyaṇas tathā \|*  
*mahābhāratam ākhyānaṁ rāja-lakṣaṇa-vedanām \|*

**Speaking foreign languages, explaining Purāṇas, Smṛtis and Rāmāyaṇa, and Mahābhaṛata, recognition of kingly qualities.**

*niyuddha-yuddhottaraṇaṁ bhūta-tantra-prahelikā \|*  
*sarpāśī-viṣa-saṁrodho bhūmi-dṛṣṭīṣṭa-sādhanam \|*

**Contact fighting, war, crossing rivers, invoking ghosts, solving riddles, counteracting snake venom, inspecting soil, obtaining desires.**

*cintā-nivartanaṁ puṣṭi-vartanaṁ piṇḍa-karṣaṇam \|*  
*vikalpoccāra-karaṇaṁ vicitra-kara-mardanam \|\|*

**Giving up worries, nourishing the body, adding sums, extracting other’s thoughts, rubbing to produce pictures.**

*likhitaṁ citra-kavitā rājanītis tathā parā \|*  
*sabhā-pāṇḍitya-vaktṛtvaṁ durga-senāri-vīkṣaṇam \|*

**Writing, painting, politics, talking in public, inspecting enemy, troops and fortress.**

*aṅga-saṁvāhanaṁ cāru-darśanaṁ copalakṣaṇam \|*  
*prasādanaṁ tu ruṣṭasya strī-citta-haraṇaṁ tathā \|\|*

**Massaging, dressing up, using metaphors, pacifying an angry person, and attracting the hearts of women.\[\[1\]\]**

This poem of Jayadeva is a series of verses (*padāvalīm*) which are sweet (*madhura*), very soft, since they are easily understood, and pleasant *(kantā*), since they can be sung. The word *madhura, sukumāra* and *kānta* are explained in the following verses:

*madhuraṁ rasavad vāci vastuny api rasasthitiḥ \|*  
*yena mādhyanti dhīmanto madhuneva madhuvratāḥ \|\|*

***Madhura*** **means filled with** ***rasa.*** **It is situated in words and objects. The intelligent people who take joy in this sweetness just as bees take joy in honey. Kāvyādarśa 1.51**

*suprayogair mahac-chabdair yuktam aspaṣṭa-sandhibhiḥ \|*  
*sukumārārtha-saṁyuktaṁ sukumāraṁ pracakṣate \|\|*

**A work is called soft (*****sukumāra)*** **when it is easy ot understand because of eloquent words, used properly, with absence of elision of vowels. Līlāravinda 2**

*janmanaḥ śrotṛ-viśadaṁ prahlāda-janakaṁ bhavet*  
*śabda-bandhānuyogena tat kāntam iti kīrtitam \|\|*

**The father of Prahlāda gives lamentation to the listener from his birth. However, by skilful use of words, the story becomes pleasing (*****kānta*****).**

There are two types of sweetness: that which takes shelter of sound *(śabda*) and that which takes shelter of meaning (*artha).* There is softness of sound and softness (ease) of meaning. There are also harsh words which may be easy to understand such as “death, the end of fame.” There is also pleasure through sound and meaning. Hearing skilful verses pleasing to the listener takes shelter of sound. When the meaning is sweet it creates intense rasa. *Rasa* means loving relationships like conjugal *rasa*.

These verses have shown the subject matter, the goal, the sambandha and the qualification.\[\[2\]\]*Rahaḥ-kelayaḥ jayanti* (May the private pastimes remain glorious) indicates the subject matter. The goal is attraction for the qualities of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, who are endowed with all qualities. The sambandha (relationship of subject to the verses) is that the poem (*pratikapādaka*) describes only its intended subject - the pastimes Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa (*pratipādya*). The qualification is the heart steeped in this *rasa*, as explained in this verse. In this way the four anubandhas have been shown.

### Text 4

*vācaḥ pallavayaty umāpati-dharaḥ sandarbha-śuddhiṁ girāṁ*  
*jānīte jayadeva eva śaraṇaḥ ślāghyo durūha-druteḥ\[\[3\]\]\|*  
*śṛṅgārottara-sat-prameya-racanair ācārya-govardhana-*  
*spardhī ko'pi na viśrutaḥ sruti-dharo dhoyī kavi-kṣmāpatiḥ \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vacah - Words; pallavayati - extends; umapati-dharah - Umapati-dhara. sandarbha-suddhirn - Excellent poetry endowed with qualities and ornaments (or the purity of the arragement); giram -in its expression (or of poetry) janite- knows; jayadeva -Jayadeva; eva - alone, saranah - Sarana; slaghyo - \[is\] praiseworthy; duruha-druteh - because of \[his\] cryptic meaning and rapidity, srngarottara - in which excellent srngara-rasa (or in which srngara-rasa is preeminent), sat-prameya-racanaih - Because of \[his\] truly exemplary compositions; acarya-govardhana - with Govardhana Acarya; spardhi - who can compete; ko’pi na - no one; visrutah - is known, sruti-dharah - \[Is able to\] remember by hearing only once; dhoyi - Dhoyi; kavi-ksamapatih - the king of poets.

**Перевод:**

**Umapati dhara writes profusely and Śaraṇa writes quickly, but with cryptic meaning. No one can compete with Govardhana who writes of excellent śṛṅgāra-rasa as an example for all. Dhoyī, the king of poets, remembers by hearing only once. But Jayadeva knows excellent poetry endowed with qualities and ornament.**

**Комментарий:**

The poet Umapati-dhara, a minister of Lakṣmaṇa Sena, writes extensively - but without suggestive meaning and without attractive qualities of sound or meaning. This is hinted at in this verse. The poet Śaraṇa is praiseworthy in composing verses quickly but which are difficult to understand. His poetry is without qualities like clarity (*prasāda*) and faulted with abstruse meanings. No one is known to compete with Govardhanācārya in writing exemplary poetic works in which there is excellence of *śrṅgāra-rasa* or predominance of *śṛṅgāra-rasa*. But he cannot write works endowed with qualities like sweetness, since he is preoccupied with material *rasa*. The word *ācārya* is used with mockery, since Govardhana was not an *ācārya* of the best spiritual poetry, since he gave material instructions. The kingly (*kṣamā-patiḥ*) poet named Dhoyī learned scriptures just by hearing them. This indicates that he is not expert at poetry. The word *kṣamā-patiḥ* suggests his pride and materialistm. Jayadeva alone and no one else knows excellent poetry endowed with qualities and ornaments (*sandarbha-śuddhim*) in its expression (*girām*), since his work describes the qualities of the Lord. It is said in Bhāgavatam:

*na yad vacaś citra-padaṁ harer yaśo*  
*jagat-pavitraṁ pragṛṇīta karhicit \|*  
*tad vāyasaṁ tīrtham uśanti mānasā*  
*na yatra haṁsā niramanty uśik-kṣayāḥ \|\|*

**Works whose attractive verses do not sing at all the glories of the Lord, which purify not only the author but the whole world, are considered by the devotees who have captured the mind of the Lord to be the place for crows, in which swans, living in pleasant lakes, take no enjoyment at all.**

*tad-vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavo*  
*yasmin prati-ślokam abaddhavaty api \|*  
*nāmāny anantasya yaśo ’ṅkitāni yac*  
*chṛṇvanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ*

**The use of words in which, though not perfectly composed, there are the names of the Lord and descriptions of his glories which devotees hear, sing, and again receive, destroys the sins of all people. SB.5.10-11**

Thus Jayadeva shows the greatness of his composition since it is endowed with excellent sound and meaning. That is, it is filled with *rasa* because it is endowed with excellent ornaments and devoid of faulty subject matter. It is said:

*nirdoṣaṁ guṇavat kāvyam alaṅkārair alaṅkṛtam \|*  
*rasānvitaṁ kaviḥ kurvan kīrtiṁ bhaktiṁ ca vindati \|\|*

**The poet, by writing poetry without fault and full of good qualities, properly ornamented and filled with rasa attains fame and devotion. Sarasvatī-kaṇṭhābharaṇa 1.2**

Viśva says that *pallava* means a bud, a bush, extensive or strong. *Amara-koṣa* says *drutam* means light or fast. *Uttaram* means an answer.

## 1: daśa-avatāra

**\|\| prathama-prabandhaḥ \|\|**  
**mālava-gauḍa-rāgeṇa rūpaka-talena giyate \| aṣṭāpadī \|**

### Text 5

*pralaya-payodhijale dhṛtavān asi vedaṁ*  
*vihita-vahitra-caritram akhedam \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-mīna-śarīra*  
*jaya jagadīśa hare \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

pralaya - Of destruction; payodhijale - in the waters of ocean; dhrtavan asi - you protect; vedam - the Vedas; vihita-vahitra -is made \[like\] a boat; caritram - whose behavior; akhedam - easily. kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; mina-sarira - by whom the form of a fish! jaya - Victory to you! jagadisa - О protector of the worlds (or О controller of \[all fourteen\] worlds); hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You easily protect the Vedas in the waters of destruction, while acting as a boat. O Keśava who has assumed the form of a fish! Victory to you! O protector of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

It was previously said “May the pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa shine eternally.” How is it possible to attain rasa by parakīya relationship, since that becomes filled with fear and condemnation and is considered vulgar? How can such a relationship shine eternally?

*asvargyam ayaśasyaṁ ca phalgu kṛcchraṁ bhayāvaham*  
*jugupsitaṁ ca sarvatra hy aupapatyaṁ kula-striyaḥ*

**For a woman from a respectable family, petty adulterous affairs are always condemned. They bar her from heaven, ruin her reputation and bring her difficulty and fear. SB 10.29.36**

The author removes this fault by establishing that this relationship is filled with eternal bliss, through invoking auspiciousness by offering respects, in order to remove all obstacles. In this prayer he establishes the supreme position of the lover Kṛṣṇa, who has the form of an uncontrolled young cowherd boy endowed with pastimes which give an experience of spiritual rasa, possessing the qualities of the avatāras, having all astonishing powers as the controller of all beings, arising from the fact that he is defined as the possessor of all astonishing śaktis. In confirmation it is said:

*gopīnāṁ tat-patīnāṁ ca sarveṣām eva dehinām*  
*yo ’ntaś carati so ’dhyakṣaḥ krīḍaneneha deha-bhāk*

**Kṛṣṇa, appearing directly in this world, moves among the** ***gopīs*** **and their apparent husbands as well as the other inhabitants of Vraja, in his eternal spiritual body performing his pastimes.**

*anugrahāya bhaktānāṁ mānuṣaṁ deham āsthitaḥ*  
*bhajate tādṛśīḥ krīḍā yāḥ śrutvā tat-paro bhavet*

**When the Lord assumes a humanlike body to show mercy to his devotees, he engages in such pastimes. Hearing about those pastimes, a person becomes dedicated to him. SB 10.33.35-36**

The Lord speaks in glorification of the gopīs.

*evaṁ mad-arthojjhita-loka-veda*  
*svānām hi vo mayy anuvṛttaye ’balāḥ*  
*mayāparokṣaṁ bhajatā tirohitaṁ*  
*māsūyituṁ mārhatha tat priyaṁ priyāḥ*

**My dear girls, understanding that simply for my sake you had rejected the authority of worldly opinion, of the Vedas and of your relatives, I acted as I did only to increase your attachment to me. Even when I removed myself from your sight by suddenly disappearing, I never stopped loving you. Therefore, my beloved gopīs, please do not harbor any bad feelings toward me, your beloved.**

*na pāraye ’haṁ niravadya-saṁyujāṁ*  
*sva-sādhu-kṛtyaṁ vibudhāyuṣāpi vaḥ*  
*yā mābhajan durjara-geha-śṛṅkhalāḥ*  
*saṁvṛścya tad vaḥ pratiyātu sādhana*

**I am not able to repay my debt for your spotless service, even within a lifetime of Brahmā. Your connection with me is beyond reproach. You have worshiped me, cutting off all domestic ties, which are difficult to break. Therefore please let your own glorious deeds be your compensation. SB 10.32.21-22**

It is has been said here that this lover is the supreme lord and the gopīs are eternally his consorts, the highest forms of prema-rasa. The conclusion proved here is that this relationship is the shelter of unlimited rasa and is the highest excellence. The Lord condemns the normal parakīya relationship:

*asvargyam ayaśasyaṁ ca phalgu kṛcchraṁ bhayāvaham*  
*jugupsitaṁ ca sarvatra hy aupapatyaṁ kula-striya*

**For a woman from a respectable family, petty adulterous affairs are always condemned. They bar her from heaven, ruin her reputation and bring her difficulty and fear. SB 10.29.26**

However, that state concerns material relationships is stated in order to test the love of the gopīs by using crooked words in order to increase rasa.

The characteristics of *mālava-rāga* ***\[\[4\]****\]*are as follows:

*nitambinī-cumbita-vaktra-padmaḥ*  
*śuka-dyutiḥ kuṇḍalavān pramattaḥ \|*  
*saṅgīta-śālāṁ praviśan pradoṣe*  
*mālādharo mālava-rāga-rājaḥ \|\|*

**The king named Mālava-rāga has a lotus face kissed by a slender waisted woman. He has the complexion of a parrot, wears earrings and a garland and is lascivious. He enters the music room in the twilight.**

*rūpake syād drutaṁ laghu*

Rūpaka-tāla is 2 + 4 = 6

The meaning of the verse is as follows. O Keśava! Victory to you! Keśava means “the Lord who controls (*vaś)* Brahmā (*ka*) and Śiva (*īśa*).” May you remain with all excellence (*jaya)*, by causing bliss to your devotees! “How is it possible to create happiness for my devotees?” You are the controller of all fourteen worlds (*jagadīśa*)! How is it not possible for the controller of the fourteen worlds to create that happiness? There is another reason why it is possible. You remove the suffering of the devotees (*hare*). The nature of the Lord to act for the devotees, though the Lord should not assume such forms, is then shown by the description of each avatāra. For instance, the Lord assumes the form of a fish. The Lord appears *(dhṛta*) in that form by his free will. The chorus is repeated with each verse.

A verse has four parts:

*udgrāho prathamo bhāgas tato melāpakaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*dhruvatvāc ca dhruvaḥ paścād ābhogaś cātiśobhanaḥ \|*

**The introductory portion is called** ***udgrāha*** **and then next portion is called** ***melāpaka*** **(bringing together). The third part is called** ***dhruva*** **because it is fixed. The last portion is called** ***ābhoga*** **(expansion), which is most splendid.**

Though the words describing the particular avatāra vary in each chorus (*dhruva*) the words are identical.

“Why did I appear in the form of a fish?” This is explained in the rest of the verse. Assuming the body of a fish, you held up the Vedas which had sunk in the water of the ocean of pralaya, by acting as a boat (*vahitra)* without being distressed (*akhedam*)--since you are the source of the highest bliss. Or you supported knowledge (*vedam*) by giving the Vedas to Satyavrata. Because of love of your devotees, you give no regard for hardships (*akhedam*).

The word *payodhi* (ocean) indicates water. *Jale* (water) repeats this. This repetition is employed to indicate that there was no place to take rest. There were no islands in that vast expanse of water. Still, you held up the Vedas. You did this by assuming the body of a fish. This indicates the Lord’s inclination to act on behalf of his devote*e. Mīna* means fish according to *Amara-koṣa.*

### Text 6

*kṣitir ativipulatare tava tiṣṭhati pṛṣṭhe*  
*dharaṇī-dharaṇa-kiṇa-cakra-gariṣṭhe \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-kacchapa-rūpa*  
*jaya jagadīśa hare \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ksitih - The Earth; ativipulatare - very wide; tava - your; tisthati - stands; prsthe - on \[your\] back; dharani-dharana - of holding up the earth; kina-cakra - because of the round scar; garisthe -venerable, kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; kacchapa-rupa - by whom the form of a turtle! jaya - Victory to you! jagadisa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**The earth remains on your wide back, worshipped because of the round scar created by holding up the earth. O Keśava who has assumed the form of turtle! Victory to you! O protector of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

Victory, O Keśava who assumed the form of a turtle. The reason is explained. The earth is situated on your back. The present tense indicates that even now the earth is situated there. This means that the act is famous. “How can the earth which is 500 million yojanas in breadth be situated on my back?” Your back is wider that the earth (*ativipulatare*). Your back is most venerable (*gariṣṭhe*) since it was marked with a circular scar from holding up the earth. Or your back is marked with a huge circular scar from holding up the earth. *Kacchapa* means turtle according to *Amara-koṣa. Kiṇa* means a scar according to *Hārāvalī*. The Lord assumes a burden such as holding up the earth because of his devotees.

### Text 7

*vasati daśana-śikhare dharaṇī tava lagnā*  
*śaśini kalaṅka-kaleva nimagnā \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-sūkara-rūpa*  
*jaya jagadīśa hare \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vasati - Remains; dasana-sikhare - to the tip of your tusk; dharani -the Earth; tava - your; lagna - stuck; sasini - to the moon; kalanka-kaleva - like a small spot; nimagna - attached, kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; sukara-rupa - by whom the form of a boar! jaya - Victory to you! jagadisa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**The earth remains stuck to the tip of your tusk, attached there like a spot on the crescent moon. O Keśava, who assumes the form of a boar! Victory to you! O protector of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

O Keśava, who assumed the form of a boar! The purpose in appearing as a boar is described. The earth, which supports all people, rests, stuck on the tip of your tusks, like a small spot attached *(nimagna*) to the moon (his tusks). The phrase suggests the long, thin shape of the young waxing moon. The phrase is thus not useless in the verse. The earth is also nourished by contact with the tusks. *Daśana* means tooth according to *Amara-koṣa. Śikhara* means the tip or the peak of a mountain. According to *Viśva, kalā* means a small portion or a sixteenth phase of the moon.

### Text 8

*tava kara-kamala-vare nakham adbhuta-śṛṅgaṁ*  
*dalita-hiraṇyakaśipu-tanu-bhṛṅgam \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-nara-hari-rūpa*  
*jaya jagad-īśa hare \|\|8\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tava - Your; kara-kamala-vare - on \[your\] hands, which are the best lotuses; nakham - there are claws; adbhuta-srngam - with astonishing tips; dalita - have torn apart; hiranyakasipu - \[demon\] Hiranyakasipu; tanu-bhrngam - wasp-like body, kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; nara-hari-riipa - by whom the form of a manlion! jaya - Victory to you! jagadlsa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**On your lotus hands you have claws with astonishing tips, which tore apart the wasp-like body of Hiraṇyakaśipu. O Keśava who assumed the form of a man-lion! Victory to you! O protector of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

O Keṣava, who assumed the form of Narasimḥa! The purpose of the *avatāra* is explained. In your hands which are the best lotuses, are claws whose tips (*śṛṅgam*) are astonishing. Why are they astonishing? They tore up the wasp-like body of the demon Hiraṇyakaśipu. The wasp is inclined to tear up flower petals. The claws of Narasiṁha, which were like flower petals tore up the wasp of Hiraṇyakaśipu. This is astonishing. By describing the demon’s huge body as a wasp, the extreme softness of Narasiṁha’s lotus hands is indicated. In order to frighten Hiraṇyakaśipu, who tormented devotees, the Lord assumed the body of a lion.

*Hari* means a lion acccording to *Amara-koṣa. Śṛṅga* means tip according to *Viśva*. *Nakha* means nails and *tanu* means body according to *Amara-koṣa. Bhṛṅga* means a wasp or bee.

### Text 9

*chalayasi vikramaṇe balim adbhuta-vāmana*  
*pada-nakha-nīra-janita-jana-pāvana \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-vāmana-rupa*  
*jaya jagad-īśa hare \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

chalayasi - You cheat; vikramane - in \[your\] steps; balim - \[King\] Bali; adbhuta-vamana - О astonishing dwarf; pada-nakha - on \[your\] toenail; rupa - with the water; janita - created; jana-pavana - who purifies the people! kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; vamana-rupa - by whom the form of a dwarf! jaya - Victory to you! jagadisa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**O astonishing dwarf! You cheated Bali by taking huge steps. O purifier of all people by the water touched by your toenail! O Keśava who assumed the form a dwarf! Victory to you! O protector of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

O Keśava, who assumed the form of a dwarf! Victory to you! The reason for this appearance is given. O astonishing dwarf (*adbhuta-vāmana*)! You cheated Bali, the king of the demons, by taking steps. Upendra, taking the form of Vāmana the dwarf, captured Bali, since he could not fulfill the third step by offering land and he then gave the three worlds to Indra. This astonishing act is well known in the Purāṇas. It is also astonishing that the Lord helped his devotee Indra by using deception, since he is controlled by his devotee. O Lord who purifies people by the Gaṅgā water created by your toe nail! Expanding himself with the three steps, the Lord pierced the earthly shell of the universe with the tip of the toenail of his big toe and then washed his lotus feet by that method. The water from the second layer of the universal shell, the water used by Brahmā as *arghya*, eventually came to the earth as the Gaṅgā and flows over people. This is famous in the Purāṇas. Expanding himself while standing on earth, by trickery, Vāmana was able to purify all the worlds by the water which touched his toe nail, without using water from within the universe. That is astonishing. The Lord’s great power is astonishing. Though he cheated Bali in the past, at present he remains near Bali. Thus the present tense is used in the verse. Ability to purify is a good quality in a hero. *Nīra* means water according to *Amara-koṣa*. Bali refers to the demon according to *Viśva*.

### Text 10

*kṣatrīya-rudhira-maye jagad apagata-pāpaṁ*  
*snapayasi payasi śamita-bhava-tāpam \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-bhṛgu-pati-rūpa*  
*jaya jagad-īśa hare \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ksatnya-rudhira-maye - Full of blood of the Ksatriyas; jagad -world; apagata papam-whose sin is \[thereby\] destroyed; snapayasi - you cause to bathe; payasi - in the lake; samita-bhava-tapam - \[and\] whose heat of samsara is thereby dispelled, kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; bhrgu-pati-rupa - by whom the form of Bhrgupati (Parasurama)! jaya - Victory to you! jagadisa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You made the world bathe and become purified of sin in the lake of kṣatriya blood and thus the world was free of saṁsāra*****.*** **O Keśava, assuming the form of Paraśurāma! Victory to you! O controller of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

O Keśava who took the form of Paraśurāma. Victory to you! The reason for his appearance is described. You make the people bathe so all their sins are destroyed (*apagata-pāpam*) in the lake *(payasi*) at Kurukṣetra produced from kṣatriyas’ blood. Killing the *kṣatriyas* twenty-one times, Paraśurāma made five lakes at Kurukṣetra from their blood and then performed tarpana. Since that time, the place has been a great place of pilgrimage. This is famous in the Purāṇas. The earth became free of saṁsāra *(bhava)*: by bathing at this site, living beings become free of all sin. For one who is repentant, by bathing in the water all suffering is destroyed. *Rudhira* means blood and *bhava* means *saṁsāra* according to *Viśva.* This action of the Lord shows that he acts for the devotees by destroying unlawful kṣatriyas in order to maintain *dharma*. The Lord’s strength and his ability to purify are also shown by creating purity, usually achieved only after millions of efforts, simply by bathing in the lake made of blood (usually considered impure).

### Text 11

*vitarasi dikṣu raṇe dik-pati-kamanīyaṁ*  
*daśa-mukha-mauli-bali ramaṇīyam \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-rāma-śarīra\[\[5\]\]*  
*jaya jagad-īśa hare \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vitarasi - You bestow; diksu - to the \[ten\] directions; rane - on the battlefield; dik-pati-kamaniyam - as desired by the devatas of the directions; dasa-mukha-mauli - the heads of ten-headed (Ravana); bali - as an offering; ramaniyam - gives relief \[to the world\]. kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; rama-sarira - by whom the form of Rama! jaya - Victory to you! jagadisa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You offered the ten heads of Rāvaṇa to the ten directions on the battlefield. This was pleasing to the devatās and gave relief to the world. O Keśava who assumes the form of Rāma! Victory to you! O protector of the words! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

O Keśava who assumes the form of Rāma! Victory to you! The activities of Rāma are described. On the battlefield, you distribute to the ten directions the heads of Rāvaṇa as desired (*kamanīyam*) by the devatās of the directions, since his death gave relief to the world (*ramaṇīyam*). Ten heads are mentioned to indicate each of them individually. *Mauli* means head. This action shows that the Lord is inclined to act for his devotees and protect the public even though he is served by them. The action shows his astonishing heroism and skill since he offered each head individually to each direction.

### Text 12

*vahasi vapuṣi viśade vasanaṁ jaladābhaṁ*  
*hala-hati-bhīti-milita-yamunābham \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-hala-dhara-rūpa*  
*jaya jagad-īśa hare \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vahasi - You wear; vapusi visade - on \[your\] white body; vasanam - a garment; jaladabham - blue like the raincloud; hala-hati - being injured by your plough; bhiti milita - afflicted by the fear of; yamunabham - shines like that Yamuna! kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; hala-dhara-rupa - by whom the form of Haladhara (Balarama)! jay a - Victory to you! jagadlsa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**On your white body you wear cloth which is blue like a raincloud, which resembles the Yamunā afflicted by fear of being injured by your plough. O Keśava who takes the form of Balarāma! Victory to you! O protector of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

O Keśava who takes the form of Balarāma! Victory to you! His pastime is explained. You wear cloth blue like the color of a rain cloud (*jaladābham*) on your white (*viśade*) body. That cloth shone like (*ābham*) the Yamunā when it was overcome (*milita*) with fear of being killed by your plough (*hala-hati-bhīti*). The cloth fluttering in the Malaya breeze is compared to the Yamunā when overcome with fear. Those in fear tremble. One time Balarāma drank Vāruṇī in Vṛndāvana with the *gopīs* and called the Yamunā in order to play in the water. Yamunā, thinking Balarāma was babbling because of intoxication, did not come to him out of pride. He became angry and pulled her towards him with his plough. The story is famous in the Purāṇas. In this avatāra, his attachment to the gopīs is seen in this pastime of pulling the Yamunā in order to enjoy along with his dear gopīs. The word Haladhara also suggests that he holds the plough to punish the wicked. Thus he engages in actions for the devotees. This is the purpose of this avatāra*. Vapu* means body according to *Amara-koṣa. Viśada* means white. *Vasanam* means cloth*. Bhīti* means trembling in fear according to Viśva.

### Text 13

*nindasi yajña-vidher ahaha śruti-jātam*  
*sadaya-hṛdaya darśita-paśu-ghātam \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-buddha-śarīra*  
*jaya jagad-īśa hare \|\|13\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

nindasi - You criticize; yajna-vidheh - because it enjoins sacrifices; ahaha - alas; sruti-jatam - those portions of the Vedas; sadaya-hrdaya - having a compassionate heart; darsita-pasu-ghatam - in which the killing of animals is explained, kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; buddha-sarira - by whom the form of Buddha! jaya - Victory to you! jagadisa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You criticize those portions of the Vedas which teach sacrifice of animals. O Lord with compassionate heart! O Keśava who assumes the form of Buddha! Victory to you! O protector of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

O Keśava who accepts the form of Buddha! Victory to you! “How can one praise a person who criticizes the Vedas?” In this form, you criticize all those statements in the Vedas (*śruti-jātam*) concerned with sacrifices *(yajña-vidheḥ*), but you do not criticize all the Vedas. Why is this part criticized? Oh what disaster (*ahaha*)! This part explained (*darśita*) the killing of animals (*paśu-ghātam*). You criticize this portion of the Vedas because it teaches killing of animals. Why do you criticize it? O Lord with a compassionate heart!

The meaning is this. Seeing uncontrolled attachment, he considered that sacrifices which prescribed killing many innocent animals for elevation to temporary enjoyment in Svarga after death, though following the path of the Vedas, were not beneficial for people. The statements in the Vedas advised killing animals while promising rewards of Svarga. He found these statements senseless and then propounded other statements on the path of renunciation for attaining the highest goal in life, such as *na hiṁsyāt sarva-bhūtāni*: one should not harm any animal. Thus you criticized the portions of the Veda which taught sacrifice, but not all the Vedas. Not understanding this intention, unfortunate people lost faith in the Vedas completely. You accepted this avatāra form to bewilder those people. The purpose of this avatāra is to fool those who are opposed to the Vedas of the highest benefit, for the benefit to your devotees. The word *ahaha* can also indicate astonishment according to *Viśva. Śruti* refers to the Vedas according to *Amara-koṣa. Jātam* means “coming from” according to *Viśva.*

### Text 14

*mleccha-nivaha-nidhane kalayasi karavālaṁ*  
*dhūma-ketum iva kim api karālam \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-kalki-śarīra*  
*jaya jagad-īśa hare \|\|14\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

mleccha-nivaha - All the mlecchas (sinful people); nidhane - to destroy; kalayasi - you wield; karavalam - a sword; dhuma-ketum - a comet; iva - like; kim api - very; karalam - fearful, kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - was assumed; kalki-sarira - by whom the form of Kalki! jaya - Victory to you! jagadisa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You wield a fearful sword resembling a comet in order to destroy all the sinful people. O Keśava who assumes the form of Kalki! Victory to you! O protector of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

O Keśava who assumes the form of Kalki. Victory to you! His activities are described. You wield a sword (*karavālam*) to destroy (*nidhane*) all the mleccas (*mleccha-nivaha*). That sword is most fearful (*karālam*), like a comet (*dhūma-ketum*), since it destroys the mlecchas when they see him on the battlefield. When living entities see a comet it indicates disaster for them. Or the sword is like fire, whose sign is smoke (*dhūma-ketum*). The sword is dark like smoke and the handle is made of gold, shining like fire.

*Mleccha* means a sinful person according to *Viśva. Nivaha* means “all” according to *Amara-koṣa. Nidhanam* means destruction according to *Viśva. Karāvala* is a sword according to *Amara-koṣa. Dhūmaketu* is a comet according to *Viśva. Karāla* means frightful according to *Viśva.* The present tense is used in all these descriptions of *avatāras* to indicate the eternal nature of the Lord’s pastimes. After praising the Lord by showing the qualities in his *avatāras*, now the author praises the source of the *avatāras*.

### Text 15

*śri-jayadeva-kaver idam uditam udāraṁ*  
*śṛṇu sukhadaṁ śubhadaṁ bhava-sāram \|*  
*keśava dhṛta-daśa-vidha-rūpa*  
*jaya jagad-īśa hare \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva-kaveh - By the poet Sri jayadeva; idam - this \[composition\]; uditam -spoken; udaram - delivers the highest goal (or great); srnu - listen; sukhadam - which gives bliss; subhadam - which gives auspiciousness; bhava-saram - \[and\] delivers \[everyone\] from material life (or \[which describes\] the essence of \[his\] birth), kesava - О Kesava; dhrta - were assumed; dasa-vidha-rupa - by whom ten forms! jaya - Victory to you! jagadisa - О protector of the worlds! hare - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**Please listen to this composition spoken by the poet Jayadeva which gives bliss, bestows the highest goal, spreads auspiciousness and delivers everyone from material life. O Keśava who assumes ten forms! Victory to you! O controller of the worlds! O destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

O Keśava who assumes ten forms! Victory to you! Keśava means one who resides *(śete)* with unlimited bliss (*ke*). Though you have unlimited bliss, you assume ten forms. By these actions on behalf of your devotees you announce your glories. Thus I take great pleasure in worshipping you. Victory to you for giving happiness to your devotees! Hear this composition (*idam*) spoken (*uditam*) by the poet Jayadeva, which gives satisfaction to the ear and mind (*sukhadam*) and gives auspiciousness *(śubhadam*) since the work explains the secret or real meaning *(sāram*) of his birth (*bhava*). Or it is the best object (*sāram*) in one’s life since it makes one’s birth successful by bestowing the highest human goal. It is also most generous, giving the highest goal *(udāram)* or it is the greatest, or quickly gives results. These modifiers are employed to glorify Kṛṣṇa and include his devotees as well. *Uditam* means spoken. *Sāra* means the best according to *Viśva.* After praising the avatāras who are his portions, in the next verse the author offers respects to Kṛṣṇa, the crown jewel of all heroes, the perfect form worthy of description in this work.

### Text 16

*vedān uddharate jaganti vahate bhūgolam udbibhrate*  
*daityaṁ dārayate baliṁ chalayate kṣatra-kṣayaṁ kurvate \|*  
*paulastyaṁ jayate halaṁ kalayate kāruṇyam ātanvate*  
*mlecchān mūrcchayate daśākṛti-kṛte kṛṣṇāya tubhyaṁ namaḥ \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vedan - The Vedas; uddharate - he saves, jaganti - The Worlds; vahate - he supports, bhugolam - The Earth; udbibhrate - he lifts. daityam - Daitya (Hiranyakasipu); darayate - he tears apart, balim - Bali; chalayate - he cheats, ksatra-ksayam - The destruction of the ksatriyas; kurvate - he brings about, paulastyam - descendant of Pulatsya (Ravana); jayate - he conquers, halam - The plough; kalayate - he wields, karunyam - Mercy; atanvate - he distributes; mlecchan - The sinful; murcchayate - he prevails against, dasakrti -\[Your\] ten forms; krte - for the sake of; krsnaya - to Krsna; tubhyam - to you; namah - my respects.

**Перевод:**

**You save the Vedas, support the worlds, lift up the earth, tear apart Hiraṇyakaśipu, cheat Bali, destroy the kṣatriyas, conquer Rāvaṇa, wield the plough, distribute mercy and destroy the sinful. I offer respects to you, Kṛṣṇa, who assume these ten forms.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa is the full embodiment of the highest powers:

*kṛṣir bhū-vācakaḥ śabdo ṇaś ca nirvṛti-vācakaḥ \|*  
*tayor aikyaṁ paraṁ brahma kṛṣṇa ity abhidhīyate \|\|*

***Kṛṣ*** **means “existence” and** ***ṇa*** **means “bliss.” Combined together, the two roots indicate that Kṛṣṇa is the supreme form of God.**

Kṛṣṇa also means “he who attracts his devotees by his supreme sweetness.” I offer respects to you Kṛṣṇa - form of the unlimited, highest, most intense bliss, giver of enjoyment to the devotees, Śyāmasundara, the complete form of *śṛṅgāra-rasa.* Śruti says *akṣarāt parataḥ paraḥ*: the Lord is superior to the Brahman. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad) *Parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śrūyate svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca:* the Lord has various superior, intrinsic energies of knowledge, strength and action. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad) Bhagavad-gītā says *aksārāt api cottamaḥ*: he is superior to the Brahman (BG 15.18) *Narākṛti paraṁ brahma sac cidānanda vigrahaḥ*: the Lord is the supreme brahman in human form, complete with eternity, knowledge and bliss. (Padma Purāṇa)

*ānanda cinmaya rasa pratibhāvitābhis*  
*tābhir ya eva nija rūpatayā kalābhiḥ \|*  
*goloka eva nivasaty akhilātma bhūto*  
*govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi \|\|*

**I worship the Supreme Lord Govinda who, though regarding all the inhabitants as his very self, resides in Goloka exclusively with the young** ***gopīs*****, who are embodiments of** ***madhura-rasa*****. He accepts them as his own, while they respond to his conjugal affection reciprocally. Brahma-saṁhitā 37**

Gīta-govinda will also say *śṛṅgāraḥ sakhī mūrtimān iva madhau mugdho hariḥ krīḍati*: innocent Kṛṣṇa plays in the spring as if he were the embodiment of Cupid. (GG 1.48) Śukadeva says *sākṣān manmatha-mamathaḥ*: Kṛṣṇa is the bewilderer of Cupid. (SB 10.32.2)

“The Lord is the source of all avatāras. That person is he who lies in the water (*keśava—ke + śete)*, using the rule *proṣodarādīni yathopadiṣṭāni* (Pāṇini 6.3.109). This refers to Nārāyaṇa. It is said *etan nānāvatārāṇāṁ nidhānam bījam avyayam:* Nāṛāyaṇa is the indestructible source of various *avatāras*. (SB 1.3.6) Why do you praise me, the young cowherd boy who plays in the forest, since the scriptures state that Viṣṇu is supreme?”

This verse answers. I offer respects to you who accept the ten forms starting with the fish. The meaning is this. Nārāyaṇa who is the Lord of the ten *avatāras*, is your *aṁśa*. Brahmā says *nārāyaṇas tvam:* you are source of Nārāyaṇa (SB 10.14.14) Śukadeva says in Bhāgavatam:

*stuvatas tasya bhagavān darśayitvā jale vapuḥ*  
*bhūyaḥ samāharat kṛṣṇo naṭo nāṭyam ivātmanaḥ*

**While Akrūra was still offering prayers, Kṛṣṇa withdrew his form that he had revealed in the water, just as an actor winds up his performance. SB 10.41.1**

In describing the *avatāras* it is said *ete cāṁśa-kalā puṁsaḥ kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam:* these are all expansions and Kṛṣṇa is the complete form. (SB 1.3.28) *Viṣṇur mahān sa iha yasya kalā-viśeṣaḥ*: Mahaviṣṇu is his special expansion. (Brahma-saṁhitā 5.48) Thus the meaning of the verse is “I praise you since you are the supreme form of the Lord.”

Having come to this conclusion, the author again summarizes by reciting the activity of each avatāra. The Lord saves the Vedas in the form of the fish. He supports the worlds (*jaganti*) in the form of the turtle. He lifts up (*udbibhrate*) the earth (*bhū-golam*) in the form of the boar. He tears apart the demon Hiraṇyakaśipu in the form of the man-lion. He cheats Bali in the form of the dwarf. He destroys the sinful kṣatriyas in the form of Paraśurāma. He conquers Rāvaṇa (*paulasytam*) in the form of Rāma. He holds the plow, the instrument to curb the wicked, in the form of Balarāma. He spreads mercy in the form of Buddha. He destroys the sinful in the form of Kalki. The verse indicates that Kṛṣṇa is the complete form, endowed with all powers, who performs these actions through his avatāras. *Ākṛti* means form according to *Viśva.*

## Song 2: śrita-kamalā

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 2 \|\|**  
**gurjarī-rāga-niḥsāra-tālābhyāṁ gīyate \|**

**Commentary:** Having described the qualifies of Kṛṣṇa through his avatāras and having indicated in brief the pastimes of the crest jewel of all heroes, the Supreme Lord, by using the words *rahaḥ kelayaḥ* (private pastimes) in the first verse, the poet now describes his qualities such as *dhīra-lalita* in śṛṅgāra-rasa by describing the independent woman *(svādhīna-bhartṛkā*) through the words of a sakhī. In this way the poet produces another invocation of auspiciousness.

Though the Lord is the possessor of all powers, his śakti, full of all blissful pastimes and rasa, is described. The śakti becomes prominent. Elsewhere other gopīs become prominent to nourish the śakti. The Lord display many forms as the hero. The order is as follows.

The Lord is *dhīroddatah* as in Vāmana and Paraśurāma.

*māsarya-darpa-bhūyiṣṭhaḥ chadmāhaṅkāravān balī*  
*caṇḍo vikatthamānaś ca dhīroddhata udāhṛtaḥ*

**The wise call dhīroddhata (haughty) the person who shows great envy and pride, deceit, anger, forcefulness and boastful nature.**

He is also fond of fame, is dexterous and attached to dharma.

The dhīroddātta is described as follows:

*kriyā-dhanapati-gambhīro vinītaś avikatthanaḥ \|*  
*dhīrodāttaḥ sa vijñeyo rāmo dāśarathir yathā \|\|*

The dhīrodātta hides his actions and wealth, is modest and is without boasting. Rāma is an example.

The dhīraśānta is peaceful, modest and intelligent. Matsya and Buddha are examples. They are famous, fond of dharma, and skilful.

Dhīra-lalita is described as follows:

*vidagdho nava-tāruṇyaḥ parihāsa-viśāradaḥ \|*  
*niścinto dhīra-lalitaḥ syāt prāyaḥ preyasī-vaśaḥ \|\|*

**He is called dhīra-lalita who is clever, endowed with fresh youth (end of kaiśora period), skilful at joking and free of worries. He is controlled by his dear devotee. BRS 2.1.230**

*śṛṅgārī nāyakas tv anyaḥ pañcamaḥ kathyate yathā \|*  
*vilāsavān vāma-śīlaḥ subhagaḥ sthira-vāk yuvā \|*  
*patiḥ sadhairyā dṛṣṭiś ca sa-vilāsa-smitaṁ vacaḥ \|\|*

**There is another fifth type of hero in śṛgāra-rasa. He is playful, obedient, prosperous, steady in speech, youthful, married, having firm glance and joking words. Saṅgīta-rāja**

In this way the hero, dear to Rādhā, is described in various ways. This is described in the following song.

### Text 17

*śrita-kamalā-kuca-maṇḍala dhṛta-kuṇḍala e*  
*kalita-lalita-vana-māla*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|17\|\| \[dhru.\]*

**Пословный перевод:**

srita - Is resorted to; kamala-kuca-mandala - by whom the circle of the breasts of Kamala (Radha) \[which is like a fortress\]; dhrta-kundala - by whom the \[makara\] earrings are worn; e - interjection kalita - is worn; lalita-vana-mala - by whom an attractive forest garland! jayajaya - Victory to you (repeated twice)! deva - О lord \[of pastimes\]; hare - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]

**Перевод:**

**O Kṛṣṇa who has taken shelter of the breasts of Rādhā! O Kṛṣṇa decorated with makara earrings! O Kṛṣṇa wearing an attractive forest garland!**  
**Victory to you! O lord of pastimes! O lord who steals the hearts of young women! (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

Gurjarī is the *rāga*\[\[6\]\] and *maṇṭha* is the *tāla*. The *rāga* is described as follows:

*śyāmā sukeśī malaya-drumāṇāṁ*  
*mṛd-ullasat-pallava-talpa-madhye \|*  
*śruti-svarāṇāṁ dadhatī vibhāgaṁ*  
*tantrī-mukhā dakṣiṇa-gurjarīyam \|\|*

**She is from southern Gujarat, dark, with beautiful hair and sits on a bed of soft, gleaming shoots amidst sandalwood trees, with her fingers held in the** ***tantrī*** **position. She intones** ***śrutis*** **(quarter tones).**

Maṇṭhaka-tāla is described as follows:

*guru-laghu-dvayaṁ yatra bhṛṅga-tālaṁ sa kathyate \|*  
*maṅgalo maṇṭhake jñeyo rase cādbhuta-saṁjñake \|\|*

**Auspicious** ***bhṛṅga-ṭala*****, also known as astonishing** ***maṇṭhaka*****, consists of 8 + 4 +4=16 beats.**

It is called astonishing since it reveals astonishing pastimes.

The meaning of the verse is as follows. O Lord who plays *(deva)* or is attached to pastimes with Rādhā! O lord who steals the hearts of all the young women in the world (*hare*)! Victory to you! Victory to you! This word is repeated out of respect. These words are the chorus.

*Śrita* means “taking shelter.” *Kamalā* is derived from the *kama* (a male lover) plus the suffix *la* (to receive). *Kamalā* means “she who controls her lover by her qualities.” Or, *kamalā* refers to Rādhā. O Kṛṣṇa who takes shelter of Rādhā’s breasts, which are like a fortress (*maṇḍala*), since she is fearful of his lust. This phrase shows the greatest qualities which are present in the crown jewel of all heroes and as well shows the quality of the heroine Rādhā, the form of greatest skill in love by the skill of her pastimes. She is an independent lover (*svādhīna-bhartṛkā*). This quality will be explained later (GG 12.1).

O wearer of makara earrings! Or, O Kṛṣṇa who wears Rādhā’s earrings with her permission! Or, O Kṛṣṇa, wearing flower earrings made and gifted by Rādhā. O Kṛṣṇa wearing (*kalita*) an attractive *(lalita)* forest garland, which reaches to the feet! His beauty is indicated by these two phrases. *Vana* means forest. *Vanamāla* can also mean a lotus garland. *Śāśvata* says that *vanamāla* refers to a garland reaching the feet. The phrase can also mean “O Kṛṣṇa who places an attractive garland of flowers or lotuses on his beloved’s throat.” Or it can mean “O Kṛṣṇa who is submerged in masses of flowering forests for decorating your beloved.” Or it can mean “O Kṛṣṇa decorated with attractive drops of perspiration (*vanamāla*) because of ecstasy arising from the touching your beloved when you decorate her.” Or “O Kṛṣṇa decorated with drops of perspiration arising from exertion during enjoyment.” In this case perspiration arising from fatigue is a *vyabhicāri-bhāva*. Or the meaning can be “O Kṛṣṇa who holds various garlands you have made for decorating your beloved.” These various explanations show his great knowledge of the arts. *Maṇḍala* means a sphere or district according to *Viśva.*

### Text 18

*dina-maṇi-maṇḍala-maṇḍana*  
*bhava-khaṇḍana e*  
*muni-jana-mānasa-haṁsa*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

dina-mani-mandala - \[Shines\] like the disk of the jewel of day (the sun) (Kaustubha); mandana - whose ornament; bhava-khandana -destroyer of rebirth; e - interjection; muni-jana - of sages (or Gopis, or Radha); manasa-hamsa - the swan engrossed in the heart (or Lake Manasa)! jaya jaya - Victory to you (repeated twice)! deva - О lord \[of pastimes\]! hare - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**O Kṛṣṇa decorated with the Kaustubha shining like the sun! O destroyer of rebirth! O swan engrossed in the heart of Rādhā! Victory to you! O lord of pastimes! O lord who steals the hearts of young women!**

**Комментарий:**

O Kṛṣṇa having sun-like ornament (Kaustubha) or O Kṛṣṇa whose ornaments are like the disk of the sun. This indicates the great wealth of Kṛṣṇa. O Kṛṣṇa who destroys further birth (*bhāva*) in this world for the *devatās* by taking birth in Nanda’s family. The *devatās* also desire to be born in the group of *gopīs*. Or O Kṛṣṇa who defeats Śiva (*bhava)* by musical skill.

*vividha-gopa-caraṇeṣu vidagdho*  
*veṇu-vādya urudhā nija-śikṣāḥ*  
*tava sutaḥ sati yadādhara-bimbe*  
*datta-veṇur anayat svara-jātīḥ*

*savanaśas tad upadhārya sureśāḥ*  
*śakra-śarva-parameṣṭhi-purogāḥ*  
*kavaya ānata-kandhara-cittāḥ*  
*kaśmalaṁ yayur aniścita-tattvāḥ*

**O pious mother Yaśodā, your son, who is expert in all the arts of herding cows, has invented many new styles of flute-playing. When he takes his flute to his bimba-red lips and sends forth the tones of the harmonic scale in variegated melodies, Brahmā, Śiva, Indra and other chief devatās listen repeatedly to the sound. Although they are the most learned authorities, with hearts in submission, they cannot ascertain the meaning and forget everything. SB 10.35.13-14**

Or, O Kṛṣṇa who destroys the appearance (*bhava*) of pain of separation for his dear devotees. This phrase indicates Kṛṣṇa’s good conduct and his skill in music.

O Kṛṣṇa, the swan in the minds (or Mānasa Lake) of the silent *gopīs* (*munijana*). Or, O Kṛṣṇa who out of great attachment has become a swan trying the fathom the intentions in the mind of Rādhā, silent because of her anger. Or, O Kṛṣṇa inclined to contemplation (*muni-jana*), thinking of the qualities of Rādhā! Or, O Kṛṣṇa who has given up all other places and dwells in the heart of Rādhā (*mānasa-haṁsa*)!

This employs the ornament called *paramparita-rūpaka (*metaphor using puns such as the word *mānasa*).

*niyatāropaṇopāyaḥ syād āropaḥ parasya yaḥ \|*  
*tat paramparitaṁ śliṣṭe vācake bheda-bhāji vā \|\|*

***Rūpaka*** **(metaphor) becomes consequential (*****paramparita*****) when one thing is called another object using a pun or words with distinct meanings. Kāyva-prakāśa 95**

*Mānasam* refers to the lake according to *Viśva*. *Haṁsa* is bird.

### Text 19

*kāliya-viṣa-dhara-gañjana*  
*jana-rañjana e*  
*yadukula-nalina-dineśa*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaliya-visa-dhara - Of the snake Kaliya; ganjana - shamer; jana-ranjana - attractor of people; e - interjection; yadukula-nalina - for the lotus of the cowherd dynasty; dinesa - the day-lord \[sun\]! jaya jaya - Victory to you (repeated twice)! deva - О lord \[of pastimes\]! hare - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**O Kṛṣṇa who humbled Kāliya. O attractor of all people! O sun which makes the cowherd families joyful! Victory to you. ! O lord of pastimes! O lord who steals the hearts of young women!**

**Комментарий:**

O Kṛṣṇa who treated the snake (*viṣadhara*) Kāliya with disrespect. This shows Kṛṣṇa’s skill in the sixty-four arts. O Kṛṣṇa who attracts everyone with sweet words (*jana-rañjana*). This shows his wonderful power of speaking. Or you attract all the sakhīs by treating Rādhā nicely. Or Kṛṣṇa who decorates Rādhā with various colors.

O Kṛṣṇa, the sun which makes the lotus of the cowherd dynasty bloom (*yadukula-nalina-dineśa*). Yadu refers to the cowherds. *Hari-vaṁśa* says *yādavānāṁ hitārthāya dhṛto girivaro māyā:* I held up Govardhana for the benefit of the Yādavas. (Thus Yadus refers to cowherds.) *Viṣadhara* means snake according to *Amara-koṣa. Nalina* means lotus. *Rañjana* means producing attachment or red sandalwood according to *Viśva.*
