## Texn 20

*madhu-mura-naraka-vināśana*  
*garuḍāsana e \|*  
*sura-kula-keli-nidāna*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

madhu-mura-naraka - Of demons Madhum, Mura, and Naraka; vinasana - destroyer; garudasana - whose seat is Garuda; e -interjection; sura-kula - for the lineage of devatas; keli-nidana - the cause of play! jaya jaya - Victory to you (repeated twice)! deva - О lord \[of pastimes\]! hare - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**Destroyer of Madhu, Mura and Naraka. O Kṛṣṇa, whose is seated on Garuḍa! O cause of play for the lineage of devatās! Victory to you! O lord of pastimes! O lord who steals the hearts of young women!**

**Комментарий:**

Destroyer of Madhu, Mura and Naraka, having Garuḍa as your seat! Or Kṛṣṇa who protects *(mura*) *madhurya-rasa* for his devotees. *Mura* is formed from the verb *mur* by the rule *igupadha.* (Pāṇini 3.1.135) By this he destroys other types of happiness (*ka*) of people (*nara*)--making that happiness insignificant. *Svarita-veṇunā suṣṭhu cumbitam itara-rāga-vismāraṇaṁ nṛṇām:* that nectar is thoroughly relished by your vibrating flute and makes people forget any other attachment. (SB 10.31.14) You have an amorous bodily pose resembling Garuḍa (*garuḍāsana*). This shows his skill in love affairs. O Kṛṣṇa, the cause of devatās’ playing (*sura-kula-keli-nidana*). *Devā hi krīḍā-caturāḥ:* the devatās are fond of playing. They have learned this from you. This indicates Kṛṣṇa’s supreme ability to engage in pastimes. *Kulam* means people of a family lineage.

### Text 21

*amala-kamala-dala-locana*  
*bhava-mocana e*  
*tribhuvana-bhavana-nidhāna*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|21\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

amala-kamala-dala - Are like flawless lotus petals; locana - whose eyes; bhava-mocana - the liberator of rebirth in the material world; e - interjection; tribhuvana - within tbe three worlds; bhavana-nidhana - whose residential place is a borne \[Vrndavanap\]! jaya jaya - Victory to you (repeated twice)', deva - O lord (of pastimes)! hare - o lord who steals \[the hearts of young women!

**Перевод:**

**O Lord with flawless lotus petal eyes! O Lord who liberates us from rebirth in the material world! O Kṛṣṇa who dwells in a house in Vṛndāvana within the three worlds! Victory to you! O lord of pastimes! O lord who steals the hearts of young women!**

**Комментарий:**

O Kṛṣṇa with eyes like flawless lotus petals. This indicates his beauty. O Lord who frees the surrendered devotees from birth in the material world (*bhava-mocana*). This indicates that the devotees worship him since he has a form of eternal bliss which he presents to them (rather than bestowing impersonal liberation). Or O Kṛṣṇa who freed Śiva (*bhava*) from the great danger of Vṛkāsura, or who has freed Śiva from intense vows of yoga by attracting him with the beauty of his lotus eyes, making Śiva favorable. O Kṛṣṇa who has a residential place (*bhavana*) called Vṛndāvana or a great treasure (*nidhānam*), a house within the three worlds! *Nidhānam* means treasure or house*. Bhavana* means house. *Dala* means a petal according to *Amara-koṣa.*

### Text 22

*janaka-sutā-kṛta-bhūṣaṇa*  
*jita-dūṣaṇa e*  
*samara-śamita-daśa-kaṇṭha*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|22\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

janaka-suta - On the daughter of Vrsabhanu (Radha); krta-bhusana - by whom the decoration is made; jita-dusana - by whom faults are conquered; e - interjection; samara-sam-ita - who have attained ecstasy in the battle \[of love\]; dasa-kantha - on whose neck \[cling\] the ten \[heroines\]! jayajaya - Victory to you (repeated twice)! deva - О lord \[of pastimes\]! hare - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**O Kṛṣṇa who decorates the limbs of Rādhā! O destroyer of all faults by your good qualities! O Kṛṣṇa on whose neck cling ten types of heroines who have attained ecstasy in the battle of love! Victory to you! O lord of pastimes! O lord who steals the hearts of young women!**

**Комментарий:**

*Janaka* means one who gives happiness (*ka*) to the people of Vraja. The main person there is Vṛṣabhānu. His daughter (*sutā)* is Rādhā. O Kṛṣṇa who decorates Rādhā (*janaka-sutā*) by drawing patterns on her limbs! O Kṛṣṇa who has conquered all faults by your good qualities (*jita-dūṣana*)! This indicates the hero’s beautiful form and his talent in all arts. O Kṛṣṇa on whose neck cling ten types of heroines who have attained *(ita)* happiness (*śam*) and are murmuring in ecstasy in the battle of love *(samara).* This indicates his skill in love. Or, O Kṛṣṇa on whose throat cling heroines who have attained a state *(daśā)* of murmuring in ecstasy in the battle of love.

### Text 23

*abhinava-jala-dhara-sundara*  
*dhṛta-mandara e*  
*śrī-mukha-candra-cakora*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|23\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

abhinava-jala-dhara - As a fresh rain cloud; sundara - as beautiful as; dhrta-mandara - by whom Mandara \[Govardhana\] was held; e - interjection; sri-mukha-candra - for the moon-like face of Sri (Radha); cakora - the cakora bird! jaya jaya - Victory to you (repeated twice)! deva - О lord \[of pastimes\]! hare - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**O Kṛṣṇa beautiful as a fresh rain cloud! O lifter of Govardhana! O Kṛṣṇa like a cakora drinking the beautiful moon-like face of Rādhā! Victory to you! O lord of pastimes! O lord who steals the hearts of young women!**

**Комментарий:**

O Kṛṣṇa beautiful as a fresh rain cloud showing great beauty! This indicates his great beauty. O Kṛṣṇa who lifted Govadhana (*mandara*)! In *Gopāla-stava*, Śaṅkarācārya has said *dhṛtaika-hasta-mandara*: Kṛṣṇa held the mountain (*mandara*) with one hand. O Kṛṣṇa who, like a cakora, solely tastes the beautiful face (*śrī-mukha*) of Rādhā, like the moon, since it gives you joy! Kṛṣṇa is compared to a cakora bird because he longs to drink the nectar of the lips of Rādhā’s moonlike face. This shows his skill in love. *Abhinava* means “new” according to *Amara-koṣa.* In seven verses the qualities of the hero have been described. It is said:

*tyāgī kulīnaḥ kuśalo rateṣu*  
*kalpaḥ kalāvit taruṇo dhanāḍhyaḥ \|*  
*bhavyaḥ kṣamī sthira-ruciḥ subhago’bhimānī*  
*strīṇāṁ abhiṣṭas tv iha nāyakaḥ syāt \|\|*

**The hero should be renounced, good mannered, skilful in love, competent, knowledgeable of the arts, young, wealthy, handsome, tolerant, fixed in taste, happy, confident, and desired by women. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka**

### Text 24

*tava caraṇe praṇatā vayaṁ*  
*iti bhāvaya e*  
*kuru kuśalaṁ praṇateṣu*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|24\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tava - Your; carane - at \[your\] feet; pranata - are offering respects; vayam - we; iti - thus, bhavaya - \[Please\] think \[of us\]; e -interjection; kuru kusalam - provide auspiciousness; pranatesu - to those who are offering respects \[to you at your feet\]! jaya jaya -Victory to you (repeated twice)! deva - О lord \[of pastimes\]! hare - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**Please think of us, who offer you respects. Please remove all obstacles for those surrendered to you. Victory to you! O lord of pastimes! O lord who steals the hearts of young women!**

**Комментарий:**

Having praised Kṛṣṇa since he is the best of all heroes, the author now prays. O Kṛṣṇa, please think of us, who offer respects at your feet. Please create auspiciousness or remove all obstacles for the devotees offering respects to you.

### Text 25

*śrī-jayadeva-kaver idaṁ*  
*kurute mudam e*  
*maṅgalam ujjvala-gītaṁ*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|25\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

srt-jayadeva-kaver - Of the poet SriJayadeva; idam - this \[song\] ; kurute - creates; mudam - joy; e - interjection; mangalam - auspicious; ujjvala - faultless; gitam - song! jaya jaya - Victory to you (repeated twice)! deva - О lord \[of pastimes\]! hare - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**This song of Jayadeva, faultless and auspicious, creates joy. Victory to you! ! O lord of pastimes! O lord who steals the hearts of young women!**

**Комментарий:**

This song of Jayadeva creates joy. The song is without fault (*ujjvala*) and auspicious. The faults of songs are mentioned by Bharata:

*śaṅkitaṁ bhītam udghṛṣṭam avyaktam anunāsikam \|*  
*kāka-svaraṁ śiraḥ-kampaṁ tathā sthāna-vivarjitam \|\|*  
*visvaraṁ virasaṁ caiva viśliṣṭaṁ viṣamāhatam \|*  
*vyākulaṁ tāla-hīnaṁ ca gīta-doṣān vidur budhāḥ \|\|*

**The wise know a song is faulty when it is sung with uncertainty, fear, or unclear intonation, inaudibly, nasalized, with the voice of a crow, while shaking the head, without proper pitch, in a disjointed manner,with dissonance, unpleasantness, unevenness, confusion, and without tāla.**

Or *maṅgalam* can mean that the poem is a prayer to his Lord, or that it is in *maṅgala-tāla.*

### Text 26

*padmā-payodhara-taṭī-parirambha-lagna-*  
*kāśmīra-mudritam uro madhusūdanasya \|*  
*vyaktānurāgam iva khelad-anaṅga-kheda-*  
*svedāmbu-pūram anupūrayatu priyaṁ vaḥ \|\|26\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

padma-payodhara-tati - By the tips of the breasts of Padma (Radha); parirambha-lagna - after being closely embraced; kasmira-mudritam - imprinted with kumkuma; urah - the chest; madhusudanasya - of Madhusudana (Krsna); vyaktanuragam - by which \[his\] passion is evident; iva - as if; khelad-ananga - of love play; kheda - arising from the fatigue; svedambu-puram - filled with streams of perspiration; anupurayatu - may it \[the chest\] completely satisfy; priyam - in an agreeable manner (or in a satisfying manner); vah - to all of you.

**Перевод:**

**May the heart of Kṛṣṇa, smeared with the kuṁkuma from the tips of Rādhā’s breasts after their embrace, covered with perspiration arising from fatigue of love play, showing the manifestation of his love for her, give full pleasure to all of you!**

**Комментарий:**

After describing the great fortune of the heroine, the author gives blessings to the listeners by invoking auspiciousness. May the heart of Madhusūdana create full satisfaction (*priyam*) for you at every moment! The adjectives show that he has ability to bestow this blessing since he is eternally attached to the pastimes. The experience of *rasa* of the *gopīs*, which is most desirable, is also hinted. The word Madhusūdana (meaning a bee, promising sweetness) is used to indicate the attainment of a taste of sweet *rasa*. *Padmā* refers to a woman who has lotuses, according to the rule *arśa ādibhyo’c*. (Pāṇinin 5.2.127) It also indicates a woman with qualities like a lotus—having the fragrance of a million lotuses. This fragrance also indicates her youthful beauty. *Padmā* thus means Rādhā. That chest has a mark of *kuṁkuma* (*kāśmīra*) from the tip of Rādhā’s breasts because he embraced her. She has put the kuṁkuma from the tip of her breast on his chest to announce that his heart belongs to her and no one else. By an embrace which touched only the tips of her breasts her skill in making amorous gestures is made known, announcing her *vāma* (obstinate) nature. His heart is covered with perspiration arising from fatigue after love play. The perspiration combined with the kuṁkuma produces the marks on his chest and indicates their intense enjoyment. This is like a visible manifestation of his affection. It seems that because of the intense affection for her internally, his chest has become colored externally, on the pretext of kuṁkuma stains. *Mudrita* means “stuck.” *Pura* means stream. *Payodhara* means breast. *Kandarpa* means Cupid. The characteristics of a *padminī* woman are as follows:

*kamala-mukula-mṛdvī phulla-rājīva-gandhiḥ*  
*surata-payasi yasyāḥ saurabhaṁ divyam aṅge \|*  
*cakita-mṛga-dṛśābhe prāntarakte ca netre*  
*kuca-yugalam anarghyaṁ śrī-phala-śrī-viḍambi \|\|*

**She is soft as a lotus bud, scented like a blossoming lotus. She has divine fragrance in her limbs which enjoy amorous delights. She has darting eyes, red at the tips, resembling those of a doe. She has priceless breasts, conquering the beauty of bilva fruits.**

*tila-kusuma-samānāṁ bibhrati nāsikāṁ ca*  
*dvija-guru-sura-pūjāṁ śraddadhānā sadaiva \|*  
*kuvalaya-dala-kāntiḥ kāpi cāmpeya-gaurī*  
*vikaca-kamala-kośākāra-kāmātapatrā \|\|*

**She has a nose like a sesame flower and is devoted to worshipping brāhmaṇas, elders and** ***devatās.*** **She has the complexion of a water lily and is sometimes golden like a campaka flower. She has a lovely umbrella resembling an open lotus bud.**

*vrajati mṛdu sa-līlaṁ rājahaṁsīva tanvī*  
*trivali-kalita-madhyā haṁsa-vāṇī suveṣā \|*  
*mṛdu śuci laghu bhuṅkte māninī gāḍha-lajjā*  
*dhavala-kusuma-vāso vallabhā padminī syāt \|\|*

**With slender body she walks gently and playfully like a swan. Her waist has three folds and she wears fine clothing. She speaks like a swan. She is highly honored and intensely shy. She eats lightly, cleanly and gently. She smells like the dhavala flower and is loved by all.**

Since it is impossible to describe Rādhā’s extraordinary qualities, the above qualities are listed so that people can understand them to some degree.

Till this point, the poet invoked auspicious verses which show the suitable qualities of the hero which are necessary in describing the expressed goal the work. Desiring to describe the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa which are predominated with *śṛṅgāra-rasa*, citing a pastime abbreviated at this time, he now begins to describe the heroine who is the highest embodiment of *śṛṅgāra-rasa* after the appearance of separation in *śṛṅgāra-rasa,* in order to nourish the rasa of union of Rādhā and Mohana who are most attached to each other, who do not want separation at any moment and who desire to begin special pastimes by this additional taste.

### Text 27

*vasante vāsantī-kusuma-sukumārair avayavair*  
*bhramantīṁ kāntāre bahu-vihita-kṛṣṇānusaraṇām \|*  
*amandaṁ kandarpa-jvara-janita-cintākulatayā*  
*valad\[\[7\]\]-bādhaṁ rādhāṁ sarasam idam ūce sahacarī \|\|27\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vasante - In spring season; vasanti-kusuma-sukumaraih - as tender as vernal flowers (madhavi flower); avayavaih - with limbs; bhramantim - who was wandering; kantare - in the vast forest; bahu-vihita - many times; krsnanusaranam - searching for Krsna; amandam - intensely; kandarpa-jvara-janita - arising from the fever of love; cintakulataya - because of the confusion of thoughts; valad-badham - whose pain was increasing; radham - to Radha; sarasam - so as to invoke rasa; idam - the following \[words\]; uce - spoke; sahacari - a fellow-friend \[sakhi\]

**Перевод:**

**A sakhī spoke the following words to Rādhā, as she repeated searched for Kṛṣṇa in the vast forest in the spring season and experienced great pain while overcome with thoughts arising from the fever of love.**

**Комментарий:**

It is said in the scriptures that separation occurs to produce excellence of union.

*na vinā vipralambhena sambhogaḥ puṣṭim aśnute \|*  
*kāṣāyite hi vastrādau bhūyān evābhivardhate \[\[8\]\]\|\|*

**Sambhoga is not strong without vipralambha. It increases in strength just as cloth increase in beauty by repeatedly dying with red color. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 15.3**

The Lord desires the experience of *śṛṅgāra* with a mixture of rasas. He is eternally endowed with desire, knowledge and ability to act. His independence is not destroyed since he is the eternal possessor of unlimited powers: he is the controller of rasa by his eternal desire.

*evaṁ śaśāṅkāṁśu-virājitā niśāḥ*  
*sa satya-kāmo ’nuratābalā-gaṇaḥ*

**With** ***gopīs*** **firmly attached to him with** ***anurāga*****, Kṛṣṇa, whose desires are always fulfilled, who had fixed in his mind enjoyment with them, served the nights lit by the rays of the moon. SB 10.33.25**

Thus by his eternal desire, the influence of time is eradicated.

The meaning of the verse is this. One day in the spring season a *sakhī (sahācarī*) with similar desires spoke to Rādhā the following words in order to invoke *rasa* (*sarasam*). Rādhā’s limbs were as tender as the flowers of a *mādhavī* creeper. *Mārdavaṁ komalasyāpi saṁsparśāsahatocyate:* when one cannot tolerate even the softest touch, it is called *mārdavam.* (Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 10.38)

*abhinava-nava-mālikām ayaṁ sā*  
*śayana-varaṁ niśi rādhikādhiśiśye \|*  
*na kusuma-paṭalaṁ darāpi jaglau*  
*tad-anubhavāt tanur eva sa-vraṇāsīt \|\|39\|\|*

**Rādhā slept last night on a bed of very fresh jasmine flowers. Not a single flowerwas evenslightly wilted. From this we understand how her delicate body became bruised. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 10.39**

She wandered in the vast forest (*kāntāre*). The reason is given. She searched many times (*bahu-vihita*) for Kṛṣṇa since she experienced increased pain, being intensely (*amandam)* overcome with thoughts arising from the fever of love. The word *avayavaiḥ* (with limbs) is a descriptive term for Rādhā using the instrumental case as in the phrase *jaṭābhis tāpasaḥ* (austere man with matted hair). *Kāntara* means a huge forest and *bādha* means pain. The verse describes the state of worry *(cintā*) with desire (*abhilāsa).* The characteristics of desire (*abhilāsa)* are as follows:

*abhilāṣaḥ spṛhā tatra kānte sarvāṅga-sundare \|*  
*dṛṣṭe śrute vā tatrāpi vismayānanda-sādhvasāḥ \|\|*

***Abhilāṣa*** **or desire means that, on seeing or hearing about the lover who is beautiful in all limbs, she experiences doubt, bliss and panic. Daśarūpaka 4.53**

This is an example:

*kathaṁ sa vallabhaḥ prāpyaḥ kiṁ kuryāṁ tasya siddhaye \|*  
*kathaṁ bhaved asau vaśya iti cintā bhaved yathā \|\|*

**The woman thinks, “Will I meet my dear lover? What should I do to attain him? What should I do to control him?” Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 2.8**

The excellence of her desire to meet Kṛṣṇa is shown by the words “wandering in the vast forest with her tender limbs.”

*citrotkīrṇād api viṣadharād bhīti-bhājo rajanyāṁ*  
*kiṁ vā brūmas tvad-abhisaraṇe sāhasaṁ mādhavāsyāḥ \|*  
*dhvānte yāntyā yad atinibhṛtaṁ rādhayātma-prakāśa-*  
*trāsāt pāṇiḥ pathi phaṇi-phaṇāratna-rodhī vyadhāyī \|\|*

**O Mādhava, how can we describe Rādhā’s reckless fear of the serpent engraved on her medallion? As she walked on the path to secretly meet you in the middle of the night, she became afraid that the effulgence of the jewel on that serpent’s hood would light up the darkness and thus covered it with her hand. Padyāvalī 196**

*dvitraiḥ keli-saroruhaṁ tri-caturair dhammilla-mallī-srajaṁ*  
*kaṇṭhān mauktika-mālikāṁ tad anu ca tyaktvā padaiḥ pañcamaiḥ \|*  
*kṛṣṇa-prema-vighūrṇitāntaratayā dūrābhisārāturā*  
*tanvaṅgī nirupāyam adhvani paraṁ śreṇī-bharaṁ nindati \|\|*

**O Kṛṣṇa! Her heart agitated with love, Rādhā has become exhausted from walking on the path to the far-away rendezvous. After taking two or three steps, she abandons her toy lotus flower. After three orfour steps,she abandons the garland of jasmine flowers decorating herhair. After five steps,she removes the pearl-necklace from her neck. After this she rebukes her hips for being so heavy,but she cannot abandonthem. Padyāvalī 211**

Or, the phrase *amandaṁ kandarpa-jvara-janita-cintākulatayā* can modify *krṣṇānusaraṇām* instead of *valad bādhām*: Rādhā searched for Kṛṣṇa many times with thoughts arising from the fever of love.

## Song 3: Springtime described

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 3 \|\|**  
**Mādhavotsava-kamalākara**  
**vasanta-rāga-yati-tālābhyāṁ gīyate**

### Text 28

*lalita-lavaṅga-latā-pariśīlana-komala-malaya-samīre \|*  
*madhukara-nikara-karambita-kokila-kūjita-kuñja-kuṭīre \|\|*  
*viharati harir iha sarasa-vasante*  
*nṛtyati yuvatī-janena samaṁ sakhi virahi-janasya durante \|\|28\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

lalita - Attractive; lavanga-lata - clove creepers; parisilana -embraces; komala-malaya-sarriire - \[in the spring\] whose mild Malaya breeze; madhuhara-nikara - \[with\] a host of bees; harambita - is mixed; kohda-kujita - with cooing cuckoos; kunja-kutire -\[and\] whose cottage in the bower; viharati - \[He\] searches; harih - Krsna; iha - here; sarasa - filled with rasa of love; vasante - in spring season; nrtyati - \[while they\] dance; yuvatl-jane - young girls (gopis)-, na - \[is there\] no; samam - together; sakhi - О \[my\] friend; virahi-janasya - for the person in separation; duranta - \[is there no\] misery?

**Перевод:**

**In the spring filled with the rasa of love, with the mild Malaya breeze embracing attractive clove creepers, replete with a cottage in a bower filled with cooing cuckoos and a host of humming bees, Kṛṣṇa searches for you while other gopīs dance together, but he is miserable because of separation from you.**

**Комментарий:**

*Vasanta- rāga*\[\[9\]\]is described as follows:

*śikhaṇḍi-barhoccaya-baddha-cūḍaḥ*  
*puṣṇan pikaṁ cūta-latāṅkureṇa \|*  
*bhraman mudā rāmam anaṅga-mūrtir*  
*marto mataṅgaḥ sa vasanta-rāgaḥ \|\|*

***Vasanta-rāga*** **has a peacock feather on the top of his head and feeds a cuckoo mango buds. The embodiment of Cupid, he wanders around in joy enjoying like an elephant.**

Yati-tāla is laghu-drutau: 4 +4 + 2 + 2

The *sakhī* speaks words which are like a great medicine to Rādhā who is in pain. O my friend Rādhā! Here in Vṛndāvana, in the spring season which gives rise to *śṛṅgāra-rasa (sarasa),* Kṛṣṇa plays. What else is happening? He plays while young girls dance gracefully (*samam*) or together (*samam*). There is misery for the person (Kṛṣṇa) in separation, is there not (*durante na*)? This phrase gives the reason for Rādhā’s meeting him. *Duranta* also means “without limit.”

Or the phrase may be analyzed as *jane ‘ana-samam durante* where *ana* means life. His whole life has become miserable. Or the meaning can be “Though Kṛṣṇa dances in the spring at this place with groups of gopīs intoxicated with his beauty, lured there by great *prema,* he does not play (*na viharati*) with them (s*amam*), who desire to dance, in the same way that he plays with you.

Others take the sentence to mean “Kṛṣṇa plays with the young women (*yuvati-janena samam*) in the spring season in Vṛndāvana and dances with them.” This however is not suitable for Rādhā’s meeting with Kṛṣṇa, because the *sakhī’s* statement would make Rādhā envious. (Therefore she would not go to meet Kṛṣṇa.)

Is this an expression of *pūrva-rāga*? That cannot be, since other descriptions indicate previous pastimes with Kṛṣṇa. It will be said later *rāse hariṁ iha vihinita-vilāsaṁ smarati mano mama kṛta-parihāsam*: I remember playful Kṛṣṇa making jokes at the *rāsa* dance. (GG 2.2) Thus the explanation given above is correct.

Returning to the subject, the *sakhī* says that he does not play with the others in the same way - since he is miserable in separation from you (*virahi-jansya durante*). The other *gopīs* and the spring season cannot give Kṛṣṇa great pleasure without your presence. He desires only you. *Viharati* also suggests that he engages in searching for Rādhā. In order to dispel any hesitation to meet Kṛṣṇa, the *sakhī* indicates that the other young women are ordinary. Therefore if you do not go after him, the spring will become miserable for you. This also is hinted. *Samam* means “with” according to A*mara-koṣa*. This statement is the chorus.

There are twelve qualities to the *sakhī* who acts as a messenger on orders (*nisṛṣṭhārta-dūtī*).

*pravṛtti-kuśalā dakṣā gūḍha-mantrā dṛḍha-priyā \|*  
*svatantrā vidhavā dāsī dhṛṣṭā pravrajitā sakhī \|\|*  
*mālākārādi-nārī ca kāryā dūtīṣṭa-siddhaye \|\|*

**The messenger should be skilful in conduct, expert, secretive, faithful, independent, single, endowed with a service attitude, bold, renounced, friendly, and skilful in making garlands**

Her characteristics are mentioned:

*kālocitodyamābhijñā suveṣā dṛḍha-mitratā \|*  
*dhṛṣṭā iṣṭā suśīlā ca dakṣā cāpalya-varjitā \|*  
*akṣudrā cāpy anudvignā praśasyā dvādaśaiva tu \|\|*

**The messenger knows what to do at a particular time and circumstance, is well dressed, has strong friendship, is bold, affectionate, of good character, expert, steady, not vulgar, undisturbed, and praiseworthy.**

*vilāsinoś ca svāntastham arthaṁ jñātvā yateta yā \|*  
*anurūpāv imau ceti hy ākalayyānukūla-kṛt \|*  
*nsṛṣṭārtheti sā jñeyā kautūhala-samanvitā \|\|*

**The designated messenger should be make efforts as the two lovers’ intermediary, understanding the goal and considering the couple ideal for each other, should work favorably for their meeting and should be most anxious for their union.**

The twenty-seven actions of the messenger are as follows:

- To create enthusiasm
- To show the goal
- Speak of the qualities of the couple
- To instill faith
- To make known the love of one for the other
- To remind the heroine to act coyly
- To dress nicely
- To tolerate grief
- To speak sweet words
- To give strong advice
- To be friendly
- To spontaneously follow
- To be knowledgeable of methods
- To know aspects of time
- To consider according to place
- To know people’s natures
- To be skilful in all actions
- To cover up faults in the heroine
- To discern good qualities
- To praise beauty
- To avoid treacherous words
- To speak mildly
- To speak practically
- To praise efforts
- To know other philosophies

Showing her qualities such as knowledge of time, enthusiasm, and showing the goal, the sakhī speaks of the rasa in the spring season. In the spring the soft breeze from Malaya Mountain embraces the attractive *(lalita*) clove creepers. The breeze here is described with these three qualities. The breeze acts as an uddīpana with the clove creeper representing the heroine. The sakhī also shows other uddīpanas. What are the other qualities of spring? In the spring the small cottage in the bower is filled with the cooing of cuckoos mixed with the humming of hoards of bees.

*Śīlana* means embracing according to *Viśva. Samīra* means wind according to *Amara-koṣa. Madhukara* means a bee. *Nikara* means all. *Karambita* means mixed. *Kuṭīra* means a cottage.

### Text 29

*unmada-madana-manoratha-pathika-vadhū-jana-janita-vilāpe \|*  
*ali-kula-saṅkula-kusuma-samūha-nirākula-bakula-kalāpe \|\|29\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

unmada-madana-manoratha - \[Is filled\] with desires \[enflamed\] by their intense passion; pathika-vadhu-jana-janita - arising from the wives of travelers; vilape - \[in spring\] whose lamentation; ali-kula-sankula - \[are\] thick with hoards of bees; kusuma-samuha - with heaps of flowers; nirakula-bakula-kalape - whose unlimited groves of bakula trees.

**Перевод:**

**In that spring there is the sound of lamentation arising from travelers’ wives, filled with desires enflamed by intense passion and there are unlimited groves of bakula trees filled with flowers and covered with hoards of bees.**

**Комментарий:**

In that spring there is lamentation arising from wives of travelling husbands, lamentation permeated with desire filled with intense passion or madness and passion (*unmāda-madana*). As well, all the unlimited bakula trees are filled with abundant flowers and hoards of bees. Or the spring is ornamented (*kalāpe)* with unlimited bakula trees. Or the spring has quivers *(kalāpa)* of unlimited bakula trees to shoot Cupid’s arrows. *Sañkula* means spread according to *Viśva. Bakula* is the *kesara* plant. *Kalāpa* means a group.

### Text 30

*mṛga-mada-saurabha-rabhasa-vaśaṁvada-nava-dala-māla-tamāle \|*  
*yuva-jana-hṛdaya-vidāraṇa manasija-nakha-ruci-kiṁśuka-jāle \|\|30\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

mrga-mada-saurabha - Of fragrant musk; rabhasa-vasamvada -\[are\] submissive to the streams; nava-dala-mala - with rows of new leaves; tamale - \[in spring\] on tamala trees, уuva-jana-hrdaya-vidarana - that tear apart the hearts of young couples; manasija - of Manasija (the Cupid); nakha-ruci - glow like the \[scarlet\] nails; kimsuka jale - whose groups of palasa buds

**Перевод:**

**The clusters of new leaves on the tamāla trees submit themselves to the streams of fragrant musk. The palāśa buds resembles the glow of Cupid’s nails tearing apart the hearts of young couples.**

**Комментарий:**

How is the spring further described? There are *tamāla* trees with clusters of new leaves which announce their submission to streams of fragrant musk. Furthermore, there are groups or buds (*jāle*) of palāśa flowers (which are scarlet) resembling Cupid’s shining nails of love, which tear the hearts of young women. This indicates the beauty of Kṛṣṇa’s nails which resemble those of Cupid’s, covered with blood when he tears open the hearts of young people. The palāśa buds are curved and red.

*Mṛgamada* is musk according to *Amara-koṣa. Rabhasa* means streams according to *Viśva. Ruci* means light. *Kiṁśuka* is the palāśa flower according to *Amara-koṣa. Jāla* means a group of flower buds according to *Viśva.*

### Text 31

*madana-mahīpati-kanaka-daṇḍa-ruci-keśara-kusuma-vikāśe \|*  
*milita-śili-mukha-pāṭala-paṭala-kṛta-smara-tūṇa-vilāse \|\|31\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

madana-mahipati - Of Lord Madana (the Cupid); kanaka-danda-ruci - glows like the golden umbrella; kesara-kusuma-vikase - \[in spring\] whos exhibition of naga-kesara flowers; milita-sili-mukha -covered with bees \[which resemble arrows\]; patala-patala - which is made from a cluster of patala flowers; smara-tuna - of the quiver of Smara (the Cupid); vilase - of whom \[there are\] activities.

**Перевод:**

**Spring blossoms with nāga-keśara flowers resembling the gleam of Lord Cupid’s golden umbrella. The spring is active with quivers made from clusters of pāṭala flowers as quivers, covered with bees, which resemble arrows.**

**Комментарий:**

The spring blossoms with nāgakeśara flowers which resembles the gleam of the golden umbrella handle or the umbrella with its golden handle, of the great Lord Cupid. The umbrella is inferred because of the inclusion of the word *mahā-pati* (king). A king is accompanied by an umbrella. Or the rod is to be used to inflict pain on those who live in separation. The spring is endowed with the activities of Cupid’s quiver produced by clusters (*paṭala*) o*f* pāṭala flowers covered with bees which resemble arrows. The bees are compared to arrows (*śilīmukha*) since they pierce others and cause pain.

*Keśara* means the nāgakeśara according to *Amara-koṣa. Śilīmukha* means a bee or an arrow. *Paṭala* means a group. *Tūṇa* is a quiver.

### Text 32

*vigalita-lajjita-jagad-avalokana-taruṇa-karuṇa-kṛta-hāse \|*  
*virahi-nikṛntana-kunta-mukhākṛti-ketaka-danturitāśe \|\|32\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vigalita-lajjita - From which shame has disappeared; jagad-avalokana - at the sight of \[people of \] the world; taruna-karuna-krta - \[is\] made by the young karuna flowers; hase - \[in spring\] whose smiling; virahi-nikrntana-for piercing \[lovers\] in separation; kunta-mukhakrti - resembling the tips of spears; ketaka-danturitase - whose directions \[are\] spread with ketaka flowers.

**Перевод:**

**In the spring the young karuṇa flowers smile on seeing people showing intense shame. The directions are filled with ketaka flowers resembling spear tips ready to pierce lovers in separation.**

**Комментарий:**

The spring smiles by having flowers of the young karuṇa trees break into blossom on seeing living beings living having intense (*vigalita*) shame (because of their desires). There is a comparison of the blossoming flowers and their white color which represents hāsya-rasa. Bharata Muni says *śveto hāsyaḥ prakīrtitaḥ*: the color of hāsya-rasa is white. The young trees are mentioned since in Vṛndāvana all trees are eternally young since they are dear to the Lord. Or in spring young couples not separated *(taruṇa*) have smiles mixed with karuṇa-rasa (compassion) on seeing people of the world who are separated and have given up *(vigalita*) shame. They have compassion and lament, “O unfortunate lovers who are separated undergo such difficulty!” They smile, saying “Those in separation have given up modesty and are lamenting.” Infants do not know rasa and old people suppress laughter in front of strangers. Those in their youth will smile and laugh. Thus the trees are described as youthful (*taruṇa*). *Lajjita* is taken in the neuter to express a state.

Spring is further described. The directions (*āśa*) are spread or made painful (*danturita*) with ketaki flowers (keora) resembling the tips of spears for piercing lovers in separation. *Vigalita* can also mean that the cuckoos (*vi*) stumble (*galita*) on the ground. They are therefore ashamed. *Jagat* can mean travelers, entities moving around. Seeing travelers (separated from their lovers) stumbling in shame on hearing the cuckoos warbling, youthful couples who are united smile with compassion (*karuṇa*). Sometimes *ketaki-danturitāśe* is seen.

*Ketaki* stands for *ketakī. Nikṛntana* comes from the root *kṛ*: to injure. *Karuṇa* means compassion according to *Viśva. Kunta* is a type of weapon according to *Amara-koṣa. Dantura* means “spread out” according to *Dharaṇi. Āśā* means directions according to *Viśva.*

### Text 33

*mādhavikā-parimala-lalite nava-mālati-jāti-sugandhau \|*  
*muni-manasām api mohana-kārīṇi taruṇa-karaṇa-bandhau \|\|33\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

madhavika-parimala - With the fragrance of madhavi creepers; lalite - it \[the spring\] is attractive; nava-malati-jati - with types of fresh jasmine; sugandhau - it is fragrant; muni-manasam - \[in\] the minds of sages; api - even; mohana-karini - it causes bewilderment; taruna - in young \[couples\]; karana-bandhau - \[and\] it causes the highest bliss

**Перевод:**

**The season is attractive with the perfume of mādhavī creepers and is fragrant with all types of fresh jasmine. It bewilders the minds of sages and causes the highest bliss in young couples.**

**Комментарий:**

**Commentary:** The season is attractive (*lalite*) with the fragrance of mādhavī creepers or with the fragrance of a consort, Rādhā (*mādhavikā),* caused by being crushed by her lover. It is said:

*āliṅgyaiva priyaṁ yāti kāma-nirjita-cetasā \|*  
*sphurati pratyukti-racanā mādhavī nāyiketi sā \|\|*

**The mādhavī heroine, her heart conquered by love, giving some response, goes and embraces her lover.**

The word *mādhava* means spring and the hero. Spring makes her hurry to meet Kṛṣṇa. Spring acts as a strong *uddīpana*. Spring is most fragrant with fresh jasmine flowers. Spring causes bewilderment even in the minds of sages, what to speak of others. Also it is suggested that spring bewilder even the minds of proud women (having *māna*). Spring causes spontaneous friendship or the highest bliss among young people. Instead of *nava-mālati-jāti* sometime *nava-mālika-jāti* is seen. This means fragrance of fresh flower garlands and jasmine. *Parimala* means fragrance arising from crushing according to *Amara-koṣa.*

### Text 34

*sphurad-atimukta-latā-parirambhana-mukulita-pulakita-cūte \|*  
*vṛndāvana-vīpine parīsara-parigata-yamunā-jala-pūte \|\|34\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sphurad-atimukta-lata - Of the splendid atimukta (vasanti) creeper; parirambhana - because of the embrace; mukulita-pulakita - \[are\] budding and bristling; cute - \[in Spring\] whose mango trees; vrndavana-vipine - in the vrndavana forest; parisara-parigata -spread everywhere around the area; yamuna-jala-pute - purified by the waters of the Yamuna \[river\],

**Перевод:**

**In the spring, the mangos’ hairs are standing on end because of the embrace of the splendid** ***vāsantī*** **creeper. The Vṛndāvana forests are surrounded compeltely by the purified waters of the Yamunā.**

**Комментарий:**

The mango trees have hairs standing on end because of the embrace of splendid or desirable *vāsantī* creepers (*atimukta*). By the embrace of the creeper trembling with *sāttvika-bhāvas* due to meeting her lover, the mango tree’s hairs stand on end. Even plants develop symptoms of love. The word *atimukta* also suggests that passion to embrace must develop even in the most detached person (*atimukta*) during spring. Or some detached persons, attracted to the intense sweetness of Kṛṣṇa, took the form of creepers in Vṛndāvana to worship him. This suggests that person in Vaikuṇṭha (*atimukta)* desire to become creepers in Vṛndāvana because of the great sweetness. It is said:

*āsām aho caraṇa-reṇu-juṣām ahaṁ syāṁ*  
*vṛndāvane kim api gulma-latauṣadhīnām*

**Oh, let me be fortunate enough to be one of the bushes, creepers or herbs in Vṛndāvana, because the** ***gopīs*** **bless them with the dust of their lotus feet. SB 10.47.61**

Up till this point the descriptions of spring indicate rasa and misery. Now details of the place for pastimes in spring are described. Kṛṣṇa plays *(harir viharati* from the chorus) in the forest called Vṛndāvana. The forest is described. It is purified by the water of the Yamunā spread everywhere (*parigata)* around the area (*parīsara*). Or Vṛndāvana is surrounded everywhere by the pure water of the Yamunā. The pure Yamunā water becomes even purer at this place. How pure Vṛndāvana must be! It is said:

*raso yaḥ paramādhāraḥ sac-cid-ānanda-lakṣaṇaḥ \|*  
*brahmety upaniṣad-gītaḥ sa eva yamunā svayam \|\|*

**Kṛṣṇa, who is Brahman, praised in the Upaniṣads, who is** ***rasa*** **itself, and supreme basis, characterized by eternity, knowledge and bliss is personally the Yamunā.**

Thus it should be concluded that Vṛndāvana is desired by the residents of Vaikuṇṭha since it purifies even Yamunā, which is greatly praised. Vṛndāvana is especially purifying because it gives an experience of the excellent rasas of Kṛṣṇa, not just giving destruction of sin. Vṛndāvana means “one who protects (*avati)* the campaka and other trees (*vṛndam*) or the groups of gopīs” since the forest produces happiness in the Lord’s pastimes. *Atimukta* means a *mādhavi* or *vāsantī* creeper. *Parīsara* means the land at the perimeter. The author in the next verse summarizes the meaning of the song by speaking of the excellence of his composition.

### Text 35

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇitam idam udayati hari-caraṇa-smṛti-sāram \|*  
*sarasa-vasanta-samaya-vana-varṇanam anugata-madana-vikāram \|\|35\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva-bhanitam - Spoken by Sri Jayadeva; idam - this \[work\]; udayati - shines; hari-carana - of the feet of Krsna; smrti-saram -\[because\] its essence is the remembrance; sarasa-vasanta-samaya - in the spring season full of \[srngara\] rasa; vana-varnanam - its description is the forest; anugata-madana - consists of the love \[of Radha\]; vikaram - \[and\] its emotion.

**Перевод:**

**This work spoken by the poet Jayadeva is the best since it gives remembrance of the feet of Kṛṣṇa, describes the forest in the spring season full of śṛṅgāra-rasa and describes the strong emotions of Rādhā’s love.**

**Комментарий:**

The author then summarizes the meaning of the song by speaking of the excellence of his composition.

This work spoken by the poet Jayadeva shines *(udayati*) as the best object (*sāram*) because it gives remembrance of the feet of Kṛṣṇa. Or the work has the essence *(sāram*), remembrance of the feet of Kṛṣṇa. How else can the work be described? It contains descriptions of the forest in the spring season and has a predominance of śṛṅgāra-rasa *(sarasam),* containing strong emotions of love—according to the words of a sakhī speaking to Rādhā, which I have recorded. Or, the work produces emotions related to love in the heart of the hearer. The phrase *anugata-madana-vikāram* can also modify *sarasa-vasanta-samaya-vana-varṇanam*: the work contains descriptions of the forest in spring full of rasa and those descriptions cause emotions of love.

### Text 36

*dara-vidalita-mallī-valli-cañcat-parāga-*  
*prakaṭita-paṭa-vāsair vāsayan kānanāni \|*  
*iha hi dahati cetaḥ ketakī-gandha-bandhuḥ*  
*prasarad-asama-bāṇa-prāṇavad gandhavāhaḥ \|\|36\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

dara-vidalita - Slightly opened; malli-valli - from jasmine creepers; cancat - emanating; paraga - as pollen; prakatita - manifested; pata-vasaih - with perfumed dust; vasayan - perfuming; kananani - the forests; iha - now (in the spring); hi - indeed; dahati - \[it\] burns; cetah - the hearts \[of separated lovers\]; ketaki-gandha - of the ketaki fragrance; bandhuh - the friend; prasarad - which spread \[everywhere\] (or developing desire); asama-bana - of he who has an odd number of (i.e. five) arrows (i.e. the Cupid); pranavat - like the life-breath; gandhavahah - bearer of fragrance (the breeze)

**Перевод:**

**In the spring the breeze, the friend of the ketaki fragrance, scenting the forests with pollen enamating from slightly blossoming jasmine creepers, burns the hearts of separated lovers and gives life to the five arrows of Cupid spreading everywhere.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse speaks of the spring breeze causing pain for the lover in separation. In the spring *(iha)* the wind (*gandha-vāhaḥ*) burns the heart of separated lovers. It perfumes *(vāsayan)* the forests with fragrant dust (*patā-vāsaiḥ*) in the form of pollen emanating from clusters of slightly opened jasmine creeper. The breeze is the friend of the ketaki flowers’ fragrance. The breeze is like the life air (*prāṇavat*) of the five arrows (*asama-bāṇa*) of Cupid spreading in all direction. Or, the fragrant breeze is endowed with power *(prāṇavat*) because of the five arrows spreading everywhere. Or the hearts of separated lovers (*cetaḥ*) are as if brought to life (*prāṇavat*) by Cupid (*asama-bāṇa),* developing desire (*prasarat).*

*Dara* means slightly according to *Viśva. Parāga* is pollen. *Paṭa-vāsaka* is fragrant powder according to *Amara-koṣa. Gandha-vāha* means the wind.

### Text 37

*adryotsaṅga-vasad-bhujaṅga-kavala-kleśād iveśācalaṁ*  
*prāleya-plavanecchayānusarati śrīkhaṇḍa-śailānilaḥ \|*  
*kiṁ ca snigdha-rasāla-mauli-mukulāny ālokya harṣodayād*  
*unmīlanti kuhūḥ kuhūr iti kalottālāḥ pikānāṁ giraḥ \|\|37\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

adryotsanga - On the lap of mountain; vasat - dwelling; bhujanga - of the snakes; kavala — caused by the biting; klesad - because of the pain; iva - as if; isacalam - Mountain of Siva (the Himalayas) praleya - into the snow; plavanecchaya - out of the desire to submerge; anusarati - pursues; srikhanda-sailanilah - the breeze from Srikhanda-saila (Malaya mountain); him ca - also; snigdha-rasala - of the charming mango trees; mauli-mukulani - the tips of the buds; alokya - having seen; harsodayad - with joy; unmilanti - becomes manifest; kuhuh-kuhuh-iti - “coo coo, coo coo” (the warbling of the cuckoos); kalottalah - sweet and excellent; pikanam - of the cuckoos; girah - voices.

**Перевод:**

**The breeze from Malaya Mountain blows towards the Himālayas with a desire to submerge itself in snow because of the pain caused by the wind being touched by mouths of snakes living in the mountain. The excellent, sweet, soft warbling of cuckoos arising out of joy on seeing the tips of buds on charming mango trees also manifests at this time.**

**Комментарий:**

The wind from Malaya Mountain (*śrīkhaṇḍa-śaila*) pursues the Himālaya, the mountain of Śiva, because of pain arising from the biting of snakes residing in the lap of Malaya Mountain. Why does the breeze move towards the Himālaya? It desires to become submerged in snow. This indicates that the pain of separated lovers increases from contact with the poison in the wind which has been devoured and then vomited by snakes. Actually the south wind is cool and pleasant but because of the intolerance of separated lovers that wind is criticized as being swallowed by poisonous snakes and needing snow to cool it.

Not only is the wind intolerable, something else is intolerable in spring. The warbling of cuckoos, which is indistinct and sweet *(kalā)* and most excellent (*uttālāḥ)* becomes manifest (*unmīlanti*) because of their bliss on seeing the tips of buds on the charming mango trees. Instead of *adryotsaṅge* sometimes *adyotsaṅga* (lap of spring) is seen. *Prāleya* means snow according to *Amara-kosa. Uttāla* means best according to *Viśva.*

### Text 38

*unmīlan-madhu-gandha-lubdha-madhupa-vyādhūta-cūtāṅkura-*  
*krīḍat-kokila-kākalī-kalakalair\[\[10\]\]udgīrṇa-karṇa-jvaraḥ \|*  
*nīyante pathikaiḥ kathaṁ katham api dhyānāvadhāna-kṣaṇa*  
*prāpta-prāṇa-samāgama-rasollāsair amī vāsarāḥ \|\|38\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

unmilan-madhu - Of oozing sap; gandha - for the fragrance; lubdha - greedy; madhupa - by the bees; vyadhuta - shaken; cutankura -in the buds of the mango trees; kridat - playing; kokila - of the cuckoos; kakali-kala-kalaih - because of the uproars \[caused\] by the sweet voices; udgirna - \[is\] caused; karna-jvarah - in which pain for the ears; niyante - are attained; pathikaih - by travelers (who are separated from their lover); katham katham- somehow; api - even; dhyanavadhana - of intense thinking \[on his wife\]; ksana - at that moment; prapta - to be obtained; prana-sama - equal to his life; samagama - for a meeting; rasollasaih - in whom desire springs up; ami-vasarah - those days \[of spring\]

**Перевод:**

**For separated lovers, the spring days produce pain for their ears on hearing the soft cooing of cuckoos as they play in the mango buds shaken by bees greedy for the fragrance and oozing sap, because at that time the lover develops eagerness for a pleasurable meeting--his very life--when he begins intensely thinking of his beloved.**

**Комментарий:**

The duration of the days of spring is also without end. The days *(vāsarāḥ*) of spring *(amī)* result in great pain for separated lovers (*pathikaiḥ*). The days manifest pain for their ears because there is a great disturbance caused by the cooing (*kākalī)* of cuckoos frolicking in the mango buds shaken by bees greedy for their fragrance and oozing honey. It is said that in the spring the cuckoos sing the fifth note. This is an uddīpana for love. The sounds create pain because lovers develop eagerness for the rasa of meeting, their life and soul, attained at the moment they hear the cuckoo by intense meditation on their beloved. It is hinted that Kṛṣṇa spends his days during the spring meditating on his beloved. *Kākalī* means a soft, sweet, indistinct sound. *Kalakalaḥ* means a great noise.

### Text 39

*aneka-nārī-parirambha-sambhrama-*  
*sphuraṇ-manohāri-vilāsa-lālasam \|*  
*murārim ārād upadarśayanty asau*  
*sakhī samakṣaṁ punar āha rādhikām \|\|39\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

aneka-nari - Of many women; parirambha - for the embrace; sambhrama - by \[his\] eagerness; sphuran - revealing (or demonstrated or manifested); manohari - of the heart stealer; vilasa - because of the arousal of love (or coquettish gestures or actions); lalasam - longing (or praying or love); murarim - the enemy of demon Mura (Krsna); arat - at a distance; upadarsayanti - while pointing out; asau-sakhi - that sakhi; samaksam - before the very eyes; punar aha - again spoke; radhikam - to Radha

**Перевод:**

**The sakhī again spoke to Rādhā, while pointing out at a distance Kṛṣṇa, who was longing for her with arousal of love, manifesting in his zealous embrace of many women.**

The *uddīpanas* have been shown. Now what the *sakhī* perceives is described. The *sakhī (asau)* speaks to Rādhā again. What is the sakhī doing? She points out Kṛṣṇa who can be seen in the distance *(ārāt*). He has attractive longing for Rādhā arising from arousal of love *(vilāsa),* revealed by his zealous embraces of many women. Or he has longing for the attractive coquettish gestures of Rādhā (*vilāsa)* which are expressed (*sphuran*) by her zealous embraces of many women. Or he prays to the *sakhīs* on remembering Rādhā’s actions in which she excitedly glanced at and embraced her *sakhīs*. Or the *sakhī* demonstrates his symptoms of love *(lālasam*) with attractive actions manifesting as zealous embracing many women. The great qualities in Kṛṣṇa are shown by describing the *gopīs* emotions on meeting him. It will be said later *pīna-payodhara*: they had swollen breasts. (GG 1.41)

*Lālasa* means longing according to *Viśva. Ārāt* means “in the distance” or “close.” according to *Amara-koṣa*. By using the word *sambhrama,* it is shown that he was more eager to embrace than the *gopīs* were. He was seen at a distance or close up (*ārāt).* The *sakhī* describes everything in the next song.

## Song 4: Krishna plays with the inexperienced gopis

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 4 \|\|**  
**rāmakarī-rāga-yati-tālābhyāṁ gīyate \|**

### Text 40

*candana-carcita-nīla-kalevara-pīta-vasana-vana-mālī \|*  
*keli-calan-maṇi-kuṇḍala-maṇḍita-gaṇḍa-yuga-smita-śālī \|\|40\|\|*  
*harir iha mugdha-vadhū-nikare vilāsini vilāsati kelī-pare \|\|dhru\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

candana-carcita - Smeared with sandal paste; nila-kalevara - \[and\] with a bluish body; pita-vasana - with yellow clothing; vana-mali - \[he is\] wearing a forest-garland; keli-calat - moving playfully; mani-kundala - with jewel earrings; mandita - is decorated; ganda-yuga — \[and\] the pair of \[his\] cheeks; smita-sall - \[he is\] full of smiles; harih - Krsna; iha - there \[in Vrndavana\]; mugdha-vadhu - of young \[unskillful (or beautiful)\] women (gopis); nikare- in the assembly of; vilasini - О mistress (Radha)! vilasati - plays; kell-pare - О \[Radha\] bent on coquetry!

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa wears a forest garland and yellow cloth and his blue body is smeared with sandalwood. He smiles and his two cheeks are decorated with jewel earrings moving about playfully.**  
**In Vṛndāvana, Kṛṣṇa plays among the assembly of innocent gopīs who are absorbed in the play. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The characteristics of the *rāmakarī-rāga*\[\[11\]\]are as follows:

*svarṇa-prabhā bhāsvara-bhūṣaṇā ca*  
*nīlaṁ nicolaṁ vahantī \|*  
*kānte padopāntam adhiśrite’pi*  
*mānonnatā rāma-karī pradiṣṭā \|\|*

***Rāma-karī*** **is a very proud woman, with golden complexion and shining ornaments, wearing a blue cloth on her beautiful body, while her lover falls at her feet.**

*Yati-tāla* was described previously.

The meaning is this. O Rādhā, fond of coquetry! In Vṛndāvana (*iha*) Kṛṣṇa plays among an assembly of beautiful *(mugdha*) women, who are absorbed in the play (*keli-pare*). The wo*rd mugdha* also indicates that these women do not know the art of gestures in love (*mugdha* means ignorant as well). They are absorbed in the playing but it is ordinary play. The word *vilāsini* addressed to Rādhā indicates that she is knowledgeable of dalliance. He who knows all arts is waiting for you. *Mugdha* means beautiful or ignorant according to *Viśva. Vilāsa* means excellent dalliance. The characteristics of *vilāsa* are as follows:

*yāna-sthānāsanādīnāṁ netra-vaktrādi-karmaṇām \|*  
*utpadyate viśeṣo yaḥ sa vilāsaḥ prakīrtitaḥ \|\|*

***Vilāsa*** **is known as manifestion of special actions such as making the eyes crooked and choosing a special carriage, place and seat. Nāṭya-śāstra**

Or *keli-pare* can also be an address to Rādhā like *vilāsini*. O Rādhā who has the most excellent (*pare*) pastimes (*keli*)! *Mugdha-vadhva* can also indicate the class of heroine called *mugdha. Hariḥ* means “he who attracts all women by the skill of his pastimes.” He who is skillful in all arts desires only you since you alone can complete his desired pastimes since others are devoid of proper dalliance.

The qualities of the *mugdha* heroine are described in Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 1.35:

*mugdhā nava-vadhūs tatra nava-yauvana-bhūṣitā \|*  
*navānaṅga-rahasyāpi lajjā-prāya-ratir yathā \|\|*

**The** ***mugdha*** **heroine is young, newely ornamented with youth and thus her love is filled with shynes, even if the love is secret.**

*kṛtāparādhe dayite bāṣpa-ruddhāvalokanā*  
*priyāpriyoktau cāśaktā mane ca vimukhī sadā*

**The** ***mugdhā*** **woman weeps after her lover commits offense, does not reply to the lover with unkind words, and avoids pride. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 5.14**

*Nava-yauvana* is described:

*ṣac-capala-netrāntaṁ smara-smera-mukhāmbujam \|*  
*sa-garva-jarajogaṇḍam asamagrāruṇādharam \|\|*  
*lāvaṇyodbheda-ramyāṅgaṁ vilasad-bhāva-saurabham \|*  
*unmīlitāṅkura-kucam asphuṭāṅgaka-sandhikam \|\|*  
*prathamaṁ yauvanaṁ tatra vartamānā mṛgekṣaṇā \|*  
*apekṣate mṛdu-sparśaṁ sahate noddhatāṁ ratim \|\|*  
*sakhī-keli-ratā svāṅga-saṁskāra-kalitādarā \|*  
*na kopa-harṣau bhajate sapatnī-darśanādiṣu \|*  
*nātirajyati kāntasya saṅgame kiṁ tu lajjate \|\|*

**She has slightly restless eyes and a lotus face with a smile of love. Her cheeks are colored with pride and her lips are unevenly red. Her attractive limbs sprout with beauty. She has the fragrance of shining bhāvas. Her breasts are becoming full. Her joints are not clear. She has eyes like a doe. This is the** ***nava yauvana.*** **She expects a light touch from the lover and cannot tolerate bold love. She is absorbed in playing with her friends. She carefully keeps her body clean. She does not show anger or joy on seeing cowives. She is not too conspicious but is shy in uniting with her lover. Rasārṇava-sudhākara 1.263-266**

In the verse, the qualities of heroines who are *dakṣiṇa* are mentioned.

*yo gauravaṁ bhayaṁ prema dākṣiṇyaṁ pūrva-yoṣiti \|*  
*na muñcaty anya-citto’pi jñeyo’sau khalu dakṣinaḥ \|\|*

**The** ***dakṣiṇā*** **(compliant woman) is the person who, though having a new lover, cannot free himself from respect, fear, and affection for his previous lover, caused by softness of his nature*****.*** **Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi** ***1*****.32**

Some say that the *dakṣina* man is one who is equal to many women (Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 1.34), but that does not apply in the verse since Kṛṣṇa is seen to have excessive attachment to Rādhā. *Sarva-gopīṣu saivaikā viṣṇor atyanta-vallabhā*: Rādhā alone is the dearest among all the gopīs. (*Padma Purāṇa*) Bhāgavatam also says *anayārādhito nūnaṁ bhagavān harir īśvaraḥ:* certainly this particular gopī has perfectly controlled the all-powerful Lord who takes away distress. (SB 10.30.28)

### Text 41

*pīna-payodhara-bhāra-bhareṇa hariṁ parirabhya sarāgam \|*  
*gopa-vadhūr anugāyati kācid udañcita-pañcama-rāgam \|\|41\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

pina-payodhara - Of \[her\] full breasts; bhara-bharena - with the heavy weight; harim - Krsna; parirabhya - having embraced; saragam - enamored \[of Radha\]; gopa-vadhuh - a cowherd damsel (gopi); anugayati - joins \[him\] in \[his\] singing; kacid - someone; udancita - lifted up; paricama-raga - endowed with the fifth note (‘pa’)

**Перевод:**

**One gopī, embracing him with her full, heavy breasts, sang following after his singing, and sang the fifth note most prominently while Kṛṣṇa was attracted to Rādhā.**

**Комментарий:**

*cāpalāvihatā dhairyaṁ cid-vṛttir avikatthanā \|*  
*priyānukaraṇaṁ līlā madhurāṅga-viceṣṭitaiḥ \|\|*

**The woman shows fickleness and patience, hides her emotions, does not boast, imitates the beloved, and makes gestures with her graceful limbs. Daśarūpaka 2.38**

Since she followed after Kṛṣṇa’s singing, it is implied that the knowledge of singing was less than that of Rādhā. Rasa arises when the knowledge is the same. For instance it is said:

*kācit samaṁ mukundena svara-jātīr amiśritāḥ*  
*unninye pūjitā tena prīyatā sādhu sādhv iti*  
*tad eva dhruvam unninye tasyai mānaṁ ca bahv adāt*

**One** ***gopī*****, joining Mukunda in his singing, sang pure melodious tones that rose harmoniously above his voice. Kṛṣṇa was pleased and showed great appreciation for her performance, saying “Excellent! Excellent!” When another** ***gopī*** **sang in the dhruva style, Kṛṣṇa praised her even more. SB 10.33.9**

The phrase *sa-rāgam* describes Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is endowed with color *(rāga)* which has fallen on him when he enjoyed with Rādhā or he has great attraction for Rādhā. The reason is given. He sings the fifth note predominantly (*udañcita-pañcama-rāgam*). It is said *madhyaṁ pañcama-bhūyiṣṭaṁ hāsya-śṛṅṅāragyor bhavet:* in hāsya and *śṛṅgāra rasas* the fifth note in the middle range predominates. (Nāṭya-śāstra) Attachment in *śṛṅgāra* increases by predominance of the fifth note in singing. It is said:

*niṣāda-rṣabha-gāndhāra-ṣaḍja-madhyama-dhaivatāḥ \|*  
*pañcamaś cety amī sapta tantrī-kaṇṭhotthitāḥ svarāḥ \|\|*

**The seven notes in singing and instrumental music are** ***sa*****,** ***re*****,** ***ga***  ***ma***  ***pa*** **(*****pañcama*****)** ***dha*** **and** ***ni*****.**

*niṣādaṁ heṣate vājī gāvas tv ṛṣabha-bhāṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*ajāvikaṁ tu gāndhāraṁ dhaivataṁ bṛṁhate gajaḥ \|\|*  
*ṣaḍjaṁ mayūrā bruvate mṛgā madhyama-bhāṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*puṣpa-sādhāraṇe kāle kokilā rauti pañcamam \|\|*

***Ni*** **resembles the horse’s neigh.** ***Re*** **resembles the cow’s lowing.** ***Ga*** **resembles the goat’s bleating.** ***Dha*** **resembles the elephant’s roar.** ***Sa*** **resembles the peacock’s cry.** ***Ma*** **resembles the deer’s cry.** ***Pa*** **resembles the cry of the cuckoo when flowers blossom.**

*ṣaḍjaṁ vaden mayūra ṛṣabhaṁ cātako vadet \|*  
*ajā vadati gāndhāraṁ krauñco vadati madhyamam \|*  
*puṣpa-sādhāraṇe kāle kokilaḥ pañcamaṁ vadet \|*  
*dardurā dhaivataṁ caiva niṣādaṁ ca vaded gajaḥ \|\|*

***Sa*** **is the cry of the peacock*****. Re*** **is the cry of the cātaka bird.** ***Ga*** **is the bleating of the goat.** ***Ma*** **is the cry of the osprey*****. Pa*** **is the song of the cuckoo at the time of flowers blooming.** ***Da*** **is the croaking of the frog and** ***Ni*** **is the roar of the elephant.**

*hāsya-śṛṅgārayor jñeyau svarau pañcama-madhyamau \|*  
*ṣaḍja-rṣabhau tathā jñeyau vīre raudrādbhute rase \|\|*  
*gāndhāraś ca niṣādhaś ca kartavyau karuṇe rase \|*  
*dhaivataś caiva kartavyo bībhatse ca bhayānake \|\|*

*bhairavaḥ pañcamo megha-mallāro gauḍo mālavaḥ \|*  
*deśākhyaś ceti ṣaḍ rāgāḥ procyante loka-viśrutāḥ \|\|*  
*sapta-svarās trayo grāmā mūrchanāś caikaviṁśatiḥ \|*  
*śrutīnāṁ dvāviṁśatiś caiva tānāḥ pañcāśad-ūnakāḥ \|\|*

***Pa*** **and** ***ma*** **indicate** ***hāsya*** **and** ***śrṅgāra*****.** ***Sa*** **and** ***re*** **indicate** ***vīra, raudra*** **and** ***adbhuta rasa. Ga*** **and** ***Ni*** **indicate** ***karuna-rasa. Dha*** **indicates** ***bībhatsa*** **and** ***bhayānaka rasa.*** **The six main** ***rāgas*** **are** ***bhairava, pañcama, megha-mallāra, gauḍa, mālava and deśa*****. There are seven notes, three grāmas and twenty-one mūrchanas. There are twenty-two śrutis and forty-nine tānas.**

It is clearly related that this gopī was bewildered by his *rāga* and became charmed as she sang imitatively while embracing Kṛṣṇa, though he sang to indicate to Rādhā and that he wanted to enjoy with her. The woman became bewildered by his singing. It is said:

*gopyas tad-gītam ākarṇya mūrcchitā nāvidan nṛpa*  
*sraṁsad-dukūlam ātmānaṁ srasta-keśa-srajaṁ tataḥ*

**The gopīs became stunned when they heard that song. O King, they did not notice that their fine garments were becoming loose and their hair and garlands disheveled. SB 10.34.24**

Śruti says:

*tad-rohi-janma te devā abruvan apa yuṣmad akramīn nāsmān upāvartate vi hvayāmahā iti brahma-gandharvā avadann āgāyan devāḥ sā devān gāyata upāvartata \| tasmād gāyantaṁ striyaḥ kāmayate kāmukā etaṁ striyo bhavanti*

**She adopted the form of a deer and ran away from the Gandharvas. The devatās said, “She has run from you; she comes not to us; let us both summon her.” The Gandharvas uttered a spell, the gods sang, and she went to the gods as they sang. Therefore women love one who sings; enamoured are women with the person who thus knows. Taittiriya-saṁhitā 6.1.6.6.4, Yajur-veda**

One *gopī* siinging in front of Rādhā shows that *gopī’s* disrespect. Pañcama refers to a type of *rāga* according to *Viśva*.

### Text 42

*kāpi vilāsa-vilola-vilocana-khelana-janita-manojam \|*  
*dhyāyati mugdha-vadhūr adhikaṁ madhusūdana-vadana-sarojam \|\|42\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kapi - A certain; vilasa - of pleasure; vilola-vilocana - of \[his\] flickering glances; khelana - by the play; janita-manojam - by which desire is produced; dhyayati - contemplates; mugdha-vadhuh - the inexperienced damsel (gopi); adhikam - excessively; madhusudana -of Madhusudana (Krsna); vadana-sarojam - the lotus face.

**Перевод:**

**One inexperienced gopī contemplates Kṛṣṇa’s lotus face which produces desire in her by the play of his flickering glances.**

**Комментарий:**

One innocent, infatuated *gopī* thinks of the lotus face of Kṛṣṇa excessively (*adhikam*). Kṛṣṇa’s face produced desire in her, which has arisen by the play of his flickering eyes. *Adhikam* can also modify *vadama-sarojam*. His lotus face is incomparable in beauty. His face is compared to a lotus because the lotus has qualities like fragrance. Thus it stimulates desire and attracts a person by the flow of sweet rasa. Kṛṣṇa’s face is similar.The word Madhusūdana suggests Kṛṣṇa’s unsteady love (like a bee) for that particular *gopī*. This *gopī* is called inexperienced (*mugdha*). She is not expert since she meditates even in the actual presence of Kṛṣṇa.

### Text 43

*kāpi kapola-tale militā lapituṁ kim api śruti-mūle \|*  
*cāru cucumba nitambavatī dayitaṁ pulakair anukūle \|\|43\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kapi - A certain (gopi); kapola-tale - at the base of his forehead; milita - having attached herself; lapitum -in order to whisper; kim - something; api - even; sruti-mule - to the base of \[his\] ear; caru -attractively (or joyfully); cucumba - \[she\] kissed \[him\]; nitambavati - with fine hips; dayitam - the beloved; pulakaih - with hairs stood on end; anukule - well disposed (indicating his desire for her).

**Перевод:**

**One gopī with fine hips whipered in his ear and, when his hairs stood on end, she kissed him on the forehead.**

**Комментарий:**

One woman with fine hips kissed her lover attractively at the base of his forehead. How is she described? She attached herself below his ear in order to whisper about some activity. He indicated his desire for her (*anukule*) by his hairs standing on end. She thought that boldly kissing Kṛṣṇa would cause laughter among her friends. On this pretext she showed her skill and began to kiss him. It is suggested however that she became unsteady by that first kiss. Her charming hips indicate beauty.

Or, some beautiful *gopī*, most skillful, your *sakhī*, attached herself to the base of his ear in order to inform him of how to meet you. Then what did she do? She then kissed him nicely on the forehead when he favorably developed goose bumps -since he developed *sāttvika-bhāva* out of love for you. Therefore she joyful (*cāru*) kissed him. When he reacts in this way on hearing news of you, it means he desires to meet you. Thus you must meet him.

### Text 44

*keli-kalā-kutukena ca kācid amuṁ yamunā-jala-kūle \|*  
*mañjula-vañjula-kuñja-gataṁ vicakarṣa kareṇa dukūle \|\|44\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

keli-kala - For pastimes; kutukena - out of desire; ca - and; kacid - a certain (gopi); amum - him (Krsna); yamuna-jala-kule - on the bank of the Yamuna \[River\]; mahjula-vanjula - of attractive reeds; kunja-gatam - \[though he had\] gone to a grove; vicakarsa - \[she\] pulled \[him\]; karena - by his hand; dukule - to a fine piece of cloth.

**Перевод:**

**One gopī eager for pastimes pulled Kṛṣṇa by the hand to a piece of cloth on the bank of the Yamunā, though he had hidden himself in an attractive grove of reeds.**

**Комментарий:**

One *gopī* pulled Kṛṣṇa *(amum*) to a cloth, with playful gestures, though he had entered an attractive grove of reeds. Some say the verse means that using her hand she pulled him by his cloth. Following your *sakhī’s* instruction he had gone there to fool the other *gopīs*. She pulled him to the bank of the Yamunā. She pulled him to the bank of the Yamunā to play with him though he had hidden himself in the grove of reeds, hoping that you would come. It is suggested that he was unsettled by the *gopī* pulling his cloth, since he was attracted to another. Or she pulled his cloth with her hand when he had gone to the reed grove on the bank of the Yamunā after he fooled the other *gopīs*, since he was most eager for pastimes with you (*keli-kalā-kutukena). Kutuka* means desire according to *Amara-koṣa. Mañjula* means attractive. *Vañjula* means reeds. *Dukūla* means fine cloth.

### Text 45

*kara-tala-tāla-tarala-valayāvali-kalita-kala-svana-vaṁśe \|*  
*rāsa-rase saha-nṛtya-parā hariṇā yuvatī praśaśaṁse \|\|45\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kara-tala-tala - \[Accompanied by\] the clapping of the surface of the hands; tarala-valayavail - the jingling series of bracelets; kalita-kala-svana - by which a soft sound is made; vamse - \[and\] the flute; rasa-rase - with a taste for the rasa dance (or in great astonishment); saha-nrtya-para - equal \[to Krsna\] in dancing; harina - by Krsna; yuvati - a young woman (Radha); prasasamse - was praised

**Перевод:**

**One young gopī, equal to Kṛṣṇa in dancing, with a taste for the rāsa dance, accompanied by hand clapping, jingling of bells and soft, sweet notes of the flute, is being praised by Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

“How can Kṛṣṇa be attracted to me if he is playing with other gopīs? Are you not speaking uselessly?” The *sakhī* then says, “Listen first hand.” One young woman called Rādhā is praised by Kṛṣṇa. How is she described? She dances equally with Kṛṣṇa, with taste for the rāsa dance, where there are many dancers or she dances equally with Kṛṣṇa in great astonishment because of the skill in singing. That taste of the *rāsa* dance has the sound of the flute along with sweet indistinct notes, mixed with moving rows of bells and clapping of hands.

The intention is this. With attraction for the rāsa dance, with clapping of hands, jingling of bells and the soft, sweet notes of the flute, Rādhā, dancing equally to Kṛṣṇa, is praised by him. At this moment he praises you by some hidden hints in playing the flute mixed with hand clapping. The other *gopīs* are not aware of his message to you. They are inexperienced and ignorant.

*Tāla* means clapping the hands according to *Viśva. Tarala* means fickle. *Vaṁśa* is a flute. *Rāsa* mean playing of the cowherd women*. Rāsa-rasa* means the *rāsa* dance.

### Text 46

*śliṣyati kām api cumbati kām api kām api ramayati rāmām \|*  
*paśyati sa smita-cāru parām\[\[12\]\]aparām anugacchati vāmām \|\|46\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

slisyati - \[He\] embraces; kam api - a certain (gopi); cumbati - \[he\] kisses; kam api - another; kam api - and another; ramayati - \[he\] plays with; ramam - beautiful woman (or a woman who is inclined to play); pasyati - \[he\] gazes at; sa-he; smita-caru - with a pleasant smile; param - a final (last gopi); aparam - and \[yet\] another \[gopi\]; anugacchati - \[he\] becomes submissive \[to yet another\]; vamam -lovely \[or devious\] woman \[gopi\].

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa embraces one gopī, kisses another, plays with another and gazes at a last gopī while smiling. He becomes submissive to another gopī who is obstinate.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa embraces one woman inclined to play *(rāmām*). He kisses another and plays with another using sweet words. He sees a last *gopī* (*parām)* while smiling. He becomes submissive (*anugacchati*) to your sakhī, another *gopī* with attractive nature or crooked nature (*vāmām*) who leaves in anger on seeing Kṛṣṇa embrace the others who approached him submissively. Or, he becomes submissive to you who are obstinate in nature (*vāmām)* after cheating the others by smiling and glancing at them. Therefore, do not delay in meeting with him. *Rāmā* means a beautiful woman according to *Amara-koṣa.*

### Text 47

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇitam idam adbhuta-keśava-keli-rahasyam \|*  
*vipina-vinoda-kalā-valitaṁ\[\[13\]\]vitanotu śubhāni yaśasyam \|\|47\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva-bhanitam - Spoken by Sri Jayadeva; idam - this \[song\]; adbhuta kesava - of astonishing Kesava (Krsna); keli-rahasyam -the expertise in pastimes; vipina - in the forest; vinoda-kala - with \[his\] skillful amusements; valitam - furnished; vitanotu - may it \[the song\] spread; subhani - auspiciousness; yasasyam - conferring fame

**Перевод:**

**May this ultimate song spoken by Jayadeva, filled with the expert pastimes of astonishing Kṛṣṇa and describing his skilful play in the forest, and which gives fame, spread auspiciousness.**

**Комментарий:**

May this ultimate song (*idam*) spoken by the poet Jayadeva spread auspiciousness. It is filled with the most expert pastimes of astonishing Kṛṣṇa. He is astonishing because he attracts many attractive *gopīs*, though he is attracted by the rasa of Rādhā. The word Keśava indicates Kṛṣṇa’s beauty. The song is also endowed with skilfulness of pastimes in the forest. It gives fame. Another version has *vṛndāvana-vipine lalitam:* this song is beautiful (*lalitam*) or filled with *śṛṅgāra-rasa* and contains the astonishing skilfl pastimes of Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana.

In this poem, the heroine goes to meet her lover (*abhisārikā)*. The hero is bold *(dhṛṣṭa*), has many lovers (*dakṣina*), is favorable (*anukūla*)\[\[14\]\]and cunning (*śatha*). His boldness is shown when it is stated that he plays with one *gopī (kām api ramayati*).

*abhivyaktānya-taruṇī-bhoga-lakṣmāpi nirbhayaḥ \|*  
*mithyā-vacana-dakṣaś ca dhṛṣṭo’yaṁ khalu kathyate \|\|*

**The lover who is fearless though bearing the marks of enjoyment with other women on his body, who is skilful at lying is called** ***dhṛṣṭa.*** **Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 1.40**

The *dakṣīna* hero was explained in the commentary on GG 1.40. Cunning is described as follows:

*priyaṁ vakti puro’nyatra vipriyaṁ kurute bhṛśam \|*  
*jñātāparādha-ceṣṭaś ca kuṭilo’sau śaṭho yathā \|\|*  
*nigūḍhāṁ aparadham ca*

**The deceitful hero speaks nicely in front of a woman, but acts against her when she is not present. He is crooked and has another love affair without her knowing. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 1.27**

Kṛṣṇa went to a grove of attractive reeds. This shows his offensive action and secret nature. Kṛṣṇa was *anukūla* to one *gopī*. This shows his favorable nature. This will be further explained in Song 16. He is always favorable to Rādhā though here he shows *dhīrodātta anukūla* nature.

*kuvalaya-dṛśaḥ saṅketa-sthā dṛg-añcala-kauśalair*  
*manasija-kalā-nāṭī-prastāvanām abhitanvatām \|*  
*na kila ghaṭate rādhā-raṅga-prasaṅga-vidhāyitā*  
*vrata-vilasite śaithilyasya cchaṭāpy agha-vidviṣaḥ \|\|*

**O lotus-eyed women waiting in the your bowers! By giving signals with skilful glances, you should display skilful introductory dances of love! But Kṛṣṇa does not become slack in executing his vow to make Rādhā become attached to Cupid’s dances. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 1.28**

Though Kṛṣṇa is actually a *dhīra-lalitānukūla* lover, all qualities of the hero manifest in Kṛṣṇa in order to nourish Rādhā’s *rasa* by the various *bhāvas.*

### Text 48

*viśveṣām anurañjanena janayann ānandam indīvara-*  
*śreṇī-śyāmala-komalair upanayann aṅgair anaṅgotsavam \|*  
*svacchandaṁ vraja-sundarībhir abhitaḥ praty-aṅgam āliṅgitaḥ*  
*śṛṅgāraḥ sakhi mūrtimān iva madhau mugdho hariḥ krīḍati \|\|48\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

visvesam - Of all beings (or of all the gopis); anuranjanena - by the conciliation; janayan - producing; anandam - bliss; indivara-sreni - like a row of blue lotuses; syamala-komalaih - tender and dark; upanayann - establishing; angair - with limbs; anangotsavam - the festival of Cupid; svacchandam - freely (or spontaneously); vraja-sundaribhih - by the beautiful women of Vraja (gopis); abhitah - near; pratyangam - in all of his limbs (or all parts); alingitah -embraced (or take shelter); srngarah - amorous love; sakhi - О sakhi!; murtiman - in bodily form; iva - as if; madhau - in spring; mugdhah - innocent (or attractive); harih - Krsna; kridati - plays.

**Перевод:**

**Innocent Kṛṣṇa plays in the spring as if he were the embodiment of love while producing bliss in all beings by satisfying them, establishing a festival of Cupid by all his dark, tender limbs, while the women of Vraja freely embrace all his limbs.**

**Комментарий:**

Since Kṛṣṇa knows all arts, why did he play with *gopīs* who were ignorant of proper etiquette in love? This verse answers. O my friend Rādhā! Innocent Kṛṣṇa plays in the spring as if he were the personification of love. What does he do? He produces bliss by gratifying all beings, or all the *gopīs* (*viśveṣām*). What else does he do? He establishes a festival of Cupid by his tender limbs dark like a host of blue lotuses. His limbs include their portions. His limbs are so soft that they cannot tolerate touch. An example was already given. This phrase indicates his beauty.

*aṅga-pratyāngakānāṁ yaḥ sanniveśo yathocitam \|*  
*susliṣṭa-sandhi-bandhaḥ syāt tat saundaryam itīryate \|\|*

**Beauty is defined as proper placement of all limbs and their parts, nicely joined together. Rasārṇava-sudhākaṛa 1.286**

Kṛṣṇa is nicely embraced by the beautiful women of Vraja on all his limbs externally (using their limbs) and internally (by consciousness) as if he were the personification of *śṛṅgāra-rasa.* He is compared to *śṛṅgāra-rasa* because he is blackish (*śyāma*). *Śyāmo bhavati śṛṅgāraḥ sito hāsaḥ prakīrtitaḥ; śyāma* is the color of *śṛṅgāra-rasa* and white is the color of *hāsya-rasa.* (Nāṭya-śāstra 6.42)

Attractive (*mugdhaḥ) śṛṅgāra-rasa* performs pastimes everywhere in the spring (*madhau*). It produces bliss in all beings by generating mutual attraction between young lovers (*anurañjanena*), bringing about a festival of Cupid with its parts like *sañcāri-bhāvas.* Not only by these causes however does *śṛṅgāra-rasa* take place. There are forests of *tamāla* trees, dark like blue lotuses, and soft beds. The tender women of Vraja take shelter of *śṛṅgāra-rasa* with all its aṅgas spontaneously. Among the *aṅgas* of *śṛṅgāra-rasa*, *tāmbūla,* garlands, and sandalwood act as *uddīpanas*. The *anubhāvas* are smiling and glances. The *sañcari-bhavas* are anxiety and envy. The *sāttvika-bhāvas* are standing of hairs on end and perspiration. The components or *aṅgas* are described by Bharata.

*vibhāvair anubhāvaiś ca bhāvaḥ sañcāri-sāttvikaiḥ \|*  
*janito bodhitaḥ sphīto vṛddhaḥ śṛṅgāra iṣyate \|\|*

***Śṛṅgāra-rasa*** **is generated, stimulated, intensified and increased by** ***vibhāvas*****,** ***anubhāvas*****,** ***sañcāri-bhavas*** **and** ***sāttvika-bhāvas*****.**

The general characteristics of *vibhāva* are described in Rasa-ratna-dīpika:

*bhāvayanti ye rasān vai manoharān \|*  
*te vibhāvās tu kathyante nāṭya-śāstra-viśāradaiḥ \|\|*

**Those knowing dramatics say that** ***vibhāvas*** **are those persons who produce attractive** ***rasas*****. Rasa-ratna-dīpika 3.1**

The *vibhāvas* of *śṛṅgāra-rasa* are described by Bharata:

*ṛtu-mālyālaṅkāraiḥ priya -jana-gāndharva-kāvya-sevābhiḥ \|*  
*upavana-gamana-vihāraiḥ śṛṅgāra-rasaḥ samudbhavati \|\|*

***Śṛṅgāra-rasa*** **arises from seasons, garlands, ornaments, the lover, music, poetry, service, going to a garden and playing. Nāṭya-śāstra 6.47**

The general characteristics of anubhāva are described:

*kriyante vai rasāḥ samyak lokānubhava-gocarāḥ \|*  
*te’nubhāvās tu kathyante nāṭya-śāstra-viśāradaiḥ \|\|*

***Anubhāvas*** **are described by the knowers of dramatics as emotions which can be perceived by others. Rasa-ratna-dīpika 3.10**

The *anubhāvas* of *śṛṅgāra-rasa* are as follows:

*nayana-vadana-prasādaiḥ smita-madhura-vaco-dhṛti-pramodaiś ca \|*  
*madhuraiś cāṅga-vihārais tasyābhinayaḥ prayoktavyaḥ \|\|*

**The general indications are joy in the eyes and face, smiling, sweet words, patience, joy, and beautiful movements of the limbs. Nāṭya-śāstra 6.48**

The general characteristics of vyabhicāri-bhāvas are described in Dāśa-rūpaka:

*viśeṣād abhimukhyena caranto vyabhicāriṇaḥ \|*  
*sthāyiny unmagna-nirmagnāḥ kallolā iva vāridhau \|\|*

**The** ***vyabhicāri-bhāvas*** **move about, are directly visibly, with particular emotion, and appear and disappear in the waves of the ocean of the** ***sthāyi-bhāva*****. Dāśa-rūpaka 4.7**

The *vyabhicāri-bhāvas* of *śṛṅgāra-rasa* are described in Śṛṅgāra-tilaka:

*śaṅkāsūyā bhayaṁ glānir vyādhiś cintā smṛtir dhṛtiḥ \|*  
*autsukyaṁ vismayo harṣo vrīḍonmādo madas tathā \|\|*  
*viṣādo jaḍatā nidrāvahitthaṁ cāpalaṁ smṛtiḥ \|*  
*iti bhāvāḥ prayoktavyāḥ śṛṅgāre vyabhicāriṇaḥ \|\|*

**The** ***vyabhicāri-bhāvas*** **of** ***śṛṅgāra-rasa*** **are generally described as anxitey, envy, fear, fatigue, illness, contemplation, remembrance, patience, zeal, disappointment, joy, shame, madness, intoxication, depression, being stunned, sleep, concealment, ficklness and death- like appearance. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 1.28-29**

The *sāttvika-bhāvas* are described in Śṛṅgāra-tilaka:

*stambhaḥ svedo’tha romāñcaḥ svara-bhaṅgo’tha vepathuḥ \|*  
*vaivarṇyam aśru pralaya ity aṣṭau sāttvikāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\|*

**The eight** ***sāttvika-bhāvas*** **are being stunned, perspiration, hairs standing on end, breaking of the voice, shivering change of color, tears and fainting. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 1.15**

Detailed characteristics are not mentioned out of fear of enlarging the work. Though Kṛṣṇa knows all arts, he plays with the women of Vraja (though they are not so skilful) according to their qualities, since he attracts them. Thus he maintains his quality of being *dakṣa* in spite of their lack of skill. It was said that Kṛṣṇa is able to attract all living entities. By mentioned ordinary *prema* in the other *gopīs*, the outstanding nature of Rādhā’s prema is indicated.

Another meaning is as follows. O friend! Producing bliss for all us *sakhīs* by developing his love for you *(anurañjanena),* he plays. You should not hate him though he enjoys with other women, since he embraces them while remaining independent of them. But he has most attraction for embracing you. The rest is the same.

*Śṛṅgāra* refers to the *rasa* of that name according to *Viśva. Viśvam* means everything. *Indīvara* means blue according to *Amara-koṣa. Śyāmala* means black. *Mūrti* means a body.

### Text 49

*rāsollāsa-bhareṇa vibhrama-bhṛtām ābhīra-vāma-bhruvām*  
*abhyarṇaṁ parirabhya nirbharam uraḥ premāndhayā rādhayā \|*  
*sādhu tvad-vadanaṁ sudhā-mayam iti vyāhṛtya gīta-stuti-*  
*vyājād udbhaṭa-cumbitaḥ smita-manohāri hariḥ pātu vaḥ \|\|49\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**May Kṛṣṇa, who steals the minds of the gopīs with his smile, and is tightly embraced on his chest by Rādhā who is blinded by intense joy and then visibly kisses him in the presence of other gopīs, who are blinded by their joy in the rāsa dance, protect you. She praises his face as nectar on the pretext of praising a song.**

**Комментарий:**

At the end of the chapter the author gives a blessing. May Kṛṣṇa protect you. How is he described? He attracts the women with his soft smile. He is clearly kissed by Rādhā, after she embraces him on the chest deeply in front of other *gopīs*. How are the other *gopīs* described? They cannot see properly and are confused (*vibhrama-bhṛtām)* because of great joy in the *rāsa* dance. Let them be without sight. But why is he kissed by Rādhā when she can see the other young women in front of her? She is blinded by extreme joy (*prema*). She sees nothing except Kṛṣṇa. Embracing him deeply, she kisses him without seeing a second person. On the pretext of praising his song, she indicates, “Your face is filled with nectar. It is beautiful *(sādhu*)!” She shows skill because she simply says “The song is beautiful, filled with nectar.” Then she kisses him.

## Part 2: Kṛṣṇa who destroys the gopīs’ suffering

**Akleśa-keśavaḥ**

### Text 1

*viharati vane rādhā sādhāraṇa-praṇaye harau*  
*vigalita-nijotkarṣād īrṣyā-vaśena gatā'nyataḥ \|*  
*kvacid api latā-kuñje guñjan-madhu-vrata-maṇḍalī*  
*mukhara-śikhare līna dīnāpy uvāca rahaḥ sakhīm \|\|1\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā, having out of anger left that place because her superior position had been thwarted while Kṛṣṇa played in the forest with equal love for other gopīs, privately spoke to a sakhī. Suffering, she remained hidden in a bower, filled with the loud humming of bees.**
