**Комментарий:**

In Part One, Rādhā’s state of thinking of Kṛṣṇa with desire was described. Now the state of remembering him in previous pastimes is described. The characteristics of this condition will be described in GG 7.2 and 8.1. Instigated by a *sakhī’s* words, Rādhā, unable to tolerate Kṛṣṇa being with other *gopīs* in her presence, speaks to the sakhī after entering a bower. This verse shows this.

Rādhā privately spoke to her *sakhī* who was present. How is she described? Swayed by anger, unable to tolerate the situation, she had gone elsewhere. The cause of her anger is given. Her superior position had been lost since she had thought, “I alone am very dear to him.” She had lost her superiority over the others. At this same time Kṛṣṇa was playing in the forest (*harau vane viharati*). How is he described? He had equal *prema* for the other *gopīs* (*sādhāraṇa-praṇaye*). The phrase can also be taken as *asādhāraṇa-praṇaye:* he had incomparable love for them. Therefore she was miserable (*dīnā*). How else is she described? She remained hidden in the thick bower of creepers, which was at the height of commotion because of a swarm of bees humming. The word *latā* is used to indicate that the bower was very dense with foliage. *Praṇaya* means *prema* according to *Viśva. Irṣya* means anger out of pride. Extra emotion is indicated by the sound of the bees. Falling under control of anger indicates the appearance of a *vyabhicāri-bhāva*.

## Song 5: Meditation on Krishna by Radha

**Prabandhaḥ 5**  
**Gurjari-rāga, yati-tāla**

### Text 2

*sañcarad-adhara-sudhā-madhura-*  
*dhvani-mukharita-mohana-vaṁśam \|*  
*calita-dṛg-añcala-cañcala-mauli-*  
*kapola-vilola-vataṁsam \|\|2\|\|*

*rāse harim iha vihita-vilāsaṁ*  
*smarati mano mama kṛta-parihāsam \|\| \[dhru\]*

**Пословный перевод:**

sancarad-adhara - Pressed to \[his\] lips; sudha-madhura - as sweet as nectar; dhvani - with \[its\] tone; mukharita - resonant; mohana -bewilders \[others\] (or the flute named Mohana); vamsam - whose flute; calita - are darting; drk-ancala - \[and\] the corners of whose eyes; cancala - is swaying; mauli - whose crown; kapola - on \[his\] cheeks; vilola - are quivering; vatamsam - \[and\] whose earrings; rase - In the rasa dance; harim - Krsna ; iha - in this forest (or in this bower); vihita-vilasam - by whom pastimes were performed \[with me\]; smarati - remembers; mano - mind; mama - my; krta-parihasam - \[and\] by whom jokes were made.

**Перевод:**

**My mind dwells on Kṛṣṇa who performed pastimes with me and joked me during the rāsa dance in the forest, who plays a flute which bewilders everyone with its sweet sounds pressed to his sweet lips, who has earrings quivering on his cheeks, a swaying crown and darting glances.**

**Комментарий:**

**Commentary:** The song is in *gurjari-rāga* and *pratimaṇṭhaka-tāla.****\[\[15\]****\]* The characteristics of the *rāga* were previously discussed. The *tāla* is described as follows:

*tāraś cāpy aparaś caiva vicāraḥ kandu-saṁjñakaḥ \|*  
*catvāraḥ kathitā hy ete pratimaṇṭhā vicakṣaṇaiḥ \|\|*

*virāmānta-druta-dvandvaṁ guruś caikas tataḥ param \|*  
*suraṅga-tālaḥ gātavyas tālas tu pratimaṇṭhakaḥ \|\|*

*gurur eko bhaved yatra sannipātaḥ sa kathyate \|*  
*apara-pratimaṇṭo’sau vidvadbhis tena gīyate \|\|*

*laghu-dvayaṁ virāmāntaṁ tāle kanduka-saṁjñake \|*  
*vicāro gīyate tena vilambaś cāllaśo bhavet \|\|*

*drutam ekaṁ bhaved yatra sa tālo khaṇḍa-saṁjñitaḥ \|*  
*druta-trayeṇa gātavyaḥ kundaś ca pratimaṇṭhakaḥ \|\|*

**The wise say there are four types of** ***pratimaṇṭhaka-tāla*****.**

***Suraṅga*****, to be sung, consists of 2 + 1+ 2 + 1 + 8 = 14.** ***Sannipāta*** **consists of 8.** ***Kanduka*****, consist of 4 + 4 + 2 = 10 is sung slowly.** ***Khaṇḍa*** **consists of 2. The beats are played in three speeds.**

There are four types of *pratimaṇṭha: suraṅga*, *sannipāta, kanduka* and *khaṇḍa.* Rādhā performs wonderful pastimes *(suraṅga*). The *tāla* is therefore called *suraṅga* or *pratimaṇṭha.* The others will be understood in later songs.

O friend! My mind remembers Kṛṣṇa who steals my mind (*harim*). She remembers what happened previously. He performed pastimes with me while performing the rāsa dance in the forest (*iha*) and he joked. Or as I hide in the bower, my mind remembers the stealer of my heart who made me engage in various types of *vibhrama (vihita-vilāsam)* so that he could joke with other women. Or, my mind remembers the stealer of my heart who made me insane with attraction (*vihita-vilāsam*). It is said concerning vibhrama:

*adhīnasyāpi sevāyāṁ kāntasyānabhinandanam \|*  
*vibhramo vāmatodrekāt syād ity ākhyāti kaścana \|\|*

**Some say that vibhrama takes place when, out of apparent contrariness,the heroine becomes displeased with herlovereven though he is submissive and eager to serve her. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 11.42**

Here is an example:

*tvaṁ govinda mayāsi kiṁ nu kavarī-bandhārtham abhyarthitaḥ*  
*kleśenālam abaddha eva cikura-stomo mudaṁ dogdhi me \|*  
*vaktrasyāpi na mājjanaṁ kuru ghanaṁ gharmāsu me rocate*  
*naivottaṁsaya mālatīr mama śiraḥ khedaṁ bhareṇāpsyati \|\|*

**O Govinda! Did I ask you to braid my hair? It is too much trouble. The unbound hair gives my joy. Do not wipe the perspiration from my face. It pleases me. Do not garland my head with these jasmine flowers. They give me pain by their weight. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 11.43**

According to *Amara-koṣa* the word *vibhrama* means “affected disregard for the lover in śṛṅgāra-rasa.” “Thinking of Kṛṣṇa” suggests that she desired the eight types of enjoyment, since remembering these eight types is a practice.

*smaraṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ keliḥ prekṣaṇaṁ guhya-bhāṣaṇam \|*  
*saṅkalpo’dhyavasāyaś ca kriyā-nivṛtstir eva ca \|\|*

*etan maithunam aṣṭāṅgaṁ pravadanti manīṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*viparītaṁ brahmacaryam etad evāṣṭa-lakṣaṇam \|\|*

**The wise explain that the eight limbs of connection between a man and a woman are remembrance, glorification, thinking of amorous play, gazing with desire to see, private conversations, thinking how to meet, mental effort, attaining happiness in union. Doing the opposite becomes the eight qualities of brahmacarya. Vairāgya-mārtaṇḍa 12.144-5**

Some commentators take the words *iha rāse* to mean “here at the rāsa dance” but this is not proper since it becomes meaningless. It is impossible the events could be taking place at that time. Bhaṭṭa says *idamaḥ prayakṣa-gatam*: the word *idam* refers to events which have passed. The rāsa dance was not visible, since it took place previously and Rādhā hid herself in the bower. The *rāsa* dance is not possible without her. This is the chorus.

The heroine is an *uttama-madhyamā.* Her qualities are as follows:

*doṣānurūpa-kopā yānunītā ca prasīdati \|*  
*rajyate ca bhṛśaṁ nāthe guṇa-hāryottameti sā \|\|*

**The uttamā woman is bountifully endowed with charming qualities, and though angered at faults, is pacified and is pleased.**

*kānte kiṁ kupitāsi ? kaḥ para-jane prāṇeśa kopo bhavet ?*  
*ko’yaṁ subhru paras tvam eva dayite ? dāso’smi kas te paraḥ ? \|*  
*ity uktvā praṇataḥ priyaḥ kṣiti-talād utthāpya sānandayā*  
*netrāmbhaḥ-kaṇikāṅkite stana-taṭe tanvyā samāropitaḥ \|\|*

**O beloved! Why are you angry? O lord of my life! How can one become angry at another person? O beloved! Who else can there be except you? I am your servant. Who else is there for you? Saying this while offering respects, the man happily raises her from the earth, and puts her on his chest stained with her tears. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 89**

Here is an example of the uttamā:

*kartuṁ śarma kṣaṇikam api me sādhyam ujjhaty aśeṣaṁ*  
*cittotsaṅge na bhajati mayā datta-khedāpy asūyām \|*  
*śrutvā cāntarvidalati mṛṣāpy ārti-vārtā-lavaṁ me*  
*rādhā mūrdhany akhila-sudṛśāṁ rājate sad-guṇena \|\|*

**Rādhā will renounce all her attainments if that will make me happy for a moment. Even if I torment her, she never becomes angry with me deep in her heart. If she hears a rumor, even if it is untrue, that I am slightly suffering, her heart cracks. With these good qualities, Rādhā reigns as the best of all the beautiful-eyed** ***gopīs*****. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 5.98**

The madhyā is described as follows*:*

*samāna-lajjā-madanā prodyat-tāruṇya-śālinī \|*  
*kiñcit-pragalbha-vacanā mohānta-surata-kṣamā \|*  
*madhyā syāt komalā kvāpi māne kutrāpi karkaśā \|\|*

**The madhyā woman has equal shyness and desire, is full youth, has somewhat bold speech, has the possibility of love affairs after fainting, is mild in anger and is sometimes rough. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 5.27**

The rest of the verse is as follows. How is Kṛṣṇa described? He has a flute which bewilders others, which is played with a sweet sound by his lips and produces various tones. He has a flute called Mohana which vibrates with sweet sounds by the nectar of his lips pressed to it. *Sañcarad-adhara-sudhā-madhura-dhvani* acts as an adverbial phrase in the neuter singular.

*kā stry aṅga te kala-padāyata-veṇu-gīta-*  
*sammohitārya-caritān na calet tri-lokyām*

**Dear Kṛṣṇa, what woman in all the three worlds wouldn’t deviate from religious behavior when bewildered by the sweet, drawn-out melody of your flute? SB 10.29.40**

An example is as follows:

*satyaṁ śṛṇomi sakhi nitya-nava-priyo’sau*  
*gopas tathāpi hṛdayaṁ madano dunoti \|*  
*yuktyā kathañcana samaṁ gamite’pi tasmin*  
*māṁ tasya kāla-muralī kavalīkaroti \|\|*

**Yes, my friend, I have heard that Kṛṣṇa is a debauchee always searching for new lovers. Still, my heart trembles with love for him. I pacified my heart with many logical arguments and was able to chase the love from it, but then, in the next moment I found myself devoured by the sweet sound of Kṛṣṇa's flute. Padyāvalī 234**

Thus the meaning is “My mind, bewildered by the flute, remembers him even though he offended me.” How else is he described? He has ear ornaments quivering on his cheeks, a swaying crown and eyes whose edges glance to respect me. Movement of his head is not a fault. By moving his earrings and crown he produces variety in his music. The phrase *ādhara-sudha* (nectar or bliss of his lips) suggests that his lips produce more bliss than the moon which normally produces bliss. *Vataṁsa* stands for *avataṁsa* and means earrings. According to Bhāguri, the *a* in *avataṁsa* can be dropped.

### Text 3

*candraka-cāru-mayūra-śikhaṇḍaka-maṇḍala-valayita-keśam \|*  
*pracura-purandara-dhanur-anurañjita-medura-mudira-suveśam \|\|3\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

candraka-caru - With the colorful eyes \[of the peacock feather\]; mayura - of peacocks; sikhandaka - of the feathers; mandala - by a circle; valayita - is surrounded; kesam - whose hair; pracura -many; purandara-dhanuh - with Indra’s bow (rainbow); anuranjita - decorated; medura-mudira - like a dense cloud; suvesam - whose form is beautiful.

**Перевод:**

**His hair is decorated with many peacock feathers having colorful eyes on their tips. He has a beautiful form, brilliant as a cloud decorated with many rainbows.**

**Комментарий:**

“He cannot be forgotten by his absence.” This is conveyed by this verse. His hair is surrounded by a circle of peacock feathers with colorful eyes on the tips. This statement suggests that on seeing peacocks at that place she remembered him. And he has a form brilliant like dense clouds colored by many rainbows. Kṛṣṇa is compared to a cloud and his various jewel necklaces, crown and bracelets are compared to many rainbows. This is an example of *adbhūtopamā,* since one does not see many rainbows. *Candraka* means the eye of the peacock feather according to *Amara-koṣa. Śikhaṇḍa* is the peacock feather. *Valayitam* means surrounded. *Mudira* means a cloud. *Veśa* means a house (body) or costume.

### Text 4

*gopa-kadamba-nitambavatī-mukha-cumbana-lambhita-lobham \|*  
*bandhujīva-madhurādhara-pallavam ullasita-smita-śobham \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

gopa-kadamba - Troop of cowherd women; nitambavati - of the excellent-hipped; mukha - mouth (lips); cumbana - for kissing; lambhita-lobham - who has desire; bandhujiva - like bandhujiva flower; madhura - is attractive; adhara - of a lower lip; pallavam - whose bud; ullasita-smita - because of his blossoming smile; sobham - beautiful.

**Перевод:**

**He is greedy to kiss the lips of the cowherd women. His tender lips are attractive like the bandhujīva flower. He has a blossoming smile.**

**Комментарий:**

**Commentary:** He is greedy to kiss all the cowherd women having excellent hips. Or he has desire to kiss me, among all the cowherd women. This means that his love for Rādhā is natural and his actions related to the other gopīs is forced by them. He has bud-like lips attractive like the red *bandhujīva* flower. Or, he has bud-like lips attractive because they are the source of nectar *(jīva*) for his devotees (*bandhu),* giving them life. The word *madhura* indicates juiciness and *pallava* indicates tenderness and youth. He is beautiful with a blossoming smile. The lips are compared to a blossoming of bandhūka flower within a blue lotus. The smile is like a jasmine blossoming. Seeing the *bandhūka* flower, she remembers his smile. The *bandhūjīva* flower is red according to *Amara-koṣa. Madhurya* means juicy or attractive.

### Text 5

*vipula-pulaka-bhuja-pallava-valayita-ballava-yuvati-sahasram \|*  
*kara-caraṇorasi maṇi-gaṇa-bhūṣaṇa-kiraṇa-vibhinna-tamisram \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vipula-pulaka - Hairs standing on end all over; bhuja - which are his arms; pallava - by the shoots; valayita - are surrounded; ballava-yuvati-sahasram - by whom thousands of young cowherdwomen; kara-caranorasi - on his hands, feet and chest; mani-gana - consisting in the jewels; bhusana - from the ornaments; kirana -with the rays; vibhinna - is destroyed; tamisram - \[and\] by whom the darkness.

**Перевод:**

**He surrounds thousands of young women with his two arms soft like shoots, whose hairs are standing on end. The rays from the jewels of his hands, feet and chest destroy the darkness.**

**Комментарий:**

He surrounds thousands of young women with his two arms soft like shoots, with hairs standing on end all over. The word *valayita* is used to suggest that his arms surround them carelessly, not fervently. He embraces intimately Rādhā only. And the rays from the jewels of his hands, feet and chest destroy the darkness. *Tamisram* means darkness according to *Amara-koṣa.* Or he spends time with the cowherd women having goose bumps, who were surrounded by his tender arms. What young women would not remember this? And he defeated my competitors (*vibhinna)* by the rays of jeweled ornaments given by him which decorated my hands, feet and chest. The competing women were defeated on seeing me decorated with his ornaments. Instead of *kiraṇa* sometimes *usra* is seen. *Usra* means illuminating.

### Text 6

*jalada-paṭala-valad-indu-vinindaka-candana-tilaka-lalāṭam \|*  
*pīna-payodhara-parisara-mardana-nirdaya-hṛdaya-kapāṭam \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

jalada-patala - In a mass of clouds; valat - enclosed; indu-vinindaka - which surpasses the moon; candana-tilaka - is a sandalwood tilaka', lalatam - on whose forehead; pina-payodhara - full breasts \[of the gopis\]; parisara-the adjoining; mardana - crushes; nirdaya-hrdaya - of whose cruel chest; kapatam - \[and\] the door bolt.

**Перевод:**

**On his forehead is sandalwood tilaka which defeats the moon in a mass of clouds. The cruel door bolt of his chest crushes the full breasts of the gopīs.**

**Комментарий:**

On his forehead is a sandalwood mark which defeats the moon attached to a mass of rain clouds. Another version has *jalada-paṭala-calad-indu-vinindaka-candana-bindu-lalāṭam*: on his forehead is a circular mark of sandalwood which defeats the moon passing over a mass of clouds.

### Text 7

*maṇi-maya-makara-manohara-kuṇḍala-maṇḍita-gaṇḍam udāram \|*  
*pīta-vasanam anugata-muni-manuja-surāsura-vara-parivāram \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

mani-maya - Made of jewels; makara - in the shape of makara; manohara-kundala - with attractive earrings; mandita - are decorated; gandam - whose cheeks; udaram - beautiful; pita-vasanam - whose clothing is yellow; anugata - who serve him; muni-manuja-surasura - of sages, men, devatas and demons; vara - consists in the best; parivaram - \[and\] whose retinue.

**Перевод:**

**His beautiful cheeks are decorated with attractive makara earrings made of jewels. He wears yellow cloth and the best of loyal sages, men, demons and devatās have become his followers.**

**Комментарий:**

His cheeks are decorated with attractive makara earrings made of jewels. It is said *bhūṣana-bhūsanaṅgam:* his form enhances the beauty of ornaments. (SB 3.2.12) *Vibhūṣaṇaṁ vibhūṣyaṁ syād yena tad rūpam ucyate:* excellent form is said to be that by which ornaments become worthy of being ornaments. (BRS 2.1.238) *Rūpam* means beauty:

*muktāphaleṣu chāyāyās taralatvam ivāntarā \|*  
*pratibhāti yad aṅgeṣu lāvaṇyaṁ tad ihocyate \|\|*

**When waves of beauty emanating from within the limbs cause reflection, as in pearls, it is called lāvaṇya. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 10.28**

An example is as follows:

*śṛṇu sakhi tava karṇe varṇayāmy atra nīcair*  
*viracaya mukha-candraṁ mā vṛthārād vivarṇam \|*  
*iyam urasi murārer asti nānyā mṛgākṣī*  
*marakata-mukurābhe bimbitāsi tvam eva \|\|30\|\|*

**My friend, listen. I shall now whisper something in your ear while I am close to you. Please do not make the moon of your face so pale. There is no other woman having your beauty. You are reflected on Kṛṣṇa’s chest, a sapphire mirror. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 10.30**

Those cheeks are beautiful (*udāram*). Why would I not remember a person with such beautiful cheeks? Or *udāram* can mean generous. *Audāryaṁ vinayaṁ prāhuḥ sarvāvasthāgataṁ budhāḥ*: *audāryam* means having a kind nature in all circumstances. (Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 11.23)

Or, I remember he who decorated my cheeks with attractive makara earrings made of jewels. He was so generous to do so. He fulfilled all my desires privately at that moment. Why should I not remember him?

He wears yellow clothing and the best of loyal sages, men, demons and devatās have become his followers. Or he is outstanding among all the best of surrendered sages, men, demons and devatās. *Viśva* says that *parivāra* means excellent. The word *vara* applies to all the types of people in the compound. He is continually served even by these people who have achieved their goals through him. Thus his kind nature (*udāram*) is announced. The best of sages are Nārada, Śukadeva and others. The best of men are persons like Parīkṣit. The best of devatās are Brahmā, Śiva and others. The best of demons are persons like Prahlāda.

Or he has the best followers in the form of the gopīs and gopas who are superior to surrendered sages, men, devatās and demons. Or he has the best obedient (*anugata*) attendants among sages, humans, devatās and demons since they are attracted by his great sweetness. *Divi bhuvi ca rasāyāṁ kāḥ striyas tad-durāpāḥ:* in heaven, on earth or in the subterranean sphere, what women are unavailable to him? (SB 10.47.15) *Ārundhānam arundhatī-hṛdayam apy ārdra-smitārdra-śriyā:* his beauty captures the heart of even Arundhatī by the tender beauty of its tender smile. (Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta 87) Or he causes the retinue of sages, humans, devatās and demons to surrender. The hero’s generosity is described as follows:

*dānam abhyupapattiś ca tathā ca priya-bhāṣaṇam \|*  
*svajane ca pare vāpi tad audāryaṁ prakīrtitam \|\|*

***Audarya*** **or generosity refers to giving gifts, giving protection or saying kind words to known or unknown persons. Nāṭya-śāstra 22.40**

### Text 8

*viśada-kadamba-tale militaṁ kali-kaluṣa-bhayaṁ śamayantam \|*  
*mām api kim api tarala-taraṅgad-anaṅga-dṛśā manasā ramayantam \|\|8\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

visada-kadamba - Of a spotless kadamba tree; tale - at the base; militam-kali - caused by \[our\] quarrels; kalusa-bhayam - \[my\] fear and confusion; samayantam - pacifying; mam - me; api - even; kim api - somehow; tarala-tarangad - is like trembling waves; ananga - in which the desire; drsa - in my eyes; manasa - \[and\] in \[my\] mind; ramayantam - and pleasing

**Перевод:**

**He was sitting at the base of a kadamba tree, and pacified my fears and confusion caused by our quarrels. Indescribably, he produces pleasure in my mind and in my eyes, which are possessed of ever-increasing desire like trembling waves.**

**Комментарий:**

I remember him sitting at the base of a spotless *kadamba* tree. Having come previously, expecting me to the base of the kadamba tree designated by me, he remained hidden there to joke with me. He thought, “No one should see me.” He pacified me of my fears and disturbances caused by quarreling. The disturbance from quarrelling is as follows. “I came according to the hints. He has not come. As to be expected, he must be obligated to another woman. If he comes I will not speak to him.” Fear is as follows. “How can I remain here all alone?” Or, being fearful of my anger and quarrels, coming from the base of the kadamba, he pacifies me with clever words.

Indescribably (*kim api*), he produces pleasure in my mind and eyes, which have desire increasing like trembling waves. Or, he causes pleasure in me by my mind which sees (*dṛśā*) sweet love arising in waves within the universe before me. I have become to object of his supreme affection. My mind remembers Kṛṣṇa who has the above qualities. *Kali* means quarrel according to *Viśva. Kaluṣa* means confusion.

### Text 9

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇitam atisundara-mohana-madhuripu-rūpam \|*  
*hari-caraṇa-smaraṇaṁ prati samprati puṇyavatām anurūpam \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva-bhanitam - Spoken by Sri Jayadeva; atisundara-mohana -which is very beautiful \[and\] bewilders \[the gopis\]; madhuripu-rupam -\[for\] the form of Krsna; hari-carana - of the feet of; smaranam - \[and for\] the remembrance; prati - for; samprati - now; punyavatam - among those who have gained merit; anurupam - is suitable for (it generates a taste for)

**Перевод:**

**This composition of Jayadeva gives to those who have gained qualification by acts of previous bhakti a taste for remembering Rādhā’s actions and is the embodiment of the form of Kṛṣṇa who bewitches the gopīs.**

**Комментарий:**

May this composition spoken by the poet Jayadeva is suitable to generate taste for similar *bhāva* in persons who have gained qualification by acts which generate remembrance of Kṛṣṇa’s feet (*puṇyavatām*) so that they can remember Rādhā’s actions in the *rāsa* dance and other pastimes described here (*hari-caraṇa-smaraṇam*). This composition contains descriptions of Kṛṣṇa’s form which bewilders the *gopīs (atisundara*). This is end of the fifth *prabandha*, called “The jeweled necklace.”

### Text 10

*gaṇayati guṇa-grāmaṁ bhrāmaṁ bhrāmād api nehate*  
*vahati ca paritoṣaṁ doṣaṁ vimuñcati dūrataḥ \|*  
*yuvatiṣu valat-tṛṣṇe kṛṣṇe viharati māṁ vinā*  
*punar api mano vāmaṁ kāmaṁ karoti karomi kim \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ganayati - \[My mind\] enumerates; guna-gramam - the aggregate of his qualities; bhamam - anger; bhramad - out of illusion; api - even; nehate - it does not desire; vahati - it bears; ca - and; paritosam -satisfaction; dosam - \[his\] faults; vimuncati - it pardons; duratah - from afar; yuvatisu - for the young women (gopis); valat-trsne - is one whose thirst is increasing; krsne - while Krsna; viharati - \[and\] plays; mam vina - without me; punar - again; api - even; mano - \[my\] mind; vamam - unfavorable; kamam-karoti - has desire; karomi kim - what can I do?

**Перевод:**

**Somehow my mind desires him even now, even though he has increasing thirst for many gopīs and increasingly inclined to play without me. My mind acts against me, because it enumerates all his qualities and out of illusion it does not desire to become angry. It pardons his faults, because it is satisfied.**

**Комментарий:**

“Kṛṣṇa plays with other *gopīs* without you. Why do you remember him?” This verse answers.

My mind has desire for Kṛṣṇa even now. What can I do? He has increasing thirst for many gopīs and increasingly inclined to play without me. My mind is unfavorable (*vāmam*). Another reason for the unfavorable mind is given. My mind enumerates all his qualities such as his beauty. Out of illusion it does not desire to become angry (*bhāmam*). It pardons his faults such as his disrespecting me completely, because it is satisfied. The ornaments of *vibhāvanā* and *viśesokti* are expressed, since attachment for Kṛṣṇa appears even without reason for the attachment in the form of surrender to him and since his fault of attachment to other women is not mentioned. In Kavya-prakaśa it is said:

*kriyāyāḥ pratiṣedhe’pi phala-vyaktir vibhāvanā \|*  
*viśeṣoktir akhaṇḍeṣu kāraṇeṣu phalāvacaḥ \|\|*

***Vibhāvanā*** **means the appearance of results even though negated by actions which oppose it.** ***Viśeṣokti*** **means not mentioning some effect, when the causes are present.**

*Grama* means a group according to Viśva. *Bhāma* means anger. *Vāmam* means enemy. *Kāmam* means desire.

## Song 6: Radha remembers being with Krishna

**Prabandha 6**  
**Mālava-gauḍa-rāgena ekatālī-tālena ca gīyate\|\[\[16\]**\]

### Text 11

*nibhṛta-nikuñja-gṛhaṁ gatayā*  
*niśi rahasi nilīya vasantam \|*  
*cakīta-vilokita-sakala-diśā*  
*rati-rabhasa-bhareṇa hasantam \|\|11\|\|*

*sakhi he! keśī-mathanam udāram*  
*ramaya mayā saha madana-manoratha-*  
*bhāvitayā sa-vikāram \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

nibhrta-nikunja - Which is a lonely bower; grham - to a dwelling; gataya - \[I\] having gone; nisi - in the night; rahasi - alone; niliya - having concealed; vasantam - \[he\] remaining; cakita - with fear; vilokita-sakala-disa - \[I\] by whom all the directions are seen; rati-rabhasa - of the agitation of love; bharena - with the burden; hasantam - \[he\] laughing.

sakhi he - О friend; kesi-mathanam - the crusher of Kesi (Krsna); udaram - kind; ramaya - make \[him\] enjoy; maya saha - with me; madana-manoratha - the desire for love; bhavitaya - having developed \[rati\]; sa-vikaram - with altered feelings.

**Перевод:**

**I have gone to this lonely bower while he has hidden himself alone in the night. I glance about with fear while he laughs while being agitated with love.**

**Комментарий:**

O friend! Make kind Kṛṣṇa, the crusher of Keśī, showing symptoms of love, enjoy with me, since I have developed *rati*, with a desire for love. (chorus)

Filled with longing and *rasa*, Rādhā explains her desires to her *sakhī*. The *rāga* is *mālava-gauḍa* and the *tāla* is *ekatālī.* The followers of Kumbha sing it in *bhairavi-rāga*. The characteristics of the *rāga* were described already. The *tāla* is as follows:

*eka-tālī tridhā proktā gīta-śāstra-viśāradaiḥ \|*  
*rāma-candrikā tadvad vipulety atha lakṣaṇam \|\|*

*drutam ekaṁ bhaved yatra tālo’yaṁ khaṇḍa-saṁjñitaḥ \|*  
*rāmānenaika-tālī tu kīrtyate gāyanottamaiḥ \|\|*

*guru-dvayaṁ bhaved yatra tālo lalita-saṁjñakaḥ \|*  
*candrikā caika-tālī syāt tena saubhāgya-dāyinī \|\|*

*kokila-priya-tāle vai drutaṁ trayam udāhṛtam \|*  
*vipulā caika-tālī syāt tena gītajña-sammatā \|\|*  
*khaṇḍa-lalita-kokila-priyāḥ iti bhedās tatra jñeyāḥ \|\|*

**Knowers of music say that there are three types of** ***ekatālī: rāma, candrikā and vipulā.*** **A** ***tāla*** **with one** ***druta*** **(2 beats) is called** ***khaṇḍa. Ekatalī*** **with this is called** ***rāma.*** **8 + 8 is called** ***lalita. Ekatālī*** **with this is called** ***candrikā.*** **2 + 2 +2 is called** ***kokilapriya-tāla. Ekatālī*** **with this is called** ***vipulā.*** **Thus the three types are** ***khaṇḍa, lalita*** **and** ***kokilapriya.***

In Orissa the *ekatālī* is described as follows:

*uddāma-manmatha-mahājvara-vepamānāṁ*  
*romāñca-kaṇṭakitam aṅgam udvahantīm \|*  
*saṁveda-vepathu-ghanotkalikā-kulāṅgīm*  
*utkaṇṭhitāṁ vadati tāṁ bharataḥ kavīndraḥ \|\|*

**Bharata, king of poets, describes** ***ekatālī*** **as full of longing with hairs standing on end, with limbs like buds with perceivable trembling because of a great fever of passion.**

The meaning of the song is as follows. O friend! Make Kṛṣṇa play with me, since I have developed rati endowed with desire for love. Or I have developed rati by the desire of Cupid. *Bhāvitayā* means “attained.” “Will he come when I ask him?” He is very kind (*udārama*). He will definitely grant your request. Or *madana* can refer to Kṛṣṇa, since Kṛṣṇa agitates Cupid. Make him play with me since I have developed the desire for enjoyment with Kṛṣṇa (*madana-manoratha*).

“How is his desire to be known?” He has changes in his mind (*sa-vikāram*). It was said that śṛṅgāra-rasa is nourished by previous attraction between the couple.

*anurāgo’nuraktāyāṁ rasāvaha iti sthitiḥ \|*  
*abhāve tv anurāgasya rasābhāsaṁ jagur budhāḥ \|\|*

**The wise say that love is pleasurable because of attractions. Without attraction there is only a shadow of the** ***rasa*** **of love.**

In this verse the heroine is *uttamā* with *mahā-bhāva*. The hero is also *uttama*. His qualities were previously listed. *Sa-vikāram* can also be an adverb: make him enjoy with me, since I, manifesting transformations, have developed desire for him. It is said:

*nābhī-mūla-kucodara-prakaṭana-vyājena yad yoṣitām \|*  
*sākāṅkṣaṁ muhur īkṣaṇaṁ skhalitatā nīvī-nibandhasya ca \|*  
*keśa-bhraṁśana-saṁyamau ca kamitur mitrādi-sandarśanaiḥ \|*  
*saubhāgyādi-guṇa-praśasti-kathanais tāsāṁ tu rāgekṣitam \|\| iti \|*

**Desire in women is manifested by revealing the navel, breasts, and belly, constant gazing, their belts becoming loose, untying and tying the hair. They glance with love which is revealed by speaking of the good qualities of the lover and seeing his friends. Rasika-sarvasva**

The words *keśi-mathana* (destroyer of Keśī) are used in order to show Kṛṣṇa’s abilities. He can arrange of pastimes with no cause of anxiety. Or, *keśi-mathana* can mean “he who disturbs the women and has beautiful hair.” Or it means “Kṛṣṇa with beautiful hair who agitates the hearts of the women by his beauty.” It is said:

*vīkṣyālakāvṛta-mukhaṁ tava kuṇdala-śrī*  
*gaṇḍa-sthalādhara-sudhaṁ hasitāvalokam*  
*dattābhayaṁ ca bhuja-daṇḍa-yugaṁ vilokya*  
*vakṣaḥ śriyaika-ramaṇaṁ ca bhavāma dāsyaḥ*

**Seeing your face encircled by curling locks of hair, your cheeks beautified by earrings, your lips full of nectar, and your smiling glance, and also seeing your two imposing arms, which take away our fear, and your chest, which is the only source of pleasure for the goddess of fortune, we must become your maidservants. SB 10 29.39**

Or Keśī can mean the controller (*īśa*) of love (*ka). Keśa* becomes *keśī* according to the rule *ajāty-arthe supi-dhātor ṇiniḥ syāt tācchīlye.* (Pāṇini3.2.78) *Mathana* is derived from the verb *math* according to the rule *matha viloḍane nandi-grahi-pacādibhyaḥ* (Pāṇini 3.1.134) Thus *keśi-mathana* means “he who controls love and disturbs my mind.” *Sākṣān manmatha-manmathaḥ*: he directly appeared as one who can bewilder the mind of Cupid, though Cupid himself bewilders the minds of ordinary people. (SB 10.32.2)

Or *keśī-manthana* can mean “he who agitates even those who control lust, the yogīs, by his beauty.” What then to speak of agitating a person who is already attracted to him. *Ka* can mean love according to *Anekārtha. Bhāvitayā* in the first explanation mean “developing rati” according to *itac tārakāidbhya.* (Pānini 5.2.36) In the second explanation, *bhavitayā* means “attained” according to *Viśva.*

The chorus has been explained. Now the rest of the verse is explained. Rādhā is described. I had gone to the bower house alone. Kṛṣṇa is described. He remains hidden in the night, alone. He has hidden himself from me to joke with me, thinking, “Rādhā should not see me” or “Others should not see me.”

Rādhā is further described. She looks in all directions in fear. “Where is Kṛṣṇa hiding?” or “No one should see me as I go to meet Kṛṣṇa.” Her vision is confused. It is said in Saṅgīta-ratnakāra:

*yā tu kvacid aviśrāntam aviṣahyaṁ vilokane \|*  
*vistīrṇa-cañcalotphulla-tārā sā dṛṣṭir ucyate \|\|*  
*vibhrāntā vibhrame vege sambhrame ca bhaved asau*

**Vision which is glances about tirelessly for something invisible, with enlarged, restless pupils is called** ***vibhrānta.*** **This means it is confused and agitated.**

Kṛṣṇa is further described. He laughs while filled with śṛṅgāra-rasa mixed with eagerness (*rabhasa*) for enjoyment of love (*rati*) or with desire (*rabhasa*) for love (*rati*). He is laughing, thinking “She does not see me, though I am right in front of her, hiding in order to reveal myself, just waiting for the proper time for play.” *Nibhṛtam* means “alone” according to *Dharaṇi. Rabhasa* means “eagerness” according to *Amara-koṣa.*

### Text 12

*prathama-samāgama-lajjitayā*  
*paṭu-cāṭu-śatair anukūlam \|*  
*mṛdu-madhura-smita-bhāṣitayā*  
*śithilī-kṛta-jaghana-dukūlam \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

prathama-samagama - At our first meeting; lajjitaya - \[I\] embarrassed; patu-catu - of skillful sweet words; sataih - with hundreds; anukulam - \[he\] bent on conciliation; mrdu-madhura-smita - with a soft and sweet smile; bhasitaya - \[I\] was addressed; sithili-krta - became loose; jaghana-dukulam - \[he by whom\] the garment at my \[my\] hips.

**Перевод:**

**I was embarrassed at our first meeting. He pacified me by hundreds of sweet words. He spoke to me with a soft sweet smile. My cloth became loose on my hips.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā is described. I was ashamed at our first meeting. Though he is the eternal enjoyer at the first meeting, she was embarrassed by the intense sweetness of rasa in Kṛṣṇa, which seemed ever fresh. *Bhāgavatam* says:

*yadyapy asau pārśva-gato raho-gatas*  
*tathāpi tasyāṅghri-yugaṁ navaṁ navam \|*  
*pade pade kā virameta tat-padāc*  
*calāpi yac chrīr na jahāti karhicit \|\|*

**Though Kṛṣṇa’s feet were next to them and in private, those feet appeared more and more beautiful at every moment. Which woman could give up those feet which Lakṣmī never gives up? SB 1.11.34**

Women in love are embarrassed at the first meeting and act reluctant. Bharata says:

*śliṣṭaḥ kaṇṭhe kim iti na mayā mūḍhayā prāṇanāthaś*  
*cumbaty asmin vadana-vidhutiḥ kiṁ kṛtā kiṁ na dṛṣṭaḥ \|*  
*noktaḥ kasmād iti nava-vadhū-ceṣṭitaṁ cintayantī*  
*paścāt-tāpaṁ vahati taruṇī premṇi jāte rasajñā \|\|*

**When innocent I did embrace neck of my lover. Why? When he kissed me, I shook my head. Why did I do this? Why did I not look at him? I did not speak to him. Thinking of the behavior of a new bride, the young woman, knowing** ***rasa*****, feels regret at the time love had arisen. Amaru- śataka 58**

Or the meaning can be “I feel embarrassed as with the first meeting.” Kṛṣṇa is described. He consoled me with hundreds of polite statements, since he is skilful at pleasing women. This indicates the hero’s skilful use of words since it is said that she immediately became pacified. The method of pleasing the woman is described:

*prathamam iha hi deyaṁ puṣpa-pūg-ādikaṁ syāt*  
*mṛdu-madhura-vacobhir yojayet sannidhāne \|*  
*karatalam atha gātre bhrāmayec chaśvad asyāś*  
*chūritam udara-pṛṣṭhe mandam āśliṣya dadyāt \|\|*

**First flowers should be given. One should meet her with soft, sweet words. One should let the hand slide over her limbs quickly. One should embrace her gently on the back. Śṛṅgāra-viveka**

He is skilful at removing faults:

*mugdhām anunayaty eṣa mṛdūpāyena sāntvayan \|*  
*nātibhīti-karair bhāvair nibandhair bāla-bhīṣakaiḥ \|\|*

**The lover consoles the innocent girl, using soft means, without strong hands, without obstructing emotions, without great force.**

*sarati saras-tīrād eṣā bhramad bhramarāvalī*  
*sumukhi vimukhī padme manye tavāsya pipāsayā \|*  
*iti nigadite kiñcid bhītyā vivartita-kandharā*  
*vadana-kamale bālā bhartrā ciraṁ paricumbitā \|\|*

**With brows like a host of bees, she moves from the bank of the lake of enjoyment. He says, “O woman with a beautiful face, O tarnished lotus! I think of you with thirst for your face.” With head turned away, she moves in fear. The young girl is kissed on her lotus face by her husband. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 1.37**

Rādhā is further described. I was addressed with a smile and soft, sweet words. This means her shame disappeared by his skilful words.

*śrutaṁ rasa-viśeṣeṇa hy abhilāṣaṁ nayec chrutim \|*  
*yan nāyikā-mukha-gataṁ mārdavaṁ tad udīritam \|*  
*mādhuryaṁ ca bhaved yatra śabde’rthe cāsphuṭo rasaḥ \|\|*

**The ear yearns for what is desired to be heard in a pleasing way. There arises sweetness when the man produces** ***rasa*** **in meaning and sound of words, which makes the woman’s face very soft.**

Kṛṣṇa is further described. He loosens the cloth on my hips, knowing that I have been pacified by his smile. The belt spontaneously will loosen on seeing Kṛṣṇa, the crest jewel of unlimited heroes. When that happens, the cloth will then fall from her hips. It is said:

*oṣṭhāgraṁ sphuratīkṣaṇe vicalataḥ kūpodare matsyavat*  
*dhammillaḥ kusumākulo vigalati prāpnoti bandhaṁ punaḥ \|*  
*pracchannau vrajataḥ stanau prakaṭatāṁ śroṇī-taṭaṁ dṛśyate*  
*nīvī ca skhalati sthitāpi sudṛḍhā kāmeṅgite yoṣitām \|\|*

**While she moves her eyes like a fish in a pool, the edge of her lips quivers. Her hair full of flowers comes undone. She again ties up her hair. When she moves, her covered breasts become visible. Her waist is visible. Her belt slackens though tied tightly, at an indication of love. Rati-rahasya**

*kānte talpam upāgate vigalitā nīvī svayaṁ bandhanād*  
*vāso viślatha-mekhalā-guṇa-dhṛtaṁ kiṁcin nitambe sthitam \|*  
*etāvat sakhi vedmi sāmpratam ahaṁ tasyāṅga-saṅge punaḥ*  
*ko'yaṁ kāsmi rataṁ nu vā katham iti svalpāpi me na smṛtiḥ \|\|*

**When the lover comes to the bed, my belt becomes untied. My cloth is held by a slackened cord around my waist. O friend! I understand by this that I am in his company. Who is he? Who am I? How did this happen? I do not remember anything at all. Amaru-śataka 101**

The state prior to rati is described:

*truṭaty aṅgaṁ sphurad-vakṣaḥ vikasan-mukha-paṅkajam \|*  
*nimīlan-nayanaṁ nīvi-nābhi-śaithilyam eva ca \|\|*

**She tears her limbs. Her chest trembles. Her lotus mouth opens. She closes her eyes and her belt becomes slackened.**

*Cātu* means words pleasing to women according to Ratna-mālā*. Jaghana* means the lower portion of the body below the waist according to *Hārāvalī.*

### Text 13

*kīsala-śayana-niveśitayā*  
*ciram urasi mamaiva śayānam \|*  
*kṛta-parirambhaṇa-cumbanayā*  
*parirabhya kṛtādhara-pānam \|\|13\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kisala-sayana - A bed of tender shoots; nivesitaya - \[I\] was placed upon; ciram - for a long time; urasi - on \[my\] chest; mamaiva - only my; sayanam - \[he\] having rested; krta - were made; parirambhana-cumbanaya - \[I\] to whom an embrace and a kiss; parirabhya -having embraced \[me\]; krta - was made; adhara-panam - \[he\] by whom my lower lip was drunk.

**Перевод:**

**I was placed on a bed of tender shoots. He rested on my chest for a long time. He embraced and kissed me. He embraced me, drinking the nectar of my lips.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā is again described. I was placed on a bed of tender buds. Kṛṣṇa is described. He lay for a long time on my chest. This indicates a special embrace. It is said:

*gātropaviṣṭād atha talpa-madhye*  
*saṁlīyate yan mithunaṁ śarīram \|*  
*kāmābhimānākṣata-pūrṇa-ceṣṭaṁ*  
*tat kṣīra-nīrākhyam idaṁ pradiṣṭam \|\|*

**When two bodies clinging together on a bed with all their limbs, with continuous effort while absorbed in passion, it is called an embrace (union of milk and water). Pañca-sāyaka**

Rādhā is again described. I was being embraced and kissed. This means a very tight embrace. The tight embrace is described in Rati-rahasya. Kissing is also described in Pañca-śayika.

Kṛṣṇa is described. After embracing me, he drank the nectar of my lips. This is called *lālāṭikam āliṅganam*. It is said:

*anyonya-saṁsakta-mukhaṁ kapolaṁ*  
*netraṁ lalāṭaṁ hṛdayaṁ ca bāhyam \|*  
*sānanda-bhāva-śrama-mīlitākṣaṁ*  
*lālāṭikaṁ tattva-vido vadanti \|\|*

**When the lover’s mouths, cheeks, eyes, foreheads, hearts and arms are in close contact, with eyes closed because of fatigue from blissful love, the wise call this** ***lālāṭika.***

*saṅketa-talpe ca sukhopaviṣṭāṁ*  
*gāḍhaṁ patiḥ śliṣyati sāpi kāntam \|*  
*anyonya-gātre viśatīva rāgād*  
*dvandvaṁ tadākṛṣṭa-jalābhimānam \|\|*

**The man deeply embraces the woman while seated comfortably on the designated bed. She also embraces him. It seems as if the two bodies have merged into each other out of passion. The two seem to be joined by attraction like water flowing into water. Pañca-sāyaka**

*Pallava* means buds according to Amara-koṣa.

### Text 14

*alasa-nimīlita-locanayā*  
*pulakāvali-lalita-kapolam \|*  
*śrama-jala-sakala-kalevarayā*  
*vara-madana-madād atilolam \|\|14\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

alasa - By the fatigue of love; nimilita - were closed; locanaya -\[I\] whose eyes; pulakavali - with rows of goose bumps; lalita -\[is\] attractive; kapolam - \[he\] whose cheeks; srama - arising from exertion; jala - with sweat; sakala - \[is\] beautiful; kalevaraya -\[l\] whose body; vara-madana-madad - because of the great joy produced by Cupid; atilolam -\[he\] unsteady.

**Перевод:**

**My eyes close from the fatigue of love. He develops goose bumps on his cheeks. My body becomes beautiful with perspiration arising from exertion. He is unsteady because of great joy produced by Cupid.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā is further described. My eyes were closed because of fatigue. This is the symptom of love called *aṅgika:*

*yatra mūrdhānam ārabhya pāda-paryantikāḥ kriyāḥ \|*  
*aṅgena vihitā yasmāt tasmād āṅgikam ucyate \|\|*

**When actions extend from the head to the foot it is called** ***aṅgika,*** **since all the limbs are used. Nāyikā-lakṣaṇa**

The eyes close from throbbing of the limbs due to pleasure in uniting.

*sphurat-saṁśliṣṭa-pakṣmāgrā sukha-viśrānta-tārakā \|*  
*mukulā dṛṣṭir ānandair ucchrayoḥ sparśa-gandhayoḥ \|\|*

**The eyes close, with pupils fatigued from happiness, and the tips of the eyelids quiver and press together because of bliss of increased stimulation of touch and smell. Saṅgīta-ratnākara**

Pañca-sāyaka also describes closing the eyes at this time. Kṛṣṇa is described. His cheeks are attractive with goose bumps. This is a *sāttvika-bhāva*. Bharata says:

*smaraṇe saṅgame caiva priyasyālokane tathā \|*  
*hetu-trayaṁ samāsādya sāttvikaḥ sampravartate \|\|*

**Sāttvika-bhāvas arise on remembering the lover, seeing the lover and uniting with the lover.**

Rādhā is further described. My body is beautified (*sakala*) by perspiration arising from fatigue after efforts in love. Another version has *śakala* (drops). Another version has *śrama-jala-sikta:* soaked in perspiration.

Kṛṣṇa is further described. He is unsteady because of great joy produced by Cupid. Or he is very unsteady because of intoxication from intense love. *Vara* means abundant or best according to *Viśva*.

This description with perspiration describes the *nirākula-pragalbhā* (steady and bold) heroine:

*dhanyās tāḥ sakhi yoṣitaḥ priyatame sarvāṅga-lagne’pi yāḥ*  
*prāgalbhyaṁ prathayanti mohana-vidhāv ālambya dhairyaṁ mahat \|*  
*asmākaṁ tu tadīya-pāṇi-kamale’py unmocayaty aṁśukaṁ*  
*ko’yaṁ kā vayam atra kiṁ nu surataṁ naiva smṛtir jāyate \|\|*

**O friend! Those women who, taking support of the methods of love, show boldness and great steadiness even when intimately united with their lover, are most fortunate. He undoes my garment with his lotus hand. I have no memory of who he is, who I am and whether we made love. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 1.43**

### Text 15

*kokila-kala-rava-kūjitayā*  
*jita-manasija-tantra-vicāram \|*  
*ślatha-kusumākula-kuntalayā*  
*nakha-likhita-ghana-stana-bhāram \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kokila - Like \[those of\] the cuckoo; kala-rava - soft and sweet \[sounds\]; kujitaya- \[I\] making sounds; jita- is mastered; manasija-tantra-vicaram - \[he\] by whom the art of love; slatha - has come undone; kusumakula - covered with flowers; kuntalaya- \[I\] whose hair; nakha-likhita - is marked with \[his\] nails; ghana-stana - of \[my\] firm breasts; -bharam - \[he\] by whom the weight.

**Перевод:**

**I make sounds like the cooing of the cuckoo. Kṛṣṇa has mastered the art of love. My hair covered with flowers has come undone. Kṛṣṇa has marked my heavy breasts with his nails.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā is described. I made soft, sweet *(kala*) sounds like warbling of the cuckoo. Or I made sounds like the pigeon (*kala-rava*) and the cuckoo *(kokila*). This description refers to external experiences of love.

*pika-śikhi-kalahaṁsa-prāya-pakṣi-vrajānāṁ*  
*svanitam anukaroti hy aṅganā manathārtā \|*  
*mukha-daśana-vivartā tat kavīndrā vadanti*  
*stanitam iti samāsāc citra-sambhoga-kāle \|\|*

**The woman makes sounds like the cuckoo, peacock and swan with changes in mouth and teeth. The poets in this way describe the sound at the time of enjoyment. Pañca-sāyaka**

*dātyūha-lāvaka-mayūra-kapota-haṁsa-*  
*pārāvatādi-rutavad dhvanitaṁ ratākhye \|*  
*tan-miśra-niḥśvasita-sītkṛta-hāni-nādaiḥ*  
*saṁyojayen madana-cumbana-maithunādau \|\|*

**Sounds like the gallinule,** ***lāvaka*****, peacock, dove, swan or pigeon combine with decreased breathing and sighing during love and kissing. Rasika-sarvasva**

It is also said:

*kānte tathā katham api prathitaṁ mṛgākṣyā*  
*cāturyam uddhata-manobhavayā rateṣu \|*  
*tat kūjitaṁ mṛdu vadadbhir aneka-vāraṁ*  
*śikṣāyitaṁ gṛha-kapota-śatair yathā syāt \|\|*

**The woman uses skill with her lover during love with increased passion. She makes sounds with soft words repeatedly as if repeatedly teaching hundreds of pigeons to coo.**

The sequence is described by Vātsyāyana and others.

*āśleṣaṁ prathamaṁ kuryād dvitīyaṁ cumbanas tathā \|*  
*tṛtīyaṁ nakha-dānaṁ ca daṁṣṭrāghātaṁ caturthakam \|\|*  
*pañcamaṁ kṣepaṇaṁ proktaṁ ṣaṣṭhaṁ praharaṇaṁ tathā \|*  
*saptamaṁ kaṇṭha-śabdaś ca bandhanaṁ cāṣṭamaṁ ratam \|\|*

**First one embraces, then kisses. Then one uses the nails and teeth. Then stroking and then striking. Then one makes guttural sounds and finally one unites with the lover.**

Applying nails and teeth in the seventh stage (making sounds) would interrupt Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure.

Kṛṣṇa is then described. He has mastered all the arts according to the scriptures on love. It is said:

*caturdaśa-vidhā bhāryā svīyā sevyā parā na tu \|*  
*parakīyām api bhajann uttamām eva kāmayet \|\|*  
*parāpi bālā madhyā ca prauḍhā vṛddhā caturvidhā \|*  
*bālā bala-karāśliṣṭā madhyā śliṣṭā rati-pradā \|\|*  
*prauḍhā bala-kṣaya-karī vṛddhā puṁstva-vināśinī \|\|*

**There are fourteen types of women to be enjoyed as one’s own. Not accepting someone’s wife one should enjoy. The four best are called** ***bālā, madhyā, prauḍhā,*** **and** ***vṛddhā*****. The** ***bālā*** **is embraced and gives strength to the man. The** ***madhyā*** **is embraced and incites his passion. The** ***prauḍhā*** **reduces one’s strength. The** ***vṛddhā*** **destroys one’s masculinity.**

Among the fourteen varieties including devatās, gāndharvas are considered madhyā and most pleasant. The qualities of the gāndharva are described:

*udyāna-kānana-ratā gīta-vādya-ratā tathā \|*  
*śiva-bhaktā ca susnātā gāndharvī sā prakīrtitā \|\|*

**The gāndharva woman is one who frequents gardens, plays musical instruments, is devoted to Śiva and bathes frequently.**

Service to her is as follows:

*snātvā sugandha-dravais tu saṅgīta-dhvani-pūrvakam \|*  
*mṛdv-āliṅgana-dānena gāndharvīṁ kāmayed budhaḥ \|\|*

**The wise man pleases the gāndharva woman by offering soft embraces after he has bathed using perfumed articles, while singing.**

Or Kṛṣṇa is skilful at love making, or has reached the state of bliss. Or Kṛṣṇa disregards the conclusions of other lover makers. It is said *sākṣān manmatha-manmathaḥ*: Kṛṣṇa bewilders the mind of Cupid, though Cupid himself bewilders the minds of ordinary people. (SB 10.32.2) *Kāmināṁ darśayan dainyam:* contrast he showed the wretchedness of ordinary lusty men and hardhearted women. (SB 10.30.34) Or Kṛṣṇa forgets the conclusions of scriptures on love.

The meaning is this. Since Kṛṣṇa is the master of the sixty-four arts, is the supreme hero of *rasa* and is the Supreme Lord, there is no fault if he does not remember the sequence in love-making since he becomes absorbed in *rasa* in the presence of the supreme heroine who is cooperative. This does not destroy *rasa*. It is said *rati-cakre pravṛttasya naiva śāstram na ca kramaḥ*: there is no specific sequence of actions and no scripture for one engaged in the whirlpool of love. (Kāma-sūtra 6.2.32)

Rādhā is again described. Her hair spread with flowers has become loosened by his grasping her hair and kissing her. Or her hair has loosened because the flowers have become undone.

Kṛṣṇa is further described. He has profusely marked her heavy breasts with his nails. Using the nails in this way is called *paṅkaja-patra.* It is said*:*

*atyūrdhvam ekaṁ sphurito’rdha-rekhaṁ*  
*prāhuḥ kṣataṁ paṅkaja-patra-saṁjñam \|*  
*dor-mūla-vakṣaḥ-stana-nābhi-sandhāv*  
*asyopayogaṁ manute maheśaḥ \|\|*

**Inflicting one vertical half-line the base of the arm, the chest, breast or navel is called** ***pañkaja-patra*** **(lotus leaf). Śiva considers this proper. Pañca-sāyaka**

*Tantra* means scriptures according to *Viśva. Kuntala* means hair according to Amara-koṣa.

### Text 16

*caraṇa-raṇita-maṇi-nūpurayā*  
*paripūrita-surata-vitānam \|*  
*mukhara-viśṛṅkhala-mekhalayā*  
*sakaca-graha-cumbana-dānam \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

carana-On \[my\] feet; ranita -jingled; mani-nupuraya - \[I\] whose ankle bells; paripurita - was made complete; surata-vitanam - \[he\] by whom the distribution of love; mukhara - jingling; visrnkhala — was dismantled; mekhalaya - \[I\] whose belt; sakaca-graha - and the grasping of the hair; cumbana-danam - \[he\] to whom there was given a kiss;

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā’s ankle bells jingled. Kṛṣṇa distributed love completely. Her jingling belt was dismantled. He is kissed by her while she grasped his hair.**

**Комментарий:**

Until now seven external types of love making have been described in five verses. Now the eighth type of love is described.

Rādhā’s jeweled ankle bells jingled. This shows Kṛṣṇa holding her in a certain way. Or, indicates Rādhā taking a reverse position in love called *bhrāmaraka.* This is described in detail in Rati-rahasya.

Kṛṣṇa is described. He distributed love completely. This refers to the male predominating. Recognizing I am tired from taking the *bhrāmaraka* position, he should give me enjoyment as I lie on the bed of new shoots.

Rādhā is described. Her belt which previously jingled now was broken. This describes the position called *preṅkholita.* This is also described in Kāma-sūtra 6.8.27 and Rati-rahasya.

Kṛṣṇa is described. He is kissed while his hair is grasped. This also describes a reverse position. It is said:

*śṛṅgāre cumbanaṁ kāryaṁ puṁsāliṅgana-pūrvakam \|*  
*viparīta-grahe nāryāḥ sakaca-graha-cumbanam \|\|*

**In love making the man kisses while embracing the woman. In the reverse position, the woman kisses the man while holding his hair.**

*Mekhala* refers to a belt according to *Viśva. Śṛṇkhala* means a belt.

### Text 17

*rati-sukha-samaya-rasālasayā*  
*dara-mukulita-nayana-sarojam \|*  
*niḥsaha-nipatita-tanu-latayā*  
*madhusūdanam udita-manojam \|\|17\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

rati - Of love; sukha - of \[attaining\] the satisfaction; samaya - at the time; rasalasaya -\[I\] indolent because of srngara-rasa; dara-mukulita - were slightly closed; nayana-sarojam - \[he\] whose lotus eyes; nihsaha - powerless; nipatita - fell \[upon a bed of lotus shoots\]; tanu-lataya - \[I\] whose creeper-like body; madhusudanam - Madhusudana (Krsna); udita - was aroused \[again\]; manojam -whose passion

**Перевод:**

**I became indolent having attained satisfaction in love. He closed his eyes slightly. Being incapable of movement, my body fell upon the bed. His passion again became aroused.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā is described. I became indolent because of *śṛṅgāra-rasa* with the highest experience at the time of attaining satisfaction in love. Kṛṣṇa is described. He closed his lotus eyes slightly. This description indicates that both had attained bliss. It is said*:*

*mūrchanā mīlanaṁ cākṣṇoś cyuti-kālasya lakṣaṇam \|*  
*sarvam etat parijñāya sādhayet tat svayaṁ tataḥ \|\|*

**Perfection is reached when there is fainting, closing the eyes, with symptoms of loss of time. Rati-rahasya**

Being incapable of anything else, my creeper-like body fell upon the bed of shoots. This indicates the time after enjoyment. It is said:

*aṅge svedaḥ ślathatvaṁ ca keśa-vastrādi-saṁvṛtiḥ \|*  
*jāte cyuti-sukhe nāryā virāmecchā ca jāyate \|\|*

**There is perspiration on her limbs. She feels languid. She rearranges her hair and cloth. With the end of happiness, the woman develops desire for rest. Bharata**

After love in the reverse position her actions are described:

*svecchayā milita-vallabho’thavā*  
*yoṣid ācarati puruṣāyitam \|*  
*ādito ghaṭitam eva yantritaṁ*  
*bhūpa-tanu-karaṇaṁ ca ceṣṭate \|\|*

**By her desire she meets her lover and takes the dominant role. After exerting herself, she acts like a king.**

Here is an example:

*atiprayatnena ratānta-tāntā*  
*kṛṣṇena talpād avaropitā sā \|*  
*ālambya tasyaiva karaṁ kareṇa*  
*jyotsnā-kṛtānandam alindam āpa \|\|*

**Languid after love because of great exertion with Kṛṣṇa, she left the bed while taking support of his hand and came to the terrace made blissful by the moon light*****.*** **Rasārṇava-sudhākara 2.14**

Kṛṣṇa is described. Madhusūdana means a person greedy to taste madhurya-rasa. Madhusūdana also means a bee. His love has again been aroused (*udita-manojam*),by Rādhā’s glance accompanied by a shy smile, and by the touch of her nearby hips and breasts as she replaced her necklace and belt which had previously fallen off. It is said:

*avasāne’pi ca prītir upacārair upasthatā \|*  
*sa-viśrambha-kathā-yogaiḥ ratiṁ janayate parām \|\|*

**After finishing love making, affection appears again with various elements. One can later generate love by using intimate talks.**

The poem describes the sweetness of the two. Madhusūdana can also mean “One who causes sweetness to flow.” He causes love because of bliss (*mudita*) from great sweetness by his skill in *śrṅgāra-rasa.* Some explain Madhusūdana as the killer of the demon Madhu to denote Kṛṣṇa’s capabilities.

### Text 18

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇitam idam ati-*  
*śaya-madhu-ripu-nidhuvana-śīlam \|*  
*sukham utkaṇṭhita-gopa-vadhū-*  
*kathitaṁ vitanotu sa-līlam \|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva-bhanitam - Spoken by Sri Jayadeva; idam - this; ati-saya-madhu-ripu - of the supreme Madhuripu (Madhu’s enemy - Krsna); nidhuvana - amorous acts; silam - \[and\] describing; sukham - happiness; utkanthita - filled with longing; gopa-vadhu -\[describing\] the wives of cowherds (Radha and the gopls) ; kathitam - composition; vitanotu - may it spread; sa-lilam - along with the activities arising from srngara-rasa

**Перевод:**

**May this composition spoken by Jayadeva, describing Rādhā and the gopīs filled with longing, filled with the amorous actions of Kṛṣṇa, give happiness to you all.**

**Комментарий:**

May what is here described by the poet Jayadeva, concerning Rādhā and the *gopīs* who had great longing, produce happiness. Let the readers hear this. It is filled with descriptions of Kṛṣṇa’s intimate pastimes (*nidhuvana*), along with the activities arising from *śṛṅgāra-rasa* (*sa-līlam). Nidhuvana* means amorous acts according to *Amara-koṣa. Kathitam* expresses a state.

### Text 19

*hasta-srasta-vilāsa-vaṁśam anṛju-bhrū-vallimad-ballavī-*  
*vṛndotsāri-dṛganta-vīkṣitam atisvedārdra-gaṇḍa-sthalam \|*  
*mām udvīkṣya vilajjitaṁ smṛta-sudhā-mugdhānana-kānane*  
*govindaṁ vraja-sundarī-gaṇa-vṛtaṁ paśyāmi hṛṣyāmi ca \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

hasta - From his hand; srasta - is fallen; vilasa-vamsam - whose pastime-flute; anrju-bhru - crooked eyebrows; vallimat - like a creeper; ballavi - of cowherdesses (gopis); vrndotsari - repel the group; drganta - from the corner of his eyes; viksitam - whose glance; atisvedardra - is very wet with perspiration; ganda-sthalam - the surface of whose cheeks; mam - me; udvlksya - having seen; vilajjitam - ashamed; smita-sudha - because of the brightness of his smile; mugdhanana - with an attractive face; kanane - in the forest; govindam - Govinda (Krsna); vraja-sundari - of cowherdesses (gopls); gana - with a group; vrtam - \[and\] surrounded; pasyami -I see; hrsyami -I rejoice; ca - and;

**Перевод:**

**I saw Govinda in the forest surrounded by other gopīs with his attractive face and bright smile, his cheeks covered with perspiration, and when he saw me, he glanced sideways to repel the group of gopīs having crooked, creeper-like brows. The flute fell from his hand. Thus I rejoice.**

**Комментарий:**

When Rādhā is led by her *sakhī*, she sees Kṛṣṇa surrounded by a group of *gopīs*. Turning back, she speaks to her *sakhī*.

O friend! I see Govinda in the forest and have become excited. The present tense is used because she just saw him. He was surrounded by a group of *gopīs*. “If you saw him with your competitors, why do you rejoice?” Seeing me, he became ashamed. He thinks,“She saw me with other women.” He had an attractive face because of his splendid, bright smile. Though surrounded by many women, he understood he had offended me and was afraid of me. Thus I rejoice. He was driving away the group of *gopīs* who had crooked eyebrows by the glance from the edge of his eye out of fear, since I had come.

He is further described. His pastime flute had fallen from his hand out of fear on seeing me. His cheeks were moist with profuse perspiration, on seeing me everywhere. The phrase “*gopīs* with creeper-like crooked brows” indicates their beauty. There is another meaning. By his glance he drove away the group of *gopīs* whose creeper brows became crooked on seeing my superiority. Another meaning is: with his crooked creeper brow he glanced to drive away the group of *gopīs*. This indicates his beauty, with brows dancing in a pleasing way. Or, he gave a side glance which spread out widely (*utsarī*) to the *gopīs* with crooked brows, to show them his mood. Or he gave side glances which spread upwards (*utsarī*) on seeing my qualities because of the other *gopīs* showing crooked brows. On seeing the best heroine endowed with all qualities, rasa with its components arose in Kṛṣṇa.

Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are the *ālambana-vibhāva*. The *sthāyī-bhāva* is *rati* with favorable and unfavorable elements. The forest is the *uddīpana-vibhāva.* The *anubhāvas* are smiling and glancing: his attractive face had a bright smile and he glanced with the edge of his eye. The *sāttvika-bhāvas* are perspiration and trembling: he perspired profusely. Sūddipta-sāttvika-bhāva is shown when his trembling caused the flute to fall from his hand. Alarm is the *vyabhicāri-bhāva*. Shame and fear are also *vyabhicāri-bhāvas.* The great poet has described the king of all *rasas*, *śṛṅgāra*, along with its *aṅgas* (*vibhāva* etc.) through which Kṛṣṇa responds to Rādhā, queen of Vṛndāvana, who is endowed with the highest qualities. What more can be said? The hero is *anukūla* (favorable). It is said:

*atiraktatayā nāryāṁ tyaktānya-lalanā-spṛhaḥ \|*  
*sītāyāṁ rāmavat so’yam anukūlaḥ prakīrtitaḥ \|\|*  
*rādhāyām eva kṛṣṇasya suprasiddhānukūlatā \|*  
*tad-āloke kadāpy asya nānyā-saṅgaḥ smṛtiṁ vrajet \|\|*

**He who gives up desire for other women with intense attachment for one woman, like Rāma for Sītā, is called the** ***anukula*** **or well-disposed lover. Kṛṣṇa’s is famous for his** ***anukūla*** **attachment to Rādhā. Whenever he sees her, he forgets other women. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 1.25-26**

An example is given:

*vaidagdhī-nikuramba-cumbita-dhiyaḥ saundarya-sārojjvalāḥ*  
*kāminyaḥ kati nādya vallava-pater dīvyanti goṣṭhāntare \|*  
*rādhe puṇyavatī-śikhā-maṇir asi kṣāmodari tvāṁ vinā*  
*preṅkhantī na parāsu yan madhuripor dṛṣṭātra dṛṣṭir mayā \|\|*

**O Rādhā! So many women whose minds are filled with playfulness, whose bodies shine with the essence of beauty, do not play in the courtyard of Nanda at this time. You are the crest jewel of pious women. O slender-waisted girl! I have never seen the glances of Kṛṣṇa fall upon other women when he is separated from you. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 1.27**

The ornament is called *preyas.* This is explained by Daṇḍi:

*prīti-prakāśaṇaṁ yat tat preya ity abhidhīyate \|\|*

***Preyas*** **refers to the manifestation of affection. Kāvyādarśa 2.297**

### Text 20

*durāloka-stoka-stavaka-navakāśoka-latikā-*  
*vikāsaḥ kāsāropavana-pavano’pi vyathayati \|*  
*api bhrāmyad-bhṛṅgī-raṇita-ramaṇīyā na mukula-*  
*prasūtiś cūtānāṁ sakhi śikhariṇīyaṁ sukhayati \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

duraloka - Is seen with pain; stoka-stavaka - on which there is a small clusters of flowers; navakasoka-latika - of the new \[pitiable\] asoka branch; vikasah - the blossoming; kasaropavana - from the pond in the garden; pavana - the wind; api - also; vyathayati -gives pain; api - \[and\] even; bhramyad-bhrngi - of the female bees; ranita - with the humming; ramantya - pleasant; na - \[does\] not; mukula-prasutih - the appearance of buds; cutanam - of the mango trees; sakhi - О friend!; sikharinlyam - on Govardhana (or having beautiful tips); sukhayati - give happiness.

**Перевод:**

**The blossoming clusters of buds on the new pitiable āśoka branch are seen with pain. The wind from the pond in the garden gives pain. The appearance of buds on the mango tree, pleasant with humming of female bees on Govardhana, does not given happiness.**

**Комментарий:**

O friend! The opening of the small clusters of buds on very new, pitiable *aśoka* branch is seen with pain *(duraloka*). *Navaka,* meaning very new, is derived from *nava* according to the rule *ke ‘ṇaḥ* (Pāṇini 7.4.13) *Latikā* means a pitiable branch according to the rule *anukampāyām* (Pāṇini 5.3. 76) The pitiable new *aśoka* branch, held in my hand, is seen with pain. The wind from the pond in the garden gives pain. Mention of the pond indicates that the wind is very cool. Mention of the garden indicates that the wind is fragrant and indolent. The wind is slowed by the obstacles created by the trees. Or, the whole first compound can modify *pavanaḥ:* the wind from the pond in the garden, which makes the small clusters on the new *aśoka* branch bloom, gives me pain.

“Come forward. The wind can be avoided by your shawl.” The wind is not visible (*duraloka*). It arises suddenly. The word *api* indicates that even the blossoming aśoka branch gives pain. The appearance of buds on the mango tree on Govardhana does not give joy. Who knows it is the state of blossoming? What will it do? Or, the appearance of buds having beautiful tips (*śikhariṇī*) gives pain. The suffix in added to *śikhara* to indicate praise. Or *śikhariṇī* can mean jasmine. The jasmine does not give joy, though it is pleasant with the humming of female bees. Mention of female bees suggests that they are looking for a male bee. *Śikhariṇī* also indicates a poetic meter. *Stoka* means small according to *Viśva. Stavaka* means a bunch according to *Amara-koṣa. Latā* means a branch according to *Viśva. Kāsāra* means a pond according to *Amara-koṣa. Śikhariṇī* means jasmine according to *Viśva.*

### Text 21

*sakūta-smitam ākulākula-galad-dhammillam ullāsita-*  
*bhrū-vallīkam alīka-darśita-bhujā-mūlārdha-hasta-stanam \|*  
*gopīnāṁ nibhṛtaṁ nirīkṣya gamitākāṅkṣaś ciraṁ cintayann*  
*antar mugdha-manoharaṁ haratu vaḥ kleśaṁ navaḥ keśavaḥ \|\|21\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sakuta-smitam - The smiling with a purpose; akulakula - completely undone; galat - \[and\] falling down; dhammillam - the ornamented hair; ullasita-bhru-vallikam - the raised brows; allka-on the pretext \[of yawning\]; darsita - revealed; bhuja-mula - the shoulders; ardha-hasta - the forearms; stanam - \[and\] the breasts; gopinam - of the \[other\] gopis; nibhrtam - secretly; niriksya - \[and\] having glanced at; gamita - is for Radha; kanksas - whose desire; ciram cintayan - thinking for a long time; antah - internally; mugdha-manoharam - \[and\] the attracting of the minds of innocent people; haratu -may \[he\] destroy; vah - your; klesam - sufferings; navah - youthful; kesavah - Kesava (Krsna).

**Перевод:**

**May youthful Keśava, with desire for Rādhā, thinking for a long time and glancing secretly at the gopīs, who with intention smiled slightly with raised brows, showing their shoulders on the pretext of yawning, trying to attract the innocent, destroy your suffering.**

**Комментарий:**

May Kṛṣṇa dispel your suffering! He is described. He has desire for Rādhā (*gamitākāṅkṣaḥ*) or on seeing Rādhā has lost all desire for other *gopīs*. What does he do? He thought for a long time internally while glancing secretly (*nibhṛtam*) or while glancing at the revelation of emotions of the *gopīs*. Why did he glance? He glanced at the group of *gopīs* who smiled slightly with a purpose (*sakūta-smitam*), whose hair had become completely undone because of intense desire for him, with hairs standing on end. They had raised brows (*ullāsita-bhrū-vallīkam*). They used some pretext like yawning to reveal portions of their upper chest and shoulder. These actions would steal the minds of innocent people. Kṛṣṇa is described as having youth (*navaḥ*). Seeing his youth, women develop desire. Or on seeing Rādhā he became new, as if never seen previously by others. This shows his manifestation of amazing qualities. Navaḥ can also mean “he who is praised with the qualities of a hero.” *Navaḥ* can also means ever fresh. He is experienced as if never experienced before, though he is constantly experienced. An example is given:

*kula-varatanu-dharma-grāva-vṛndāni bhindan*  
*sumukhi niśita-dīrghāpāṅga-ṭaṅka-cchaṭābhiḥ*  
*yugapad ayam apūrvaḥ kaḥ puro viśva-karmā*  
*marakata-maṇi-lakṣair goṣṭha-kakṣāṁ cinoti \|\|*

**O beautiful-faced one, who is this creative person standing before us? With the sharp chisels of his loving glances, he is splitting the hard stones of many women's devotion to their husbands. And with the luster of his body, surpassing the brilliance of countless emeralds, he is simultaneously constructing private meeting places for his pastimes. Lalita-mādhava**

If nibhṛtam means “revealing emotions” the four phrases describing the *gopīs* expand upon the emotions. They acted attractively to bewilder others (*mugdha-manoharam*).

The title of this section Akleśa-keśava means “He who destroys the suffering of the gopīs” or “Because of his joy on seeing Rādhā, he had his suffering destroyed.”

## Part 3: Attractive Madhusūdana

### Text 1

*kaṁsārir api saṁsāra-vāsanā-bandha-śṛṅkhalām \|*  
*rādhām ādhāya hṛdaye tatyāja vraja-sundarīḥ \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kamsarir - The enemy of Kamsa (Krsna); api - even; samsara - of madhurya-rasa (or of worldly things); vasana - with impressions; bandha - for binding \[him\]; srnkhalam - a chain; radham - Radha; adhaya - having taken; hrdaye - to \[his\] heart; tatyaja - abandoned; vraja-sundarih - the \[other\] cowherdesses (gopls).

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa, taking Rādhā into his heart, because she was a chain binding him with impressions of madhurya-rasa, gave up the other gopīs.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā’s longing for Kṛṣṇa was described in Part Two. Kṛṣṇa’s longing for Rādhā is now described. Kṛṣṇa, enemy of Kaṁsa, gave up the other *gopīs* since he had placed Rādhā in his heart. This indicates Kṛṣṇa’s intense attraction for Rādhā. Rādhā is described? She acted as a chain to bind him to impressions of desire (*saṁsāra*). This indicates the first state called *abhilāsa* (desire). Its characteristics were described previously. (GG 7) It is also said:

*saṅgamopāya-racita-prārabdha-vyavasāyataḥ \|*  
*saṅkalpecchā-samudbhūtir abhilāṣo’tra vikriyāḥ \|\|*  
*praveśa-nirgamau tūṣṇīṁ tad-dṛṣṭi-patha-gāminau \|*  
*rāga-prakāśana-parāś ceṣṭāḥ svātma-prasādhanam \|*  
*vyājoktayaś ca vijane sthitir ity evam ādayaḥ \|\|*

**In the stage of** ***abhilāsa*** **a person makes first resolve find a way of meeting. Desire to make a decision manifests. He goes outside and stands silently looking at the path where the lover should appear. He performs actions which reveal his attachment: he dresses up, speaks with double meaning, and remains in a solitary place. Rasārṇava-sudhākara 2.379-380**

He thus entered preliminary states with their effects, since this is the first cause and produced intense sweetness in *prema*.

Or *saṁsāra* can mean the complete (*sam)* essence (*sāra), mādhurya-rasa,* full of bliss since the Lord is its subject. Thus Rādhā was like a chain for binding him to eternal impressions of *mādhurya-rasa*.

*ānanda-cinmaya-rasa-pratibhāvitābhis*  
*tābhir ya eva nija-rūpatayā kalābhiḥ \|*  
*goloka eva nivasaty akhilātma-bhūto*  
*govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi \|\|*

**I worship the Supreme Lord Govinda who, though regarding all the inhabitants as his very self, resides in Goloka exclusively with the young** ***gopīs*****, who are embodiments of** ***mādhurya-rasa*****. He accepts them as his wives, while they respond to his conjugal affection reciprocally. Brahma-saṁhitā 5.37**

Or *saṁsāra* can indicate Rādhā, the final goal of all his efforts. Thus she was a chain for controlling him by making him continually think (*vāsana*) about her (*saṁsāra*). The word *kaṁsāriḥ* (Kaṁsa’s enemy) also means “he who distributes (*sārati*) happiness (*kam*).” Since Kaṁsa feared him, the name also shows Kṛṣṇa’s abilities. It is said *bhayāt kaṁsaḥ*: out of fear Kaṁsa obtained Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. (SB 7.1.31) *Śṛṅkhala* means chains according to Viśva.

### Text 2

*itas tatas tām anusṛtya rādhikām*  
*anaṅga-bāṇa-vraṇa-khinna-mānasaḥ \|*  
*kṛtānutāpaḥ sa kalinda-nandinī-*  
*taṭānta-kuñje viṣasāda mādhavaḥ \|\|2\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

itas tatas - Then; tam - for her; anusrtya - having searched; radhikam - for Radha; ananga - of the Cupid; bana - of the arrows; vrana -with the wounds; khinna - was oppressed; manasah - whose mind; krtanutapah - by whom remorse was made; sa - he; kalinda-nandini - of the Yamuna River; tatanta - on the edge of the bank; kunje - in a grove; visasada - lamented; madhavah - Madhava (Krsna).

**Перевод:**

**After searching here and there for Rādhā, in a bower on the edge of the bank of the Yamunā, in a hut made of creepers, Kṛṣṇa lamented remorsefully. His mind was in pain because of the wounds inflicted by the arrows of Cupid.**

**Комментарий:**

What else did Kṛṣṇa do? In a bower on the edge of the bank of the Yamunā, in a hut made of creepers, he lamented. Mādhava means the husband (*dhava*) of Lakṣmī (*mā*). This indicates his pain in separation and the great good fortune of Rādhā. What had he done previous to this? Here and there he had searched for Rādhā, who was fixed in his heart, giving up his associates at their place where they were to meet. His mind was in pain because of the wounds inflicted by the arrows of Cupid. And he was repentant. “Why did I not pacify her before this? How is it possible to exist without her now?” The word *anaṅga* (Cupid) suggests that this could not be avoided. This verse describes the second stage called *cintā* (anxiety). Its characteristics were described previously.

## Song 7: Krishna laments losing Radha

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 7 \|\|**  
**Gūrjarī-rāga, yati-tāla**

### Text 3

*mām iyaṁ calitā vilokya vṛtaṁ vadhū-nicayena \|*  
*sāparādhatayā mayāpi na vāritā’tibhayena \|\|3\|\|*

*hari hari hatādaratayā gatā sā kupiteva \|\|dhruva-padam \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

mam - Me; iyam - she; calita - departed; vilokya - having seen; vrtam - surrounded; vadhu-nicayena - with a group of women (gopis). saparadhataya - Because of \[my\] being an offender; mayapi - even by me; na - \[she could\] not \[be\]; varita’ - stopped; tibhayena -because of \[my\] great fear.

hari-hari - О what misfortune! (or О Krsna! О Krsna!) hatadarataya - Because \[she\] has lost \[her\] respect; gata - has gone; sa - she; kupiteva - \[and\] is angered.

**Перевод:**

**Seeing me surrounded by other gopīs, she went away. She could not be stopped, because I was fearful, having committed offense.**  
**Ah! She is angry because she lost great respect. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The *rāga* is *gurjarī* and the *tāla* is *pratimaṇṭha.* Both have already been explained. The meaning of the song is as follows. O what misfortune (*hari hari)*! She, dearer that millions of my lives, with famous qualities, Rādhā, has become angry because of losing great respect. She had become angry because of a quarrel in jest, since there cannot be complete anger within love. This is the *vyabhicāri-bhāva* called anger. This is the chorus.

“How do you know that she is angry?” Seeing me surrounded by all the *gopīs*, she went away. The verb *vilokya* indicates that right after seeing this, because of this, she left. Thus I know she is angry.

“Why did you not stop her from leaving?” Because of great fear, I could not stop her. How could I speak in front of her? The cause of the fear was his offense. The offense was to participate with other *gopīs* in play without Rādhā.

### Text 4

*kiṁ kariṣyati kiṁ vadiṣyati sā ciraṁ viraheṇa \|*  
*kiṁ dhanena janena kiṁ mama jīvitena gṛheṇa \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kim - What; karisyati - will \[she\] do? kim - What; vadisyati - will \[she\] say? sa - she; ciram - for a long time; virahena - because of separation, kim - What is the use; dhanena - of wealth? janena - of \[other\] women; kim - What is the use? mama - my, jivitena - \[What is the use of my\] life? grhena - \[What is the use of\] a house?

**Перевод:**

**What will she do in long separation from me? What will she say to me when we meet? What is the use of wealth? What is the use of other women? What is the use of my life? What is the use of a house?**

**Комментарий:**

What will she do in separation from me for a long time? By what method will she alleviate her pain? He disregards his own pain and is overcome by her pain. This shows his great love. For instance it is said:

*yat te sujāta-caraṇāmburuhaṁ staneṣu*  
*bhītāḥ śanaiḥ priya dadhīmahi karkaśeṣu*  
*tenāṭavīm aṭasi tad vyathate na kiṁ svit*  
*kūrpādibhir bhramati dhīr bhavad-āyuṣāṁ naḥ*

**O dearly beloved! Your lotus feet are so soft that we place them gently on our breasts, fearing that your feet will be hurt. Our life rests only in you. Our minds, therefore, are filled with anxiety that your tender feet might be wounded by pebbles as you roam about on the forest path. SB 10.31.19**

Or knowing that I suffer from separation for a long time, will she perhaps accept me? *Kim* indicates conjecture. In this case, even a moment’s separation appears as a long time. It is said:

*gopīnāṁ paramānanda āsīd govinda-darśane*  
*kṣaṇaṁ yuga-śatam iva yāsāṁ yena vinābhavat*

**The young** ***gopīs*** **took the greatest pleasure in seeing Govinda, since for them even a moment without his association seemed like a hundred ages. SB 10.19.16**

This is experienced in *mahābhāva*.

What will she say to me? Seeing me falling at her feet in order to beg forgiveness for my offense, what words of scolding will she give? Or, will she speak good news to me, with sweet words? Or will she speak soothing words to me or point out my faults? Or, when I meet with her and speak skillfully, will she become angrier? Will she use rough words? Or will she give up anger and, being pleased, speak sweet words? Such thoughts are described in Hari-vaṁśa as well. Jayadeva thus describes the many actions of Kṛṣṇa, who is the very form of pastimes which yield a taste of rasa. One should not think these explanations are imaginary.

“Why be so eager for her? You should change your heart by cultivating other *gopīs*.” Without her what is the use of other *gopīs* (*kiṁ janena*)? What use do I have for all the wealth of my father Nanda, lord of Vraja (*kiṁ dhanena*)? I have no use at all for it. Without her, I reject all faces gorgeously decorated, since it will bring me no satisfaction. She alone is my ornament and my wealth. It will be said later *tvam asi mama jīvanam:* you are my life. *(*GG 19.3)

“But can you be happy in the house without wealth?” What is the use of a house? “Can you not be happy without her?” What is the use of my happiness? What is the use of any happiness without her? She is the form of my happiness. Or what is the use of happiness related to myself since every woman who gives me happiness will only give suffering to me, since I experience her suffering. If she is happy then I am happy. Instead of *sukhena* sometimes *jīvitena* is seen: what is the use of my living? What is my life since I cannot tolerate her suffering? My life is worthless.

This verse describes the third state called *smṛti.* It is described as follows in Rasārṇava-sudhākara:

*arthānām anubhūtānāṁ deśa-kālānuvartinām \|*  
*sāntatyena parāmarśo mānasaḥ syād anusmṛtiḥ \|\|*  
*tatrānubhāvā niḥśvāso dhyāṇaṁ kṛtya-vihastatā \|*  
*śayyāsanādi-vidveṣa ity ādyāḥ smara-kalpitāḥ \|\|*

**Remembrance of objects and experiences at various places and times, takes place continually in the mind. In that condition there are indications of emotion, heavy breathing, meditation, giving up duties, and dislike of beds and chairs. This is all produced because of love. Rasārnava-sudhākara 2.385-6**

### Text 5

*cintayāmi tad-ānanaṁ kuṭila-bhru kopa-bhareṇa \|*  
*śona-padmam ivopari-bhramatākulaṁ bhramareṇa \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

cintayami - I think about; tad-ananam - her (Radha’s) face; kutila-bhru - whose eyebrow is frowning; kopa-bharena - because of the intensity of \[her\] anger; sona-padmam - the red lotus; iva - like; upari - above \[it\], bhramatakulam bhramarena - with a swarm of hovering bees.

**Перевод:**

**I think of Rādhā with face like a red lotus because of intense anger, with a frowning brow like a swarm of bees hovering above.**

**Комментарий:**

Though he is overcome by her sorrow, he becomes absorbed in thinking solely of her, though she is angry. This shows the excellence of his love.

I remember her face, which is like a red lotus, flushed with intense anger. The redness in her face due to anger is compared to a red lotus. Above the lotus were hovering bees. Her eyebrows are compared to bees. This verse represents the fourth stage called glorifying qualities (*guṇa-kīrtana*).

*aṅga-pratyaṅga-līlābhir vāk-ceṣṭā-hasitekṣaṇaiḥ \|*  
*na tasya sadṛśaḥ kaścid ity-ādi-guṇa-kīrtanam \|\|*

**Glorifying qualities consists of saying that nothing can equal the grace of lover’s limbs, her glances, smile, actions and words.**

*Śona* means the color of the red lotus according to *Amara-koṣa.* This shows her infallible beauty since anger can reveal beauty. This is called *mādhurya.* It is said:

*mādhuryaṁ nāma ceṣṭānāṁ sarvāvasthāsu cārutā \|\|*

***Mādhuryam*** **means beauty of actions in all circumstances. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 11.19**

### Text 6

*tām ahaṁ hṛdi saṅgatām aniśaṁ bhṛśaṁ ramayāmi \|*  
*kiṁ vane’nusarāmi tām iha kiṁ vṛthā vilapāmi \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**
