tam - Her; aham -I; hrdi - into \[my\] heart; sangatam - having come; anisam - constantly; bhrsam - fervently; ramayami - \[I\] enjoy, kim - Why \[do\]; vane - in the forest; ’nusarami -I follow; tam - her; iha -here? kim - Why \[do\]; vrtha - uselessly; vilapami -I lament?

**Перевод:**

**Why do I follow her in the forest? Why do I uselessly lament? I enjoy constantly with her in my heart.**

**Комментарий:**

Why do I pursue for her in the forest? Here in the forest why do I uselessly lament? I play fervently (*bhṛśam)* with her constantly in my heart. This is a sphūrti of union during separation. It is a transformation in mahābhāva*.* Or “Should I play with her constantly in my heart or should I pursue her in the forest? Uselessly I lament.”

### Text 7

*tanvi khinnam asūyayā hṛdayaṁ tavākalayāmi \|*  
*tan na vedmi kuto gatāsi natena te’nunayāmi \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tanvi - О slender-waisted woman (Radha)! khinnam - \[is\] pained; asuyaya - with indignation \[because of finding fault in me\]; hrdayam - heart; tava - your; ahalayami - I know, tan - Therefore; na - \[do\] not; vedmi -I know; kuto - where; gatasi - you have gone. natena - by offering respects \[to the ground\]; te’ - you; nunayami -I conciliate.

**Перевод:**

**O Rādhā with slender waist! I know your heart is pained by finding fault in me. I do not know where you have gone. Therefore I conciliate you by offering respects on the ground.**

**Комментарий:**

O woman with slender waist! I know (*ākalayāmi*) your heart is pained because of finding fault in me (*asūyayā).* This is the state called *udvega* (distress). It is said:

*yasmin ramyam aramyaṁ vā na ca harṣāya jāyate \|*  
*pradveṣaḥ prāṇitavye’pi sa udvegaḥ smṛto yathā \|\|*

***Udvega*** **refers to the state in which both pleasant and unpleasant situations do not give rise to joy since she despises her lover.**

This state of distress is also inferred in Rādhā. Therefore he addresses her as slender (*tanvi),* since she has become thin because of mental pain.

I do not know where you have gone. I conciliate you with obeisances (*natena).* Or seeing the path you took, by following that (*tena*), I will conciliate you. The word *na* would indicate conjecture. The word *te* as genitive indicates relationship. This verse shows the state of *vilāpa* (lamentation). It is said:

*bambhramīti mano yasmin ratyautsukyād itas tataḥ \|*  
*vācaḥ priyāśritā eva sa pralāpaḥ smṛto yathā \|\|*

***Pralāpa (or vilāpa)*** **is described as speaking words in which the mind constantly wanders here and there because of eagerness for love.Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 2.12**

### Text 8

*dṛśyase purato gatāgatam eva me vidadhāsi \|*  
*kiṁ pureva sa-sambhramaṁ parirambhaṇaṁ na dadāsi \|\|8\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

drsyase - You are seen; purato - in front; gatagatam - coming and going; eva - only; me - of me. vidadhasi - You perform, kim - Why; pureva - as previously; sa-sambhramam - with respect; parirambhanam - an embrace; na - \[do\] not; dadasi - you give?

**Перевод:**

**I see you in front of me. You come and go. Why do you not respectfully embrace me as you did previously?**

**Комментарий:**

O beautiful Rādhā! Why do you not give embraces as previously *(pura iva*), with respect or with a pretense of fright *(sa-sambhramam)?* The word *sa-sambhramam* hints at the anubhāva called *bibboka.* On embracing the lover the woman makes a show of indifference. It is said:

*bibboko māna-garvābhyāṁ syād abhīṣṭe’py anādaraḥ \|\|*

**Disrespect towards the lover or articles given by the lover out of anger or pride is called** ***bibboka.*** **Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 11.52**

Here is an example:

*priyokti-lakṣeṇa vipakṣa-sannidhau*  
*svīkāritāṁ paśya śikhaṇḍa-maulinā \|*  
*śyāmātivāmā hṛdayaṅgamām api*  
*srajaṁ darāghrāya nirāsa helayā \|\|*

**Look! Śyāmā has accepted a garland offered by Kṛṣṇa who speaks to her with many words in the presence of her rivals. Contrary Śyāmā, though pleased with it,smells it slightly, and then throws it away indifferently. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 11.53**

Here is another example:

*sphuraty agre tiṣṭhan sakhi tava mukha-ksipta-nayanaḥ*  
*pratīkṣāṁ kṛtvāyaṁ bhavad-avasarasyāgha-damanaḥ \|*  
*dṛśoccair gāmbhīrya-grathita-guru-helā-gahanayā*  
*hasantīva kṣīve tvam iha vanamālāṁ racayasi \|\|54\|\|*

**Kṛṣṇa stands before you, gazing at your face,and waiting for an opportunity. O intoxicated girl! You seem to mock him with a glance like a garland of great neglect strung with flowers of inscrutability,and string a flower garland with your hands. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 11.54**

I see you before me. You come and go *(gatāgatam*). Persons experiencing separation see the beloved everywhere because of association in the heart. An example is given:

*prāsāde sā diśi diśi ca sā pṛṣṭhataḥ sā puraḥ sā*  
*paryaṅke sā pathi pathi ca sā tad-viyogāturasya \|*  
*haṁho cetaḥ prakṛtir aparā nāsti me kāpi sā sā*  
*sā sā sā sā jagati sakale ko'yam advaita-vādaḥ \|\|*

**The person in separation sees his beloved on the terrace, in all directions, behind, in front, on the couch, on the path. “Ah heart! There is no other woman for me in the whole universe. Why talk of all being the same?” Amaru-śataka 102**

### Text 9

*kṣamyatām aparaṁ kadāpi tavedṛśaṁ na karomi \|*  
*dehi sundari darśanaṁ mama manmathena dunomi \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ksamyatam - Forgive \[me\], aparam - Again; kadapi - ever; tava -against you; idrsarh - such \[an act\]; na - not; karomi -1 will commit. dehi - Give \[me\]; sundari - о beautiful woman (Radha); darsanam -the sight \[of you\]; mama - \[as a mercy\] to me. manmathena - From love; dunomi -I am suffering.

**Перевод:**

**Please forgive my offense. I will never again commit such an act. O beautiful Rādhā! Please reveal yourself as mercy to me. I am suffering from love.**

**Комментарий:**

O beautiful Rādhā pleasing to see (*sundari*)! Please forgive my offense. I will never again commit such offense (*aparam*). This indicates the state of *unmāda.* In Rasārṇava-sudhākara it is said *atasmiṁs tad iti bhrāntir unmādo virahodbhavaḥ*: *unmāda* arising from separation produces illusion so that an object appears differently from what it really is.

Then he recovers his senses a little. O beautiful Rādhā! Please let me see you. *Mama* indicates relationship in the genitive. Show yourself to me as mercy to me, though I am unqualified. By addressing as beautiful, he tries to encourage her. I suffer because of love.

### Text 10

*varṇitaṁ jayadevakena harer idaṁ praṇatena \|*  
*kindubilva-samudra-sambhava-rohiṇī-ramaṇena \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

varnitam - Was described; jayadevakena - by Jayadeva; harer - of Krsna; idam - this \[description\]; pranatena - surrendered \[to the lord\]; kindubilva - of Kindubilva village; samudra - from the ocean; sambhava - which arises; rohini-ramanena - \[like\] the moon.

**Перевод:**

**Offering respects to the Lord, Jayadeva, like the moon in the ocean of Kindubilva village, has written this description of Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

*Jayadevaka* means the same as Jayadeva. This story of Kṛṣṇa is related by Jayadeva who is surrendered to the Lord (*praṇatena*). Another version has *pravaṇena* (by Jayadeva who is dedicated to the Lord). The poet is like the moon (*rohinī-ramaṇena*) which arises from the ocean of Kindubilva, the ancestral town of Jayadeva. As the full moon increases the ocean, Jayadeva has increased the fame of his family. The word Kindubilva indicates Jayadeva’s ancestors. *Rohiṇī-ramaṇa* means the moon according to Hārāvalī. The phrase also indicates the excellence of Jayadeva’s wife Padmāvatī.

### Text 11

*hṛdi bisa-latā-hāro nāyaṁ bhujaṅgama-nāyakaḥ*  
*kuvalaya-dala-śreṇī kaṇṭhe na sā garala-dyutiḥ \|*  
*malayaja-rajo nedaṁ bhasma priyā-rahite mayi*  
*prahara na hara-bhrāntyā’naṅga krudhā kim u dhāvasi \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

hrdi - On \[my\] chest; bisa-lata - of water lilies (water-lily stems); haro - \[is\] a necklace, nayam - It \[is\] not; bhujahgama - of the snakes; nayakah - the king \[Vasuki\]. kuvalaya - Of the blue lotus; dala - of petals; sreni - a string; kanthe - \[is\] on \[my\] throat, na sa - It \[is\] not; garala - of poison; dyutih - the \[blue\] streak \[in Siva’s throat\]. malayaja - Of sandalwood; rajo - the dust \[is on my limbs\]. nedam- It \[is\] not; bhasma - the ashes \[smeared on Siva’s body\]. priya - From \[my\] love; rahite - separated; mayi - me; prahara -beat; na - \[do\] not; hara - for Siva; bhrantya - because of mistaking \[me\]; anahga - о Cupid; krudha - out of anger, kim и - Perhaps; dhavasi - you pursue \[me\].

**Перевод:**

**Do not beat me out of anger. Perhaps you pursue me out of anger, thinking I am Śiva. On my chest is a necklace of water lily stems, not the snake Vāsuki. On my throat is a string of blue lotuses. It is not Śiva’s throat, blue because of poison. I have sandalwood dust on my limbs, not Śiva’s ashes.**

**Комментарий:**

Cupid alone is the cause of my grief. I will scold him. With that intention Kṛṣṇa speaks. O Cupid! Do not beat me. Perhaps (k*im u*) you pursue me, out of anger, mistaking me for Śiva. Or O Cupid *(u anaṅga)!* Do you chase me because you think I am Śiva? I am without my lover (*priyā-rahite*). Śiva is always in the company of his beloved. Since I am without my lover it is not proper to mistake me for Śiva. And when I am with my beloved, even then you should not beat me. Śiva previously burned up Cupid’s body. He was without limbs (*anaṅga*). The word *anaṅga* is used to show the great enmity of Cupid towards Śiva.

The root of the mistaken identity is eradicated. This is a necklace of water lily stems on my chest, which I wear to relieve myself of the pain of separation. It is not the king of snakes Vāsuki. This is a string of blue lotus petals on my throat for cooling my body. It is not Śiva’s throat made blue because of swallowing poison. I wear sandalwood dust to lessen the heat in all my limbs. It is not Śiva’s ashes. The sandalwood powder that he smears on his limbs to cool them appears to be dust, because of the heat in his body caused by separation. You think my necklace of water lily stems is Vāsuki because it is long and cool. The blue lotuses appear to be Śiva’s blue throat because the color is similar. You mistake my sandalwood dust for ashes. It is not correct to think that I am Śiva.

This verse has the poetic ornament called *bhrāntimān.* Perceiving one object to be another is called *bhrāntimān. Kim* or *kim uta* indicates conjecture according to *Amara-koṣa. U* indicates anger according to *Viśva.*

### Text 12

*pāṇau mā kuru cūta-sāyakam amuṁ mā cāpam āropaya*  
*kṛīḍā-nirjita-viśva mūrcchita-janāghātena kiṁ pauruṣam \|*  
*tasyā eva mṛgī-dṛśo manasija preṇkhat-katākṣāśuga-*  
*śreṇī-jarjaritaṁ manāg api mano nādyāpi sandhukṣate \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

panau - In \[your\] hand; ma - \[do\] not; kuru - put; cuta - of mango buds; sayakam - the arrow, amum - That; ma - \[do\] not; capam - bow; aropaya - string, krida - By \[your\] play; nirjita-visva - by whom the universe is conquered; murcchita - who have fainted; janaghatena - because of killing people; kim - are \[you\]; paurusam - a human being? tasya - Of that woman; eva - only; mrgi-drso - of the doe-eyed woman (Radha); manasija - о Cupid (or о one who is born from the mind); prenkhat - wandering; kataksasuga - of arrows which are the glances; sreni - by masses; jarjaritam - torn \[to pieces\]; manag - at once; api - even; mano - \[my\] mind; nadyapi - no longer; sandhuksate - lives.

**Перевод:**

**O Cupid! Do not put the mango bud arrow in your hand. Do not string that arrow on your bow. O Cupid, conqueror of the universe by play! Are you humane in shooting at people who have fainted? My mind, cut to pieces by masses of arrows of Rādhā’s glances which wander about, can no longer live.**

**Комментарий:**

Cupid is famous for having mango bud arrows. Placing arrows of mango buds on his bow, he shoots. Worried about this, Kṛṣṇa becomes humble before Cupid.

O Cupid (*manasa-ja*)! Do not put that arrow made of the mango flower in your hand. Seeing the blossoming of spring flowers he speaks. Do not aim your bow. Or do not take up your mango flower arrow in your hand. But if you put in your hand, do not string it on your bow. The word *manasa-ja* also indicates “You should not cause be pain, since are born from my mind.”

O Cupid, who conquer the universe with your play! Are you humane (*kim pauruṣam*) if you beat persons like me who have fainted? *Api tu* means *kim api*. Or, are you a vile person (*kim pauruṣam*) in beating people who have fainted? The cause of his fainting is her doe-like eyes. Because of great separation he cannot even utter her name. My mind, cut to pieces by masses of arrows of Rādhā’s glances which wander about, cannot live. I faint because of the arrows of her glances. Now the injury from your mango flower arrows kills the person who is already dead. In this way, playfully you have conquered the universe. You have destroyed your good name among people who have fainted.

*Śāyaka* is an arrow according to *Vīśva. Dhanus* means a bow according to *Amara-koṣa. Āsuga* means an arrow. *Kim-pauraṣam* is a compound indicating “bad person.” This is according to the rule *kim kṣepe.* (Pāṇini 2.1.64)

### Text 13

*bhrū-pallavaṁ dhanur apāṅga-taraṇgitāni*  
*bāṇā guṇaḥ śravaṇa-pālir iti smareṇa \|*  
*tasyām anaṅga-jaya-jaṅgama-devatāyāṁ*  
*astrāṇi nirjita-jaganti kim arpitāni \|\|13\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

bhru - Of \[her\] brows; pallavam - the shoots; dhanur - \[are his\] bow; apanga-tarangitani - \[her\] shifty side-glances; band - \[are his\] arrows; gunah - \[is his\] bow-string; sravana - of \[her\] ears; pdlir -\[and\] the edge, iti - Thus; smarena - by the Cupid; tasyam - in her (Radha); ananga - to the Cupid; jaya - victory; jangama - for giving; devatayam - the devata; astrani - the weapons; nirjita-jaganti - by which the worlds are conquered; kim - somehow; arpitani - \[are\] established.

**Перевод:**

**The weapons for conquering the universe have perhaps been established by Cupid in Rādhā, who is the devatā meant for giving victory to Cupid. The shoots of her brows are his bow, her shifty side glances are his arrows and her ears are the bow string.**

**Комментарий:**

“You feel sorrow on remembering her glance. Why find fault with Cupid?” I speculate *(kim)* that Cupid has given these weapons to Rādhā. “But should not such weapons be given to someone’s hand by a warrior and not to others?” These weapons have conquered the whole universe: having conquered the three worlds by these weapons Cupid has established them in Rādhā. “Why did no one else get them?” She is the devatā who enables Cupid to be victorious. The weapons have been given to her to conquer the three worlds. For that purpose they have been established in her. It is also hinted that a weapon given by a devatā cannot be counteracted by anyone. What power of this devatā! Though he has no limbs, by his mercy the universe is conquered. Cupid, being conquered by her, rejects his lord, Kṛṣṇa, and takes shelter of Rādhā alone.

The weapons are described. She has bows in the form of her eye brows. The movements of her glances are the arrows. Her ears are the bow strings. Her brows are compared to young shoots since they are dark and glossy. Her glances are compared to arrows because they cannot be counterattacked. Her ears are compared to bow strings because they are long. *Apaṅga* means the edge of the eye according to *Viśva. Pāli* refers to the edge of the ear.

### Text 14

*bhrū-cāpe nihitaḥ kaṭākṣa-viśikho nirmātu marma-vyathāṁ*  
*śyāmātmā kuṭilaḥ karotu kavarī-bhāro’pi mārodyamam \|*  
*mohaṁ tāvad ayaṁ ca tanvi tanutāṁ bimba-dharo rāgavān*  
*sad-vṛttaḥ stana-maṇḍalas tava kathaṁ prāṇair mama krīḍati \|\|14\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

bhru - Of \[your\] brow; cape - on the bow; nihitah - strung; kataksa - of \[your\] glance; visikho - the arrow; nirmatu - let it inflict; marma - to \[my\] body; vyatham - pain, syamatma - Whose self is dark; kutilah - crooked; karotu - let it make; kavari-bharo - \[your\] bound-up hair; api - even; marodyamam - an attempt at \[my\] death, moham - The loss of \[my\] consciousness; tavad - indeed; ayam - this; ca - and; tanvi - о slender woman; tanutam - let it cause; bimbadharo -\[your\] lips \[like\] the bimba fruit; ragavan - red. sad-vrttah - Perfectly round; stana - of breasts; mandalas - the circle; tava - your; katham - why; pranair - with life; mama - my; kridati - play?

**Перевод:**

**Let the arrow of your glance strung on the bow of your brow inflict pain to my body. Let your dark, crooked bound-up hair attempt to kill me. Let your bimba-red lips, filled with hatred, cause me to lose consciousness. Why do your round breasts play with my life?**

**Комментарий:**

Now the beauty of Rādhā’s limbs gives me pain. I should scold her for this. With this intention Kṛṣṇa ignores Cupid and addresses Rādhā as if she were in front of him.

Let the arrow of your glance strung *(nihitaḥ*) on the bow of your eye brow cause pain to my limbs. Since that is the nature of your glance, let it be so. Let your bound up hair, black and crooked (*kuṭila*), try to kill (*māra*) me. Or let it try to incite passion (*māra*) in me. Someone who is crooked (*kuṭila),* with inner darkness *(śyāmātma*), naturally tries to kill others. O slender woman (*tanvi*)! Let your lips, red (*rāgavān*) like the bimba fruit, make me faint. Such a person is filled with hate (*rāgavān*) and makes others lose consciousness. Why do your breasts, perfectly round *(sad-vṛttaḥ*), play with my life airs, trying to take them away? It is not proper that one of good character (*sad-vṛttasya*) should take others’ lives. This is the ornament called *virodha* (contradiction). It is actually not proper that a good person takes others’ lives. Kāvya-prakāśa (10.24) says *virodhaḥ so ‘virodh ‘pi viruddhatvena yad vacaḥ:* when there is no contradiction but things are described as contradictory it is called *virodha.*

*Viśikhaḥ* means an arrow according to *Viśva. Śyāma* means something condemned according to *Dharaṇi. Kavarī* means bound up hair according to *Amara-koṣa. Māra* means death and Cupid according to *Viśva. Bimba* refers to a fruit. *Rāga* refers to hatred and being colored. *Vṛttam* means character and round according to *Śāśvata. Ātmā* means nature according to *Viśva.*

### Text 15

*tāni sparśa-sukhāni te ca taralāḥ snigdhā dṛśor vibhramās*  
*tad-vaktrāmbuja-saurabhaṁ sa ca sudhā-syandi girāṁ vakrimā \|*  
*sa bimbādhara-mādhurīti viṣayāsaṅge’pi cen mānasaṁ*  
*tasyāṁ lagna-samādhi hanta viraha-vyādhiḥ kathaṁ vardhate \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tani - these; sparsa - \[derived from\] touching; sukhani - these happinesses; te - your \[limbs\]; ca - and; taralah - playful; snigdha - affectionate; drsor - of \[your\] eyes; vibhramas - movements; tad-vaktrambuja - of \[your\] lotus-face; saurabham - the fragrance; sa -the; ca - and; sudha - of nectar; syandi - the flow; giram - of \[your\] words; vakrima - crooked; sa - the; bimbadhara - of \[your\] bimba-lips; madhuri - the sweet taste; iti - thus; visayasange - with the senses adhering to their objects; api - even; cen - if; manasam - \[my\] mind; tasyarh - in her (Radha); lagna-samadhi - \[is one\] whose absorption is intense; hanta - alas; viraha - of separation; vyadhih -the pain; katham - how; vardhate - does increase.

**Перевод:**

**I experience the happiness of touching your limbs, seeing the affection movements of the pupils of your eyes, smelling the fragrance of your lotus face, hearing the flow of nectar of your crooked words and tasting the sweetness of your bimba lips. If I experience in this way with my senses while absorbing my mind in you, how can the pain of separation increase?**

**Комментарий:**

“This condition is not Rādhā’s fault and is not Cupid’s fault. Suffering arises because of the temporary nature of that experience. You should think of Rādhā whom you previously experienced with your senses.”

The happiness of touching her limbs, which I previously experienced, I am experiencing now. This is experienced through the sense of touch. I experienced the flickering pupils of your eyes filled with affection and playfulness. This is experienced through sight. I experienced the fragrance of your lotus mouth. This is attachment arising from the senses of smell. I experienced the crooked flow of nectar of your words, by hearing. Your lips sweet as bimba fruit which I previously experienced I experience now. This is experienced through the sense of taste. If my mind concentrates completely on Rādhā, even (*api*) with each sense contacting its object (*viṣayāsaṅge*), oh, why does the pain arising from separation still increase?

The word *api* indicates the ornament of *virodha* since it is impossible during *samādhi* to have the external senses contact the sense objects. *Samādhi* means complete concentration of the mind while stopping function of the external senses.

Separation will occur only when there is non-attainment of the desired objects of the respective senses. Because my senses are not separated from their objects and my mind thinks of her constantly, there should be no separation. How can pain arising from separation then occur? It is hinted that in *samādhi* also he experiences pain. The objects are form, sound, smell, taste and touch according to *Amara-koṣa.* The conclusion of this verse is that Kṛṣṇa experiences the most astonishing transformations in *prema*.

### Text 16

*tiryak-kaṇṭha-vilola-mauli-taralottaṁsasya vamśoccarad-*  
*gīti-sthāna-kṛtāvadhāna-lalanā-lakṣair na saṁlakṣitāḥ \|*  
*sammugdhe madhusūdanasya madhure rādhā-mukhendau sudhā-*  
*sāre kandalitāś ciraṁ dadatu vaḥ kṣemaṁ kaṭākṣormayaḥ \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tiryak - \[Is\] bent; kantha - whose neck; vilola - \[is\] swaying; mauli - whose crown; taralottamsasya - \[and\] whose earrings \[are\] quivering; vamsoccarad - playing on \[his\] flute; giti - of a song; sthana - to the notes; krtavadhana - because \[his\] attention \[is\] directed; lalana - of beautiful women \[gopis\]; laksair - by the groups; na - not; samlaksitah - noticing; sammugdhe - tender (or beautiful); madhusudanasya - of Madhusudana (Krsna); madhure -\[and\] charming; radha. - of Radha; mukhendau - on the moon-face; sudha - of nectar; sare - the essence; kandalitas - increased; ciram - for a long time; dadatu - may it bestow; vah - \[upon\] you; ksemam - auspiciousness; kataksormayah - the waves of \[his\] side-glances.

**Перевод:**

**May the side glance of Kṛṣṇa, which increases on seeing the tender, charming moon face of Rādhā, the shelter of sweetness, the essence of nectar, and which does not notice other gopīs as he attentively plays melodies on his flute, with bent neck, swaying crown and quivering earrings, bestow auspiciousness on you forever.**

**Комментарий:**

This is a blessing at the end of this section. May the waves of Kṛṣṇa’s glances from the edge of his eyes bring you auspiciousness (*kṣemam*). The word Madhusūdana (killer of the demon Madhu) is used to indicate that Kṛṣṇa can give auspiciousness by destroying the sinful. Or it can indicate his fickle nature (he is like a bee). These waves of glances are described. They shoot up like waves in the presence of Rādhā’s moon-like face, the shelter of sweetness *(madhure*), indistinctly quivering (*sammugdhe*), just as the waves of the ocean increase on seeing the moon. There is another version:

*premṇā kandalitāḥ sumugdha-madhure rādhānanendau sudhā-*  
*sāre vo madhusūdanasya dadatu kṣemaṁ kaṭākṣormayaḥ \|\|*

In this case *sumugdha-madhure* means Rādhā’s face, which is charming (*madhure*) and very tender. That face is the essence of nectar (*sudhā-sāre*). “Since he sees other gopīs, why do his glances act like waves only for the face of Rādhā?” When he attentively (focusing his mind - *kṛtvāvadhāna*) plays arrangements of notes on his flute, those glances do not look at the group of women. All the women, enchanted by the sound of the flute, do not see that Kṛṣṇa glances only at Rādhā. His appearance in playing the flute is described. His neck is bent, his crown sways and his earrings quiver. This is a *karma-dhāraya* compound. *Gīti* means song according to *Amara-koṣa. Uttaṁsa* means earrings according to *Dharaṇi. Kandala* means new sprouts. *Ūrmi* means wave according to *Viśva.*

## Part 4: Tender Kṛṣṇa

### Text 1

*caturthaḥ sargaḥ*  
*snigdha-madhusūdanaḥ*  
*yamunā-tīra-vānīra-nikuñje mandam āsthitam \|*  
*prāha prema-bharodbhrāntaṁ mādhavaṁ rādhikā-sakhī \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

yamuna - Of the Yamuna; tira - on the bank; vanira - of reeds; nikunje - in a bower; mandam - motionless; asthitam - sitting; praha - spoke; prema - of \[his\] affection; bhara - by the weight; affection; udbhrantam - overcome; madhavam - to Madhava \[Krsna\]; radhika - of Radha; sakhi - a female friend.

**Перевод:**

**A sakhī of Rādhā spoke to Kṛṣṇa, overcome by his strong affection, as he sat motionless in a reed bower on the bank of the Yamunā.**

**Комментарий:**

In the previous section Kṛṣṇa’s condition in separation was described. Now the *sakhī* describes Rādhā’s condition to Kṛṣṇa. The suffix *ka* in Rādhikā suggests pity. The *sakhī* of Rādhā spoke directly (*prāha)* to Kṛṣṇa. He was confused by his strong affection (*prema bhara*) for Rādhā. Thus he could not move. He was sitting in a reed bower on the bank of the Yamunā in a state of torpor (*mandam)*, having given up searching for her. Or the word can be *amandam.* The *sakhī* spoke quickly (*amandam*), seeing that Kṛṣṇa was so disturbed. *Manda* means slow according to *Viśva. Sa premā bheda-rahitaṁ yūnor yad bhāva-bandhanam:* *prema* means the bond of love between two young people without interruption. (Rasārṇava-sudhākara 2.258)

## Song 8: Radha’s separation &amp; preparing for Krishna

**prabandhaḥ 8 \|\|**  
**Karṇāta-rāga, ekatālī-tāla**

### Text 2

*nindati candanam indu-kiraṇaṁ*  
*anuvindati khedam adhīram \|*  
*vyāla-nilaya-milanena garalaṁ*  
*iva kalayati malaya-samīram \|\|2\|\|*

*sā virahe tava dīnā \|\|*  
*mādhava manasija-viśikha-bhayād*  
*iva bhāvanayā tvayi līnā \|\|*  
*\|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

nindati - \[She\] condemns; candanam - sandalwood; indu - of the moon; kiranam - \[from\] the ray; anuvindati - \[she\] obtains; khedam - pain; adhiram - intense; vydla - of snakes; nilaya - with the abode; milanena - by \[its\] mixture; garalam - poison; iva - as if; kalayati -\[she\] considers; malaya - from the Malaya mountain; samlram - the breeze.

sa - She; virahe - in separation; tava - from you; dina - miserable; madhava - о Madhava \[Krsna\]; manasija - of the Cupid; visikha - of the arrows; bhayad - in fear; iva - as if; bhavanaya - by meditation; tvayi - into you; lina - \[she has\] merged.

**Перевод:**

**She condemns sandalwood and feels intense pain from the rays of the moon. She takes the breeze to be poisonous, since it has been mixed with the poison of snakes in the sandalwood trees of Malaya mountain.**  
**O Mādhava! In misery of separation she merges into you by meditation, as if in fear of Cupid’s arrows. (chorus)**

**Commentary:** The qualities of *karṇāta-rāga* are as follows:

*kṛpāṇa-pāṇir gaja-danta-patraṁ*  
*ekaṁ vahan dakṣiṇa-karṇa-pūre \|*  
*saṁstūyamānaḥ sura-cāraṇaughaiḥ*  
*karṇāta-rāgaḥ śithikaṇṭha-nīlaḥ \|\|*

**Karṇāta-rāga,\[\[17\]\]praised by devatās and Cāraṇas, is blue like a peacock’s throat, with a sword in his hand and carrying an ivory ornament in his right ear ornament**

*Ekatalī-tāla* was previously described. (6.1) The heroine has longing in separation.

*naivāgataḥ samuciteṣv api vāsareṣu*  
*prāṇeśvaraḥ sva-guru-kārya-vaśena yasyā \|*  
*durdhāra-vṛtta-viraha-jvara-tāpitāṅgīm*  
*utkaṇṭhitāṁ vadati tāṁ bharataḥ kavīndraḥ \|\|*

**Bharata describes the woman in longing as one whose limbs are pained by an intolerable fever of separation, whose lover has not come on the appointed days because of obligations to his superiors.**

O Mādhava! She suffers in separation from you. The word Mādhava (husband of Lakṣmī) suggests that he does not know suffering since he has other women*. Hā* indicates misfortune. If she is suffering, how does she pass her time? She merges into you, out of fear of Cupid’s arrows. Since Cupid arises from your mind, when she merges in you, she no longer has fear.

“How does she merge in me?” She remembers *(bhāvanayā)* you while criticizing all objects. She criticizes sandalwood. It heats up people in separation. It is useless to be known for its cooling effects. She feels excessive (*adhīram*) pain on seeing the rays of the moon. *Dhīra* means “a little” according to *Viśva.* Or she criticizes with unsteadiness. Or *adhīram* (*adhika* + *īrayati*= agitates excessively) can modify *candanam* with the meaning “inspiring the arrows of Cupid.”

She thinks of the wind from Malaya Mountain as poison because of its contact with the abode of the snakes--the sandalwood trees growing there. The Malaya winds, after having been consumed and vomited by the snakes in the trees, blows here. The wind moves from place to place full of poison. The wind is like poison because it makes everyone faint when it contacts their bodies. Causes of happiness for others become causes of sorrow for persons suffering in separation. She has attained a state of remembering (*smaraṇa)* due to longing in separation. It is said:

*dveṣo yatrānya-kāryeṣu tad-ekāgraṁ ca mānasam \|*  
*śvāsair manorathaiś cāpi ceṣṭās tāḥ smaraṇaṁ yathā \|\|*

***Smaraṇa*** **is that condition in which the mind and activities are concentrated on one’s aspirations and desires, with hatred for other activities.**

*induṁ nindati padma-kandala-dalais talpaṁ na vā manyate*  
*karpūraṁ kirati prayāti na ratiṁ prāleya-dhārā-gṛhe \|*  
*śvāsaiḥ kevalam eva khedita-tanur dhyāyaty asau bālikā*  
*yat tat ko’pi yuvā dhruvaṁ smara-suhṛc cetasy amuṣyāḥ sthitaḥ \|\|*

**That girl criticizes the moon and had no regard for a bed made of lotus buds and petals. She throws camphor away. She does not have attraction for a house with showers of hail. With pained body, while sighing she meditates. Some youth, a lover, is situated in her heart. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 2.9**

Vyāla means a snake according to Viśva. Garalam means poison.

### Text 3

*avirala-nipatita-madana-śarād iva bhavad-avanāya vīśālam \|*  
*sva-hṛdaya-marmaṇi varma karoti sajala-nalinī-dala-jālam \|\|3\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

avirala - constantly; nipatita - falling \[upon her\]; madana - of Cupid; sarad - from the arrow; iva - as if; bhavad - of you; avanaya - for the protection; visalam - extensive; sva-hrdaya - of her heart; marmani - in the sensitive area; varma - \[into\] armor; karoti - \[she\] makes; sajala - with water; nalini - of the blue lotus; data - of the petals; jalam - a cover.

**Перевод:**

**She makes extensive armor of blue lotus petals and water over the sensitive area of her heart to protect you from Cupid’s arrows which constantly fall upon her.**

**Commentary:** She makes extensive armor of blue lotus petals and water over the sensitive area of her heart, in order to protect you from Cupid’s arrows which fall upon her constantly. You always reside in her heart. She thinks, “He should not be afflicted by Cupid’s arrows.”

Another meaning could be: she makes armor in order to protect herself like you from the arrows of Cupid. *Avana* means protection according to *Viśva-prakāśa. Marma* means a place in the body which cannot tolerate pain. *Viśālam* means broad. The first meaning indicates her strong affection for him. This shows the sakhī’s skill as a messenger.

### Text 4

*kusuma-viśikha-śara-talpam analpa-vilāsa-kalā-kamaṇīyam \|*  
*vratam iva tava parirambha-sukhāya karoti kusuma-śayanīyam \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kusuma - of flowers; visikha - of the \[Cupid’s\] arrows; sara - \[made\] of the shafts; talpam - a bed; analpa - unlimited; vilasa - of play; kala - for the art; kamaniyam - attractive; vratam - a vow; iva - as if; tava - your; parirambha - of \[your\] embrace; sukhaya - for the purpose of the happiness; karoti - \[she\] makes; kusuma - of flowers; sayamyam - a bed.

**Перевод:**

**She makes a bed of flowers attractive for the art of unlimited play, which is like a vow, a bed made of Cupid’s flower arrows, in order that you give her the happiness of your embrace.**

**Commentary:** She makes a bed of flowers. This is described. It is most attractive for many arts of playing. In her condition, she remains with eyes on the path on which you tread while making the flower bed with intense love, with expectation that you will come. This bed, a the resting place for the arrows of Cupid in the form of the flowers, is like a vow to attain the happiness of your embrace. *Talpa* means a bed according to *Viśva.* These three lines of the verse indicate *udvega* (disturbance), the fifth state. It is hinted that flowers which are pleasant to touch have become like arrows. Or, she makes a flower bed pleasant for many arts of play, like a vow in the form of lying on a bed of Cupid’s flower arrows to gain the happiness of your embrace.

### Text 5

*vahati ca valita-vilocana-jaladharam ānana-kamalam udāram \|*  
*vidhum iva vikaṭa-vidhuntuda-danta-dalana-galitāmṛta-dhāram \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vahati - \[She\] carries; ca - and; valita - spreading \[sideways\] glances; vilocana - \[are\] eyes; jaladharam - whose clouds; anana-kamalam - a lotus-face; udaram - beautiful; vidhum - the moon; iva - as if; vikata - terrible; vidhuntuda - of Rahu; danta - of the teeth; dalana - because of the affliction; galita - \[are\] trickling; amrta-dharam -whose streams of nectar.

**Перевод:**

**She has a beautiful lotus face, a face like the moon trickling with tears of nectar because of affliction by terrible Rāhu’s teeth, with clouds for eyes spreading sideways glances.**

**Commentary:** She carries her beautiful (*udāram)* lotus face. Or *udāram* can refer to generosity in giving tears of sweet prema. The face is described. It has two eyes like clouds which shower rasa, spreading sideways (*valita)* in order to gaze at your path. Her eyes are compared to clouds since they constantly shower tears. That face is like the moon (*vidhum).* The face flows with trickling (*galita*) nectar streams (*dhāram*) because of affliction from the huge or terrible *(vikaṭa*) teeth of Rāhu *(vidhuntuda*). Her face is compared to the moon and her tears are compared to showers of nectar. This is *vākyārtha upamā.****\[\[18\]****\]* Change of complexion is also hinted. *Vikaṭa* means huge according to *Viśva. Vidhuntuda* means Rāhu according to *Amara-koṣa.*

### Text 6

*dhyāna-layena puraḥ parikalpya bhavantam atīva durāpam \|*  
*vilapati hasati viṣīdati roditi cañcati muñcati tāpam \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

dhyana - In meditation \[on you\]; layena - by absorption; purah - in front \[of herself\]; parikalpya - imagining; bhavantam - you; ativa - exceedingly; durapam - hard to attain; vilapati - \[she\] laments; hasati - mocks; visidati - becomes depressed; roditi - weeps; cancati - moves about; muncati - \[and\] becomes released; tapam - \[from\] pain.

**Перевод:**

**In deep meditation, she brings your form, hard to attain, in front of her. She laments, mocks, becomes depressed, weeps, moves about and becomes released from pain.**

**Commentary:** By complete concentration on you, who are difficult to attain, she makes you appear in front of her, after having drawn a picture of you. Or by complete absorption in you she attempts to make you come to her directly since you are hard to attain by other means such as messengers. She laments. *Vilpah duḥkhajam vacaḥ*: lamentation consists of speaking out of grief. (Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 11.83) She mocks him. “This deceiver takes so much time. You had better go there.” She becomes depressed: he came before me, grasped my feet and pacified me. She cries. “How can he again be brought under control?” *Ruditam uditam astram yoṣitām vigraheṣu:* women use crying as an weapon in quarrels of love. (*Māgha-kāvya*) Seeing him follow while uttering conciliatory words, she goes away. Then being pacified, embraced by him, she becomes free of suffering, giving up the pain of separation. Or, she cries and then scolds *(muñcati*) her conciliating lover out of affectionate anger. Then she attains *(cañcati*) suffering (*tāpam).*

The ornament in this verse i*s dīpaka.* The state is *unmāda.* It is said:

*śvāsa-prarodanotkampa-vasudhollekhanair api \|*  
*vyāpāro jāyate yatra sa unmādaḥ smṛto yathā \|\|*

***Unmāda*** **refers to the condition in which a person acts with heavy breathing, wailing, and trembling, while scratching the earth. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 2.12**

The *bhāva* is *kilakiñcitā.* This is described in Daśa-rūpaka as *krodhāśru-harṣa-bhītyādeḥ saṅkaraḥ kilakiñcitam: kilakiñcita* refers to a mixture of anger, tears, joy and fear. *Dīpaka* is defined as *saiva kriyāsu bahvīṣu karakasyeti dīpakam: dīpaka* occurs when many verbs are connected with one noun. (Kāvya-prakāśa 10.103)

### Text 7

*vilikhati rahasi kuraṅga-madena bhavantam asama-śara-bhūtam \|*  
*praṇamati makaram adho vinidhāya kare ca śaraṁ nava-cūtam \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vilikhati - \[She\] draws; rahasi - in a solitary place; kuranga - of deer; madena - with the musk; bhavantam - you; asama - of Cupid; sara-bhutam - in the form; pranamati - \[and she\] offers respects; makaram - a makara; adho - below; vinidhaya - placing; kare - in \[his\] hand; ca - and; saram - an arrow; nava-cutam - whose mango buds \[are\] fresh.

**Перевод:**

**She draws your picture in the form of Cupid with arrows of mango buds in his hand, using musk. She draws the form of a** ***makara*** **as the vehicle, and then offers respects to it.**

**Commentary:** She paints a picture of you as the form of Cupid (*asama-cāra-bhūtam*) using musk in a solitary place. After drawing a *makara* below as his seat, she offers respects. The meaning of the *makara* as Cupid’s vehicle is that she requests to you to pacify her. Drawing the *makara* also indicates “Mounting his vehicle, my lover will come quickly.” This is the action of a person in separation. Bharata says:

*devatā-pūjanaṁ kuryād dadyād balibhuje balim \|*  
*likhet kānta-pratikṛtiṁ pāṭhayec chuka-śārikām \|*  
*gaṇayec cāvadhir dinaṁ gītaṁ gāyet tad-aṅgakām \|*  
*evaṁ-vidha-vinodena nayet kālaṁ viyoginī \|\|*

**The woman in separation worships the devatās, makes offerings to crows, draws images of her lover, teaches the parrot and śārika to speak, counts the days and sings songs, and in this way brings the limbs of the lover before her.**

*Mṛga-mada* or *kuraṅga-mada* means musk according to *Hārāvalī.* Even though drawing the image of Cupid should not occur for the woman in separation, Rādhā, drew Kṛṣṇa with Cupid’s form (since he is actually the svarūpa of Cupid) because her heart overcome with desire of Kṛṣṇa.

### Text 8

*prati-padam idam api nigadati mādhava tava caraṇe patitāham \|*  
*tvayi vimukhe mayi sapadi sudhā-nidhir api tanute tanu-dāham \|\|8\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

prati-padam - With every step; idam - this; api - even; nigadati -\[she\] speaks, madhava - О Madhava (Krsna); tava - your; carane - at \[your\] feet; patitaham -I have fallen, tvayi - You; vimukhe - \[when you are\] separated \[from me\]; mayi - to me; sapadi - at once; sudha-nidhir - the ocean of nectar (the moon); api - even; tanute - gives; tanu - in \[my\] body; daham - pain.

**Перевод:**

**With every step she speaks. “O Mādhava! I fall at your feet. In separation from you, even the moon gives pain to me.”**

**Commentary:** She utters words at each step as she moves here and there. “O Mādhava! I have fallen at your feet. I offer you respects.” When separated from you, the ocean of nectar (the moon) produces pain in my body (*tanu-dāham*).” Or the sentence can mean “The moon gives pain of love (*atanu-dāham*). Or the moon gives pain to a great degree (*atanu*). Or the moon gives pain so that my body disappears. You are the husband of Lakṣmī. (The moon is her brother, since they both arose from the ocean). If you are close to me, Lakṣmī, even as competitor, can do nothing to me. If you are unfavorable, remembering me as the competitor of your sister, the moon will give pain to my body. This is the suggestion of the word Mādhava. That the moon gives pain is contrary to its character. This is the ornament called *virodha****.***

### Text 9

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇitam idam adhikaṁ yadi manasā naṭaṇīyam \|*  
*hari-virahākula-ballava-yuvati-sakhī-vacanaṁ paṭhanīyam \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva - by Sri Jayadeva; bhanitam - spoken; idam - this \[composition\]; adhikam - excellent;yadi - if; manasa - by the mind; nataniyam - is enacted; hari - from Krsna; viraha - in separation; akula - suffering; ballava-yuvati - of the young cowherdess (Radha); sakhi - of the female friend; vacanam - \[and\] the words; pathaniyam - should be carefully read \[Prabodhananda explains: then it will bestow a similar desire for Krsna\]

**Перевод:**

**If the composition of Jayadeva becomes enacted and relished by the mind, it will bestow a similar desire for Kṛṣṇa. Therefore carefully read the words of the sakhī sent by Rādhā who is suffering in separation concerning her happiness.**

**Commentary:** If this composition of Jayadeva becomes attractive to the mind, enacted in it, it will produce (*dam*) similar desire *(īh)* for Kṛṣṇa. Therefore the present words of the sakhī sent by Rādhā, suffering in separation from him, concerning her happiness (*adhikam*), should be read.

### Text 10

*āvāso vipināyate priya-sakhī-mālāpi jālāyate*  
*tāpo’pi śvasitena dāva-dahana-jvāla-kalāpāyate \|*  
*sāpi tvad-viraheṇa hanta hariṇī-rūpāyate hā kathaṁ*  
*kandarpo’pi yamāyate viracayan śārdūla-vikrīḍitam \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

avaso - \[Her\] house; vipinayate - resembles a forest; priya-sakhi - of \[her\] dear friends; malapi - even the set; jalayate - resembles a net; tdpo’pi - even \[her\] pain; svasitena - because of her breathing; dava - forest-fire; dahana - of a burning; jvala - of flames; kalapayate -resembles the collection; sapi - even she; tvad - from you; virahena - because of separation; hanta - alas; harini - a doe; rupayate -resembles, hd - Alas; katham - how; kandarpo - the Cupid; api -even; yamayate - does resemble Yama \[the god of death); viracayan - producing; sardula-vikriditam - the sport of a tiger \[this is also the name of the verse’s meter\]?

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā has become pale as a doe. She takes her house to be the forest and her friends are like a net. Her pain is like forest fire fanned by the wind of her breathing. What misery! Cupid, becoming a tiger, brings about her death.**

**Commentary:** In separation from you, she has become like a doe (*hariṇī rūpāyate).* Her complexion has become pale like a doe due to sickness arising from separation. And the doe, confronted with a forest fire, becomes agitated, and in great fear, seeing a hunter’s net spread out in front, glances there and there with flickering eyes. In this way Rādhā sometimes takes her house to be a forest *(āvāso vipināyate*). Her group of sakhīs becomes the net *(jālāyate*). When she desires to give up her life, unable to tolerate her grief in separation, they prevent her. Or the garland given by her sakhī to appease her pain is condemned as a net to bind up a deer.

The pain of separation, by breathing, like the assistance of wind, seems to become a forest fire. The sakhī expresses her anguish. O misfortune (*hanta*)! How can she exist since Cupid seems to play like a tiger to bring about her death (*yamāyate*)? The word *hariṇī* (doe) also indicates that she has great affection for Kṛṣṇa, just as an animal has affection for its master.

*Śārdūla-vikrīḍitam* also indicates the meter. The first part of the verse shows *udvega* and the second half shows the eighth condition called *vyādhi* (sickness).

*abhīṣṭa-saṅgamābhāvād vyādhiḥ santāpa-lakṣaṇaḥ \|*  
*atra santāpa-niḥśvāsau śīta-vastu-niṣevaṇam \|\|*  
*nottaraṁ bhāṣate praśne nekṣate na śṛṇoti ca \|\|*

***Vyādhi*** **is a symptom of pain arising from separation from the beloved. She experiences pain, breathes heavily, and uses cool objects. She does not answer questions, does not see and does not hear. Rasārṇava-sudhākara 2.398, 401**

## Song 9: A sakhi describes Radha’s pain of separation

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 9 \|\|**  
**Deśākhya-rāga, ekatālī tāla**

### Text 11

*stana-capi hāram udāram \|*  
*sā manute kṛśā-tanur iva bhāram \|\|11\|\|*

*rādhikā tava virahe keśava \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

stana - \[her\] breast; vinihitam - upon; api - even; haram - the necklace; udaram - great; sa - she (Radha); manute - considers; krsa - \[is\] wasting away; tanur - whose body; iva - as if; bharam - a burden, radhika - Radha; tava - from you; virahe - in separation; kesava - о Kesava (Krsna)!

**Перевод:**

**O Keśava! In separation from you (chorus), Rādhā considers the necklace on her breast to be very heavy since her body is wasting away.**

**Commentary:** *Deśākhya-rāga* is described as follows:

āsphoṭanāviṣkṛta-romaharṣo  
niyuddha-sannaddha-viśāla-bāhuḥ \|  
prāṁśuḥ pracaṇḍa-dyutir indu-gauro  
deśākhya-rāgaḥ kila malla-mūrtiḥ \|\|

*Deśya-rāga\[\[19\]\]is wrestler with tall figure, with huge arms raised for battle, hair standing on end, slapping his arms together, effulgent with a complexion like the golden moon.*

*Ekatālī* was described previously. (6.1)

O Keśava! In separation from you, Rādhā considers the attractive (*udāram*) necklace worn on her breast since it had touched your limbs during embraces, or which was given from his neck, to be very heavy. Or she considers the necklace on her breast to be very (*udāram*) heavy. This is because her body has become very thin. You should show affection to her since she cannot even carry on her chest the necklace given by the sakhīs to protect her life. The thin body indicates *vyādhi* (sickness). It is also an anubhāva.

Keśava mean “the controller (*iśa*) of happiness (*ka)* possessing nectar (*vam*) to revive a dead person.” Because of you, having this nature, my dear friend suffers. That is not proper. That is the significance of the word Keśava.

### Text 12

*sa-rasa-masṛṇam api malayaja-paṅkam \|*  
*paśyati viṣam iva vapuṣi sa-śaṅkam \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sa-rasa - \[And\] liquid; masrnam - \[it is\] soft; api - even though; malayaja - of sandalwood; pankam - the pulp; pasyati - she \[Radha\] sees; visam - poison; iva - as if; vapusi - on \[her\] body; sa-sankam -with anxiety.

**Перевод:**

**In separation from you, with anxiety she sees sandalwood to be poison on her body, though it is soft and liquid.**

**Commentary:** She sees sandalwood on her body with anxiety to be like poison even though it is very soft (*sa-rasa-masṛṇam). Masṛṇa* means soft and liquid according to *Viśva. Sarasa* can also mean “giving rise to rasa.”

### Text 13

*śvasita-pavanam anupama-pariṇāham \|*  
*madana-dahanam iva vahati sa-dāham \|\|13\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

svasita - \[is\] exhaled \[heavily\]; pavanam - by whom breath; anupama - \[is\] incomparable; parinaham - \[and\] whose extent; madana - of Cupid; dahanam - the fire; iva - as if; vahati - \[she\] breathes; sa-daham - painful.

**Перевод:**

**In separation from you, she breathes heavily, incomparably slow and painful, as if she were the fire of Cupid.**

She exhales wind for her breathing. This is described. It is incomparably drawn out (*parināham*). It is like the fire of passion. It is painful. Or she breathes with pain (*sa-dāham*).

### Text 14

*diśi diśi kirati sajala-kaṇa-jālam \|*  
*nayana-nalinam iva vigalita-nālam \|\|14\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

disi disi - In all directions; kirati - \[she\] casts; sajala - \[is\] watery; kana - of tears; jalam - whose multitude; nayana-nalinam - \[her\] lotus-eyes; iva - as if; vigalita - \[are\] gone; nalam - \[lotuses\] whose stems.

**Перевод:**

**In separation from you, with tearful eyes she throws glances in all directions with her lotus eyes, as if they were lotuses without stems.**

**Commentary:** She casts her lotus eyes in all directions. The eyes are described. They are filled with profuse *(jālam)* drops of tears. Those eyes are like lotuses without stems (*vigalita-nālam)*. The lotus sprinkled with water drops, devoid of a stem, is compared to her constant glance outwards from the place of tears. It is also suggested that she sprinkles the path by which you will approach with her lotus eyes, out of affection.

### Text 15

*nayana-viṣayam api kiśalaya-talpam \|*  
*kalayati vihita-hutāśa-vikalpam \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

nayana - To \[her\] eyes; visayam - \[it is\] visible; api - even though; kisalaya - of new shoots; talpam - the bed; kalayati - \[she\] experiences; vihita-hutasa - \[that it is\] covered with fire; vikalpam - the doubt.

**Перевод:**

**In separation from you, Rādhā considers the bed of new shoots to be covered with fire, though it is visible to her eyes.**

**Commentary:** In doubt (*vikalpam*) she considers the bed of new shoots to be made of fire, though it is very attractive (*nayana-viṣayam*) or though it is perceivable by the eye. It is visible to the eye, what to speak of touching it. It is also suggested that “My friend has given up sleeping.” This verse describes the condition called *udvega.*

### Text 16

*tyajati na pāṇi-talena kapolam \|*  
*bāla-śaśinam iva sāyam alolam \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tyajati - \[She\] does leave; na - not; pani - of \[her\] hand; talena - with the surface; kapolam - \[her\] cheek; bala - young \[on the second day\]; sasinam - the moon; iva - as if; sayam - in the evening; alolam - stationary.

**Перевод:**

**In separation from you, in the evening she keeps her palm on her stationary cheek, which is like moon on the second day.**

**Commentary:** In the evening she does not give up placing her palm on her cheek. Her cheek is described. The cheek is stationary (*alolam*). In the evening she thinks, with her palm on her cheek, “In the day he can be brought, but at night how can I be brought to meet him.”

The cheek is further described. It is like the moon on the second day after the new moon. Her hand covers half her face and half is visible. Because of separation the color of her face is pale. Thus it appears to be a thin and motionless moon, rising in the evenig. This verse expresses the condition called *jadātā:* being stunned. It is said:

*yatra dhyāyati niḥsaṁjñaṁ jaḍatā sā prakīrtitā \|*  
*atra sparśānabhijñatvaṁ vaivarṇyaṁ śithilāṅgatā \|*  
*akāṇḍa-huṅkṛtiḥ stambho niḥśvāsa-kṛśatādayaḥ \|\|*

***Jaḍatā*** **refers to the condition in which a person meditates but seems unconscious. The complexion changes, the limbs become relaxed, and the person is unaware of the sense of touch. The person sighs and utters sounds of disgust. The person is stunned and becomes thin. Rasārṇava-sudhākara 2.402**

### Text 17

*harir iti harir iti japati sa-kāmam \|*  
*viraha-vihita-maraṇeva nikāmam \|\|17\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

harir iti - “O attractor of my mind (or О Hari)!” harir iti - “O Attractor of my mind (or О Hari)!” japati - she utters; sa-kamam -full of desire; viraha - by separation \[from you\]; vihita - is arranged; maraneva - as if one whose death; nikamam - \[and\] intently.

**Перевод:**

**In separation from you, she utters “O attractor of my mind! Attractor of my mind!” intently and full of desire, as if she is dying from separation.**

### Text 18

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇitam iti gītam \|*  
*sukhayatu keśava-padam upanītam \|\|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva - By Sri Jayadeva; bhanitam - spoken; iti - thus; gitam - the song; sukhayatu - may \[the song\] give happiness; kesava - of Kesava \[Krsna\]; padam - to the feet; upanitam - \[and\] brought \[by the sakhi\].

**Перевод:**

**May the song brought by the sakhī recorded by Jayadeva give happiness at the feet of Kṛṣṇa.**

**Commentary:** May the song of Rādhā’s sakhī, recorded by Jayadeva, and brought to Kṛṣṇa, give happiness to the feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, so that he will quickly go and meet her! Or, may the song which the sakhī related to Kṛṣṇa, put to *rāga* and *tāla* by Jayadeva, give happiness to the singers and hearers. Or may the song offered to the spiritual abode (*padam*) of Keśava, Vaikuṇṭha-loka, give happiness to all. This means the song is sung even in Vaikuṇṭha. Or may the song which has arrived give happiness to the devotee who is protected by Keśava (*keśava-padam*)! Or may the song give happiness to Kṛṣṇa’s place. *Padam* means place or protection, according to *Viśva.*

### Text 19

*sā romāñcati śītkaroti vilapaty utkampate tāmyati*  
*dhyāyaty udbhramati pramīlati pataty udyāti mūrcchaty api \|*  
*etāvaty atanu-jvare vara-tanur jīven na kiṁ te rasāt*  
*svar-vaidya-pratimā prasīdasi yadi tyakto’nyathā hastakaḥ \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sa - She; romancati - \[her\] hair stands on end; sitkaroti - \[she\] hisses; vilapaty - \[she\] laments; utkampate - \[she\] trembles; tamyati - \[she\] becomes fatigued; dhyayaty - \[she\] meditates; udbhramati - \[she\] becomes confused; pramilati - \[she\] closes \[her\] eyes, pataty - \[she\] falls \[on the ground\]; udyati - \[she\] rises up; murcchaty - \[and she\] faints; api - even, etavaty - In such a state; atanu - of the Cupid; jvare - in the fever; vara-tanur - whose body is best; jiven - would \[she\] live; na - not; kim - not; te - your; rasat - because of rasa; svar - of heaven; vaidya - of the physician (Danvatari); pratima - the incarnation; prasidasi - you are pleased; yadi - if. tyakto - \[Will be\] given up; anyatha - otherwise; hastakah - \[her\] hand.

**Перевод:**

**Her hairs stand on end. She hisses, laments, trembles, becomes fatigued, meditates, becomes confused, closes her eyes, falls on the ground, sees you internally and faints.**  
**If you, the incarnation of the heavenly physician, are pleased, Rādhā possessing the supreme body but afflicted by the fever of love, will live because of your sweet rasa.**

**Commentary:** Having Kṛṣṇa hear of Rādhā’s extreme pain in separation, up to the tenth state, the sakḥī speaks of her hopeless heart.

O image of the heavenly physician Danvantari! If you are pleased to give a cure for her sickness of being separated from your form and beauty, will not Rādhā, possessing the best body (*vara-tanuḥ*) whose beauty is meant for you, live because of the great life-giving medicine in the form of the nectar of your form and enjoyment with you in śṛṅgāra-rasa (*te rasāt*)? Yes she will live. Otherwise, not attaining your rasa (*anyathā)*, she will give up moving her hands to communicate (*hastakaḥ*). In a state of swoon, unable to speak, she interacts with the sakhīs by moving her hands. But in the absence of meeting you, that will be given up. Or, otherwise, medical procedures which involve using the hands will be given up. Another version has *tvatto ‘nyathānantaka*: if you are not pleased, then her death will be caused by you.

The actions caused by the fever of love are described. Her hairs stand on end. She makes a hissing sound. Sound arises in her throat when her hair stands on end due to desire. She laments, “The deceiver does not meet me.” Or she speaks about your qualities with intense longing. She shivers. “How can I endure his unique qualities?” She becomes exhausted (*tāmyati*). This indicates a change of complexion, a sāttvika-bhāva. She thinks, “How can I attain him?” by intense though she becomes confused. This indicates *unmāda.*

More details are given. She closes her eyes, because of external confusion. She then concentrates internally on you, and develops your form in her heart. Being thin, she falls to the ground. Not attaining you, she faints. This is the state of *mūrcchā.*

In such a state (*eva)*, she desires only you, the crest jewel of unlimited heroes, the abode of all good qualities, indicated by the appearance of rasa in her limbs-- by the states described such as *abhilāsa,* by the vyabhicāri-bhāvas such as cintā, by the anubhavās such as hissing and the sāttvika-bhāvas such as hairs standing on end. You should save her life.

Meeting her, you will taste a festival of astonishing madhura-rasa*.* Her body is described as thin *(vara-tanuḥ*) to inform Kṛṣṇa that he should not tolerate separation from her any longer. In the condition of fever, the characteristics are hairs standing on end because of coolness, hissing because of pain, lamenting out of bewilderment, speaking, shivering, thinking “How can I live?” confusion, exhaustion, and fainting. Though she is in this condition, she can live by the perfect rasa administered by the great physician. She can live by meeting you. The ornament is *kriyā-dīpaka* and the meter is *śārdūla-vikrīḍitam.*

### Text 20

*smarāturāṁ daivata-vaidya-hṛdya*  
*tvad-aṅga-saṅgāmṛta-mātra-sādhyām \|*  
*nivṛtta-bādhāṁ kuruṣe na rādhām*  
*upendra-vajrād api dāruṇo’si \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

smaraturam - \[Who is\] lovesick; daivata - of the gods; vaidya - \[than\] the physicians; hrdya - \[more\] attractive; tvad-anga - of your limbs; sangamrta - by the nectar of bliss through association; matra-sadhyam - only to be cured; nivrtta - is relieved; badham - one whose suffering; kuruse - you do make; na - not; radham - Radha. upendra - Of Indra; vajrad - than the thunderbolt; api - even; daruno - harder; ’si - you are.

**Перевод:**

**O Kṛṣṇa, more attractive than the heavenly physicians! You are harder than Indra’s thunderbolt. You do not relieve Rādhā of her pain in the forest, though she is suffering because of Cupid. Her life can be maintained only by your association.**

**Commentary:** O you who are more attractive than the physicians of the devatās, the Aśvinī-kumaras! Or O knower of rasa who can revive dead lovers! Or O attractive physician in the form of our worshipable deity, since you rescue us from the sickness of separation! You do not relieve Rādhā, who is in the nearby forest (*upa*), who is suffering from love (*smarāturam*), of her pain (*nivṛtta-bādhām*). You are harder than Indra’s thunderbolt.

Or O Upendra, who can perform actions impossible for Indra’s thunderbolt! Thus you are employed by Indra for his protection. Thus you are harder than his thunderbolt.

“One must first consider the proper medicine, and then make it. How can this medicine be obtained?” The only cure is the medicine for giving life. This is the rasa of the supreme bliss (*amṛta*) related to your body. Or the only cure which gives life *(amṛta)* is contact with your limbs. *Tvad-aṅga-saṅga* can also mean “happiness of your limbs.” *Saṅga* can mean happiness or attachment according to *Ratna-kośa*. *Upendra-vajra* is also the meter of the verse.

### Text 21

*kandarpa-jvara-saṁjvarātura-tanor aścaryam asyāś ciraṁ*  
*cetaś candana-candramaḥ-kamalinī-cintāsu santāmyati \|*  
*kintu kṣānti-vaśena śītala-tanuṁ tvām ekam eva priyaṁ*  
*dhyāyantī rahasi sthitā katham api kṣīṇā kṣaṇaṁ prāṇiti \|\|21\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kandarpa - Of Cupid; jvara - of the fever ; samjvaratura - \[is\] afflicted by the pain; tanor - whose body; ascaryam - how astonishing! asyas - of she; ciram - for a long time; cetas - the mind; candana -of sandalwood; candramah - of the moon; kamalini - \[and\] of the lotus; cintasu - in thoughts; santamyati - becomes exhausted; kintu -but; ksanti-rasena - with tolerance \[or: klanti-vasena - with fatigue\] ; sitala - \[is\] cooling \[the fever\]; tanum - whose body; tvam - \[on\] you; ekam - alone; eva - only; priyam - \[her\] beloved; dhyayanti - meditating; rahasi - in a solitary place; sthita - situated; katham -somehow; api - even; ksina - weak; ksanam - for a moment; praniti - \[she\] remains alive.

**Перевод:**

**With her body afflicted by the pain of Cupid’s fever, her mind has become exhausted for a long time by thinking of sandalwood, the moon and the lotus. Tolerating this, somehow meditating in a solitary place only on you, her beloved, whose body can cool the fever, she maintains her life for a few moments more.**

**Commentary:** “Until I come, give treatment by cooling substances.”

How astonishing! Her heart becomes exhausted on remembering the lotus, the moon light and the Malaya breeze. This has happened for a long time to Rādhā whose body is pained by affliction of Cupid’s fever. This is the ornament called *virodha.*

“How can she live with this suffering? It is astonishing.” With tolerance, (*kṣānti-rasena*) she meditates on you, her dear beloved, whose body can cool her fever of love, though it is difficult for her to meditate. Weakened, she will live only for a moment. In that moment you should go to her. You will no longer be able to see her. You must hurry. Or at every moment (*kṣaṇam)* she mediates on you. By that thought, she continues to live. So by seeing you, she will be cured. Astonishment is shown by describing her as exhausted by thinking of cool things and living by gaining Kṛṣṇa’s cool association. Another version has *klānti-vaśena* (being fatigued) instead of *kṣānti-rasena* (being tolerant). The meter is *śārdūla-vikrīdītam.*

### Text 22

*kṣaṇam api virahaḥ purā na sehe*  
*nayana-nimīlana-khinnayā yayā te \|*  
*śvasiti katham asau rasāla-śākhāṁ*  
*cira-viraheṇa vilokya puṣpitāgram \|\|22\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ksanam - For a moment; api - even; virahah - the separation; pura -hitherto; na - not; sehe - is tolerated; nayana - of \[her\] eyes; nimilana - with the blinking; khinnaya - weary, yaya - by she (Radha) who; te - from you; Svasiti - \[she\] laments; katham - how; asau - she; rasala - of the mango tree; sakham - the branch; cira - long; virahena - in separation; vilokya - having seen; puspitagram - with a flowering tip?

**Перевод:**

**For a long time Rādhā has not been able to tolerate separation even for a moment. She laments, blinking her eyes. How can she remain alive in long separation on seeing the flowering tip of the mango branch?**

**Commentary:** This verse described her feelings of separation in the presence of uddīpanas. Separation cannot be tolerated by Rādhā (*yayā)* for a moment. She suffers from closing her eyes. The creator has made eyes blink so that there is an obstacle to seeing the beloved.

*aṭati yad bhavān ahni kānanaṁ*  
*truṭi yugāyate tvām apaśyatām*  
*kuṭila-kuntalaṁ śrī-mukhaṁ ca te*  
*jaḍa udīkṣatāṁ pakṣma-kṛd dṛśām*

**When you go off to the forest during the day, a tiny fraction of a second becomes like a millennium for us because we cannot see you. And even when we can eagerly look upon your beautiful face, so lovely with its adornment of curly locks, we think the creator was foolish in making eyelids. SB 10.31.15**

How can she live (*śvasiti)* in the spring, seeing the tip of the flowering mango branch, in long separation from you? Or can she remain breathing which is endowed with such suffering? The meter is *puṣpitāgrā.*

### Text 23

*vṛṣṭi-vyākula-gokulāvana-rasād uddhṛtya govardhanaṁ*  
*bibhrad ballava-vallabhābhir adhikānandāc ciraṁ cumbitaḥ \|*  
*darpeṇeva tad-arpitādhara-taṭī-sindūra-mudrāṅkito*  
*bāhur gopa-tanos tanotu bhavatāṁ śreyāṁsi kaṁsa-dviṣaḥ \|\|23\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vrsti - Of rain; vyakula - from the disturbance; gokulavana - in the protection of Gokula; rasad - out of courage (or out of emotion); uddhrtya - having raised \[him\] up; govardhanam - Govardhana; bibhrad - suppporting; ballava - of the cowherds; vallabhabhir - by the wives; adhikanandac - out of intense bliss; ciram - for a long time; cumbitah - kissed; darpena - out of pride \[in their good fortune\]; iva - as if; tad-arpitadhara-tati - of their sloping lower-lips; sindura - of sindura (vermilion); mudrankito - \[and\] marked with the stamp; bahur - the arm; gopa - \[is that\] of a cowherd; tanos - whose body; tanotu - may \[it\] bestow; bhavatam - \[upon\] you; sreyamsi - auspiciousness; kamsa - of Kamsa; dvisah - of the enemy.

**Перевод:**

**May the arm of the killer of Kaṁsa, the arm of the cowherd, which raised and supported Govardhana to show courage in protecting Gokula from the disturbance of rain, the arm which was with sindūra from the lips of the gopīs when they felt proud of their good fortune, and the arm which was kissed for a long time out of intense bliss by the gopīs, bestow auspiciousness on you!**

**Commentary:** May the arm of Kṛṣṇa, killer of Kaṁsa, spread auspiciousness to you, the listeners. People give gifts by using the hand or arm. Kṛṣṇa is called killer of Kaṁsa to show his ability. He is described. He has the body of a cowherd, having been born to most magnanimous Nanda, king of the cowherds. His power of giving is illustrated. He lifted and carried Govardhana Mountain. Why? He had prowess or passion *(rasa)* to protect Gokula which was suffering because of rain. The arm is described. It was kissed by his beloved gopīs out of intense bliss. The arm is further described. It is marked out of pride in their good fortune with vermilion by the edges of their lips through kissing. Or it is marked with their lips red like sindhūra. The meter is *śārdūla-vikrīḍitam.*

Song Nine is called the bracelet of rasa.

## Part 5: Lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa full of desire

### Text 1

*pañcamaḥ sargaḥ*  
*sākāṅkṣa-puṇḍarīkākṣaḥ*  
*aham iha nivasāmi yāhi rādhām*  
*anunaya mad-vacanena cānayethāḥ \|*  
*iti madhu-rīpuṇā sakhī niyuktā*  
*svayam idam etya punar jagāda rādhām \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

aham - “I; iha - here; nivasami - will remain, yahi - Go; radham -to Radha, anunaya - Pacify \[her\]; mad-vacanena - with my words. canayethah - And you should bring \[her to me\].” iti - Thus; madhuripuna - by the enemy of Madhu (Krsna); sakhi - a female friend; niyukta - \[was\] commanded, svayam - She herself; idam as follows; etya - having approached \[her\]; punar - again; jagada - spoke; radham - to Radha.

**Перевод:**

**“Go to Rādhā and pacify her with my words. Bring her here. I will stay here.” With these words, Kṛṣṇa engaged the sakhī. She went and spoke as follows to Rādhā.**

**Commentary:** In the previous part, Rādhā’s actions in prema were described. Now in the fifth part, Kṛṣṇa’s actions are described. Having heard of Rādhā’s nature in separation, following her, he situates himself in an appointed bower and then personally approaches with heart fearful of his offense. He sends the sakhī to her to pacify her by explaining his suffering during separation.

A sakhī ordered in this way by Kṛṣṇa, went and spoke to Rādhā the following verses. The name Madhuripu (enemy of sweetness) indicates his heavy pain. What did he say? “Go to Rādhā and pacify her with my words.” “But she is angry. Should you not go personally to pacify her?” “ Since I have committed offense I cannot personally speak. You should pacify her. And bring her here (*anayethāḥ*).” “The person who has fickleness cannot attain her.” He speaks with humility, “I am determined to stay here waiting for her for a long time.”

## Song 10: A sakhi descsribes Krishna pain of separation

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 10 \|\|**  
**Deśī-varāḍi-rāga, Rūpaka-tāla**

### Text 2

*vahati malaya-samīre madanam upanidhāya \|*  
*sphuṭati kusuma-nikare virahi-hṛdaya-dalanāya \|\|2\|\|*

*tava virahe vana-mālī sakhi sīdati \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vahati - While \[it\] blows; malaya - of Malaya mountain; samire - the breeze; madanam - passion; upanidhaya - inciting; sphutati - \[and\] while \[it\] blooms; kusuma - of flowers; nikare - the multitude; virahi - of the separated \[lover\]; hrdaya - of the heart; dalanaya - for the piercing;tava - from you; virahe - in separation; vana - of the forest; mali -wearing a garland; sakhi - о friend; sidati - \[Krsna\] remains.

**Перевод:**

**O friend! While the Malaya breeze blows, inciting passion, while all the flowers bloom, to pierce the heart of the lonely lover,  Kṛṣṇa, wearing a flower garland, remains morose, in separation from you. (chorus)**

**Commentary:** The *varāḍī-rāga* is described as follows:

*vinodayantī dayitaṁ sukeśī*  
*sukaṅkaṇā cāmara-cālanena \|*  
*karṇe dadhānā sura-puṣpa-gucchaṁ*  
*varāṅganeyaṁ kathitā varāḍī \|\|*

**Varāḍī-rāga is said to be a beautiful woman with beautiful hair, beautiful bracelets, wearing** ***sura*** **flowers in her ear, who plays with her lover while waving a cāmara.**

The sakhī describes Kṛṣṇa in intense separation by listing his uddīpanas. O friend Rādhā! Wearing a forest garland, Kṛṣṇa is morose in separation from you. He wears a garland which touched your limbs, which was obtained from you after many requests while previously enjoying together. He is inclined to you and morose. You should go and meet him. This is suggested by mentioning the flower garland. Or the previous garlands have been given up. All alone he wears another garland in the designated attractive bower and waits there. Therefore you must go there. This is the chorus.

He remains there morose while the Malaya wind blows, producing intoxication (*madanam*), while the flower clusters blossom to give pain to his heart. *Madanam* means that which produces intoxication or joy. For others it produces joy and for him it produces madness, since he gets pierced by Cupid’s flower arrows. The wind obedient to Cupid and the arrows sent by Cupid cause injury. Or, while the wind blows, inciting desire, and while all the flowers blossom, Kṛṣṇa sits there morose.

### Text 3

*dahati śiśira-mayūkhe maraṇam anukaroti \|*  
*patati madana-viśikhe vilapati vikalataro’ti \|\|3\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

dahati - While \[it\] gives pain; sisira - of the moon; mayukhe - the light; maranam - death; anuharoti - \[he\] imitates; patati - \[and\] while \[it\] falls; madana - of Cupid; visikhe - the arrow; vilapati -\[he\] laments; vikalataro’ti - to the extreme.

**Перевод:**

**While the moon gives him pain, he reaches a death-like condition. While Cupid’s arrows fall upon him, he laments to the extreme.**

**Commentary:** While the cool moon gives him pain, he appears to be dying. He experiences the pain arising from the tenth state—death. Experiencing your face, he criticizes the moon. Though the moon alleviates everyone’s pain, in separation from you he experiences pain in the moon’s presence. The word *anukaroti* (imitates) indicates that death actually cannot take place. He experiences pain similar to death. He maintains his life with difficulty, with the hope of attaining you. While Cupid’s arrows are hitting him, he laments to the extreme (*vikalataraḥ)*. “O beloved! Where are you? Save me, save me!”

### Text 4

*dhvanati madhupa-samūhe śravaṇam apidadhāti \|*  
*manasi kalita-virahe niśi nisi rujam upayāti \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

dhvanati - When \[it\] hums; madhupa - of bees; samuhe - the flock; sravanam - \[his\] ear; apidadhati - \[he\] blocks; manasi - \[and\] when \[his\] mind; kalita - is produced; virahe - \[is one\] in which separation; nisi nisi - every night; rujam - \[greater\] pain; upayati -\[he\] experiences.

**Перевод:**

**When all the bees hum, he blocks his ears. With his mind afflicted by separation, every night his pain increases.**

**Commentary:** Moreover, while all the bees, honey drinkers, hum, he covers his ears with his hands. The bees, followers of Cupid, drunk with drinking honey, proclaim the victory of Cupid. In terror, he blocks his ears. Fixed only on you, day and night he desires only you, giving up all objects of the senses such as sound, in order to attain you. Or, having drunk the nectar of your most sweet, enchanting words, how can he hear any other sounds?

“By day he is disturbed by whatever is seen and heard. Thus he rejects all the objects of the senses. But at night can he not be happy?” With his mind feeling separation from you, every night he experiences even greater pain. This is because night increases the sickness of separation. With such increase of sickness at night, a moment appears like a yuga.

### Text 5

*vasati vipina-vitāne tyajati lalita-dhāma \|*  
*luṭhati dharaṇi-śayane bahu vilapati tava nāma \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vasati - \[He\] dwells; vipina - of the forest; vitane - in the expanse; tyajati - \[he\] gives up; lalita - attractive; dhama - \[his\] house; luthati - \[he\] rolls; dharani - of earth; sayane - on \[his\] bed; bahu -repeatedly; vilapati - \[and he\] utters; tava - your; nama - name.

**Перевод:**

**Giving up his attractive house, he lives in the depths of the forest. He rolls on his bed the ground and repeatedly utters you name.**

**Commentary:** “He is adored by many people. Can they not make him happy?” He gives up Nanda’s attractive house and Vraja. He remains in the depth of the forest. Giving up friends, like a yogī, he lives deep in the forest. How can they make him happy? Or he rejects his beautiful body. Without you, it appears perishable. *Dhāma* means a house or a body according to *Viśva-prakāśa.* This also means he gives up everything else.

“What is the proof that he has such love for me?” This is shown by describing his anubhāvas. He rolls around on his earthen bed made on the forest soil sprinkled with the scent of your lotus feet. He intently *(vi*) utters *(lapati)* your name repeatedly. “O dear Rādhā! O goddess of my life!” You should not criticize him, since he is fixed on you alone, having given up all other methods and goals.

### Text 6

*bhaṇati kavi-jayadeve viraha-vilasitena \|*  
*manasi rabhasa-vibhave harir udayatu sukṛtena \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

bhanati - While \[he\] speaks; kavi-jayadeve - the poet Jayadeva; viraha - of separation; vilasitena - with the pastimes; manasi - in the mind \[of the listeners\]; rabhasa - \[is\] frenzied love; vibhave - whose condition; harir - Krsna; udayatu - may \[he} appear; sukrtena - with \[a song\] well-composed.

**Перевод:**

**While Jayadeva recites this poem, may Kṛṣṇa with his pastimes of separation appear in the listener’s mind which is filled with his frenzied love and qualified by past devotional acts.**

**Commentary:** While Jayadeva recites this poem, may Kṛṣṇa manifest himself in the minds of the singers and listeners with his pastimes of separation. The minds should be filled with his frenzied prema (*rabhasa-vibhave*). This will happen because of good acts in the form of worshipping Bhagavān’s form, or because the song written by Jayadeva (*kṛtena)* is splendid (*su).*

Seeing Rādhā afflicted in her heart, unable to tolerate hearing the suffering of her beloved, the sakhī stopped speaking. The above song thus has only five verses (instead of eight).

### Text 7

*pūrvaṁ yatra samaṁ tvayā rati-pater āsāditāḥ siddhayas*  
*tasminn eva nikuñja-manmatha-mahā-tīrthe punar mādhavaḥ \|*  
*dhyāyaṁs tvām aniśaṁ japann api tavaivālāpa-mantrāvaliṁ*  
*bhūyas tvat-kuca-kumbha-nirbhara-parīrambhāmṛtaṁ vāñchati \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

purvam - Previously; yatra - where; samam tvaya - \[along with\] you; rati-pater - of Cupid; asaditah - were attained; siddhayas - the powers; tasminn - that; eva - only; nikunja - \[which is\] a bower; manmatha - of Cupid; maha-tirthe - in \[that\] great tirtha \[holy place\]; punar - again; madhavah - Madhava (Krsna); dhyayams - meditating; tvam - \[on\] you; anisam - constantly; japann - muttering; api - even; tavaivalapa - \[which are\] as if your conversations; mantravalim - a ( host of mantras; bhuyas - again; tvat - your; kuca - with breasts; kumbha - pot; nirbhara - deep; parirambhamrtam - the nectar (bliss) of embrace; vanchati - \[he\] desires.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa again desires the bliss of deeply embracing your pot-like breasts. He meditates on you constantly, while muttering a host of mantras in the form of your conversations in a bower sanctified as the great tīrtha of Cupid where previously you attained his powers.**

**Commentary:** Previously it was said that, fixed on Rādhā, he desired only her. That is shown in this verse. O friend! Kṛṣṇa again (*bhūyas)* desires the great bliss of the deep embrace of your pot-like breasts, like nectar because of its taste and life-giving qualities. It is suggested that her breasts are like two golden pots filled with nectar. The word Mādhava (husband of Lakṣmī) suggests that Kṛṣṇa, endowed with the previously mentioned qualities, is controlled by Rādhā. Why does he desire this supreme bliss without worshipping devatās? He meditates continually on you. You are his worshipable deity. And he utters a host (*āvalim)* of mantras in the form of your private conversations which shower the greatest sweetness. Her words are called mantras since he repeats them like sacred incantations in his heart.

“Will not uttering mantras bring quick success if uttered at the holy place (*tīrtha)* of one’s devatā?” He utters the mantras at the great holy place (*mahā-tīrtha)* of Cupid’s *(manmatha)* bower. *Manmatha* means one who agitates the mind. A *nikuñja* is a hollow area within creepers. This has taken the form of Cupid. Or he utters mantras in the bower where the pastimes of Cupid are performed. He dwells in that great holy place which quickly produces results for the mantra. The place’s greatness is shown. There, previously the siddhis of Cupid were attained by you, dearer to him than millions of lives.

The word *āvali* (group) indicates that he uttered whole groups of conversations which were like a garland of mantras. When the mantra reaches success, it is able to bestow results.

*svapna-prāpte striyā datte mālā-mantre tathaiva ca \|*  
*vaidikeṣu ca sarveṣu siddhādīn naiva śodhayet \|\|*  
*gṛha-goṣṭha-vanārāma-nadī-naga-surālaye \|*  
*samīpe vā guroḥ siddhiḥ śīghraṁ syād uttarottarā \|\|*

**One should not purify mantras to gain siddhis in the case of Vedic mantras, mantras attained in dreams, mantras given by a woman, or māla-mantras. The siddhis of mantras quickly manifest if chanted in a house, cowshed, forest, garden, river, mountain, temple, or in front of a guru with increasing effect.**

The word *eva* indicates that Kṛṣṇa is fixed in the bower. He will remain there (*vasan*) until his chanting succeeds in bringing a result. Meditating on her, uttering the siddha-mantras at a siddha-kṣetra*,* while being completely steady, one will quickly attain the siddhi. This skillful description gives rise to śṛṅgāra-rasa of the couple.

Thus ends the tenth song called *Hari-samudayā-garuḍa-pada* : Kṛṣṇa tries to extract the poison of separation like Garuḍa destroying snakes.

## Song 11: A sakhi implores Radha to go to Krishna

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 11 \|\|**  
**Gurjarī-rāga, ekatālī-tāla.**

### Text 8

*rati-sukha-sāre gatam abhisāre madana-manohara-veśam \|*  
*na kuru nitambini gamana-vilambanam anusara taṁ hṛdayeśam \|\|8\|\|*

*dhīra-samīre yamunā-tīre vasati vane vana-mālī \|*  
*pīna-payodhara-parisara-mardana-cañcala-kara-yuga-śālī \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**
