rati - Of love; sukha - with the happiness; sare - excellent; gatam -having gone; abhisare - to the meeting place; madana - to Cupid; manohara - \[are\] attractive; vesam - whose clothes; na - \[do\] not; kuru - make; nitambini - о woman (Radha) with beautiful hips; gamana - of \[your\] going; vilambanam - a delay; anusara - \[and\] go to; tam - him \[Krsna\]; hrdayesam - the lord of \[your\] heart (or he whose lord is your heart), dhira - \[is\] blowing; samire - \[where\] the breeze; yamuna - of the Yamuna; tire - on the bank; vasati - he waits; vane - in the glade; vana - of the forest; mall - wearing a garland; pina - mature; payodhara - \[your\] breasts; parisara - \[which are\] adjacent; mardana - for massaging; cancala - restless; kara - of hands; yuga - a pair; salt - possessed of.

**Перевод:**

**Please go to Kṛṣṇa, the lord of your heart and do not be late. O Rādhā with beautiful hips! Wearing clothing attractive even to Cupid, he has gone to the meeting place, excellent with the happiness of love.**  
**Wearing a forest garland he waits in a glade on the bank of the Yamunā with gentle breeze blowing. His hands are restless to massage your mature breasts. (chorus)**

**Commentary:** The *rāga* is *gurjarī* and the *tāla* is *ekatāli.* The meaning is this. O Rādhā with beautiful hips! Go and meet the lord of your heart, dearer than a million lives. The phrase “lord of your heart” suggests “Just as Kṛṣṇa cannot tolerate a moment’s separation, your heart also is controlled by him.” Or *hrḍayeśam* can mean “He whose lord is your heart.” This means he is obedient to your heart.

*Nitambini* indicates that because of her wide hips she walks slowly. But now she should not delay. Do not be late (*vilambanam)* for the meeting. Kṛṣṇa is described. He has gone to the place appointed for the meeting*. Abhisāra* means that place in which one takes shelter. That place is described. It has excellence (*sāre)* of happiness of enjoyment. Or it is the highest form (*sāre),* since it provides for the happiness of union (*rati-sukha).*

Kṛṣṇa is described. He is wearing decorations fit for the meeting, which attract the mind of Cupid. How to describe the beauty of his limbs? Or he wears cloth which attracts the mind and destroys Cupid *(ma—danam*). Or he is Cupid (*madanam*) and the form (*veṣa)* which broke the cart (*anohara*). This shows his prowess as a hero. Or he wears clothing which makes him more attractive than Cupid. Since he strongly attracts your heart, as the Cupid for women of Vraja, you should meet with him quickly.

The meeting place is described. Wearing a forest garland, he waits in a deep glade on the bank of the Yamunā. Cheating countless other women who tried to meet him, wearing another forest garland, he waits in the deep glade hiding himself in order to meet you. Wearing a flower garland indicates his great desire to embrace her. The bank of the Yamunā has gentle breezes blowing. The bank of the Yamunā and the forest indicate coolness and fragrance. Thus the place is served by gentle, cool, fragrant breezes. *Dhīra* can also mean clever. The wind is careful to serve you with all its qualities. *Dhīra* means wise and slow.

### Text 9

*nāma-sametaṁ kṛta-saṅketaṁ vādayate mṛdu veṇum \|*  
*bahu manute nanu te tanu-saṅgata-pavana-calitam api reṇum \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

nama - With \[your\] name; sametam - furnished; krta - \[is\] made; sanketam - by which an indication \[for the meeting\]; vadayate -\[he\] plays; mrdu - softly; venum - \[his\] flute, bahu - Greatly; manute - \[he\] reveres; nanu - ah! te - your; tanu - body; sangata - touching; pavana - by the wind; calitam - carried; api - even; renum - the dust.

**Перевод:**

**He softly plays his flute which gives indications for the meeting, while uttering your name. Ah! He greatly reveres the dust which has been carried by the wind which has touched your body.**  
**“Why does he wait for me? Why is he attracted to me?”**

**Commentary:** Your beloved now is playing his flute softly (*mṛdu*) since it is filled with his heart, melted by singing your name, while not letting other women hear. He is experiencing anurāga for you. The flute also indicates the meeting (*kṛta-saṅketam*) with names (*nāma-sametam*) by saying “O Rādhā! O goddess of my life!” It reveals a hidden meaning through its song. Or the flute is played where there will be a meeting with me (*kṛta-saṅketam*) “When I play the flute, you bring Rādhā to that place.” Or flute proclaims your good fortune by uttering your names: “She is my lover, dearer than a million lives. I am waiting to meet her. She should not bring others. She should not go to another place by mistake.”

*Nanu* is an exclamation. He holds in great reverence particles of dust which by chance have been carried to him, not on her order, by the wind which touched your limbs or had some contact by touching your cloth. He greatly respects those particles which came by chance. The dust which has been touched by the wind which touched your limbs is most fortunate. I am fortunate to have been able to touch that dust touched by the wind which touched you. If that is so, what can one say of the rasa arising from directly contacting your limbs?

Instead *of nanu* sometimes *tanu* is seen. O Rādhā with beautiful body! Or it can be the verb *tanute*. He respects the dust and then glorifies it (*tanute)* while making gestures. “This dust gives the highest bliss, and is cool.” Or *tanu* can stand for the adverb *atanu* (not a little).

### Text 10

*patati patatre vicalati patre śaṅkita-bhavad-upayānam \|*  
*racayati śayanaṁ sacakita-nayanaṁ paśyati tava panthānam \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

patati - \[While they\] fly; patatre - the birds; vicalati - \[and while they\] flutter; patre - the leaves; sankita - \[is\] expected; bhavad-upayanam - on which your approach; racayati - \[he\] makes; sayanam - a bed; sacakita-nayanam - with restless eyes; pasyati - \[and he\] sees; tava - your; panthanam - path.

**Перевод:**

**He makes a flower bed and gazes at the path that you are expected to take, while birds fly from tree to tree and the leaves flutter.**

**Commentary:** Kṛṣṇa makes a bed for you out of the most tender shoots and flower petals using his two tender hands which are like shoots. The bed is for pastimes of love and is furnished with various colorful scented pillows. He gazes at the path by which you come with glances restless because of longing, passion and love. This indicates that all his senses, not only his eyes, are absorbed in Rādhā.

He shows that by gestures. He gazes at the path on which you are supposed (*śaṅkita)* to come (*bhavad-upayānam*). *Bhavatī* is in the masculine (*bhavad)* since pronouns can be masculine in compounds. He gazes while birds fly from tree to tree and the leaves flutter. This reflects the condition of his mind.

### Text 11

*mukharam adhīraṁ tyaja mañjīraṁ ripum iva keli-sulolam \|*  
*cala sakhi kuñjaṁ sa-timira-puñjaṁ śīlaya nīla-nicolam \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

mukharam - Noisy; adhiram - ignorant; tyaja - give up; manjiram -\[your\] ankle-bells; ripum - an enemy; iva - as if; kelisu - in pastimes; lolam - fickle; cala - go; sakhi - о friend; kunjam - to the bower; sa-timira - \[is\] covered with darkness; punjam - whose mass; silaya - \[and\] wear; nila - black; nicolam - a mantle.

**Перевод:**

**O friend! Go to the dark bower, wearing a black mantle. Give up your ignorant, noisy ankle bells, since they are a like an enemy, acting fickle when you engage in pastimes.**

**Commentary:** “Just as Kṛṣṇa has gone for the meeting you should prepare for the meeting.”

O friend! Go to the bower. Wear a black mantle, so you will appear as darkness. Shining with effulgence will be an obstacle to the meeting. Or go to the bower which is most dark. The dark bower and the dark cloth are elements which nourish rasa by being favorable to the meeting. It is also hinted that by wearing the dark cloth, her golden beauty will be most attractive to Kṛṣṇa.

Unfavorable elements must be discarded. Give up your ankle bells which are ignorant (*adhīram)* or bold, since they are noisy. This is unsuitable for the meeting. Moreover the ankle bells are fickle in playing (*keliṣu lolam*). *Keli-sulolam* is another version. They are most fickle when you play. They are like enemies since they are an obstacle for tasting the nectar of clever conversations in the pastimes of enjoyment. They should be given up like enemies. Since they are somewhat favorable in śṛṅgāra-rasa, they are worn by persons who are silent.

### Text 12

*urasi murārer upahita-hāre ghana iva tarala-balāke \|*  
*taḍid iva pīte rati-viparīte rājasi sukṛta-vipāke \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

urasi - On the chest; murarer - of Murari (Krsna); upahita - is placed; hare - on which a necklace; ghana - a cloud; iva - as if; tarala - \[are\] fickle; balake - on which women \[or: on which cranes are moving\]; tadid - lightning; iva - as if; pite - golden; rati - in lovemaking; viparite - \[when you take\] the dominant role; rajasi - you \[will\] shine; sukrta - of good character; vipake - \[as\] the final result.

**Перевод:**

**O golden Rādhā! When you take the dominant role, you will shine like lightning on Kṛṣṇa’s chest, which, devoid of ornaments, is like a cloud with a line of moving cranes, the final result of splendid actions.**

**Commentary:** “Beauty will be lost by giving up ornaments. There will be great beauty by the limbs and ornaments meeting together.” The sakḥī stimulates her longing with this verse.

O golden limbed Rādhā (*pīte)!* You will shine on the chest of Kṛṣṇa *(murāreḥ*). Murari means “one who surrounds (*murayati*) with his arms, thirsty for embracing.” *Rājasi,* in the present tense, expresses the near future. (Pāṇini 3.3.13) This will happen when you take the dominant position in love making (*rati-viparīte).* His chest is described. It is devoid of ornaments which create obstacles to embracing you, when you take the male role. Or he has placed a pearl necklace on his neck to show special beauty by his skillful embraces. His chest is like a cloud with a line of flying cranes. It is suggested that the necklace is also fickle in taking the male role.

Rādhā is like lightning (*taḍit)* which astonishes a person with its variety. It also suggests her fragrance. This is an example of *adbhūtopamā*: in the sky there is a rain cloud and above it a line of flying cranes and above that is the fickle lightning causing astonishment. If she is there, then the rest of the scene manifests.

When that happens, there is more attraction. The chest becomes the highest astonishing result (*vipāke)* of splendid actions (*sukṛta*). Or the chest is the result of excellent pastimes when the couple’s roles are reversed.

Or *sukṛta-vipake* can modify *taraka-balāke*. His chest is the residence of fickle (*tarala)* women overcome with desire for enjoyment *(balāke*) which becomes golden (*pite*) with minerals from the woman being in the dominant position, which is the final result in the form of Rādhā’s actions for Kṛṣṇa (*sukṛta).*

Or the phrases can be calling out to Rādhā. O Rādhā with a necklace gifted by Kṛṣṇa on the first meeting (*upahita-hāre*)! O Rādhā, fickle girl (*tarala-balāke*)! This shows pacification by giving gifts to her. That necklace on your neck will look splendid when you are on his chest. *Tarala-baḷake* indicates her nature to dominate. Thus she is addressed as “O woman in the dominant role (*rati-vipārite*).” O ultimate form *(vipāke*) of his highest piety (*sukṛta)!* She is addressed in this way since he desires her as his only goal. Or this phrase can modify *rati-vipārite.* O woman in the dominant role, the form of the highest piety for both of you! The reason is that she is *tarala-baḷāke* and *upahita-hāre.*

*Balāka* means a girl or a woman full of desire according to *Śāśvata. Vipāka* means something hard to attain according to *Viśva.*

### Text 13

*vigalita-vasanaṁ parihṛta-raśanaṁ*  
*ghaṭaya jaghanam apidhānam \|*  
*kiśalaya-śayane paṅkaja-nayane*  
*nidhim iva harṣa-nidhānam \|\|13\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vigalita - \[Is\] fallen; vasanam - from which cloth; parihrta - \[is\] removed; rasanam - from which the belt (with bells); ghataya -place; jaghanam - \[your\] hips; apidhanam - without a covering; kisalaya - of shoots; sayane - on the bed; pankaja-nayane - о lotuseyed woman \[Radha\]; nidhim - a treasure; iva - as if; harsa - of bliss; nidhanam - the abode.

**Перевод:**

**O lotus eyed Rādhā! Place your hips devoid of cloth and bells, the wealth of Kṛṣṇa and the abode of bliss, on the bed of shoots.**

**Commentary:** Having described Rādhā’s dominant role, her actions are described. O lotus eyed Rādhā! Place your hips on the fresh bed of shoots made by Kṛṣṇa. The cloth has fallen off your hips during the exploits of śṛṅgāra-rasa of the most expert person, in the war with your lover who is more attractive than millions of Cupids. Your hips are now without covering *(apidhānam*). Small bells have been removed by lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa who is dear to your heart and is under the influence of Cupid’s arrows. Those hips are the abode of the highest bliss, like a treasure *(nidhim*)—the desired treasure of the crest jewel of heroes.

Or her role as the dominant lover is shown. O friend! Join your hips devoid of cloth and bells with lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa, on the bed. Since the sound of the bells in unsuitable on this occasion, it is proper to have the hips devoid of cloth. The rest is the same.

### Text 14

*harir abhimāṇī rajanir idānīm*  
*iyam api yāti virāmam \|*  
*kuru mama vacanaṁ satvara-racanaṁ*  
*pūraya madhu-ripu-kāmam \|\|14\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

harir - Krsna; abhimanli - regards \[you as his beloved\], rajanir -Night; idanim - now; iyam - this; api - even, yati - is going; viramam - to \[its\] end. kuru - Follow; mama - my; vacanam - orders; satvara - \[is\] swift; racanam - whose accomplishment, puraya - Fulfill; madhu-ripu - of Madhuripu (Krsna, the enemy of spring); kamam - the desire.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa accepts you as his beloved. The end of night is coming. Quickly follow my orders. Fulfill the desires of Kṛṣṇa who has been overcome with love.**

**Commentary:** Seeing Rādhā’s delay, she shows negative results of not going *(bheda*).

The most attractive male (*hariḥ*) regards you as dearest in all ways (*abhimāṇī*) or has the greatest affection for you in prema. The meaning is this. Just as you think of him as the dearest, he also thinks that you are as his dearest who can satisfy all his desires. Therefore fully satisfy the desires (in the form of enjoyment with you) of he who is the enemy of spring (*madhu-ripu*) ,since spring causes disturbances. Overcome by the enemy he will fall under his control with your help on being made blissful.

This night is going to end now. Please follow my instructions so that you can go quickly (*satvara-racanam*). Or please follow my instructions to go quickly. I said, “Go to the bower.” Please follow that instruction, to fulfill the desires of your beloved. If you become indifferent at the end of night because of stubbornness, you will fall ill in the sickroom with sorrow from causeless separation. This is *bheda,* showing negative results by not following the instructions.

Some say that *abhimānī* means proud Kṛṣṇa. But this is not correct since he is submissive to Rādhā. Assuming qualities of the *dhṛṣta-nāyaka* would destroy the rasa. Another version has *atimānī* instead of *abhimānī. Hariḥ* then means “he who prevents destruction of love.” He gives great respect (*atimānī*) to enjoyment. With the appearance of ingredients to nourish rasa, now make complete Kṛṣṇa’s desire to enjoy.

### Text 15

*śrī-jayadeve kṛta-hari-seve*  
*bhaṇati parama-ramaṇīyam \|*  
*pramudita-hṛdayaṁ harim atisadayaṁ*  
*namata sukṛta-kamanīyam \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeve - Sri Jayadeva; krta - is done; hari - to Krsna; seve -by whom service; bhanati - \[since he\] recites \[this story\]; parama-ramaniyam - most attractive; pramudita - \[is\] blissful; hrdayam - whose heart; harim - Krsna; atisadayam - most compassionate; namata - offer respects; sukrta - with splendid pastimes; kamamyam - \[and\] pleasant

**Перевод:**

**Offer respects to Kṛṣṇa, whose heart is blissful, who is most compassionate to devotees, since Jayadeva, doing service to Kṛṣṇa, has recited this most attractive story, pleasant with pastimes.**

**Commentary:** O devotees! Offer respects to Kṛṣṇa who has been described. Worship him by cultivating the actions described. His heart is endowed with bliss because of zeal to meet his beloved. He is most compassionate (*atisadayam*) since he is affectionate to his devotees. Or *sadayam* can mean the good fortune (*ayam*) of devotees (*sad*). Kṛṣṇa creates good fortune for the devotees by attracting everyone’s minds through revealing his complete qualities when he relates to the supreme heroine. Jayadeva thus records his own realization. It is commonly said that Kṛṣṇa appeared in the house of Jayadeva because of his writing this work.

Why? One should offer respects since Jayadeva, who has done this service for Kṛṣṇa (*kṛta-hari-seve*), narrates this story of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa which is most attractive (*parama-ramaṇīyam*). It is supreme because it is the form of the highest bliss. It is attractive because it contains rasa. It is most desirable (*kamanīyam*) with splendid actions (*sukṛta*). Or it is attained by performing millions of deserving acts—by associating with great devotees through Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. The words *parama-ramaṇiyam* can modify *harim*. Kṛṣṇa is most desirable. *Sukṛta-kamanīyam* can also modify *harim.* Kṛṣṇa is beautiful (*kamanīyam*) with splendid actions (*sukṛta*)*.*

This verse describes the heroine preparing to meet her lover (*abhisārikā*). The qualities are explained:

*madanānala-santaptā yābhisārayati priyam \|*  
*jyotsnā-tāmasvinī yāna-yogyāmbara-vibhūṣaṇā \|\|*

**The women who goes to meet her lover burns with the fire of passion, has a vehicle, is dressed and ornamented properly and eclipses the moon light. Rasārṇava-sudhākara**

### Text 16

*vikirati muhuḥ śvāsān āśāḥ puro muhur īkṣate*  
*praviśati muhuḥ kuñjaṁ gūñjan muhur bahu tāmyati \|*  
*racayati muhuḥ śayyāṁ paryākulaṁ muhur īkṣate*  
*madana-kadana-klāntaḥ kānte priyas tava vartate \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vikirati - \[He\] heaves; muhuh - constantly; svasan - sighs; asah - in every direction; puro - in front \[of him\]; muhur - every moment; iksate - he gazes; pravisati - \[he\] enters; muhuh - repeatedly; kunjam - the bower; gunjan - buzzing; muhur - constantly; bahu -very; tamyati - \[he\] becomes tired; racayati - \[he\] makes; muhuh - repeatedly; sayyarh - a bed; paryakulam - full of consusion; muhur - repeatedly; iksate - \[he\] gazes, madana - \[Inflicted by\] Cupid; kadana - from the pains; klantah - exhausted; kante - о beautiful woman (Radha); priyas - lover; tava - your; vartate - has become;

**Перевод:**

**O Rādhā! Your lover has become exhausted from the pains inflicted by Cupid. He exhales constantly in pain, and gazes in all directions in front of him at every moment. He repeatedly enters the bower filled with the humming of bees and constantly develops great fatigue. He repeatedly makes a bed and repeatedly looks around in confusion.**

**Commentary:** Other activities in going for the meeting are described. O Rādhā who control your lover by your beauty (*kānte*)! Your lover is fading away from love’s pain due to separation from you. He contently exhales sighs of pain by thinking of your coming. He constantly looks at the directions (*āśāḥ*) in front of him. This means he looks at all direction simultaneously, thinking in confusion, “From which direction will she come?” Not seeing you there, he repeatedly enters the appointed bower. He looks at the path and thinks, “By which path will she enter the bower?” and then enters the bower. This shows his great longing. The bower resounds with bees. He then becomes very (*bahu*) tired. In spite of this condition, he repeatedly makes a bed using astonishing methods, out of anurāga for you. There he again looks in all directions.

Another version has *vikirati muhuḥ śvāsān nāsa-puro muhur īkṣate, praviśati muhuḥ kuñjaṁ kūjan muhur bahu tāmyati:* he meditates on you (*nāsa-puro muhur īkṣate*), enters the bower while uttering in pain (*kūjan)* “O beloved, goddess of my life!” Then he becomes exhausted. The meter *is hariṇī.* The ornament is *dīpaka.*

### Text 17

*tvad-vāmyena samaṁ samagram adhunā tigmāṁśur astaṁ gato*  
*govindasya manorathena ca samaṁ prāptaṁ tamaḥ sāndratām \|*  
*kokānāṁ karuṇa-svanena sadṛśī dīrgha-mad-abhyarthanā*  
*tan mugdhe viphalaṁ vilambanam asau ramyo'bhisāra-kṣaṇaḥ \|\|17\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tvad-vamyena - with your obstinacy; samam - along; samagram -completely; adhuna - now; tigmamsur - the sun; astam gato - \[has\] set; govindasya - of Govinda (Krsna); manorathena - with the desire; ca - and; samam - along; praptam - \[has\] attained the state; tamah -the darkness; sandratam - of thickness, kokanam - Of the cakravaka birds; karuna - pitiable; svanena - the cry; sadrsi - \[is\] like; dlrgha -long \[-cherished\]; mad - my; abhyarthana - request, tan - Therefore; mugdhe - о beautiful woman \[Radha\]; viphalam - \[is\] pointless. vilambanam - delay, asau - This \[is\]; ramyo - pleasant; bhisara - for meeting; ksanah - a time.

**Перевод:**

**O beautiful Rādhā! Delay is of no value. This is the appropriate and pleasant time for meeting. Along with your obstinacy, the sun has now set completely. Along with Govinda’s desire, the darkness has become intense. My long cherished request is like the pitiable cry of the cakravāka.**

**Commentary:** You should go immediately. The reason is given. O Rādhā, controlling your lover by your beauty (*mugdhe*)! O beautiful Rādhā! Also the word suggests “O Rādhā, ignorant of performing your actions (*mugdhe*)!” Delaying is useless since it is simply an obstacle to attaining happiness. This is the appropriate time for the meeting. It is most attractive.

This is illustrated. Along with (*samam*) your crooked nature, the sun (*tigmāṁśuḥ*) has now completely (*samagram*) set. Or endowed with complete sweetness (*samagra-madhunā*) the harshness of your heart, arising from the pain of separation, has disappeared. The darkness has become thick along with the desire of Govinda. His desire and the darkness have both become intense. His desire has become intense because darkness acts as an uddīpana or stimulus for love and darkness is a good reason for his beloved to come.

This has been my request for a long time, equal to the pitiable cry of the cakravāka bird. The cakravāka at night cries in separation from its lover and thus its cry is pitiable. It is not proper to delay since your lover is solely devoted to you and eager, since there is thick darkness and since you should be merciful to me, most fallen and crooked.

The poetic ornament is *sahokti.****\[\[20\]****\]* This is explained in *Kāvyādarṣa* written by Daṇḍī. *sahoktiḥ saha-bhāvena kathanaṁ guṇa-karmayoḥ*: *sahokti* means describing qualities and actions accompanied by other things. (Kāvyādarṣa 2.351) An example is *saha dīrghā mama śvāsair imāḥ samprati rātrayaḥ:* now the nights are long along with my breathing.

### Text 18

*āśleṣād anu cumbanād anu nakho-lekhād anu svāntaja-*  
*prodbodhād anu sambhramād anu ratārambhād anu prītayoḥ \|*  
*anyārthaṁ gatayor bhramān militayoḥ sambhāṣaṇair jānator*  
*dampatyor iha ko na ko na tamasi vrīḍā-vimiśro rasaḥ \|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

aslesad - the embrace; anu - after; cumbanad - the kiss; anu - after; nakho - of the nails; lekhad - the scratch; anu - after; svantaja - of the Cupid; prodbodhad - the appearance; anu - after; sambhramad - eagerness; anu - after; ratarambhad - the beginning of union; anu - \[and\] after; pritayoh - of the two lovers; anyartham - for another reason; gatayor - having come; bhraman - from wandering about; militayoh - encountering; sambhasanair - by conversations; janator - becoming acquainted; dampatyor - the couple; iha - now; ko na ko na - does \[it\] not \[arise\]; tamasi - in the darkness; vrida - with shyness; vimisro - mixed; rasah - the rasa?

**Перевод:**

**Does not the couple’s rasa mixed with shyness arise in such darkness? They go with other intentions, and, after wandering, meet and get to know each other with words. First they embrace, then kiss, then scratch each other, and on arousing Cupid, become most passionate and then unite. The couple then experiences the highest bliss.**

**Commentary:** The sakhī described the pleasant moment for meeting in the last verse. Now she describes the scene after becoming intensely eager for their meeting by showing the astonishing nature of various rasas during the dense darkness.

In this darkness, cannot the madhurya-rasa of a couple endowed with shyness not take place? Yes it can. The couple is described. They have gone while indicating other intentions. They meet by wandering about. They know each other through talking to each other. “I am her husband, he is my husband.” The intention to enjoy is indicated by sāttvika-bhāvas like choked voice or by the manifestation of emotions*.*

Their actions are described. After (*anu*) embracing, they kiss. After that, they mark each other with their nails in order to agitate intense desire. After that, Cupid (*svānta-ja)* bursts forth. After that, there arises eagerness (*sambhrama*) to touch her skirt out of great passion. After that, amorous union begins as described in *Kāma-śāstra*. After that the couple simultaneously experience bliss (*prītayoḥ*). Bharata says:

*āśleṣa-cumbana-nakha-kṣata-kāma-bodha-*  
*śīghratva-maithunam ananta-sukha-prabodham \|*  
*prītis tato’pi rasa-bhāvanam eva kāryam*  
*evaṁ nitānta-caturāḥ suciraṁ ramante \|\|*

**With embraces, kissing, scratching with nails, arousal of love, and quickly engaging in lovemaking, unlimited bliss arises. Affection and then a state of rasa arise. Those who are most skillful enjoy in this way for a long time.**

Or which rasa should not arise? All rasas should manifest. Thus in the dense darkness out of fear, lamentation is imitated. This is karuṇa-rasa*.* Scratching with nails indicates enthusiasm in vīra-rasa. The experience of unprecedented joy indicates adbhūta-rasa because of the astonishment. With the arousal of Cupid, hāsya-rasa is indicated. With the commencement of union (*ratārambhā)*, the king of all rasas, the friend of rati, śṛṅgāra-rasa, shines. It is said:

*ratir hāsaś ca śokaś ca krodhotsāhau bhayaṁ tathā \|*  
*jugupsā vismayaś śamāḥ sthāyi-bhāvāḥ prakīrtitāḥ \|\|*

**The sthāyi-bhāvas are celebrated** ***as rati (śṛṅgāra*****),** ***hāsa*** **(joking),** ***śoka*** **(lamentation),** ***krodha*** **(anger),** ***utsāha*** **(enthusiasm),** ***bhaya*** **(fear),** ***jugupsā*** **(disgust) and** ***vismaya*** **(astonishment) and** ***śama*** **(peace). Nāṭya-śāstra 6.17**

Disgust, anger and peacefulness are not described in the poem since they are contrary to śṛṅgāra-rasa. The others should be considered nourishers of śṛṅgāra-rasa and are not independent. It is also hinted that though the two go with other intentions, they, the forms of complete rasa, should not delay meeting because the sakhī has narrated the elements such as the darkness which give rise to rasa.

Others explain the verse differently. A couple go for a meeting, but by mistake they meet a different man and woman. Thinking “She is my lover, he is my lover” they each associate with another man and woman. After enjoyment they get to know each other by speaking. Then both develop rasa mixed with shyness. But that is not a proper explanation since it impossible for a sakhī to speak of material love affairs in front of the consort of the Lord. The meter is *śārdūla-vikṛḍitam.*

### Text 19

*sa-bhaya-cakitaṁ vinyasyantīṁ dṛśaṁ timire pathi*  
*pratitaru muhuḥ sthitvā mandaṁ padāni vitanvatīm \|*  
*katham api rahaḥ prāptām aṅgair anaṅga-taraṇgibhiḥ*  
*sumukhi subhagaḥ paśyan sa tvām upaitu kṛtārthatām \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sa-bhaya-cakitam - Trembling fearfully; vinyasyantim - placing \[in all directions\]; drsam - \[your\] glance \[like a doe\]; timire - in the darkness; pathi - on the path; pratitaru - from tree to tree; muhuh - now; sthitva - having stood; mandam - slowly; padani - \[your\] steps; vitanvatim - placing; katham - somehow; api - even; rahah -to a solitary place; praptam - coming; angair - with limbs; anahga - by the Cupid; tarangibhih - agitated; sumukhi - о beautiful-faced woman (Radha); subhagah - the fortunate one (Krsna); pasyan -seeing; sa - he; tvam - you; upaitu - may \[he\] obtain; krtarthatam - success.

**Перевод:**

**O Rādhā with a beautiful face! May fortunate Kṛṣṇa on seeing you as you glance in all directions on the path in the darkness with doe-like eyes, and as you wander from tree to tree with slow steps, in a solitary place, with limbs agitated by Cupid, attain success.**

**Commentary:** His only goal is to attain you. The sakhī explains this.

O Rādhā with beautiful face! May Kṛṣṇa (*subhagaḥ*), seeing you, attain success. From seeing you he should attain all good fortune. It is said:

*tābhir vidhūta-śokābhir bhagavān acyuto vṛtaḥ*  
*vyarocatādhikaṁ tāta puruṣaḥ śaktibhir yathā*

**Encircled by the gopīs, who were now relieved of all distress, Kṛṣṇa, perfect in himself, without fault, shone forth more splendidly than previously. My dear King, Kṛṣṇa thus appeared like the Lord encircled by his spiritual potencies. SB 10.32.10**

Rādhā is described. You glance with eyes of a young doe in all directions, with agitation for the meeting. This shows the beauty of her glance. You stand at each tree repeatedly thinking, “My lover will come here” and walk with a splendid gait very gracefully. Somehow by good fortune you attain a solitary place with your limbs excited by Cupid. Instrumental case is used (*aṅgaiḥ*) to describe her. It is also suggested that her limbs become splendid (*ullāsa*) because of her excellent beauty. The affix *su* in *subhagaḥ* and *sumukhī* indicate that the couple is suitable for mutual affection. Because her face is like the moon, there will be light even in the darkness.

### Text 20

*rādhā-mugdha-mukhāravinda-madhupas trailokya-mauli-sthalī-*  
*nepathyocita-nīla-ratnam avanī-bhārāvatārāntakaḥ \|*  
*svacchandaṁ vraja-sundarī-jana-manas-toṣa-pradoṣodayaḥ*  
*kaṁsa-dhvaṁsana-dhūma-ketur avatu tvāṁ devakī-nandanaḥ \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

radha - Of Radha; mugdha - beautiful; mukhdravinda - on the lotus-face; madhupas - the bee; trailokya - of the three worlds; mauli - \[which is\] the crest; sthali - of the place \[Vrndavana\]; nepathyocila - suitable \[to be\] the ornament; nila - blue \[sapphire\]; ratnam - the jewel; avam - for the earth; bhara - \[which are\] a burden; avatar a - who have appeared \[the demons\]; antakah -the destroyer; svacchandam - spontaneously; vraja-sundari-jana -of the cowherdesses (gopis); manas - of the minds; tosa - for the satisfaction; pradosodayah - \[and\] the evening; kamsa - of Kamsa; dhvamsana - for the destruction; dhuma-ketur - the comet; avatu -may \[he\] protect; tvam - you; devaki-nandanah - the son of Devaki (Krsna).

**Перевод:**

**May the son of Yaśodā-- the bee on the beautiful lotus face of Rādhā, the sapphire jewel suitable to ornament Vṛndāvana, the crest jewel of the three worlds, the comet to destroy Kaṁsa and the evening incarnate to satisfy the hearts of the gopīs spontaneously--protect you.**

**Commentary:** At the end of this section, the poet desires to give blessings.

May the son of Devakī (Yaśodā) protect you. He is the bee, the person who protects sweetness (madhupaḥ) on the beautiful (mugdha) lotus mouth of Rādhā. He causes an increase in her face’s sweetness by being eternally inclined to Rādhā. Or madhupaḥ can mean “he who drinks with great thirst the sweetness of her face.” He is the suitable sapphire to ornament Vṛndāvana, which is the crest jewel of the three worlds. It is said aho madhupurī dhanyā vaikuṇṭhāc ca garīyasi: ah, Mathurā district is more fortunate and greater than Vaikuṇṭha. (Padma Purāṇa) He is the destroyer of demons who are born as burdens to the earth. He is the malefic planet for destroying Kaṁsa. It is suggested that just by Kṛṣṇa’s birth, Kaṁsa was destroyed. Or dhūmaketu can mean fire, a natural destroyer. This indicates that Kṛṣṇa’s quality called sāmarthya or being fully capable is useful to produce his spontaneous pastimes. Thus it is said in this verse that by his will he is the boundless (ciram) night for fulfilling the desires in the hearts of the gopīs. Or ciram can modify the verb. May Kṛṣṇa eternally protect you. Some quote the following from the Purāṇas (to explain the word Devakī being used in Vraja):

*dve nāmnī nanda-bhāryāyā yaśodā devakīty api \|*  
*ataḥ sakhuyam abhūt tasyāḥ devakyā śauri-jāyayā \|\|*

**Nanda’s wife is called by two names: Yaśodā and Devakī. Thus Yaśodā was friends with Devakī, wife of Vasudeva.**

This fifth section, titled Sākāṅkṣa-puṇḍarikākṣa, in which Kṛṣṇa’s eyes are overcome by Cupid because of his desire to meet with Rādhā, increased by her love for him, is like the fifth arrow of Cupid.

## Part 6: Kṛṣṇa full of longing

### Text 1

*ṣaṣṭhaḥ sargaḥ*  
*sotkaṇṭha-vaikuṇṭhaḥ*  
*atha tāṁ gantum aśaktāṁ ciram anuraktāṁ latā-gṛhe dṛṣṭvā \|*  
*tac-caritaṁ govinde manasija-mande sakhī prāha \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

atha - Then; tam - her (Radha); gantum - to move; asaktam -unable; ciram - for a long time; anuraktam - attached \[to Krsna\]; lata - of creepers; grhe - in a bower; drstva - having seen; tac - of her (Radha); caritam - \[about\] the activities; govinde - to Govinda (Krsna); manasija - by the Cupid; mande - pained; sakhi - the female friend; praha - spoke.

**Перевод:**

**The sakhī spoke to Govinda, pained by Cupid, about the activities of Rādhā, attached to him for a long time, after seeing her in a creeper bower and unable to move.**

**Commentary:** In the fifth part, Kṛṣṇa’s longing and going to the meeting was described. Now in the sixth part the sakhī speaks to Kṛṣṇa about Rādhā, who cannot go, since she is suffering from the arrows of Cupid, and is situated in an attractive designated bower doing various actions.

The word *atha* is used to indicate auspiciousness or the next event. The sakhī spoke to Govinda about the activities of Rādhā (*tac-caritam*). Kṛṣṇa was tortured by Cupid (*manasija)* or he considered Cupid to be insignificant since he himself is a million Cupids. She spoke to him after having seen Rādhā, attached to Govinda for a long time, in the creeper house. Rādhā knew that he would come there and pacify her. Rādhā was unable to move because of the actions of Cupid. She saw that Rādhā was attached to Govinda and unable to go for a long time because of pain.

## Song 12: Radha anxiously waits for Krishna

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 12 \|\|**  
**Guṇakarī-rāga, rūpaka-tāla**

### Text 2

*paśyati diśi diśi rahasi bhavantam \|*  
*tad-adhara-madhura-madhūni pibantam \|\|2\|\|*

*nātha hare sīdati rādhā vāsa-gṛhe \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

pasyati - \[She\] looks; disi disi - in all directions; rahasi - alone; bhavantam - for you; tad - her; adhara - of lips; madhura - sweet; madhuni - the nectar; pibantam - drinking, natha - О master! hare -О Krsna! sidati - Lies; radha - Radha; vasa-grhe - in the bower.

**Перевод:**

**Alone, Rādhā looks in all directions for you who drink the sweet nectar of her lips.**  
**O master! O Hari! Rādhā lies morose in the bower. (chorus)**

**Commentary:** *Guṇakarī rāga* is described as follows:

*rāgotsukā kānta-pathi pratīkṣāṁ*  
*āpālayantī mṛdu-puṣpa-talpā \|*  
*itas tataḥ prerita-dṛṣṭir ārtā*  
*śyāmā tanur guṇa-karī prasiddhā \|\|*

**Guṇakarī is famous as having a blackish body, glancing here and there, being afflicted, having a soft flower bed, gazing towards the path of her lover, and being fond of music.**

The *tāla* was described previously. The meaning is this. O master! O Hari! Rādhā lies morose in the appointed bower. Hari indicates “one who can remove the suffering of separation.” *Nātha* means a person who is requested. Thus *natha* implies “O master, it is not proper to allow the suffering of your devotee to continue, since you are capable of stopping it.” This is the chorus.

Now the rest of the verse is explained. She shows the remarkable transformation of anurāga. All alone, she looks in all directions for you alone, who drink the tasty nectar of her lips, or who hear the syllables which are sweet like honey since they touched her lips. Out of respect “hearing” is called “drinking.”

Instead of *tad-adhara* sometimes *tvad-adhara* is seen. Some explain that *tad* would mean that he drinks the nectar from the lips of another woman. This is not a proper explanation however since that would be contrary to the characteristics of a woman ready to meet her lover. It is said *tribhuvanam api tan-mayaṁ virahe*: in separation the whole world also feels the same.

### Text 3

*tvad-abhisaraṇa-rabhasena valantī \|*  
*patati padāni kiyantī calantī \|\|3\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tvad - With you; abhisarana - for a meeting; rabhasena - out of eagerness; valanti - turning; patati - \[she\] falls; padani - steps; kiyanti - a few; calanti - moving.

**Перевод:**

**Turning out of eagerness to meet you, moving a few steps, she falls on the ground.**

**Commentary:** With eagerness to meet you she turns, proceeds some steps, and then falls. She cannot walk since she faints out of separation from you.

### Text 4

*vihita-viśada-bisa-kiśalaya-valayā \|*  
*jīvati param iha tava rati-kalayā \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vihita - \[Are\] made; visada - white; bisa - of the water lily; kisalaya - of the buds; valaya - whose bracelets; jivati - \[she\] lives; param -only; iha - now; tava - your; rati - for love; kalaya - out of desire.

**Перевод:**

**She continues to live only because of desire for your love which she previously experienced. She wears bracelets made of white water lily buds.**

**Commentary:** If it is impossible for you to come, then how can she remain alive? She continues to live only waiting for you to come, with a desire for your love which she previously experienced, or she lives only because of the increase of skill in your love making. She wears bracelets make of white water lily buds or suitably made by sakhīs (*vihita*) to relieve her of pain. *Kalā* means increase, time or desire according to *Viśva.*

### Text 5

*muhur avalokita-maṇḍana-līlā \|*  
*madhu-ripur aham iti bhāvana-śīlā \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

muhur - Repeatedly; avalokita - \[that she has\] seen; mandana - \[is your\] decoration; lila - \[she is one\] whose imitation; madhu-ripur - “Madhuripu (Krsna); aham - I \[am\]”; iti - thus; bhavana - \[is\] thinking; sila - \[and she is one\] whose habit.

**Перевод:**

**Repeatedly she imitates your decorations that she has seen. She thinks, “I am Kṛṣṇa, the enemy of Madhu.”**

**Commentary:** She imitates (*līlā*) your form with decorations like peacock feather crown and makara earrings which she has seen previously. She becomes absorbed in thought thinking, “I am the enemy of Madhu.” The inclination is to think, “Secretly wearing your clothing, I am the enemy of Madhu.” *Līla* is a particular type of gesture. Vatsyāyana says:

*priya-janasya ceṣṭā vānurāgātiśayena vā \|*  
*kriyate’nukṛtiḥ seyaṁ līloktā muni-sattamaiḥ \|\|*

**The best of sages say that** ***līlā*** **refers to actions of the lover or imitation of the lover because of extreme anurāga.**

*gatyānurāga-smita-vibhramekṣitair*  
*manoramālāpa-vihāra-vibhramaiḥ \|*  
*ākṣipta-cittāḥ pramadā ramā-pates*  
*tās tā viceṣṭā jagṛhus tad-ātmikāḥ \|\|*

**As the cowherd women remembered Kṛṣṇa, their hearts were overwhelmed by his movements and loving smiles, his playful glances and enchanting talks, and by the many other pastimes he would enjoy with them. Thus absorbed in thoughts of Kṛṣṇa, the Lord of Rādhā, the gopīs began acting out his various pastimes.**

*gati-smita-prekṣaṇa-bhāṣaṇādiṣu*  
*priyāḥ priyasya pratirūḍha-mūrtayaḥ \|*  
*asāv ahaṁ tv ity abalās tad-ātmikā*  
*vyavediṣuḥ kṛṣṇa-vihāra-vibhramāḥ \|\|*

**Because the beloved gopīs were absorbed in thoughts of their beloved Kṛṣṇa, their bodies imitated his way of moving and smiling, his glances, his speech and other distinctive features. Deeply identifying with and maddened by remembering his pastimes, they declared to one another, “I am Kṛṣṇa!” SB 10.30.2-3**

Madhu-ripu means “he is even the enemy of spring.” Thinking of his hard heart, the sakhī criticizes him indirectly. “He passes time with great determination in his heart.” Or Madhu-ripu can mean “He has made himself useless without pastimes with Rādhā.”

### Text 6

*tvaritam upaiti na katham abhisāram \|*  
*harir iti vadati sakhīm anuvāram \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tvaritam - “Quickly; upaiti - has he come; na - not; katham - why; abhisaram - to the meeting place; harir - Hari?” iti - Thus; vadati - she says; sakhim - to \[her\] female friend; anuvaram - repeatedly.

**Перевод:**

**She repeatedly says to her sakhī “Why has Hari not come quickly to the meeting place?”**

**Commentary:** Overcome by love, she expresses pain in trying to meet him. Hari means he who removes the pain of separation or one who steals my heart. Repeatedly she says “Why has he not come quickly to the meeting place?”

### Text 7

*śliṣyati cumbati jala-dhara-kalpam \|*  
*harir upagata iti timiram analpam \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

slisyati - \[She\] embraces; cumbati - \[and she\] kisses; jala-dhara - a cloud; kalpam - \[which is\] like; harir - “Hari; upagata - \[has\] come”; iti - \[thinking\] thus; timiram - the darkness; analpam - many times.

**Перевод:**

**Thinking you have come, she embraces and kisses the darkness, black as a rain cloud, many times.**

**Commentary:** Confused by the madness of love, absorbed in you, she thinks many times (*analpam*) or with great reverence “Hari has come.” She embraces and kisses the darkness. *Analpam* can modify the verb. She embraces and kisses the darkness many times. The darkness is like a rain cloud.

### Text 8

*bhavati vilambini vigalita-lajjā \|*  
*vilapati roditi vāsaka-sajjā \|\|8\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

bhavati - \[She\] is; vilambini - \[when you are\] late; vigalita - \[is\] given up; lajja - \[one\] by whom shyness; vilapati - \[she\] laments; roditi - \[and she\] weeps; vasaka-sajja - the woman decorated to receive her lover.

**Перевод:**

**Having decorated herself nicely in expectation, she gives up shyness when you are late, laments and begins to weep.**

**Commentary:** Since you are late, she laments with no sense of shame. “O merciless one! Why do you show such indifference to me?” She weeps (*roditi)*. She had decorated herself *(vāsaka-sajjā)* suitably for all pastimes in meeting with her lover in the pastime bower.

*bharatādyair abhidadhe strīṇāṁ vāsas tu vāsakaḥ \|*  
*sva-vāsaka-vaśāt kānte sameṣyati gṛhaṁ prati \|*  
*sajjīkaroti cātmānaṁ yā sā vāsaka-sajjakā \|\|*

**Bharata and others call the clothing of women** ***vāsaka.*** **The** ***vāsaka-sajjā*** **decorates herself since the lover will come to her room because of her decoration.**

She is a *vāsaka-sajjā*. This is described:

*bhaved vāsaka-sajjāsau sajjitāṅga-ratālayā \|*  
*niśityāgamanaṁ bhartur dvārekṣaṇa-parā yathā \|\|*

**The** ***vāsaka-sajjā*** **decorates her body and the room for pastimes. At night she constantly looks to the door expectedly her lover to come. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 1.76**

### Text 9

*śrī-jayadeva-kaver idam uditam \|*  
*rasika-janaṁ tanutām atimuditam \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva - Sri Jayadeva; haver - by the poet; idam - this \[song\]; uditam - recited; rasika - who taste rasa; janam - \[to\] those; tanutam - may \[it\] give; atimuditam - great bliss.

**Перевод:**

**May this song recited by Jayadeva give great bliss to those who taste rasa.**

**Commentary:** May this song narrated by the poet Jayadeva give great bliss to persons whose hearts appreciate śṛṅgāra-rasa. This means that the work should be relished by those with real taste for bhakti endowed with śrṅgāra-rasa.

### Text 10

*vipula-pulaka-pāliḥ sphīta-sīt-kāram antar-*  
*janita-jaḍimam kāku-vyākulaṁ vyāharantī \|*  
*tava kitava vidhāyāmanda-kandarpa-cintāṁ*  
*rasa-jala-nidhi-magnā dhyāna-lagnā mṛgakṣī \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vipula - Are abundant; pulaka - of whose bristling hairs; palih -the tips; sphita - loud; sit-karam - making hissing noises; antar -internally; janita - arises; jadimam - in whom a stupor; kaku - with broken words; vyakulam - confusedly; vyaharanti - speaking; tava -\[of\] your \[form\]; kitava - о cheater; vidhaya - placing \[in her heart\]; amanda - many; kandarpa - more beautiful than a million Cupids (or as Cupids); cintarh - thoughts; rasa - of rasa; jala-nidhi - in the ocean; magna - merged; dhyana - in meditation; lagna - absorbed; mrgaksi - the fawn-eyed woman (Radha).

**Перевод:**

**O cheater! Rādhā with fawn-like eyes is merged in the ocean of śṛṅgāra-rasa, absorbed in meditation, concentrating on the form more beautiful than a million Cupids, uttering broken words with pitiful sound, making loud, hissing noises, and becoming stunned from internal transformations, while her hairs stand on end.**

**Commentary:** O cheater! Rādhā with fawn-like eyes is submerged in the ocean of śṛṅgāra-rasa, absorbed in meditation, placing (*vidhāya*) in her heart thoughts of your form more attractive than millions of Cupid (*amanada-kandarpa-cintām*) or placing in her heart thoughts of unlimited love for you, or placing in her heart unlimited thoughts of love—such as “When will he, dearer than a million lives, come to me?”

What is she doing? She utters (*vyāharantī*) sounds in a pitiful tone (*kāku-vyākulam*) on observing your attachment to touching her breast, drinking the nectar of her lips and loosening her belt when you become agitated with madness by touching her. This describes her in the state called *kuṭtamitā*. She speaks, trying to forbid him, saying angrily, “Let go of me! Let go of me!” *Kākuḥ striyāṁ vikāro yaḥ śoka-bhīty-ādibhir dhvaneḥ: kāku* refers to a woman’s condition in which she makes sounds of lamentation and fear. *Kuṭtamita* is described as follows:

*keśādharādi-grahaṇe modamāne’pi mānase \|*  
*duḥkhiteva bahiḥ kupyed yatra kuṭṭamitaṁ hi tat \|\|*

**In the state of** ***kuṭtamita*** **the woman appears distressed and exhibits outward anger when her lover holds her hair or kisses her, though internally she enjoys it. Rasārvaṇa-sudhākara 1.358**

She also makes loud hissing sounds (*sphīta-śītkaram*). She makes loud sounds while scratching him with her nails. Having described external transformations, she now describes internal transformations. She becomes stunned, which arises from internal change—bliss in the heart. This is the bhāva called *rata.* Because of these bhāvas she becomes submerged in the ocean of śṛṅgāra-rasa. When bliss spreads through the body, the body becomes stunned, unable to act. This hints at the ninth stage called *jaḍatā* (being stunned). Though the symptoms are not seen by others, who is capable of describing what type of rasa will arise on meeting you? However, even others when merged in the ocean of rasa become stunned, their hairs stand on end, they making hissing sounds, utter broken words, and are full of thoughts.

### Text 11

*aṅgeṣv ābharaṇaṁ karoti bahuśaḥ patre’pi sañcāriṇi*  
*prāptaṁ tvāṁ pariśaṅkate vitanute śayyāṁ ciraṁ dhyāyati \|*  
*ity ākalpa-vikalpa-talpa-racanā-saṅkalpa-līlā-śata-*  
*vyāsaktāpi vinā tvayā vara-tanur naiṣā niśāṁ neṣyati \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

angesv - In \[her\] limbs; abharanam - decoration; karoti - \[she\] makes; bahusah - in many ways; patre’pi - even the leaves; sancarini - \[when they\] move; praptam - \[have\] come; tvam - \[that\] you; parisankate - she thinks; vitanute - \[she\] spreads; sayyam - the bed; ciram - for a long time; dhyayati - \[and she\] meditates; ity - thus; akalpa - in dressing; vihalpa - in speculating \[that you have come\]; talpa - the bed; racana - in making; sahkalpa - of thinking; Illa -ways; sata - \[and\] in a hundred; vyasaktapi - even absorbed; vina - without; tvaya - you; vara - is beautiful; tanur - \[Radha\] whose body; na - not; esa - she; nisam - the night; nesyati - will \[be able to\] pass.

**Перевод:**

**Without you, beautiful Rādhā, absorbed in dressing, speculating that you have come, making the bed, and thinking in a hundred ways, will not be able to pass the night. She decorates her limbs in many ways and concludes you have come when the leaves in the trees move. She spreads the bed with pillows and meditates for a long time.**

**Commentary:** “She has already enjoyed with me.Why should I go there?” Though she has enjoyed with you, without your personal presence it will not be possible for her to pass the night in her present state.

O Kṛṣṇa! Without you, Rādhā with beautiful limbs will not be able to live until the dawn (*na niśām neṣayti*). She has decorated herself (*ākalpa*), thinking you are coming (*vikalpa*), prepares the bed, and thinks hundreds of thoughts, such as making you come by sending a messenger. Desiring to meet you, her mind does not stop thinking.

Her attention to various actions is described. She puts ornaments on her limbs in many ways for your happiness or auspiciousness. This describes her dressing *(ākalpa).* When leaves move because of the birds, she thinks (*śaṅkate)* definitely *(pari*) that you have come. This shows her thinking (*vikalpa*).

She spreads the bed meant for your amorous play with various colorful pillows and other items. This shows making the bed. She meditates for a long time on your method of coming. This illustrates *saṅkalpa*. By these actions she spends her time with hope that you will come. This behavior is called *ākalpa.*

*ākalpa sā tu kathitā priyasyāgamane sati \|*  
*didṛkṣayobhayos tadvat sa-sambhramam alaṅkṛtiḥ \|\| iti \|*

***Ākalpa*** **means that the women decorated with ornaments in confusion looks with both eyes to see her lover come.**

*Ākalpa* means dressing up according to *Amara-koṣa. Vikalpa* means conjecture. *Saṅkalpa* means activity of the mind

### Text 12

*kiṁ viśrāmyasi kṛṣṇa-bhogi-bhavane bhāṇḍīra-bhūmī-ruhi*  
*bhrātar yāsi na dṛṣṭi-gocaram itaḥ sānanda-nandāspadam \|*  
*rādhāyā vacanaṁ tad adhvaga-mukhān nandāntike gopato*  
*govindasya jayanti sāyam atithi-prāśastya-garbhā giraḥ \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kim - Why; visramyasi - are you staying; krsna-bhogi - of the black snake (or, of Krsna the enjoyer); bhavane - in the house; bhandira - Bhandira; bhumi-ruhi - at the \[banyan\] tree; bhratar - о brother; yasi - you go; na - not; drsti - of view; gocaram - in the field \[in the vicinity or neighborhood); itah - from there; sananda - prosperous; nandaspadam - to the house of Nanda; radhaya - of Radha; vacanam - the words; tad - then; adhvaga - of \[their meeting\]; mukhan - the occasion; nandantike - in front of Nanda; gopato - keeping secret; govindasya - of Govinda (Krsna); jayanti - may \[they\] remain with excellence; sayam - in the evening; atithi - of the guest; prasastya -the praiseworthiness; garbha - containing; girah - the words.

**Перевод:**

**The guest, repeating Rādhā’s words, said, “Do not remain at the house of the black snake at the Bhāṇḍira banyan tree. Go from there to the prosperous house of Nanda in the neighborhood.” May the words of Govinda praising that guest in front of Nanda, but which held a hidden meaning in reply to Rādhā’s words, remain ever excellent.**

**Commentary:** At the end of this section the poet offers blessings.

May the words remain with excellence (*jayanti)* since they have been produced with a wealth of enjoyment by the desire of senior devotees, with skilful descriptions and truth spoken by the author with great bhāva. These words were the praise uttered to the guest, welcoming him, in the evening. Within the words (*garbhā*) there is great happiness since they reveal indications of the meeting of the lovers. The words are endowed with special intentions within, when Kṛṣṇa praises the guest. These are the words of Govinda, who keeps secret Rādhā’s message filled with hints of a meeting in front of Nanda. What are the words of the guest? “O brother traveler!” The word “brother” indicates gentleness. “Do you take rest at the banyan tree? Do not relax there since that is the house of the black snake (the pastime place of Kṛṣṇa the enjoyer of śṛṅgāra-rasa). In either case you should not stay there.” This is advice to the brother. This is an example of *śleṣa* or pun. “Go from the banyan tree to the residence of Nanda filled with all prosperity. Give up the empty snake hole and be happy as the guest in that prosperous house. It can be seen from here. It is close.” May the words of Rādhā remain forever excellent, since they revealed Kṛṣṇa’s skill in words in understanding the hidden meaning of the traveler’s words.

Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are the *ālambanas,* filled with qualities like cleverness. The *svayam-dūtī* (natural messenger) is the leader of a group of gopīs. The king of rasas, śṛṅgāra, is described. The uddipana is the banyan tree.

In this section titled *Dhanya-vaikuṇṭha*, Kṛṣṇa recovers from fainting (*vaikuṇṭha*) and is fortunate *(dhanya*) because of being the object of Rādhā’s love.

## Part 7: Clever Kṛṣṇa

### Text 1

*saptamaḥ sargaḥ*  
*nāgara-nārāyaṇaḥ*  
*atrāntare ca kulaṭā-kula-vartma-pāta-*  
*sañjāta-pātaka iva sphuṭa-lāñchana-śrīḥ \|*  
*vṛndāvanāntaram adīpayad aṁśu-jālair*  
*dik-sundarī-vadana-candana-bindur induḥ \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

atrantare - Then; ca - and; kulata - of the gopis; kula - of the group; vartma - the path; pata - by obstructing; sahjata - \[was\] caused; pataka - \[that\] by which \[its\] sin; iva - as if; sphuta - is evident; lanchana - of a mark; srih - on which the radiance; vrndavanantaram - the middle of the Vrndavana forest; adipayad - lights up; amsu - of rays; jalair - with a mass; dik - of the direction; sundarl - eastern; vadana - on the face; candana - of sandalwood; bindur - a spot; induh - the moon.

**Перевод:**

**The moon, like a sandalwood spot on the face of the eastern direction, shines with a mass of rays in the middle of the dense Vṛndāvana forest. It has a conspicuous beauty mark, which is like an indicator of its sin-caused by obstructing the path of the gopīs trying to meet Kṛṣṇa in that forest.**

**Commentary:** Having described the anurāga of the couple previously, now, in this section, the poet describes vipralambha-śṛṅgāra-rasa along with its cause, by revealing intense bhāva with astonishing rasa, by entering into a different type of rasa.

On this occasion the moon illuminates the middle of Vṛndāvana forest with all its rays. This moon is extraordinary since it lights up the middle of that forest and nowhere else. Because the forest is very thick in the middle, the light must be very bright. Or the moon lights up the limbs of the young man and woman situated within the forest since it is an assistant in their love. The moon acts as an uddīpana as follows.

It is like a round tilaka mark of sandalwood mixed with kuṁkuma, on the face of the beautiful woman known as the eastern direction. This shows Kṛṣṇa’s desire to put tilaka on Rādhā’s face. The moon is described as a cause of separation. It has a splendid mark, very visible. The cause of separation is explained through a detail. This mark is like its indication of its sin, caused by the moon obstructing the path of the women going to meet Kṛṣṇa. It is said:

*kim uttīrṇaḥ panthāḥ kupita-bhujagī-bhoga-viṣamo*  
*viṣoḍhā bhūyasyaḥ kim iti kulapālī-kaṭu-giraḥ \|*  
*iti smāraṁ smāraṁ dara-dalita-śīta-dyuti-rucau*  
*sarojākṣī śoṇaṁ diśi nayana-koṇaṁ vikirati \|\|*

**What kind of path is this? It twists like the winding coils of an angry snake. Why must we tolerate this?” Repeatedly remembering the gopī’s harsh lotus eyed Rādhā cast an angry glance from the reddish corner of her eyes in the direction of the very faint moon. Padyāvali 196**

The ornaments are *rūpaka* and *utprekṣa.*

Some explain that this verse shows that one sakhī could not tolerate the separation experienced by Rādhā and thus described a meeting at the end of the last section. In this seventh part she describes astonishing transformations of the rasa called *vaicittya-vipralamba.\[\[21\]\]*

The sequence is as follows. In the previous section a union took place by means of a messenger. The following eight verses describe her lamentation in that prema (during which there is imagined separation). After that, an unnamed sakhī describes Rādhā’s enjoyment in two songs with feelings of disappointment by using the third person to indicate Rādhā. In the last verse of this section, drinking the nectar of their words of affection, she describes Kṛṣṇa in front of Rādhā, with signs of their intimate meeting. In the eighth part, Rādhā yells “Go, go!” though she is conciliated, since Rādhā is mad with intense emotions. That will be clear later.

This has been supplied to satisfy the intimate devotees. Please tolerate it. By this verse, it is also understood that meetings with other gopīs do not take place when Kṛṣṇa plays with Rādhā. Though the moon developed a blemish by obstructing the other gopīs’ path, it is still splendid by producing affection between the couple. The rest is the same. The rest of the verse has the same meaning as the first explanation.

### Text 2

*prasarati śaśa-dhara-bimbe vihita-vilambe ca mādhave vidhurā \|*  
*viracita-vividha-vilāpaṁ saparitāpaṁ cakāroccaiḥ \|\|2\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

prasarati - \[While it\] spreads \[its rays\]; sasa-dhara - of the rabbitbearer (the moon); bimbe - the disk; vihita - is made; vilambe - \[while he is one\] by whom a delay; ca - and; madhave - Madhava (Krsna); vidhura - the woman (Radha) suffering from separation; viracita - performed; vividha - in various ways; vilapam - lamentations; saparitapam - with great pain; cakara - makes; uccaih - loudly.

**Перевод:**

**Suffering separation, Rādhā wails loudly in various ways and experiences pain, while the moon spreads its rays and Kṛṣṇa delays.**

**Commentary:** Having shown the cause of separation, now separation itself is described. Rādhā laments loudly in various ways. “O lover where are you? O deceiver, why do you ignore me?” She experiences great pain (*saparitāpam*). She is pained by separation (*vidhurā).* At that time, the disk of the moon appeared while the crest jewel of heroes, Mādhava, was late in keeping the appointment. He was hiding, in agreement with the sakhīs, to show some fun in prema. The moon is an uddīpana.

## Song 13: Krishna has not come to meet Radha

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 13 \|\|**  
**Mālava-rāga yati-tāla**

### Text 3

*kathita-samaye’pi harir ahaha na yayau vanam \|*  
*mama viphalam idam amala-rūpam api yauvanam \|\|3\|\|*

*yāmi he kam iha śaraṇaṁ sakhī-jana-vacana-vañcitā \|\|dhruva\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kathita - Appointed; samaye - at the time; ’pi - even; harir - Krsna; ahaha - alas; na - not; yayau - did come; vanam - to the bower, mama - Of mine; viphalam - \[are\] useless; idam - this; amala - pure; rupam - form; api - \[and\] even, yauvanam - \[my\] youth.

yami - Should I go; he - о \[friend\]; ham - to whom; iha - now; saranam - refuge \[for alleviating my inner grief\]; sakhi-jana - of \[my\] friends; vacana - by the words; vancita - cheated?

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa did not come to the bower at the appointed time. My exquisite beauty and astonishing youth are useless.**  
**O friend! At this time to whom should I surrender for alleviating my inner grief, after being cheated by my friends? (chorus)**

**Commentary:** Her lamentation is described in eight verses. The *rāga* and *tāla* were described previously. The heroine is *vipralabḍhā* (cheated):

*preṣya dūtīṁ svayaṁ dattvā saṅketaṁ nāgataḥ priyaḥ \|*  
*yasyās tena vinā duḥsthā vipralabdhā tu sā yathā \|\|*

**The** ***vipralabhā*** **woman is unsettled without her lover, when her lover does not come after personally sending a messenger and appointing the place. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 1.78**

O friend! At this time to whom (*kam)* should I surrender for alleviating my inner grief? Or should I surrender to water (*kam*) to alleviate the pain? Without him I will drown myself in water. I have been cheated by my friends. I have been cheated by their words of instruction. They are like other people (*jana*). I have been prevented by their various words again and again, discouraging my efforts to see him. *Kam* can mean water according to *Viśva*. *Śaraṇam* means a house or protector according to *Amara-koṣa*. This is the chorus.

“Have you only been cheated by your friends?” No Kṛṣṇa has cheated me also. Hari means “he who takes away all suffering just by remembering him.” At the time which was agreed, he should have come to the place appointed by me. Ah! How unfortunate (*ahaha)!* He has not arrived at that appointed bower. Thus my newly astonishing youth and my pure beauty are both useless. The characteristics of youth are described:

*nitambo vipulo madhyaṁ kṛśam aṅgaṁ vara-dyuti \|*  
*pīnau kucāv uru-yugmaṁ rambhābhaṁ pūrṇa-yauvane \|\|*

**In the fourth stage of kaiśora, a girl's hips become broad,her waist becomes slender,her body becomes lustrous, her breastsbecome large,and her thighs become like the trunks of a banana tree. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 10.21**

The sweetness of youth is described:

*na vitrastā kā te pratiyuvatir āsīn mukha-rucā*  
*dadhāra staimityaṁ praṇaya-ghana-vṛṣṭyā tava na kā \|*  
*vraje śiṣyā kābhūn na hi tava kalāyām iti harer*  
*nikuñja-svārājye tvam asi rasike paṭṭa-mahiṣī \|\|*

**What rival gopī is not afraid of the beauty of your face? What gopī does not become stunned by seeing the showers from the cloud of your love? What girl in Vraja hasnot become your student in the various arts? Oexpert at relishing rasa! You are the reigning queen of bowers. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 10.23**

*bhūṣaṇābhūṣaṇābhyāṁ hi camatkāreṇa netrayoḥ \|*  
*paraspara-smṛta-mudam amalaṁ taṁ prakīrtitam \|\|*

**Beauty is described as pure joy creating astonishment to the eyes, with ornaments or without ornaments.**

### Text 4

*yad-anugamanāya niśi gahanam api śīlitam \|*  
*tena mama hṛdayam idam asama-śara-kīlitam \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

уad - Him (Krsna); anugamanaya - in order to follow; nisi - at night; gahanam - the dense forest; api - even; silitam - \[is\] experienced. Lena - Because of him \[Krsna\]; mama - of mine; hrdayam - heart; idam - this; asama - of love; sara - by the arrow; kllitam - \[is\] pierced.

**Перевод:**

**My heart is pierced by the arrow of love because of Kṛṣṇa. In pursuit of him I must experience this dense forest at night.**

**Commentary:** To follow Kṛṣṇa (*yad*) I must experience the dense forest at night. Or I must frequent the forest though that should not be done. Because of him my vitals are pierced by the crooked arrows of love. They are crooked because love follows a crooked path.

### Text 5

*mama maraṇam eva varam ati-vitatha-ketanā \|*  
*kim iha viṣahāmi virahānalam acetanā \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

mama - my; maranam - death; eva - only; varam - \[is\] better; ati-vitatha - of the truth; ketana - \[since I am one in whom there is\] an indication (or, since my body is completely false); kim - How; iha -here; visahami - \[can\] I endure; virahanalam - the fire of separation; acetana - without knowledge.

**Перевод:**

**It is better that I die, since the indications of the truth are now evident. How can I, without knowledge in this forest, endure the fire of separation in the forest?**

**Commentary:** Because of being cheated, it is better than I die rather than maintain my body. My body is completely false (*ati-vitatha*) since it does not perform its functions. Or I now have an indication (*ketana*) of truth, surpassing illusion (*ati-vitatha*), though I was expecting my lover to come. Or I know the invitation given by my sakhīs has been completely fruitless (*ati-vitatha*). Or my bower *(ketana*) decorated with a bed has become completey useless. The reason is given. I am without knowledge in this forest (*iha*). Or I am conscious (*cetanā*) of Kṛṣṇa (*a*=name of the lord). Or my intelligence is fixed on Kṛṣṇa in this way. How can I tolerate the fire of separation? Therefore it is somewhat (*varam*) better if I attain him in the next life.

*Varam* means a little desirable according to *Dharaṇi. Vitatha* means false according to *Amara-koṣa. Ketanam* means a house or a mark or invitation according to *Viśva.* Another version has *ativitatha-cetanā.* All my aspirations are useless. *Cetanam* can mean expectation according to *Viśva.*

### Text 6

*mām ahaha vidhurayati madhura-madhu-yāminī \|*  
*kāpi harim anubhavati kṛta-sukṛta-kāminī \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

mam - Me; ahaha - ah; vidhurayati - causes pain; madhura - pleasant; madhu - spring; yamini - the night, kapi - What; harim - Krsna; anubhavati - experiences; krta - performed; sukrta - by pious acts; kamini - woman whose desires are fulfilled.

**Перевод:**

**Ah! The pleasant spring night only brings me pain. What woman with fulfilled desires, after performing pious acts, can experience Kṛṣṇa?**

**Commentary:** Ah, what misfortunate *(ahaha*)! This charming spring night pains me. What woman whose desires are fulfilled (*kāminī*) because of performing pious acts experiences the person who takes away her suffering (*harim)* by embraces? *Vidhura* means distressed according to *Viśva. Madhura* means pleasant or attractive according to *Dharaṇi.*

### Text 7

*ahaha kalayāmi valayādi-maṇi-bhūṣaṇam \|*  
*hari-viraha-dahana-vahanena bahu-dūṣaṇam \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ahaha - How astonishing; kalayami - I see; valayadi - such as bracelets; mani - on which \[there are\] jewels; bhusanam - the ornements; hari - from Krsna; viraha - of separation; dahana - the fire; vahanena - because of carrying; bahu - much; dusanam - \[ to be those\] in which \[there is\] fault.

**Перевод:**

**How astonishing! I see that the jeweled ornaments such as bracelets are filled with faults since they possess the same fire caused by separation from Kṛṣṇa.**

**Commentary:** How astonishing (*ahaha)*! I regard the ornaments made of jewels or inlaid with jewels to be filled with faults (*bahu-dūṣanam*) since they carry the same fire caused by separation from Kṛṣṇa. By the burning of her body, the ornaments also become hot.

### Text 8

*kusuma-sukumāra-tanum atanu-śara-līlayā \|*  
*srag api hṛdi hanti mām ativiṣama-śilayā \|\|8\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kusuma - As a flower; sukumara - \[is\] as delicate; tanum - whose body; atanu - of the Cupid; sara - of the arrows; lilaya - by means of the play; srag - the garland; api - even; hrdi - in the mind; hanti - \[it\] beats; mam - me; ativisama - is most unjust; silaya - whose nature.

**Перевод:**

**The garland beats my mind using the play of Cupid’s arrows, which are most unjust, since I have a body as delicate as a flower.**

**Commentary:** The garland beats my mind by its play of Cupid’s arrows. A garland is made of flowers and flowers are also called “arrows of Cupid.” That play of arrows is most irregular (*ativiṣama-śilayā*) in nature. It is said:

*apūrveyaṁ dhanur vidyā manmathasya mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*śarīram akṣataṁ kṛtvā bhinatty antar-gataṁ manaḥ \|\|*

**The military skill of the great Cupid is astonishing. Wounding the body, he pierces the mind within.**

The arrows released by one person strike another person. But these arrows held by one person pierce the same person. This is most irregular. Or by the action of gross arrows, Cupid (*atanu*) pierces the body. This is never seen and cannot be stopped. Rādhā’s body is more delicate than a flower. A person should be killed by a person of equal status. But in this case it is not so since she is most tender, whereas Cupid is harsh. Her tenderness also indicates that she cannot tolerate the pain. Another version has *kusuma-sukumāra-tanuḥ,* which modifies the garland. The garland is slender (*tanu*) and soft with flowers. Here also there is irregularity since the garland is soft but its arrows are harsh. *Śīla* means nature.

### Text 9

*aham iha nivasāmi na-gaṇita-vana-vetasā \|*  
*smarati madhusūdano mām api na cetasā \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

aham - I; iha - here; nivasami - will remain; na - not; ganita - \[are\] regarded; vana - of the forest; vetasa - by whom the reeds, smarati -Does remember; madhusudano - Madhusudana \[Krsna\]; mam - me; api - even; na - not; cetasa - with \[affectionate\] mind.

**Перевод:**

**I will remain here in the bower, giving no regard for the thick cane. Kṛṣṇa does not remember me with affection.**

**Commentary:** Though suffering, I will stay in this bower, dedicated to him. I will not be aware of the network of forest reeds. This means she will overcome forest obstacles difficult to pass. Or having counted all the trees in searching for him (*gaṇita-vana-vetasā*), will I not (*na)* remain in this bower? Yes I will remain here. Kṛṣṇa does not remember me in his heart. The word Madhusūdana means “he who destroys spring *(madhu*) or madhurya-rasa” and indicates his hard nature and fickleness. Since remembering (*smarati)* means using the mind, the word *cetasā* would then be redundant. Thus the meaning is “He does not remember me with an affectionate mind *(cetasā*).”

### Text 10

*hari-caraṇa-śaraṇa-jayadeva-kavi-bhāratī \|*  
*vasatu hṛdi yuvatir iva komala-kalāvatī \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

hari - Of Krsna; carana - at the feet; sarana - by whom refuge \[was taken\]; jayadeva - Jayadeva; kavi - of the poet; bharati - the speech; vasatu - may \[it\] remain; hrdi - in \[your\] heart; yuvatir - a young woman; iva - as if; komala - soft spoken; kalavati - \[and\] skillful \[in the sixty-four arts\].

**Перевод:**

**May the words of the poet Jayadeva who has surrendered to the feet of the Lord, which are easy to understand and full of poetic skill, like a soft-spoken woman, skilful in the arts, remain in your heart.**

**Commentary:** May the words of Jayadeva who has surrendered at the feet of Kṛṣṇa remain in the hearts of the devotees of Kṛṣṇa! Since he has surrendered to Kṛṣṇa his words are not ordinary. They are easily understood words (*komala*)*. Komala* means having clear meaning or easy to pronounce. The words are endowed with poetic artistry (*kalāvatī).* The words are like a young woman (*yuvatī*) who speaks softly *(komala*) and is skillful at the sixty-four arts (*kalāvatī*).

Or O charming listener (*komala*)! O listener surrendered to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet! In this description of quarrel (*kalau*), the words surpass (*ati*) the wealth of liberation (*ī*). The singer and the listener have no regard for liberation. Or, though young, she surpasses Lakṣmī during quarrels. Or she has a wealth of good fortune (*ī*) among the best of faces in quarreling.

Or O Kṛṣṇa tender with affection for devotees! May your devotee’s words describing the actions of Rādhā remain in your mind like the young girl Rādhā (*yuvatī*). May she also remain there.

### Text 11

*tat kiṁ kām api kāminīm abhisṛtaḥ kiṁ vā kalā-kelibhir*  
*baddho bandhubhir andhakāriṇi vanābhyarṇe kim udbhrāmyati \|*  
*kāntaḥ klānta-manā manāg api pathi prasthātum evākṣamaḥ*  
*saṅketī-kṛta-mañju-vāñjula-latā-kuñje’pi yan nāgataḥ \|\|11\|\|—*

**Пословный перевод:**

tat - Then; kim - perhaps; kam api - some; kaminim - lusty woman; abhisrtah - being approached by; kim - perhaps; va - or; kala-kelibhir - with skillful playing; baddho - obstructed; bandhubhir - by \[his\] friends; andhakarini - dark; vanabhyarne - in the proximity of the forest; kim - \[or\] perhaps; udbhramyati - \[he\] is lost; kantah - the lover; klanta - \[is\] tired; mana - whose mind; manag - for a moment; api - even; pathi - on the path; prasthatum - to proceed; evaksamah - only unable; sanketi-krta - appointed for the meeting; manju - lovely; vanjula - of reeds; lata - \[and\] of creepers; kunje - in the bower; ’pi - even; yan nagatah - \[has\] not arrived.

**Перевод:**

**Why did the lover not come to the attractive reed bower for the appointment? Perhaps he was waylaid by some lusty woman? Or perhaps friends distracted him with skillful playing. Or perhaps he got lost in the dark forest. He must have become tired in mind and could not proceed on the path for a moment.**

**Commentary:** Having described the cheated woman (vipralabdhā) in the previous eight verses, the author now describes her longing, in order to show her prema for Kṛṣṇa.

Why has the attractive lover (kāntaḥ) not come to the attractive cane bower though he made an appointment there? Did he meet with some amorous woman (kāminīm)? The word kāminīm suggests that she considers herself more fortunate since Kṛṣṇa by his nature desires Rādhā. Did she forcibly take him away?

“How could he give me up and meet with others?” Perhaps he was obstructed by constant friends playing skillfully? That also did not happen. Since he is attracted to pastimes with me, he cannot be obstructed by them. Perhaps (kim) coming to meet me and not getting to the appointed place, he is wandering near the appointed place, which is dark because of the shade of trees.

“How can the crest jewel of heroes make a mistake about a place known a thousand times?” It must be that, tortured in mind because of sorrow of separation, he is unable to proceed on the path for a moment. The heroine is called uktā:

*utkā bhavati sā yasyāḥ saṅketaṁ nāgataḥ priyaḥ \|*  
*tasyānāgamane hetuṁ cintayanty ākulā yathā \|\|*

**An uktā woman is one who, when the lover does not keep an appointment, becomes agitated and thinks of the cause of the lover not coming. Śṛṅgāra-tilaka 1.75**

The ornament is called saṁsayākṣepa. This is described by Daṇḍi:

*kim ayaṁ śarad-ambhodaḥ kiṁ vā haṁsa-kadambakam \|*  
*rutaṁ nūpura-saṁvādi śrūyate tan na toyadaḥ \|*  
*ity evaṁ saṁśayākṣepaḥ saṁśayo yan nivartate \|*  
*dharmeṇa haṁsa-sulabhenāspṛṣṭa-ghana-jātinā \|\|*

**“Is that an autumn cloud or a sound of a group of swans?” On hearing the sound of ankle bells, she concludes it could not be a cloud. Where doubt arises because the swan was more likely and cloud was a remote possiblity, it is called saṁsayākṣepa (casting doubt). Kāvyādarśa 1.163-164**

Kim indicates conjecture. Upānta (instead of vanābhyarṇe) means “near” according to Viśva.

### Text 12

*athāgatāṁ mādhavam antareṇa*  
*sakhīm iyaṁ vīkṣya viṣāda-mūkām \|*  
*viśaṅkamānā ramitaṁ kayāpi*  
*janārdanaṁ dṛṣṭavad etad āha \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

athagatam - Then having arrived; madhavam - Madhava (Krsna); antarena - without; sakhim - the female friend; iyam - she (Radha); viksya - having seen; visada - out of sadness; mukam - speechless; visankamana - suspecting; ramitam - \[that he\] was enjoying; kayapi - with some \[other\] woman, janardanam - Krsna; drstavad - as if seeing \[it\]; etad - as follows; aha - \[she\] spoke.

**Перевод:**

**Seeing the sakhī return without Kṛṣṇa, Rādhā speculated that he was enjoying with some other woman. As if seeing the incident, she spoke as follows.**

**Commentary:** After that (*atha*) Rādhā spoke of what was happening as if seeing it. She saw a sakhī approaching without (*antareṇa*) Kṛṣṇa. The sakhī was unable to speak (*viṣāda-mukam*) because of great sorrow. And then Rādhā speculated that Kṛṣṇa was enjoying with some other woman. Janardana indicates “one whose nature is to give pain *(ardana)* to people (*jana)*. Therefore it is to be expected that he will cause me suffering. The meter is *upendra-vajra.* Or the sakhī saw Rādhā who had not attained Kṛṣṇa at the appointed bower. Seeing her friend Rādhā silent because of devastation, the sakhī speculated that Kṛṣṇa was enjoying with another woman.

## Song 14: A competitor gopi with great qualities plays with Krishna

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 14 \|\|**  
**Vasanta-rāga, yati-tāla**

### Text 13

*smara-samarocita-viracita-veśā \|*  
*galita-kusuma-dara-vilulita-keśā \|\|13\|\|*

*kāpi madhuripuṇā vilasati yuvatir adhika-guṇā \|\| dhruva \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

smara - lovemaking; samarocita - approved for; viracita - \[is\] made; vesa - whose clothing; galita - falling out; kusuma - with flowers; dara - slightly; vilulita - \[is\] disheveled; kesa - whose hair.

kapi - Some; madhuripuna - with Madhuripu (Krsna); vilasati - is playing; yuvatir - young woman; adhika - \[are\] great; guna - whose qualities.

**Перевод:**

**She wears clothing suitable for love and her hair is slightly disheveled, with flowers falling out.**  
**Some young woman with great qualities is playing with Kṛṣṇa. (chorus)**

**Commentary:** The *rāga* is *vasanta* and the *tāla* is *ekatālī.* Some woman is playing with Kṛṣṇa. She has many good qualities like beauty. The woman is the initiator of action. This indicates that she takes the dominant role. Thus by saying she has many good qualities she says that this woman has scant qualities since she uses force to enjoy with the man in front of her. Madhuripunā (by the enemy of *madhu* or the enemy of madhura-rasa) is a criticism of Kṛṣṇa, suggesting he does not know about madhurya-rasa and is fickle.

She is dressed suitably for love. Flowers fall from her slightly disheveled hair because Kṛṣṇa grasped her breast and kissed her. As explained before, the woman in a dominant role kisses him while grasping his hair. Another version has *kusuma-bhara*. She has disheveled hair with a mass of flowers falling out.

### Text 14

*hari-parirambhana-calita-vikārā \|*  
*kuca-kalaśopari taralita-hārā \|\|14\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

hari - of Krsna; parirambhana - by the embrace; calita - \[is\] produced; vikara - whose transformation of love; kuca - \[which are her\] breasts; kalasopari - above the pots; taralita - \[is\] swaying; hard - whose necklace.

**Перевод:**

**She develops symptoms of love by the embrace of Kṛṣṇa. She wears a necklace that sways above her two pot-like breasts.**

Commentary: She develops transformations of love by the embrace of Kṛṣṇa. She wears a necklace that sways above her two pot-like breasts. This description suggests that auspicious pots have been established for carrying out a festival of love-making without obstacles, since Hari means “he who takes away suffering in love.” Or it means “one who takes away worries by his expertise in love.”

### Text 15

*vicalad-alaka-lalitānana-candrā \|*  
*tad-adhara-pāna-rabhasa-kṛta-tandrā \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vicalad - Moving; alaka - with locks; lalita - \[is\] attractive; anana-candra - whose moon-face; tad - his; adhara - of lower lips; pana - of the drinking; rabhasa - from the passion; krta - \[is\] produced; tandra - whose exhaustion.

**Перевод:**

**Her moon-like face is attractive with moving locks. She is exhausted from the passion of drinking his lips.**

**Commentary:** She has a moon-like face attractive (*lalita*) with locks of hair swaying because of the play. The face, attractive with locks, suggests a moving moon with its blemishes. Again she is described. She is exhausted by the passion of drinking Kṛṣṇa’s lips. She closes her eyes with the experience of that happiness. She becomes dizzy with the intoxication of drinking his lips’ exquisite sweetness.

### Text 16

*cañcala-kuṇḍala-lalita -kapolā \|*
