*mukharita-raśana-jaghana-gati-lolā \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

cancala - Swaying; kundala - with earrings; lalita - \[are\] attractive; kapola - whose cheeks; mukharita - jinging; rasana - with bells; jaghana - of her hips; gati - with the gait; Iola - shaking.

**Перевод:**

**Her cheeks are attractive with swaying earrings. Her hips move about, jingling with bells.**

**Commentary:** “I cannot stay a moment without you. You alone are my dear love.” Hearing these conciliatory words of Kṛṣṇa, she says, “Cheater! I know about you!” She has cheeks attractive with earrings quivering from shaking her head while saying this, or from the passion of playing. Another version has *dalita* (crushed) instead of *lalita.* Her cheeks are crushed by the earrings swaying back and forth since her cheeks are very soft. She moves her hips and this makes her bells (*raśana*) jingle. As warriors become agitated because of the various instruments playing during battle, she becomes agitated by her bells jingling during pastimes of love.

### Text 17

*dayita-vilokita-lajjita-hasitā \|*  
*bahu-vidha-kūjita-rati-rasa-rasitā \|\|17\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

dayita - By \[her\] lover (Krsna); vilokita - having been seen; lajjita -ashamed; hasita - smiling; bahu-vidha - of many sorts; kujita - \[like\] the cooing \[of birds\]; rati - of lovemaking; rasa - because of the rasa; rasita - making sounds.

**Перевод:**

**She smiles after showing shame when Kṛṣṇa glances at her. She makes a variety of sounds like the sounds of birds.**

**Commentary:** She smiles and becomes ashamed because of Kṛṣṇa’s glances which show his intentions. She becomes ashamed on seeing him in the lower position and herself in the higher position, but since shame is unsuitable in love, she then smiles in enjoyment. It is said that other than in pastimes of love shame is the highest ornament. She makes sounds (*rasitā*) because of attachment during the pastimes of love (*rati-rasa*)*,* like the various sounds of the cuckoo, peacock, dove and swan. *Rasitā* means making sounds.

### Text 18

*vipula-pulaka-pṛthu-vepathu-bhaṅgā \|*  
*śvasita-nimīlita-vikasad-anaṅgā \|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vipula - Extensive; pulaka - \[and\] goose-bumps; prthu - great; vepathu - of shivering; bhanga - \[in whom there are\] waves; svasita - breathing heavily; nimilita - \[and\] having closed the eyes; vikasad - \[is\] appearing; ananga - \[in whom\] the Cupid appears

**Перевод:**

**She experiences waves of shivering and extensive goose bumps. She experiences the height of bliss with heavy breathing and closed eyes.**

**Commentary:** She experiences waves of great shivering and extensive goose bumps. They are compared to waves since they appear again and again with greater intensity. *Bhaṅgā* means wave according to *Viśva.* “In this experience of happiness, there should be no grief.” She experiences symptoms of impregnation (*anaṅgā*), manifested with heavy breathing out of great joy, indicating the approach of the highest bliss and by closing of her eyes because of that bliss. These symptoms are described in *Rati-rahasya.*

### Text 19

*śrama-jala-kaṇa-bhara-subhaga-śarīrā \|*  
*paripatitorasi rati-raṇa-dhīrā \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

srama - Of exertion; jala - of the water \[perspiration\]; kana - of the drops; bhara - with the bearing; subhaga - \[is\] attractive; sarira -whose body; paripatita - \[she\] has fallen; urasi - on \[his\] chest; rati - of love; rana - in the battle; dhira - expert.

**Перевод:**

**Her body is attractive with drops of perspiration. Expert in the battle of love, she has fallen on his chest.**

**Commentary:** By all the drops of perspiration because of efforts in love, her body looks attractive like a pearl necklace. She falls upon Kṛṣṇa’s chest, covering him with her arms for more enjoyment, fearing he will rise up. “Falling down” also indicates she is now weak. This indicates the end of love pastimes. It is said:

*aṅge svedaḥ ślathatvaṁ ca keśa-vastrādi-saṁyatiḥ \|*  
*jāte cyuti-sukhe nāryā virāmecchā ca jāyate \|\|*

**There is perspiration on her limbs and slackness. She rearranges her hair and clothing. When the action is completed with satisfaction, the woman gives up her desire.**

*nārī pāda-dvayaṁ dattvā kāntasyoru-yugopari \|*  
*kaṭim āndolayed āśu bandho’yaṁ haṁsa-līlakaḥ \|\|*

**The woman puts her feet above the thighs of her lover and quickly rocks her hips. This position is called** ***haṁsa-līlaka.***

The woman is described. She is skillful or steady in love making (*rati-raṇe dhīrā*). *Kaṇa* means a drop. *Subhaga* means beautiful according to *Viśva.* In seven verses, the inferior qualities of the woman mentioned previously are proved by describing her actions in relation to the hero who is indifferent to her.

### Text 20

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇita-hari-ramitam \|*  
*kali-kaluṣaṁ janayatu pariśamitam \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva - By Sri Jayadeva; bhanita - spoken; hari - of Krsna; ramitam - the pastimes; kali - of the Kaliyuga; kalusam - the foulness \[opposition to worshipping the lord\]; janayatu - may \[they\] bring; parisamitam - to cessation.

**Перевод:**

**May the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa described by Jayadeva alleviate the atheism of Kaliyuga.**

**Commentary:** May the playing of Kṛṣṇa described by the poet Jayadeva bring about unlimited cessation (*pariśamitam*) of sins of Kaliyuga--in the form of opposition to worship of the Lord, for the singers and listeners.

### Text 21

*viraha-pāṇḍu-murāri-mukhāmbuja-*  
*dyutir ayaṁ tirayann api vedanām \|*  
*vidhur atīva tanoti mano-bhuvaḥ*  
*suhṛd aye hṛdaye madana-vyathām \|\|21\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

viraha - Because of separation; pandu - \[which is\] pale; murari -of Murari (Krsna); mukhambuja - \[is like\] the lotus-face; dyutir -whose brightness; ayam - this; tirayann - makes disappear; api -even; vedanam - knowledge; vidhur - moon; ativa - exceedingly; tanoti - \[and\] spreads; mano-bhuvah - of Cupid \[the mind-stealer\]; suhrd - the friend; aye - ah; hrdaye - in \[my\] heart; madana - of Cupid; vyatham - the pain.

**Перевод:**

**Ah! The moon, the friend of Cupid, whose complexion is like lotus face of Kṛṣṇa, pale because of separation, and which makes my knowledge disappear, gives intense love pains to my heart.**

## Song 15: Krishna plays with a competitor gopi

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 15 \|\|**  
**Gurjarī-rāga, etak-tālī**

### Text 22

*samudita-madane ramaṇī-vadane*  
*cumbana-valitādhare \|*  
*mṛga-mada-tilakaṁ likhati sa-pulakaṁ*  
*mṛgam iva rajanī-kare \|\|22\|\|*

*ramate yamunā-pulina-vane vijayī murārir adhunā \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

samudita - Is aroused; madane - in whom passion; ramani - of the woman; vadane - on the face; cumbana - for kissing; valita - \[is\] endowed with; adhare - whose lip; mrga-mada - of musk; tilakam - a tilaka; likhati - he draws; sa-pulakam - with hairs standing on end; mrgam - a dear; iva - as if; rajani-kare - on the moon \[the night-maker\], ramate - Plays; yamuna - of the Yamuna; pulina - on the bank; vane - in the forest; vijayi - expertly; murarir - Murari \[Krsna\]; adhuna - now.

**Перевод:**

**With hairs standing on end he draws musk tilaka on the woman’s face, who is aroused with passion, her lips ready for kissing. The tilaka is like the deer on the moon.Kṛṣṇa now plays in the forest on the bank of the Yamunā. (chorus)**

**Commentary:** Having ascertained the enjoyment of another woman in the previous song, the independent woman (Rādhā) now describes Kṛṣṇa using her imagination.

*Vijayī* means one who is most expert in skillful dressing etc. or who is free of fear. Murāri indicates that Kṛṣṇa is without love, since he exhibits rough behavior in killing the demon. Or Murāri can mean “he who surrounds (*murayati)* a woman with unlimited play during love.” He is enthusiastic for love. Now he plays in the forest on the sandy bank of the Yamunā. This shows the good qualities of a suitable place for gaining relief from the fatigue of love.

The playing is described. He draws tilaka of great variety, using musk, on the woman’s face, while his hairs stand on end. *Ramaṇi* indicates a woman who gives joy to Kṛṣṇa. That face has developed desire by the favorable touch of the lover (*samudite madane).* Or that face produces desire in Kṛṣṇa. That face is endowed with lips turned towards Kṛṣṇa and contracted for kissing. Or the face has lips long worshipped (*valita*) by kisses. Or the face has lips which request to be kissed. *Valita* means “requested” according to *Viśva.* He draws tilaka which looks like the deer in the full moon. On kissing the lips there is sweetness like nectar flowing from the moon. The musk is like the deer and the face is like the moon, surrounded by locks of hair. The description of the face indicates the extreme passion of the woman rather than the man. This is a criticism of the woman. Like the moon’s, her beauty is not stable. Rādhā’s beauty however is permanent.

### Text 23

*ghana-caya-rucire racayati cikure*  
*taralita-taruṇānane \|*  
*kurubaka-kusumaṁ capalā suṣamaṁ*  
*rati-pati-mṛga-kānane \|\|23\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ghana - Of clouds; caya - \[as\] a mass; rucire - attractive; racayati -\[he\] arranges; cikure - in \[her\] hair; taralita - \[is\] agitated; taruna - of the young men; anane - by which the best; kurubaka - golden; kusumam - flowers; capala - \[as\] lightning; susamam - beautiful; rati-pati - \[who is\] the Cupid; mrga - of the deer; kanane - the home.

**Перевод:**

**He arranges unfading golden flowers, beautiful as lightning, in her locks which are as attractive as a mass of clouds, which are the home of the deer known as Cupid and which agitate Kṛṣṇa, the best of young men.**

**Commentary:** He arranges unfading golden flowers *(kurubaka*) in her locks, which are attractive like a mass of clouds and are the forest home of the deer known as Cupid (*rati-pati*). The locks agitate the best of young men (*taruṇa ānane*) with love. Or the locks make the mouth of young Kṛṣṇa glorify her qualities. Or the phrase *taruṇānane* can modify *kānane.* She has locks of hair like a forest in which there are flowers of young trees *(taruṇa*) growing. This is compared with her hair tied with flowers, since both induce love and both have flowers blooming. The flowers are beautiful like lightning (*capalā-suṣamam*). The ornament is *rūpaka.* If the verse means that Kṛṣṇa strings flowers in another woman’s hair, then his hands do not shake. This means he is not attached to her.

*mā garvam udvaha kapola-tale cakāsti*  
*kṛṣṇa-svahasta-likhitā nava-mañjarīti \|*  
*anyāpi kiṁ na sakhi bhājanam īdṛśīnāṁ*  
*vairī na ced bhavati vepathur antarāyaḥ \|\|*

**My friend, please do not be proud because Kṛṣṇa has decorated your forehead with his own hand. It may be that Kṛṣṇa is yet attracted by some other beautiful girl. I see that the decoration on your forehead is very nicely made, and so it appears that Kṛṣṇa was not too disturbed in painting it. Otherwise, he could not have painted such exact lines! Padyāvali 302**

*Mrga* means a deer according to *Viśva. Kānana* means a forest or a house. *Cikuram* means hair. *Suṣamā* means shining brightly. *Cikura* means hair or locks according *to Amara-koṣa.*

### Text 24

*ghaṭayati sughane kuca-yuga-gagaṇe*  
*mṛga-mada-ruci-rūṣite \|*  
*maṇi-saram amalaṁ tāraka-paṭalaṁ*  
*nakha-pada-śaśi-bhūṣite \|\|24\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ghatayati - \[He\] makes; sughane - very firm; kuca - of \[her\] breasts; yuga - of the pair; gagane - on the sky; mrga-mada - of musk; ruci - with the shining; rusite - spread; mani-saram - a pearl necklace; amalam - spotless; taraka - of stars; patalam - \[into\] a host; nakha - of nails; pada - \[which are\] the marks; sasi - with \[half-\]moons; bhusite - ornamented.

**Перевод:**

**He makes a pearl necklace become a host of stars on the sky of her firm breasts, spread with shining musk, ornamented with nail marks like crescent moons.**

**Commentary:** He makes a spotless pearl necklace become a host of stars in the sky of her two breasts. He produces the beauty in the sky. That sky of her breasts is very firm (*sughane*), and spread with a glow of musk. And it is ornamented with the moons of nail marks. The nail marks are called *ardhendu* (half moon). It is said:

*ardhendu-saṅkāśam idaṁ nakha-jñair*  
*ardhendu-saṁjñaṁ kathitaṁ samāsāt \|*  
*kaksā-nitamba-stana-pārśva-kaṇṭhe*  
*dātavyam etat karajaṁ sadaiva \|\|*

**Marks made by the finger nails which resemble half moons are called** ***ardhendu.*** **These are made on the throat, side, breast, waist or arm pit. Pañca-sāyaka**

The breasts are compared to the sky which is most extensive (*sughane*) or which has beautiful clouds. And there is a host of pure stars ornamented with the half moon. It is a tradition among poets that the sky is blackish. In this way *rūpaka* ornament is presented.

*Ghana* means firm or broad according to *Viśva. Rūṣita* means strung.

### Text 25

*jita-bisa-śakale mṛdu-bhuja-yugale*  
*kara-tala-nalinī-dale \|*  
*marakata-valayaṁ madhukara-nicayaṁ*  
*vitarati hima-śītale \|\|25\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

jita - which have surpassed; bisa - of the lotus stalk; sakale - pieces of; mrdu - soft; bhuja - of \[her\] arms; yugale - to the pair; kara -whose hands; tala - \[is\] the surface; nalini - of the lotus; dale - the petal; marakata - of sapphire; valayam - bracelets; madhukara - of bees; nicayam - \[which are\] a swarm; vitarati - \[he\] gives; hima - as snow; sitale - cool.

**Перевод:**

**He places sapphire bracelets, like a swarm of bees, on her cool arms, which resemble water lily stems, with hands resembling lotus petals.**

**Commentary:** He offers sapphire bracelets, a swarm of bees, to her tender arms which surpass water lily stems in softness and whiteness, with hands like lotus petals, very cool, since they remove heat. Though poets normally do not compare hands to lotus petals, it is acceptable here when the arms are compared to water lily stems. The bracelets on her arms are like bees clustered on a water lily stem. *Nalinī* is a lotus according to *Dharaṇi. Vitara* means “to give” according to *Amara-koṣa.* The ornament is *rūpaka* (metaphor).

### Text 26

*rati-gṛha-jaghane vipulāpaghane*  
*manasija-kanakāsane \|*  
*maṇi-maya-raśanaṁ toraṇa-hasanaṁ*  
*vikirati kṛta-vāsane \|\|26\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

rati - of love; grha - \[which are\] the abode; jaghane - on \[her\] hips; vipulapaghane - very fleshy; manasija - of Cupid; kanakasane - the golden throne; mani - in pearls; maya - consisting; rasanam - a belt; torana - an auspicious garland; hasanam - deriding; vikirati - \[he\] throws; krta - \[is\] placed; vasane - \[and\] on which cloth.

**Перевод:**

**He throws a pearl belt, which derides an auspicious garland, on her fleshy hips, the abode of love, the golden throne of Cupid, covered with a cloth.**

**Commentary:** He throws a belt of pearls on her hips, the abode of śṛṅgāra-rasa (*rati-gṛha*). The word *kirati* (throws) indicates that his trembling hands throw the belt over her hips, since he cannot tie the belt. His hands tremble with fear on touching her hips as he tries to tie the belt. The hips are very fleshy (*vipulāpaghane*). Or they are great (*vipula*), and without hardness (very soft—*apaghana*). Or the hips are a limb covered with goose bumps *(vipula).* She shows sāttvika-bhāvas. The hips are the golden throne of Cupid. The belt derides (*hāsanam*) an auspicious garland (*toraṇa*). When someone sits on a throne, followers place a garland around the throne. On the throne of her hips, an auspicious garland of bells is placed. Garments are also placed on her hips (*kṛta-vāsane).* Or the hips are perfumed with a special aphrodisiac incense to bring Kṛṣṇa under control. Or the hips make Kṛṣṇa desire to enjoy on seeing her. Or the hips have become the abode of Cupid. This is logical since her hips are the throne of Cupid. He constantly resides there.

*Apaghana* means limb or body according to *Amara-koṣa. Vāsana* means desire, cloth or place according to *Viśva. Pula* means goose bumps. *Rati* means Cupid’s wife, attachment or amorous actions. The ornament is *rūpaka.*

### Text 27

*caraṇa-kiśalaye kamalā-nilaye*  
*nakha-maṇi-gaṇa-pūjite \|*  
*bahir-apavaraṇaṁ yāvaka-bharaṇaṁ*  
*janayati hṛdi yojite \|\|27\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

carana - Which are \[her\] feet; kisalaye - on the buds; kamala - of Kamala (Laksmi); nilaye - the abode; nakha - \[which are\] nails; mani - of jewels; gana - with groups; pujite - ornamented; bahir - external; apavaranam - the covering; yavaka - of lac; bharanam - wearing; janayati - \[he\] produces; hrdi - to \[his\] chest; yojite -attached.

**Перевод:**

**Putting her feet on his chest, he decorates the buds of her feet, the abode of Lakṣmī, ornamented with jewels in the form of her toe nails, with lac, serving only as an external covering.**

**Commentary:** He puts lac on the buds of her feet. The feet are compared to buds because they are tender and red. They are the abode of Lakṣmī and are ornamented (pūjite) with jewel-like nails. Lakṣmī, who finds it hard to attain by the Lord, has merged into those feet worshipped by the vibhūtis in the form of her jewel-like nails, to offer herself to the Lord. The lac is an external covering on her feet (bahir-apavaraṇam). Her feet, by nature reddish, do not need a covering.

Those feet are on his chest out of great attachment. Placing her foot on his chest, he decorates it with lac. Or his chest is the abode of Lakṣmī, worshipped by jewels in the form of nail scratches. He holds her foot on his chest, even though his chest is endowed with Lakṣmī. This indicates the woman’s beauty and good fortune.

Or that chest is the abode of the best of women, Rādhā, and is decorated with her nail marks. The foot is placed on this chest and he decorates it in an ordinary way, but not with great details. That lac is scattered here and there (bahir-apavaraṇam).

Since buds and jewels do not usually go together the ornament is called ayukta-rūpaka. This is described by Daṇḍī:

*idam ārdra-smitaṁ jyotsnaṁ snigdha-netrotpalaṁ mukham \|*  
*iti jyotsnotpalāyogāt ayuktaṁ nāma rūpakam \|\|*

**An example of ayukta-rūpaka is the following: her face is slightly smiling like moonlight with attractive lotuses for eyes. Moon light and lotuses do not exist together. Kāvyādarśa 2.78**

Nakha means nail according to Viśva.

### Text 28

*ramayati subhṛśaṁ kam api sudṛśaṁ*  
*khala-haladhara-sodare \|*  
*kim aphalam avasaṁ ciram iha virasaṁ*  
*vada sakhi viṭapodare \|\|28\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ramayati - \[While he\] enjoys; subhrsam - profusely; kam api - some woman; sudrsam - beautiful-eyed; khala - \[who is\] a debaucherous person; haladhara - of Balarama; sodare - the brother (Krsna); kim - should; aphalam - uselessly; avasam - I stay; ciram - for a long time; iha - here; virasam - without rasa; vada - tell \[me\]; sakhi - о female friend; vitapodare - in the bower

**Перевод:**

**O my sakhī! Please tell me if I should stay a long time in this bower uselessly, without rasa, while the rascal brother of Balarāma heartily enjoys with some beautiful-eyed woman?**

**Commentary:** O friend! While the rascal (*khala*) Kṛṣṇa, brother of Balarāma, enjoys profusely (*subhṛśam*) with some beautiful-eyed woman, please tell me, should I stay a long time in this bower uselessly without rasa? By addressing Kṛṣṇa as the brother of Balarāma she scolds Kṛṣṇa using crude speech. *Khala* means a lusty person according to *Viśva.* Or should I remain in this place in order to be with a rascal (*viṭapodare*) for a long time? Locative case can be used to indicate “in order to.”

The previous seven verses can be taken as the words of a sakhī. In that case, the last verse has the following meaning. Will my stay in the forest be without result and without rasa when the crest jewel, the brother of the person who holds a plough to punish the sinful, enjoys with Rādhā in the bower? It will be with result and full of rasa! Mention of Balarāma indicates strength to subdue the wicked. It is hinted that Balarāma will assist in producing pastimes without fear.

### Text 29

*iha rasa-bhaṇane kṛta-hari-guṇane*  
*madhuripu-pada-sevake \|*  
*kali-yuga-racitaṁ na vasatu duritaṁ*  
*kavi-nṛpa-jayadevake \|\|29\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

iha - In the writer of this poem; rasa - srngara rasa; bhanane -describing; krta - is made; hari - of Krsna; gunane - by whom the description of the qualities; madhuripu - of Madhuripu (Krsna); pada - at the feet; sevake - serving; kali-yuga - in Kaliyuga; racitam -performed; na - not; vasatu - may \[it\] dwell; duritam - the sin; kavi - of poets; nrpa - the king; jayadevake - in Jayadeva

**Перевод:**

**May the sins performed in Kali-yuga not dwell in the writer of this poem, Jayadeva, king of poets, who describes śṛṅgāra-rasa, who makes one think of the Lord, and who serves the feet of Kṛsṇa.**

**Commentary:** May the sins (*duritam*) performed in Kali-yuga or by Kali-yuga not dwell in the writer of this poem (*iha*), Jayadeva, king of poets, who describes śṛṅgāra-rasa, who makes one think of Kṛṣṇa (*kṛta-hari-guṇane*)*,* and who serves the feet of Kṛṣṇa (*madhu-ripu*). The word *madhu-ripu* (enemy of the demon Madhu) indicates that Kṛṣnā is capable of destroying the wicked.

### Text 30

*nāyātaḥ sakhi nirdayo yadi śaṭhas tvaṁ dūti kiṁ dūyase*  
*svacchandaṁ bahu-vallabhaḥ sa ramate kiṁ tatra te dūṣaṇam \|*  
*paśyādya priya-saṅgamāya dayitasyākṛṣyamāṇaṁ guṇair*  
*utkaṇṭhārti-bharād iva sphuṭad idaṁ cetaḥ svayaṁ yāsyati \|\|30\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

nayatah - \[Has\] not come; sakhi - о female friend; nirdayo - heartless; yadi - if; sathas - the cheater; tvam - you; duti - messenger; kirn -why; duyase - do grieve? svacchandam - Fearlessly; bahu - of many \[women\]; vallabhah - lover; sa - the; ramate - enjoys, kim - What; tatra - with repsect to that; te - you; dusanam - \[is\] fault? pasyadya - Now see; priya - with \[my\] beloved; sangamaya - for a meeting; dayitasya - of \[my\] lover; akrsyamanam - attracted; gunair - by the qualities; utkantharti - of longing and pain; bharad - out of the burden; iva - as if; sphutad idam - this; cetah - heart; svayam - my own; yasyati - goes.

**Перевод:**

**O messenger! If the heartless cheater has not come, why do you grieve? The lover of many women is enjoying fearlessly. That is not your fault. See at this moment! My heart, attracted by the qualities of my lover and leaving the body out of longing and pain, goes to meet him.**

**Commentary:** O gopī expert at delivering messages (*dūti*)*!* If the cheater—the great offender (*śaṭhah),* devoid of mercy, has not come, why should you grieve? Not only is he a cheater. He is arrogant. Affectionate to many women, he enjoys without fear (*svacchandam*). This means he is enjoying with some other gopī. What fault do you have for that (*tatra*)? You have no fault at all.

“Recover for a moment. While you do so, I will go to him and bring him here.” See something astonishing right now (*adya*). The phrase may also be taken as *paśya ādya-priya-saṅgamāya* where *ādya-priya* refers to Kṛṣṇa. My mind (*idam cetaḥ*) will go spontaneously (*svayam*), not with attention, to meet Kṛṣṇa (*priya).* My mind is forcibly pulled by the qualities (*guṇaiḥ*) of this person dearer than a million lives (*dayitasya*). Others are pulled elsewhere forcibly by ropes *(guṇaiḥ*). That mind leaves the body (*sphutam*) because of the extreme pressure of pain and longing. Others, who are overcome with the burden of a host of other objects, have their hearts broken. Since I cannot tolerate the sorrow any longer, remembering his qualities, I will give up my life. Then that mind will go easily to him. So what is the use if you go to him?

In the first line the hero is called a cheater. His arrogance is conveyed by the word *svacchandam* (fearless). Those qualities are described as follows. *Śaṭho gūḍhāparādha-kṛt dhṛṣṭo vyaktāny-yuvatī-bhoga-lakṣmāpi nirbhayaḥ:* the deceiver commits a grievous offense and the arrogant lover is fearless though he explicitly displays the marks of enjoyment with another woman. (Rasārṇava-sudhākara 1.81) The words describing Kṛṣṇa are spoken with angry affection, for ultimately he is most subservient to Rādhā. This is described later. *Krīte dāsa ivopasevaita-padṁabhoje:* he is the servant of your lotus feet. (GG 11.22) The ornament is *utprekṣā.*

## Song 16: The competitor gopi is happy with Krishna, unlike Radha

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 16 \|\|**  
**Deśa-vrādī rāga rūpaka-tāla**

### Text 31

*anila-tarala-kuvalaya-nayanena \|*  
*tapati na sā kiśalaya-śayanena \|\|31\|\|*

*sakhi yā ramitā vana-mālinā \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

anila - In the wind; tarala - swaying; kuvalaya - \[are like\] lotuses; nayanena - whose eyes; tapati - suffer; na - not; sa - she; kisalaya - of shoots; sayanena - on the bed; sakhi - о female friend; ya - who; ramita - is delighted; vana-malina - by he who wears a forest-garland \[Krsna\].

**Перевод:**

**O friend! She who enjoys with Kṛṣṇa (chorus) whose eyes are like lotuses swaying in the wind does not suffer on the bed of shoots**

**Commentary:** In eight verses she describe the pain of the woman in separation and the happiness of the woman enjoying with Kṛsṇa.

The song is in *deśākhya-rāga, rūpaka-tāla.*\[\[22\]\]

O friend! She who enjoys with Kṛṣṇa (*vana-mālinā*) does not suffer on the bed of new shoots. That bed gives happiness in association with Kṛṣṇa’s limbs. Or the woman who enjoys with Kṛṣna suffers on the bed of new shoots. This is said in a plaintive voice while shaking the head.

*dina-parikṣaye nīla-kuntalair*  
*vanaruhānanaṁ bibhrad āvṛtam*  
*dhana-rajasvalaṁ darśayan muhur*  
*manasi naḥ smaraṁ vīra yacchasi*

**At the end of the day you repeatedly show us your lotus face, covered with dark blue locks of hair and thickly powdered with dust. Thus, O hero, you arouse desires in our minds. SB 10.31.12**

One who has no experience of Kṛṣṇa does not have to suffer. Or one who enjoys with Kṛṣṇa, is not satisfied on the bed of shoots because of thrist for more union repeatedly while trembling out of astonishing affection. It is said:

*calasi yad vrajāc cārayan paśūn*  
*nalina-sundaraṁ nātha te padam*  
*śila-tṛṇāṅkuraiḥ sīdatīti naḥ*  
*kalilatāṁ manaḥ kānta gacchati*

**Dear master, dear lover, when you leave the cowherd village to herd the cows, our minds are disturbed with the thought that your feet, more beautiful than a lotus, will be pricked by the spiked husks of grain and the rough grass and plants. SB 10.31.11**

Kṛṣṇa is described. He has eyes like a lotus moving in the wind. Or he has eyes beautiful as lotuses and moving like the wind. The word *vana-mālinā* indicates śṛṅgāra-rasa. *Vana-mālīnā* can also be *vana-mā alinā:* by the bee, lusty for tasting the rasa in the forest of Rādhā. The woman who is enjoyed by this lusty bee does not suffer on the bed of shoots since he is compliant.

### Text 32

*vikasita-sarasija-lalita-mukhena \|*  
*sphuṭati na sā manasija-viśikhena \|\|32\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vikasita - blossoming; sarasija - as a lotus; lalita - \[is as\] attractive; mukhena - by he (Krsna) whose face; sphutati - is injured; na - not; sa - she \[who is enjoyed\]; manasija - of Cupid; visikhena - by the arrows.

**Перевод:**

**She who is enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa whose face is as attractive as a blossoming lotus is not injured by the arrows of Cupid.**

**Commentary:** This woman, who is enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa, is not wounded by the arrows of Cupid. Kṛṣṇa is described. He has a face attractive like a bossoming lotus. It is astonishing that the pain of love is extinguished by the lotus of his face.

### Text 33

*amṛta-madhura-mṛdutara-vacanena \|*  
*jvalati na sa malayaja-pavanena \|\|33\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

amrta - Than nectar; madhura - \[are\] sweeter; mrdutara - \[and\] softer; vacanena - by he \[Krsna\] whose words; jvalati - does burn; na - not; sa - she \[who is enjoyed\]; malayaja - born in the Malaya mountain; pavanena - because of the wind.

**Перевод:**

**She, enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa who speaks sweet, soft words, does not burn because of the Malaya wind.**

**Commentary:** She who is enjoyed by Kṛṣnā does not become enflamed by the wind coming from Malaya Mountain. Kṛṣṇa speaks most soft, sweet words. It is said:

*madhurayā girā valgu-vākyayā*  
*budha-manojñayā puṣkarekṣaṇa*  
*vidhi-karīr imā vīra muhyatīr*  
*adhara-sīdhunāpyāyayasva naḥ*

**O lotus-eyed one, by your sweet voice and charming words, which attract the minds of the intelligent, we have fainted. Our dear hero, please revive your maidservants with the nectar of your lips. SB 10.31.8**

The fever caused by the wind mixed with poison from the snake in the sandalwood tree is removed by the nectar of his words. This verse illustrates the hero’s quality of speaking sweetly.

### Text 34

*sthala-jala-ruha-ruci-kara-caraṇena \|*  
*luṭhati na sā himakara-kiraṇena \|\|34\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sthala - On land (the hibiscus); jala-ruha - like the lotus; ruci - \[are\] charming; kara-caranena - by he (Krsna) whose hands and feet; luthati - does roll on the ground \[in pain\]; na - not; sa - she \[who is enjoyed\]; himahara - of the moon; kiranena - because of the rays.

**Перевод:**

**She who enjoys with Kṛṣṇa whose hands and feet are charming like the hibiscus does not roll on the ground in pain because of the moon light.**

**Commentary:** She who is enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa does not roll on the ground because of the rays of the moon. Kṛṣṇa has charming hands and feet like lotuses growing on land (hibiscus), or which are charming like lotuses growing on land.

### Text 35

*sajala-jalada-samudaya-rucireṇa \|*  
*dalati na sā hṛdi viraha-bhareṇa\[\[23\]\]\|\|35\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sajala - Full of water; jalada - of clouds; samudaya - as the assemblage; rucirena - by he (Krsna) attractive; dalati - does burn na - not; sa - she \[who is enjoyed\]; hrdi - in \[her\] heart; viraha - of separation; bharena - from the burden.

**Перевод:**

**She who is enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa, attractive as a cloud full of rain, does not suffer from intense separation in her heart.**

**Commentary:** She who enjoys with Kṛṣṇa does not burn with intense separation in her heart. Kṛṣṇa is attractive like a rising cloud full of water. This verse indicates the Lord’s ever-fresh nature, his attractiveness, his ability to destroy pain, his affection, his ability to cause increase, and his nature of being full of rasa.

### Text 36

*kanaka-nikaṣa-ruci-śuci-vasanena \|*  
*śvasiti na sā parijana-hasanena \|\|36\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kanaka - Of gold; nikasa - on a testing stone; ruci - as the appearance; suci - \[is\] as effulgent; vasanena - by he \[Krsna\] whose clothing; svasiti - does sigh; na - not; sa - she \[who is enjoyed\]; parijana - of \[her\] associates; hasanena - because of the mocking.

**Перевод:**

**She who is enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa, who wears clean cloth effulgent like golden marks on a testing stone, does not breath in pain because of being mocked by her associates.**

**Commentary:** The woman who is enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa does not release breaths of pain because of laughter from other people around her (*parijana).* Kṛṣṇa wears pure cloth whose effulgence is like golden letters on a testing stone. *Nikaṣa* is a stone used for testing gold. This indicates that the cloth is yellow, but never fades due to water or other substances. Or he has effulgence like a golden stone in a cloth made of śṛṅgāra-rasa (*śuci*). Or he has effulgence like a golden testing stone and wears clean cloth. *Śuci* means *śṛṅgāra. Nikasa* means a testing stone according to *Hārāvalī. Śuci* means pure according to*Viśva.*

### Text 37

*sakala-bhuvana-jana-vara-taruṇena \|*  
*vahati na sā rujam atikaruṇena \|\|37\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sakala - Entire; bhuvana - in the world; jana - of \[all\] people; vara - \[is\] the best; tarunena - by he (Krsna) whose youth; vahati - does bear; na - not; sa - she \[who is enjoyed\]; rujam - pain; atikarunena - because of \[his\] great compassion \[to her\].

**Перевод:**

**The woman enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa, whose youthful beauty is the best in all the worlds, does not have pain arising from separation because he shows great compassion to her.**

**Commentary:** The woman enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa does not have pain arising from separation (*rujam*) because of the great comparison that he displays. Kṛṣṇa has astonishing beauty with better youth than all youthful people in the world. He is eternally *kaiśora.* Or he has more youthful beauty than the people of the world existing with beauty (*sa-kala*). It is said*sva-mūrtyā loka-lāvaṇya- nirmuktyā locanaṁ nṛṇām*: I steal people’s eyes with my form which makes them reject all other beauty. (SB 11.1.6) Other types of beauty arise from only a particle of his beauty. Or he has better youth than the lord (*jana*) of the splendid (*sakala*) world, Vaikuṇṭha, because of his superior beauty. The lord of Vaikuṇṭha is famous for being ancient. It is said *vismpanam svasya ca:* that form astonishes even the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha. (SB 3.2.12) *Taruṇa* refers to the middle part of *kaiśora.* This middle age is described as follows:

*ūru-dvayasya bāhvoś ca kāpi śrīr urasas tathā \|*  
*mūrter madhurimādyaṁ ca kaiśore sati madhyame \|\|*

**During the middle period of the** ***kaiśora*** **period, Kṛṣṇa displays indescribable beauty in his two thighs, his two arms and his chest, and sweetness in his whole form. BRS 2.1.230**

The sweetness of this age is illustrated:

*navaṁ dhanur ivātanor naṭad-agha-dviṣor bhrū-yugaṁ*  
*śarālir iva śāṇitā nakhara-rājir agre kharā \|*  
*virājati śarīriṇī rucira-danta-lekhāruṇā*  
*na kā sakhi samīkṣaṇād yuvatir asya vitrasyati \|\|*

**The two eyebrows of the enemy of Agha are dancing like new bows of Cupid. The tips of his rows of fingernails are so sharp that they shine like a line of arrows. The glistening rows of his attractive teeth embody the redness of dawn. What young woman would not be afraid of seeing him? BRS 2.1.318**

Or he has beauty superior to that of all the boys of Vraja. *Atikaruṇena* can also modify Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is most affectionate endowed with the highest rasa. Or he is without mercy (*ati*=gone beyond).

It was explained at the beginning of this part that there are two meanings. In the first meaning, Rādhā speaks in four ways (in four songs). In the second meaning, a sakhī speaks to inform her in seven verses. “O friend! You are an independent woman. You have enjoyed with him. Your lover is by your side. Why should you lament in separation. She who has enjoyed should act in the following way (as described in the above verses).” Or “she who has enjoyed” indicates her victory in love. She who has enjoyed will never be without taste for him.

### Text 38

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇita-vacanena \|*  
*praviśatu harir api hṛdayam anena \|\|38\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sn-jayadeva - By Sri Jayadeva; bhanita - spoken; vacanena - by words \[of Radha\]; pravisatu - may \[he\] enter; harir - Krsna; api - even; hrdayam - \[your\] heart; anena - those.

**Перевод:**

**May Kṛṣṇa enter your heart by the words of Rādhā described by Jayadeva.**

**Commentary:** By the words of Rādhā or her sakhī as related by the poet Jayadeva, may Kṛṣṇa, who steals the heart, enter into the hearts of the devotees, or into Rādhā’s heart. Or Hari indicates he who has astonishing prowess. May Kṛṣṇa, though uncontrolled by anyone, be captured. Having entered the trap he cannot get out.

### Text 39

*manobhavānandana candanānila*  
*prasīda re dakṣiṇa muñca vāmatām \|*  
*kṣaṇaṁ jagat-prāṇa nidhāya mādhavaṁ*  
*puro mama prāṇa-haro bhaviṣyasi \|\|39\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

manobhava - For Cupid; anandana - bliss; candananila - о sandalwood breeze; prasida - settle down; re - o; daksina - gentle \[one\]; munca - \[or\] give up; vamatam - \[your\] contrary nature, ksanarh - For a moment, jagat-prana - о life of the world; nidhaya - having placed; madhavam - Madhava (Krsna); puro - before \[me\]; mama - my; prana - of life; haro - the taker away; bhavisyasi - \[otherwise\] you will become.

**Перевод:**

**O sandalwood breeze, giving bliss to Cupid! O gentle wind! Please give up your uncooperative nature. O life of the universe! Having placed Mādhava before me for a moment, you then become responsible for my life.**

**Commentary:** O sandalwood breeze! Please be merciful to me. O you with favor to all, inclined to pastimes (*dakṣiṇa*)! Give up your contrary nature (*vāmatām*)! O breeze! It is not proper to act crookedly since you have a nature cooperative (*dakṣiṇa*) for the person in love, through your contact with sandalwood. Or O compiant one, be merciful. But if you are not merciful, then show your contrary nature.

“I do not have a contrary nature.” O person who gives bliss to Cupid! You act against me. If you do not reject that contrary nature, then, O life of the universe, having put Kṛṣṇa in front of me for a moment, you will become the power to take my life. By addressing the wind as “life of the universe” she indicates that by not bringing him again, the wind becomes the power to end her life. This means that because of affection for Kṛṣṇa who she cannot give up, she prays for life. By attaining him she can remain living. Or by putting one who is enjoyed by other women in front of her, she will die in a moment. The word Mādhava also suggests one who causes unsteadiness by his attractive nature.\[\[24\]\]

The ornament called v*irodha* is shown by describing the contrary nature of the compliant wind and its ability to take life, though it is the life of the universe. *Vāma* means inimical. *Dakṣiṇa* means compliant.

### Text 40

*ripur iva sakhi-saṁvāso’yaṁ śikhīva himānilo*  
*viṣam iva sudhā-raśmir yasmin dunoti mano-gate \|*  
*hṛdayam adaye tasminn evaṁ punar valate balāt*  
*kuvalaya-dṛśāṁ vāmaḥ kāmo nikāma-niraṇkuśaḥ \|\|40\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

ripur - \[Is\] an enemy; iva - as if; sakhi - with a friend; samvaso - living; ayam - this; sikhi - \[is\] a fire; iva - as if; himanilo - cool breeze; visam - \[is\] poison; iva - as if; sudha - of the moon; rasmir - \[and\] the ray; yasmin - in him; dunoti - \[when\] is absorbed; mano-gate - \[my\] thought, hrdayam - \[My\] heart; adaye - cruel; tasminn - in him; evam - only; punar - again; valate - goes; balat -with force, kuvalaya-drsam - For lotus-eyed women; vamah - \[is\] uncooperative; kamo - the Cupid; nikama - excessively; nirankusah - \[and\] uncontrolled.

**Перевод:**

**When my mind is absorbed in him, living with friends give pain as if they were enemies. The cool breeze gives pain like fire. The rays of the moon give pain like poison. My heart goes to that cruel person with great force. For lotus-eyed women, Cupid is most uncontrolled and uncooperative.**

**Commentary:** Everything is my mistake and no one else’s. With my mind on Kṛṣṇa (*yasmin mano-gate*), living with friends gives pain like living with enemies. The cool breeze gives pain like fire. The rays of the moon give pain like poison. He is without mercy (*adaye*) since he gives pain to others in this way.

Because of this, he should not be addressed by name. He is referred to by the words *yasmin* and *tasmin.* Or he is not named because a husband’s name should not be spoken by the wife. The heart—mind, intelligence, ego and citta—most forcibly go to him (*valate*), though prevented.

For lotus-eyed women, Cupid is completely uncontrolled and unfavorable (*vāmaḥ).* Cupid is attractive for those who have mutual attraction. In this case however, Kṛṣṇa has no attraction for me. Only my heart runs after him. This makes Cupid unfavorable to me.

Or when he goes elsewhere (*yasmin gate*), living with friends gives pain to the mind (*mano*) as if they were enemies. Friends are like enemies, the cool breeze is like fire and the rays of the moon are like poison. This is the ornament called *virodha. Yamaka* (repetition of sounds) is also present*. Śikhi* means fire according to *Amara-koṣa.*

### Text 41

*bādhāṁ vidhehi malayānila pañca-bāṇa*  
*prāṇān gṛhāṇa na gṛhaṁ punar āśrayiṣye \|*  
*kiṁ te kṛtānta-bhagini kṣamayā taraṅgair*  
*aṅgāni siñca mama śāmyatu deha-dāhaḥ \|\|41\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

badham - Pain; vidhehi - give \[me\]; malayanila - о Malaya wind, panca - \[Are\] five; bana - о he whose arrows (the Cupid); pranan - \[my\] life; grhana - take, na - Not; grham - home; punar - again; asrayisye - I will go. kith - What \[is the purpose of\]; te - your; krtanta - of death \[Yama\]; bhagini - о sister \[Yamuna\]; ksamaya  - peace? tarangair - With waves; angani - limbs; sinca - sprinkle; mama - my. samyatu - May \[it\] be extinguished; deha - in \[my\] body; dahah - the fire.

**Перевод:**

**O Malaya wind! Give me pain. O Cupid holding five arrows! Take my life. I will not go home. O Yamunā, sister of death! Why be peaceful? Cover my body with your waves. May the fire in my body be thus extinguished!**

**Commentary:** Not able to tolerate the pain of separation she now decorates herself with the Malaya wind. O wind coming from Malaya Mountain! Give me pain. It is hinted that it is not proper to refuse the request of a person who has completely surrendered. O Cupid (*pañca-bāṇa*)! Take my five prāṇas with your five arrows. Cupid is addressed as “holder of five arrows” since it is thus easy for him to take her five life airs.

“Why do you not go home?” I will not go again to my house. Without Kṛṣṇa my house will only give me more pain. O sister of Yama (Yamunā)! What is the use in being so peaceful (*kṣamayā*)? Flood my limbs with waves. May the burning in my body caused by separation be extinguished by this. Help your brother Yama (death) by taking my life airs. Since you are the sister of merciless death, showing compassion to me now is not proper. This is indicated by addressing her as the sister of death. *Bhaginī* means sister according to *Amara-koṣa.*

*sammohanaḥ kṣobhanaś ca dahanaḥ śoṣaṇas tathā \|*  
*uccāṭanaś ca kāmasya bāṇāḥ pañca prakīrtitāḥ \|\|*

**The five arrows of Cupid are bewilderment, agitation, burning, drying and ruination.**

### Text 42

*prātar nīla-nicolam acyutam uraḥ saṁvīta-pītāṁśukaṁ*  
*rādhāyāś cakitaṁ vilokya hasati svairaṁ sakhi-maṇḍale \|*  
*vrīḍā-cañcalam añcalaṁ nayanayor ādhāya rādhānane*  
*svādu-smera-mukho’yam astu jagad-ānandāya nandātmajaḥ \|\|42\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

pratar-In the morning; nila- \[is\] blue \[belonging to Radha\] ;nicolam - whose cloth; acyutam - Krsna; urah - the breast; samvita - \[is\] covering \[her\] ; pita - yellow; ariisukarh - whose cloth \[belonging to Krsna\]; radhayas - of Radha; cakitam - frightened; vilokya - having seen; hasati - \[while it\] laughs; svairam - freely; sakhi - of female friends; mandale - the group; vrida - with bashfulness; cancalam - restless; ancalam - the corner; nayanayor - of his eyes; adhaya -having placed; radha - of Radha; anane - on the face; svadu - \[is\] sweet; smera - \[and\] smiling; mukho - whose face; ayam - this; astu - may \[he\] be; jagad - of the world; anandaya - for the joy; nanda - of Nanda; atmajah - the son.

**Перевод:**

**May the son of Nanda, with his wondrous, smiling face, give joy to the universe as he glances from the corner of his restless, shy eye at Rādhā’s face--when the group of sakhīs laugh freely on seeing frightened Kṛṣṇa wearing her blue cloth and Rādhā covering her breast with his yellow cloth in the morning.**

**Commentary:** At the end of the section, the poet offers benedictions. May the son of Nanda give bliss to the universe. His face has a wonderful smile. He has glanced at Rādhā’s face from the corner of his eye, restless with shyness. It is said:

*yad gatāgati-viśrānti-vaicitryeṇa vivartanam \|*  
*tārakāyāḥ kalābhijñās taṁ kaṭākṣaṁ pracakṣate \|\|*

***Kaṭākṣa*** **is defined by the knower of arts as astonishing, repeated movement of the pupils towards the object, staying a moment and then withdrawing. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 7.50**

In the morning, when he glances, the group of sakhīs were laughing freely and smiling after seeing Kṛṣṇa who has eternal pastimes with her (*acyutam*). Or the name Acyuta indicates that he showed his powers by hiding the pastimes, while absorbed in enjoyment with her. He wore her blue garment and she wore on her chest his yellow garment. The *sakhīs* laughed because the two had exchanged their clothing.

“The son of Nanda’ indicates the *dhīra-lalita* hero. It is said:

*gahanād anurāgataḥ pitṛbhyām*  
*apanīta-vyavahāra-kṛtya-bhāraḥ \|*  
*viharan saha rādhayā murārir*  
*yamunā-kūla-vanāny alaṁcakāra \|\|*

**Because of his unfathomable attachment, his parents avoid giving him responsibilities. Playing with Rādhā incessantly, Kṛṣṇa ornaments the forests on the bank of the Yamunā River. Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 1.29**

The meter is *śārdūla-vikrīḍitam.* The title is *nāgara-nārāyaṇa* (clever Kṛṣṇa, shelter of women) because though he was present among the other gopīs, Rādhā did not see him.

## Part 8: Unashamed Kṛṣṇa

### Text 1

*aṣṭamaḥ sargaḥ*  
*vilakṣa-lakṣmīpatiḥ*  
*atha katham api yāminīṁ vinīya*  
*smara-śara-jarjaritāpi sā prabhāte \|*  
*anunaya-vacanaṁ vadantam agre*  
*praṇatam api priyam āha sābhyasūyam \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

atha - After that; katham - somehow; api - even; yaminim - the night; viniya - having passed; smara - of Cupid; sara - by the arrows; jarjarita - torn to pieces; api - though; sa - she (Radha); prabhate - in the morning; anunaya - of conciliation; vacanam - words; vadantam - \[who\] spoke; agre - in \[her\] presence; pranatam - \[and who\] offered respects; api - even; priyam - to her lover (Krsna); aha - she spoke; sabhyasuyam - with anger.

**Перевод:**

**Somehow passing the night, in the morning Rādhā, though pained by Cupid’s arrows, spoke to Kṛṣṇa, who offered respects to her and spoke conciliatory words. But she spoke with anger.**

**Комментарий:**

The word *atha* indicates auspiciousness or can mean “after that.” She who is endowed with the greatest good fortune (*sāḥ*), Rādhā, in the morning, spoke in anger to her beloved. What was she doing? She had passed the night with difficulty (*katham api)*. The word *yāminīm* (night—consisting of many *yāmas*), by mentioning its constituent parts *(yāmas*), suggests that the night was long, because of separation from her beloved, just as describing a young woman as having a large abdomen suggests she is pregnant. She was pierced with Cupid’s arrows, but in a reverse way. She was pained by seeing the marks of other women’s nails on his body, which was intolerable. Though pained because of this, she spoke. The lover is described. Her lover spoke conciliatory words—humble words to pacify her anger at his offenses. “I will not act like this again.” He bowed to her feet.

Or she began chastising him by finding faults in his character, concluding that he had enjoyed with another woman by the signs, though she was pleased with him. *Yāminī* means night according to *Amara-koṣa*.

“Though full of pain, she spoke to him in anger while he offered respects.” This indicates extra rasa since splendid rasa arises through *vāmatā* (uncooperative nature), whereas *rasābhāsa* arises by a woman’s compliant mood. Therefore, this action is most praiseworthy for Rādhā. Those who imagine otherwise are devoid of the excellence of *rasa*.

## Song 17: Go from here, Krishna, to your other woman!

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 17 \|\|**  
**bhairavi rāga, yati tāla**

### Text 2

*rajani-janita-guru-jāgara-rāga-kaṣāyitam alasa-nimeṣam \|*  
*vahati nayanam anurāgam iva sphuṭam udita-rasābhiniveśam \|\|2\|\|*

*hari hari yāhi mādhava yāhi keśava mā vada kaitava-vādam \|*  
*tām anusara sarasīruha-locana yā tava harati viṣādam \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

rajani - during the night; janita - produced; guru-jagara - by the extended wakefulness; raga - red; kasayitam - reddened; alasa -\[are\] lazy; nimesam - whose eyelids; vahati - bear; nayanam - \[your\] eyes; anuragam - redness; iva - as if; sphutam - clearly; udita - \[is\] indicated; rasa - for rasa \[with some woman\]; abhinivesam - that by which \[your\] desire.

hari - Alas! hari - Alas! yahi - Go; madhava - о Madhava \[Krsna\]! yahi - Go; kesava - о Kesava \[Krsna\]! ma - Do not; vada - speak; kaitava - false; vadam - words, tam - The woman; anusara - pursue; sarasiruha - \[are like\] lotuses; locana - whose eyes; ya - who; tava - your; harati - takes away; visadam - sorrow.

**Перевод:**

**Your eyes seem to be clearly red, indicating attraction to śṛṅgāra-rasa with some woman, and are reddened by staying awake all night, with heavy eyelids.**  
**Ah! Go away Mādhava! Go away Keśava! Do not speak false words. O lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa! Pursue the woman who takes away your sorrow. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

What Rādhā said is presented in a song.

The characteristics of the *rāga* are as follows:

*sarovara-sthe sphaṭikasya maṇḍape*  
*saroruhaiḥ śaṅkaram arcayantī \|*  
*tāla-prayoge pratibandha-gītā*  
*gaurī-tanur nārada bhairavīyam \|\|*

**O Ṇārada, Bhairavīi has a golden body, constantly sings songs with tāla, and worships Śiva with lotuses in a crystal pavilion in a lake.**

Ah, what misfortune *(hari hari*)! O Mādhava, O Keśava! Go, go from here. She repeats her words out of anger. Mādhava (husband of fickle Lakṣmī) indicates that he also must be fickle in love. Having criticized him, she is unsatisfied and addresses him as Keśava. Keśava means “person with hair.” This is according to the rule *keśād vo ‘nyatarasyām.* (Pāṇini 5.2.109 *keśa + va*) His hair has been ruffled since it was pulled by another woman. He says “I am surrendered only to you.” She says, “Do not speak false words.” *Kaitava* means false according to *Viśva.*

“I suffer for you.” You only flatter me with sweet words. O lotus-eyed! Follow that woman who takes way your sorrow. It is proper that the woman for whom you suffer can take away your sorrow. I am suffering. How can I take away your suffering?

His eyes are compared to lotuses since they are reddish and half opened at the end of night. Or she criticizes him for closing his eyes in mediation on his lover and having red eyes because of attachment to that woman.

“But I am attached to you. Why do you think I am attached to someone else?” O Kṛṣṇa! Your eye carries attachment to her. *Vahasi* is sometimes seen instead of *vahati*. You have eyes that seem attached to her. Those eyes are described. They are slightly red because of staying awake all night. Your eyelids are lazy. This laziness comes from carrying some other object. Clearly they indicate (*udita*) that you have been absorbed in śṛṅgāra-rasa with some other woman. The words hint that it is proper that your eyes should be red out of affection for me and they are filled with anger (*rāga*) while thinking, “Uselessly I have stayed awake in association with another woman.” *Sphuṭam* can modify *anurāgam*. Your eyes are reddish inside and outside and speak of your attachment to her (*udita-rasābhiniveṣam*). *Hari* expresses lamentation. *Kaśāya* means reddish.

### Text 3

*kajjala-malina-vilocana-cumbana-viracita-nīlima-rūpam \|*  
*daśana-vasanam aruṇaṁ tava krsṇa tanoti tanor anurūpam \|\|3\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kajjala - With mascara; malina - dark; vilocana - eyes; cumbana -by kissing; viracita - produced; nilima - \[is\] black; rupam - whose color; dasana - of \[your\] teeth; vasanam - the garment (your lips); arunam - red; tava - your; krsna - о Krsna; tanoti - possesses; tanor - of body; аnuruраm - the corresponding \[color\].

**Перевод:**

**Your red lips have become the color of your body, blackish because of kissing eyes anointed with mascara.**  
**“My eyes are red from staying awake to search for you. Why be afflicted with sorrow?”**

**Комментарий:**

O Kṛṣṇa! The red of your lips has become similar in color to your body. Your red lips have become black. They have become black from kissing eyes stained with black mascara. This clearly shows your devious nature. It is said that kisses are placed on the cheek, eyes and forehead. That his lips are stained with mascara indicates his lack of expertise. *Daśana-vāsas* means lips according to *Amara-koṣa.*

### Text 4

*vapur anuharati tava smara-saṅgara-khara-nakhara-kṣata-rekham \|*  
*marakata-śakala-kalita-kala-dhauta-liper iva rati-jaya-lekham \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

vapur - Body; anuharati - resembles; tava - your; smara - of love; sangara - in the battle; khara - sharp; nakhara - with fingernails; ksata - \[are\] inflicted; rekham - whose scratches; marakata - of emerald; sakala - on a piece; kalita - made; kala-dhauta - of gold; liper - of letters; iva - as if; rati - of Cupid; jaya - of victory; lekham - a letter.

**Перевод:**

**Your body resembles a victory letter of Cupid, with fingernail injuries inflicted during the battle of love, appearing like golden letters inscribed on a sapphire gem.**

**Комментарий:**

Your body resembles (*anuharati*) a victory letter of Cupid (*rati-jaya-lekham*), in which Cupid says “I have conquered this body.” The body has lines produced by injuries from sharp fingernails arising in the battle of love, which resemble golden (*kala-dhauta*) words written on a piece of sapphire. Kṛṣṇa’s black body is compared to a piece of sapphire. The marks from the nails are compared to a golden inscription. Gold is often described as reddish by poets. *Kaladauta* means gold according to *Viśva. Śakala* means a piece. *Nakhara* means a fingernail.

### Text 5

*caraṇa-kamala-galad-alaktaka-siktam idaṁ tava hṛdayam udāram \|*  
*darsayatīva bahir madana-druma-nava-kiśalaya-parivāram \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

carana-kamala - From a \[woman’s\] lotus-foot; galad - flowing; alaktaka - by lac; siktam - watered; idam - this; tava - your; hrdayam - heart; udaram - attractive; darsayati - shows; iva - as if; bahir - externally; madana - of love (or of Cupid); druma - \[arising\] from the tree; nava - new; kisalaya - of shoots; parivaram - the host.

**Перевод:**

**Your attractive heart externally shows the host of new shoots arising from the tree of love, watered by lac flowing from a woman’s lotus foot on your chest.**  
**“Forgetting the path, as I was coming, I was wounded by thorns on my body. That is what you see, not nail marks of a woman.”**

**Комментарий:**

Your heart here externally shows a group of new sprouts growing from the tree of love within your heart. Or *nava* can mean praiseworthy since shoots are new by their nature. *Nava* means praise according to *Viśva-koṣa.* The sprouts are watered by lac flowing from a woman’s lotus foot. Other trees however show sprouts after being watered at the root. This is the ornament called *rūpaka.* Your heart is very attractive (*udāram)* or huge, incapable of being hidden. The lotus feet indicate good fragrance. The tree of Cupid indicates attraction. Watered with lac indicates the coolness of the tree. The amorous position is called *krauñca*. This is described in Smara-dīpikā.

*Parivāra* means all.

### Text 6

*daśana-padaṁ bhavad-adhara-gataṁ mama janayati cetasi khedam*  
*kathayati katham adhunāpi mayā saha tava vapur etad abhedam \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

dasana - Of teeth; padam - the mark; bhavad - your; adhara - on lips; gatam - situated; mama - my, janayati - produces; cetasi - in heart; khedam - pain, kathayati katham - Why do you say; adhunapi - even now; maya - me; saha - with; tava - your; vapur - body; etad - this; abhedam - \[is\] identical?

**Перевод:**

**Why do you now say that your body is one with mine? The tooth marks on your lips give pain to my heart.**  
**“Sympathizing with your lamentation, I feel sickness in my heart. How can you think otherwise?” That is true. If you were not one with me, I should not have pain from seeing the wounds on your body. With this in mind she speaks.**

**Комментарий:**

The marks of teeth on your lips give me pain. How can you now say your body is one with me? Two bodies are not different if the injuries in one body are experienced as pain in another body. Indirectly it is inferred “How can our bodies be one, since I experience great suffering on seeing the signs of enjoyment (not suffering) of another woman on your body?”

By thinking of causes of difference, non-difference is denied. This is an example of *vibhāvana alaṅkāra.* Daṇḍī describes this:

*prasiddha-hetu-vyāvṛttyā yat kiñcit kāraṇāntaram \|*  
*yatra svābhāvikatvaṁ vā bhāvyaṁ sā ca vibhāvanā \|\|*

***Vibhāvanā*** **refers to thinking of another cause or assuming a natural condition, while excluding the well-known cause.**

### Text 7

*bahir iva malinataraṁ tava kṛṣṇa mano'pi bhaviṣyati nūnam \|*  
*katham atha vañcayase janam anugatam asama-śara-jvara-dūnam \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

bahir - Externally; iva - as if; malinataram - contamination; tava - your; krsna - о Krsna; mano - mind; api - even; bhavisyati - will become; nunam - for certain, katham - Why; atha - then; vancayase - do you cheat; janam - people; anugatam - surrendered \[to you\]; asama - of Cupid; sara - of the arrows, jvara - from the pain; dunam - \[and\] suffering?

**Перевод:**

**Your mind seems to show its contamination externally. Why do you cheat persons who are surrendered to you and suffering from the pains of Cupid?**  
**“I broke off a lotus to bring to you, but a bee suddenly emerged from the flower and bit my lip. This is not an injury from a woman’s tooth.”**

**Комментарий:**

O Kṛṣṇa! It is certain that your heart will become very contaminated like your body. “How can your know about contamination in my mind?” *Atha* indicates a question. It is not just internal contamination. Why do you cheat a person like me, dedicated to you, suffering from Cupid? This means that internal contamination of cheating a surrendered person cannot remain internal. It suggests that great contamination is visible from your trying to hide your injuries from teeth. *Atha* indicates a question according to *Viśva. Utprekṣā* ornament is used. Daṇḍī says:

*manye śaṅke dhruvaṁ prāyo nūnam ity evam ādibhiḥ \|*  
*utprekṣā vyajyate śabdair iva-śabdo’pi tādṛśaḥ \|\|*

***Utprekṣa*** **(simile) is indicated by use of words like** ***nūnam manye*****,** ***śaṅke, dhruvan*** **and** ***prāyaḥ*** **which indicate similarity like** ***iva.***

### Text 8

*bhramati bhavān abalā-kavalāya vaneṣu kim atra vicitram \|*  
*prathayati pūtanikaiva vadhū-vadha-nirdaya-bāla-caritram \|\|8\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

bhramati - Wanders; bhavan - your honor; abala - feeble women; kavalaya - for devouring; vanesu - in forests, kim - What; atra - with respect to that; vicitram - \[is\] astonishing? prathayati - Reveals; putanikaiva - indeed Putanika; vadhu - of young women; vadha -in the killing; nirdaya - merciless; bala - baby; caritram - \[your\] behavior.

**Перевод:**

**You wander in the forests in order to kill innocent women. That is not astonishing. The case of Pūtanā has shown that even as a baby you were merciless in killing women.**

**Комментарий:**

She clarifies what she has said by showing the cruel effects.

You wander in the forests in order to devour young women. What is astonishing about that? Nothing. The reason is given. Pūtanā, who was sent by Kaṁsa, shows your merciless nature as a child, in killing women. As a baby you killed a Rākṣasī who was giving her breast to you. In your *kaiśora* age now, is it astonishing if you kill girls with tender limbs like me?

Or, O merciless killer of women! Pūtaṇa proclaims your infant act (*bāla-caritam*) of killing the sister of Kaṁsa. The suffix *kā* in *pūtanikā* means the same as Pūtanā. The *khaṇḍitā* (betrayed) heroine is described in this verse. Bharata says:

*nidrā-kaṣāya-kalūsīkṛta-tāmra-netro*  
*nārī-nakha-vraṇa-vicitritāṅgaḥ \|*  
*yasyā kuto’pi gṛham eti patiḥ prabhāte*  
*sā khaṇḍiteti kathitā kavibhiḥ purāṇaiḥ \|\|*

**The betrayed woman is described by ancient poets as follows. Her husband comes from somewhere to the house in the morning with red eyes because of the contamination of sleep and marks of women’s nails on various parts of his body.**

### Text 9

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇita-rati-vañcita-khaṇḍita-yuvati-vilāpam \|*  
*śṛṇuta sudhā-madhuraṁ vibudhā vibudhālayato'pi durāpam \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva - By Sri Jayadeva; bhanita - described; rati - of love; vancita - cheated; khandita - \[and\] betrayed; yuvati - of the girl; vilapam - the lamentation; srnuta - hear; sudha - \[as\] nectar; madhuram - sweet; vibudha - о intelligent people; vibudhalayato -for the devatas', api - even; durapam - difficult to attain.

**Перевод:**

**O intelligent people! Please hear the lamentation of the girl betrayed, cheated of love, as described by Jayadeva. It is the sweetest nectar, not attained by the devatās.**

**Комментарий:**

O learned people who taste Kṛṣṇa’s *mādhurya-rasa*! Please hear the lamentation of the betrayed lover, devoid of enjoyment with Kṛṣṇa as described by Jayadeva. It is sweeter than nectar. It cannot be attained even in Svarga. It is that rare. It is said:

*tasyaiva hetoḥ prayateta kovido*  
*na labhyate yad bhramatām upary adhaḥ \|*  
*tal labhyate duḥkhavad anyataḥ sukhaṁ*  
*kālena sarvatra gabhīra-raṁhasā \|\|*

**The wise man strives for that happiness which is not attained by** ***jīvas*** **wandering from Brahma-loka to the lowest species. Material happiness is attained in all cases without endeavor, by the law of karma, just like distress, through the force of fast moving time. SB 1.5.18**

*tava kathāmṛtaṁ tapta-jīvanaṁ*  
*kavibhir īḍitaṁ kalmaṣāpaham*  
*śravaṇa-maṅgalaṁ śrīmad ātataṁ*  
*bhuvi gṛṇanti ye bhūri-dā janāḥ*

**The nectar of your topics is the life of those suffering in this material world. These narrations, praised by learned sages, eradicate one’s sinful reactions and bestow good fortune upon whoever hears them. These narrations are most excellent and are broadcast all over the world. Those who speak these topics are the greatest benefactors. SB 10.31.9**

Or it is harder to attain than Vaikuṇṭha (*vibudhālayataḥ*) since Kṛṣṇa and Rādhā are the *ālambana* of unlimited *prema*. It is said in Bhāgavatam:

*yad-vāñchayā śrīr lalanācarat tapo*  
*vihāya kāmān su-ciraṁ dhṛta-vratā*

**For this end, the goddess of fortune performed austerities for centuries, giving up all other desires and taking austere vows. SB 10.16.36**

*Vibhudḥa* means a learned person or a devatā according to *Viśva.*

There is another meaning. O hear about the false enactment of betrayal with attraction (*rati*) having a variety of symptoms in separation. Or hear about the dry crying of the young woman whose lips were bitten and was cheated in love, all on the pretext of obtaining enjoyment. Thus she cried out “Go away, go away.” That scene is rarely attained by the devatās, and tastier than nectar.

“I am grieving because of love. Do not say that I should go away. Shall I follow you to enjoy?” Rādhā says, “That is not my desire. Go and follow her.”

Kṛṣṇa says, “Your eye carries the attraction or color of Cupid. Why do you cheat me?”

Rādhā says, “Your body is filled with intense lust, since it is black (the color of śṛṅgāra-rasa)*.* Because of that, you red lips have become black like your body. You actually see everything in terms of lust. Thus your lips were blackened by kissing the eyes of a woman anointed with mascara.” She speaks till the end of verse 5.

Then Kṛṣṇa speaks. “Let that be. Please give a festival of love to me, your beloved.” He then speaks of oneness. “We are one. If you were not one with me, why could you say that your body is non-different?”

Rādhā says, “Cheater! Do you desire that again? How can you injure an obedient person with your nails and then cheat?” (verse7) She continues till the end of verse 8.

In this way with various gestures and moods, the crest jewels of love, completely overcome, enjoy and speak in a joking way. This is described by the famous poet in eight verses. It will be said *adhara-sudhā-pāne kathā-narmabhiḥ*: with joking words he drank the nectar of her lips. This explanation also agrees with the next verse. It is spoken with jokes, out of enthusiasm for play. She feels embarrassed in front of the sakhīs by the display of attraction to amorous play. She laments from pain during extreme enjoyment because of her soft limbs.

### Text 10

*tavedaṁ paśyantyāḥ prasarad-anurāgaṁ bahir iva*  
*priyā-pādālakta-cchuritam aruṇa-cchāya-hṛdayam \|*  
*mamādya prakhyāta-praṇaya-bhara-bhaṅgena kitava*  
*tvad-ālokaḥ śokād api kim api lajjāṁ janayati \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tava - Your; idam - this; pasyantyah - for one who sees prasarad - \[is\] strong; anuragam - whose love; bahir - externally; iva - as if; priya -of a lover; pada - from the foot; alakta - with the lac; cchuritam - \[is\] anointed; aruna - \[is\] red; cchaya - whose color; hrdayam - chest; mama - my; adya - today; prakhyata - famous; pranaya - of \[our\] attraction; bhara - great; bhangena - because of \[your\] breaking; kitava - о cheater; tvad - of you; alokah - the sight; sokad - beyond lamentation; api - even; kim api - unspeakable; lajjam - shame; janayati - produces.

**Перевод:**

**O cheater! Seeing you today produces shame more than lamentation, because you broke our famous, intense relationship. I see your chest stained red from the lac of a lover’s foot, as if your love for her has become externalized.**

**Комментарий:**

O cheater! Seeing you produces unspeakable (*kim api*) shame more than lamentation, because of the destruction of our famous, intense attraction. Seeing you also produces lamentation, but more that lamentation, it produces shame. In your presence, as a lover, seeing you produces the greatest bliss. Now since you are not my lover, seeing you produces lamentation. I see your heart directly (*idam*) covered with lac from the foot of another woman. It is beautiful red. Another version has *aruṇa-dyoti* instead *of arunā-cchāya*. Your chest shines like the color of sunrise. It is like attraction spilling out of your heart because it is so full. More unfortunate than lamentation is the worry of being mocked by women in the opposing camp. *Chāyya* means beauty. *Aruṇa* means the color of sunrise or red.

### Text 11

*antar-mohana-mauli-ghūrṇana-calan-mandāra-visraṁśana-*  
*stabdhākarṣaṇa-dṛṣṭi-harṣaṇa-mahā-mantraḥ kuraṅgī-dṛśām \|*  
*dṛpyad-dānava-dūyamāna-diviṣad-durvāra-duḥkhāpadāṁ*  
*bhraṁśaḥ kaṁsa-ripor vyapohayatu vaḥ śreyāṁsi vaṁśī-ravaḥ \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

antar - heart; mohana - for \[their\] bewilderment; mauli - of \[their\] heads; ghurnana - for the shaking; calan - trembling; mandara - of \[their\] mandara flowers; visramsana - for the falling-down; stabdha - of the proud \[women\] akarsana - for the bringing under control; drsti - to \[their\] eyes; harsana - \[and\] for the delight; maha - great; mantrah - a mantra; kurangi-drsam - of doe-eyed women; drpyad -proud; danava - by demons; duyamana - afflicted; divisad - of the devatas; durvara - unavoidable; duhkha - of the pain; apadam - of the obtaining; bhramsah - \[and\] the decrease; kamsa-ripor - of the destroyer of Kamsa (Krsna); vyapohayatu - may \[it\] bestow; vah - upon you; sreyamsi - auspiciousness; vamsl - of the flute; ravah -the sound.

**Перевод:**

**May the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute, a powerful mantra that causes bewilderment in the hearts of doe-eyed women, that makes them shake their heads in approval, that makes the** ***mandāra*** **flowers in their hair fall to the ground, that brings even proud women under control, that makes the eyes open wide in joy, and that allays devatās’ worries of unavoidable pain inflicted by proud demons, bestow auspiciousness upon you!**

**Комментарий:**

The poet offers blessings at the end of the section.

May the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute bestow *(vyapohayatu*) auspiciousness to you. Destroyer of Kaṁsa (*kaṁsa-ripoḥ*) indicates his ability to give auspiciousness. The sound is described. It is a powerful mantra for producing results in the form of making eyes of doe-eyed women open on hearing it, bringing even proud women under control *(stabdha akarṣaṇa*), making the flowers on their heads fall to the ground (*visramśana*) when late in going to meet him, making their heads nod in praise, and bewildering their minds. That sound destroys the devatās’ worries arising from unavoidable suffering inflicted by proud demons. Hearing the sound, the devatās are free of fear of suffering from demons. This description indicates the great ability of the hero. Moreover the flute produces delight in the devatās. What then to speak of the delight they receive from the pastimes of the Supreme Lord?

## Song 18: Please forgive Krishna, oh friend Radha

**\|\| prabandhaḥ 18 \|\|**  
**Gurjarī-rāga yati-tāla**

## Part 9: Attractive Mukunda

### Text 2

*harir abhisarati vahati madhu-pavane \|*  
*kim aparam adhika-sukhaṁ sakhi bhuvane \|\|2\|\|*

*mādhave mā kuru mānini mānam aye \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

harir - Krsna; abhisarati - is coming to meet \[you\]; vahati - \[while it\] blows; madhu - of Spring; pavane - the breeze, kim - What; aparam - other; adhika - great; sukham - happiness; sakhi - о female friend; bhuvane - \[is there\] in the world?

madhave - With Krsna; ma - do not; kuru - create; manini - о angry woman; manam - anger; aye - o! (or having good fortune)

**Перевод:**

**He is coming to meet you while the spring breeze blows. O friend, who can give greater happiness in this world?**  
**O angry woman! Do not be angry with Kṛṣṇa. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

O angry woman! Do not be angry with Kṛṣṇa*. Aye* (having good fortune) can also modify *kṛṣṇe.* Do not show anger which will delay meeting him. The word Mādhava indicates he will please her, though he is the wife of Lakṣmī. Therefore do not be angry. This is the chorus.

While the spring breeze blows, Kṛṣṇa is coming. O friend! What great happiness is there in the three worlds other than *(aparam*) meeting Kṛṣṇa? There is no other happiness. Or what happiness is experienced with Lakṣmī and others in Vaikuṇṭha? None at all. Hari means “he who can destroy your pain.”

### Text 1

*navamaḥ sargaḥ*  
*mugdha-mukundaḥ*  
*tām atha manmatha-khinnāṁ*  
*rati-rasa-bhinnāṁ viṣāda-sampannām \|*  
*anucintita-hari-caritāṁ*  
*kalahāntaritām uvāca rahasi sakhī \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tam - To her (Radha); atha - then; manmatha - because of Cupid; khinnam - suffering; rati - of love; rasa - by the srhgara-rasa; bhinnam - broken; visada - of distress; sampannam - possessed; anucintita -\[were\] repeatedly thought; hari - with Krsna; caritam - by whom pastimes; kalaha - because of \[her\] harsh words; antaritam - \[and\] having separated \[from Krsna\]; uvaca - spoke; rahasi - privately; sakhi - a female friend.

**Перевод:**

**The sakhī spoke to distressed Rādhā, suffering because of love, whose heart had filled with śṛṅgāra-rasa, who had rejected her lover with harsh words and who was now continually thinking of his previous pastimes.**

**Комментарий:**

After Rādhā castigates Kṛṣṇa out of anger in love and then repents, a *sakhī* speaks to her. The word *atha* indicates auspiciousness or “shortly after that.” The sakhī spoke in a private place to Rādhā, separated from her lover after she spoke harsh words *(kalahāntaritām*), in repentant state. She was suffering because of love. Her heart had been mixed with *śṛṅgāra-rasa* or torn apart by attachment to enjoyment and therefore was distressed (*viṣāda-sampannām*). Therefore she again and again remembered the previous pastimes she had with Kṛṣṇa or his upright character at that time. This is the *kalahāntaritā* heroine. Bharata describes her:

*prāṇeśvaraṁ praṇaya-kopa-bhareṇa bhītaṁ*  
*yā cāṭū-kāram acirād avadhīrya paścāt \|*  
*santapyate madana-vahni-śikhā-sahasrair*  
*bāṣpākulā ca kalahāntaritā tu sā syāt \|\|*

**The** ***kalahāntaritā*** **woman is full of tears and is pained by thousands of Cupid’s fiery arrows, after having disrespected with intense anger in love her frightened lover who spoke pleasing words.**

*Bhinna* means mixed or split.

### Text 3

*tāla-phalād api gurum atisarasam \|*  
*kiṁ viphalī-kuruṣe kuca-kalaśam \|\|3\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

tala - Tala; phalad - than the fruit; api - even; gurum - \[which are\] better; atisarasam - \[and\] very full of rasa-, him - why; viphall -useless; kuruse - do you render; kuca-kalasam - \[your\] pot-breasts?

**Перевод:**

**Why do you make useless your pot-like breasts, better than the tāla fruit, full of śṛṅgāra-rasa?**

**Комментарий:**

Why do you make your pot-like breasts useless? They will be successful by offering them to Kṛṣṇa’s hands. Otherwise they are useless. They are better than *tāla* fruits, since they are firmer and bigger. They are filled with *śṛṅgāra-rasa*. Just as the *tāla* fruit by squeezing produces juice, so your breasts by squeezing produce *śṛṅgāra-rasa*. A pot is offered for auspiciousness to the lover’s hand when the lover comes. You should to the same with your breasts.

### Text 4

*kati na kathitam idam anupadam aciram \|*  
*mā parihara harim atīśaya-ruciram \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kati - How many times; na - not; kathitam - \[was\] spoken \[by me\]; idam - this; anupadam - at every step; aciram - at this time? ma - Do not; parihara - give up; harim - Krsna; atisaya - preemminently; ruciram - attractive.

**Перевод:**

**Did I not tell you many times about this at every step? Do not give up most attractive Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Did I say nothing about this at every step at this time? Yes, I did. Do not disregard the very attractive Kṛṣṇa who steals the heart.

### Text 5

*kim iti viṣīdasi rodiṣi vikalā \|*  
*vihasati yuvatī-sabhā tava sakalā \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

kim - Why; iti - so; visidasi - are you emotional; rodisi - \[and\] weeping; vikala - depressed? vihasati - Are laughing \[at this\]; yuvati - of young women; sabha - party; tava - your; sakala - entire.

**Перевод:**

**Why are you depressed? You are emotional and weeping. All the young women are laughing at this.**

**Комментарий:**

Why do you lament? “I am not lamenting.” You weep while being upset. All *(sakalā)* the young women are laughing. Or they laugh at your actions. Or the young woman endowed with the sixty four arts, expert in rasa (*sakalā)* laugh.

### Text 6

*sajala-nalinī-dala-śīlita\[\[25\]\]-śayane \|*  
*harim avalokaya saphalaya nayane \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sajala - Moist; nalini - of the lotus; dala - of the petals; silita - \[is\] made; sayane - о she whose bed; harim - Krsna; avalokaya - look at; saphalaya - \[and\] make successful; nayane - \[your\] eyes.

**Перевод:**

**O Rādhā, lying on a bed of moist lotus petals! Look at Kṛṣṇa and make your eyes successful.**

**Комментарий:**

“What should I do?” O Rādhā, lying on a bed of moist lotus petals! Look at Kṛṣṇa the jewel of heroes endowed with all qualities like beauty and make your eyes successful. It is said *akṣanvatām ḥalam idam*: we do not know any other success for those with eyes than the face of the younger one who plays the flute. (SB 10.21.7) You are pained by separation, and lying on a bed of moist lotus petals. By glancing at him affectionately, since he is following you, become successful. Or have I not told you many times as you lie on a bed of moist lotus petals because of the pain of separation. Or look at Kṛṣṇa lying on a cool bed of moist lotus petals, grieving in separation from you?

### Text 7

*janayasi manasi kim iti guru-khedam \|*  
*śṛṇu mama vacanam anīhita-bhedam \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

janayasi - Do you produce; manasi - in \[your\] mind; kim - why; iti - such; guru - intense; khedam - suffering? srnu - hear; mama - my; vacanam - words; anihita - of \[what is\] disagreeable; bhedam - the breaking.

**Перевод:**

**Why do you grieve so intensely in your mind? Hear my words, which are beneficial.**

**Комментарий:**

Why do you produce intense suffering in your heart? Hear the words I will speak. The words are against anything disagreeable. They are beneficial for young couples. Or hear my words so that there is no difference with me. You should understand, “This woman is my friend. She is not different from me. She does not speak anything to unsettle me.” With this understanding, listen to my words.

### Text 8

*harir upayātu vadatu bahu-madhuram \|*  
*kim iti karoṣi hṛdayam atividhuram \|\|8\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

harir - Krsna; upayatu - may \[he\] come; vadatu - \[and\] speak; bahu - very; madhuram - sweetly! kim - Why; iti - such; karosi - do you cause; hrdayam - \[your\] heart; atividhuram - more suffering?

**Перевод:**

**May Kṛṣṇa come! May he speak sweetly! Why do you cause your heart more suffering?**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa should come near you. He should speak every sweetly, or in a pleasing manner. If that is so, why make your heart suffer?

### Text 9

*śrī-jayadeva-bhaṇitam atilalitam \|*  
*sukhayatu rasika-janaṁ hari-caritam \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

sri-jayadeva - By Sri Jayadeva; bhanitam - described; atilalitam - attractive with srhgara-rasa; sukhayatu - may \[they\] delight; rasika - skilled in tasting the \[excellent\] rasa \[of bhakti\]; janam - devotees; hari - of Krsna; caritam - the activities.

**Перевод:**

**May the activities of Kṛṣṇa filled with attractive śṛṅgāra-rasa*****,*** **described by Jayadeva, create joy in the devotees, skillful at tasting the excellent rasa of bhakti.**

**Комментарий:**

These activities are descriptions of *śṛṅgāra-rasa* and attractive with songs (*atilalitam).*

*śṛṅgāra-pracurā ceṣṭā yatra taṁ lalitaṁ viduḥ \|\|*

**Where there are activities of explicit conjugal nature it is known as** ***lalita,*** **voluptuousness. BRS 2.1.267**

### Text 10

*snigdhe yat paruṣāsi praṇamati stabdhāsi yad rāgiṇi*  
*dveṣasthāsi yad unmukhe vimukhatāṁ yātāsi tasmin priye \|*  
*tad yuktaṁ viparīta-kāriṇi tava śrīkhaṇḍa-carcā viṣaṁ*  
*śītāṁśus tapano himaṁ hutavahaḥ krīḍā-mudo yātanāḥ \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

snigdhe - \[When he is\] gentle; yat - since; parusa - harsh; asi - your are; yat - since; pranamati - \[when he\] offers respects; stabdha - obstinate; asi - you are; yad - since; ragini - \[when he\] shows affection; dvesa - in hatred; stha - engaged; asi - you are; yad - \[and\] since; unmukhe - \[when he is\] facing \[you\]; vimukhatam - to \[a state of\] turning away \[from him\] ;yata - gone; asi - you are; tasmin - he; priye - \[your\] beloved; tad - therefore; yuktam - appropriate \[condition\]; viparita - opposite; karini - о woman who makes; tava - your; srikhanda - of the sandal tree; carca - the paste; visam -\[becomes\] poison; sitamsus - the moon; tapano - \[is\] causing pain; himam - snow; hutavahah - \[becomes\] fire; krida - of pastimes; mudo - \[and\] the pleasures; yatanah - \[become\] agonies.

**Перевод:**

**O Rādhā, you take things in the wrong way! Because of this, everything has happened. Sandalwood becomes poison. The moon gives pain. Snow becomes fire. Pleasure of pastimes becomes agony. This is because, when he speaks gently, you reply harshly. When he offers respect, you do not respond. When he shows affection, you show hatred. When he faces you, you turn and go away.**

**Комментарий:**

O Rādhā, who takes things in the opposite way! For this reason *(tat)* everything has happened to you. One who acts in an opposite way has opposite results. What is that? Sandalwood paste becomes poison, giving pain. And the moon gives pain like the sun, though its nature is cooling. Snow gives pain like fire. Pleasure of playing becomes pain because you use harsh words when your beloved *(snigdhe*) shows affection with sweet words. When he offers respect, you do not respond. When he shows affection, you remain there showing hatred. When he stands before you to embrace you, you turn and go away. *Yātanā* means sharp pain according to *Amara-koṣa.* The ornament is called *virodha.* The meter is *śārdūla-vikrīḍitam.*

### Text 11

*sāndrānanda-purandarādi-diviṣad-vṛndair amandādarād*  
*ānamrair mukuṭendra-nīla-maṇibhiḥ sandarśitendindiram \|*  
*svacchandaṁ makaranda-sundara-galan-mandākinī-meduraṁ*  
*śrī-govinda-padāravindam aśubha-skandāya vandāmahe \|\|11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**To destroy sins which obstruct bhakti we offer respects to Govinda’s lotus feet, which are charming with the Gaṅgā flowing beautifully and freely from them like honey, covered with bees in the form of sapphires from the crowns of blissful devatās offering respects in great awe.**

**Комментарий:**

The author offers blessings at the end of the section.

We offer respects to the lotus feet of Govinda. Why? To destroy sins which are obstacles to bhakti (aśubha*-skandāya*). Dative case is used to indicate “for a purpose.” Plural is used to indicate his students as well. Destroying sins is easily done, for those feet are attractive, with the Gaṅgā flowing beautifully and freely like honey. Flowing from his feet the Gaṅgā purifies the whole world. What then to speak of the purifying power of his feet themselves? There is honey flowing from the lotus, which is compared to the Gaṅgā. This is *upamā alaṅkāra*. The lotus feet seemed to be covered with bees manifested in the form of sapphire crowns, as groups of blissful devatās headed by Indra bow down completely out of great reverence. This illustrates that his feet are worthy of respect. The lotus is described with the feet being lotuses, the Gaṅgā being their honey and the sapphires in the devatās crowns being the bees. The word Mandākinī (flowing steadily) indicates the fame of the Lord since that river flows constantly, spreading out everywhere like his fame. The title of this section Manda-mukunda means indolent or diligent Kṛṣṇa.\[\[26\]\]
